# Every theorem in the library

Generated by `python3 tools/theorems_md.py` from `build/theorems.tsv`, which `Check.lean` writes. Every theorem below compiles with core Lean 4.37.0-rc1 and uses only Lean's standard axioms (propext, Classical.choice, Quot.sound), with no `sorry`.

## FusedAlgebra.Base (46)

- `CircleOp.circInv_circ`
- `CircleOp.circK_assoc`
- `CircleOp.circK_comm`
- `CircleOp.circK_def`
- `CircleOp.circK_either`: k = −1 is "at least one of two independent events".
- `CircleOp.circK_flip`: Flipping both signs flips the weight.
- `CircleOp.circK_lift`: The weighted lift: 1 + k·(a ◯[k] b) = (1 + k·a)(1 + k·b).
- `CircleOp.circK_neg_one_mirror`: k = −1 is circle-operation mirrored: a ◯[−1] b = −((−a) ◯ (−b)).
- `CircleOp.circK_one`: k = 1: the plain circle operation.
- `CircleOp.circK_scale`: The circle operation with dial k is the plain circle operation seen through x ↦ k·x.
- `CircleOp.circK_wall`: The weighted wall: −1/k absorbs everything under ◯[k].
- `CircleOp.circK_weight_zero`: k = 0: no corner, plain addition.
- `CircleOp.circK_zero`
- `CircleOp.circ_assoc`
- `CircleOp.circ_circInv`: Everywhere but the wall there is an exact way back.
- `CircleOp.circ_comm`
- `CircleOp.circ_def`
- `CircleOp.circ_excess`: The circle operation is never less than its two pieces added: the corner a·b is extra.
- `CircleOp.circ_lift`: The lift: the circle operation is multiplication seen through x ↦ 1 + x.
- `CircleOp.circ_lift'`: The lift with the 1 on the right (handy on Nat, where we add 1 to both sides).
- `CircleOp.circ_zero`: 0 is the identity of the circle operation (because 1 is the identity of ×).
- `CircleOp.foldCirc_append`: The whole is the first part ◯ the second part.
- `CircleOp.foldCirc_lift`: The list lift: 1 + (x₁ ◯ … ◯ xₙ) = (1 + x₁)⋯(1 + xₙ).
- `CircleOp.foldCirc_perm`: Order never matters: any reshuffle gives the same total.
- `CircleOp.inv_one_add`: Under the lift the inverse is the ordinary reciprocal.
- `CircleOp.mpow_add`: Powers add: a^(◯(m+n)) = a^(◯m) ◯ a^(◯n).
- `CircleOp.mpow_eq`: The power in closed form: a^(◯n) = (1 + a)^n − 1.
- `CircleOp.mpow_lift`: 1 + a^(◯n) = (1 + a)^n.
- `CircleOp.msub_circ`: Subtraction undoes the circle operation (Fisher's rule for real rates).
- `CircleOp.mul_pow'`
- `CircleOp.neg_one_absorbs`: The wall: −1 absorbs everything combined with it by the circle operation.
- `CircleOp.no_circInv_at_wall`: At the wall there is none: nothing combines by the circle operation with −1 to give 0.
- `CircleOp.one_add_foldCirc_eq_corners`: The corner expansion: 1 + (x₁ ◯ … ◯ xₙ) is the sum over every corner of the cube (every sub-list) of the product of its entries.
- `CircleOp.one_pow'`
- `CircleOp.pow_add'`
- `CircleOp.pw_add`
- `CircleOp.pw_eq_pow`
- `CircleOp.pw_mul`
- `CircleOp.pw_mul_base`
- `CircleOp.pw_one`
- `CircleOp.sumL_append`
- `CircleOp.sumL_map_cons`
- `CircleOp.wpow_lift`: 1 + k·a^(◯[k]n) = (1 + k·a)^n.
- `CircleOp.wpow_neg_one`: The weighted power at k = −1: n independent chances, 1 − (1 − a)^n.
- `CircleOp.wpow_one`
- `CircleOp.zero_circ`

## FusedAlgebra.Rungs (36)

- `CircleOp.circ_iter`
- `CircleOp.fexp_base_iter`: Rung 3 iterated on the base side with a fixed step c: lifts to a double exponential, the discrete flow behind Y' = λ·Y·log Y.
- `CircleOp.fexp_carries_circ`: The fused exponent carries the circle operation inside it.
- `CircleOp.fexp_circ_right`: Right distributivity over the circle operation: fexp (a ◯ b) c = fexp a c ◯ fexp b c.
- `CircleOp.fexp_example`
- `CircleOp.fexp_iter`
- `CircleOp.fexp_lift`
- `CircleOp.fexp_not_assoc`
- `CircleOp.fexp_not_comm`
- `CircleOp.fexp_sadd`: Sum law: fexp a (sadd b c) = fexp a b ◯ fexp a c, with sadd the rung-1 shifted add.
- `CircleOp.fexp_tower`: Tower law: fexp (fexp a b) c = fexp a (b ◯ c).
- `CircleOp.fexp_zero_left`: 0 is a left absorber, so there is no left identity of the form 0.
- `CircleOp.fexp_zero_right`
- `CircleOp.fpent_iter`: Rung 5 is rung 4 iterated on the exponent side.
- `CircleOp.fpent_lift`

- `CircleOp.fpent_zero_right`
- `CircleOp.ftet_iter`: Rung 4 is rung 3 iterated on the exponent side.
- `CircleOp.ftet_lift`
- `CircleOp.ftet_no_circ_dist`: The rung-3 laws stop at rung 4: distributivity over the circle operation fails ...
- `CircleOp.ftet_no_sum`: ... and the sum law fails.
- `CircleOp.ftet_no_tower`: ... the tower law fails ...

## FusedAlgebra.Foundations.FusedPeano (65)

- `CircFound.FusedPeano.add_assoc'`
- `CircFound.FusedPeano.add_comm'`
- `CircFound.FusedPeano.add_definable`: + from ×: Julia Robinson's trick (1949). For z ≠ 0, x + y = z exactly when (xz + 1)(yz + 1) = z²(xy + 1) + 1.
- `CircFound.FusedPeano.add_right_cancel'`
- `CircFound.FusedPeano.add_succ'`

- `CircFound.FusedPeano.add_zero'`
- `CircFound.FusedPeano.circ_assoc`: The circle operation is associative: a 3-D box built two ways.
- `CircFound.FusedPeano.circ_comm`: The circle operation is commutative: the box turned on its side.
- `CircFound.FusedPeano.circ_eq`: In the derived semiring, a + b + a·b is the primitive ◯.
- `CircFound.FusedPeano.circ_eq'`
- `CircFound.FusedPeano.circ_is`
- `CircFound.FusedPeano.circ_sadd`: Rung 2 distributes over rung 1 exactly, with no correction term: a ◯ (sadd b c) = sadd (a ◯ b) (a ◯ c). Two boxes of the same width, stacked. Lineage: one of four earlier proofs of the rung-1 distributive law; the unified, standalone proof is `RungOne.circ_sadd_distrib` (Foundations.RungOneSemiring), and Foundations.RungOneLineage rederives this one from it. Standard name: the distributive law of the transported ring (generated semiring with g(x) = 1 + x).

- `CircFound.FusedPeano.circ_zero`

- `CircFound.FusedPeano.hyp_one`
- `CircFound.FusedPeano.hyp_pow`
- `CircFound.FusedPeano.ind`

- `CircFound.FusedPeano.ladN_succ`
- `CircFound.FusedPeano.ladN_two`: The schema's rung 2 is the primitive ◯.
- `CircFound.FusedPeano.ladN_zero_right`
- `CircFound.FusedPeano.lad_lift`: Every rung of the fused ladder is the ordinary hyperoperation seen through x ↦ 1 + x: F n a b + 1 = H n (a + 1) (b + 1), for every n.
- `CircFound.FusedPeano.lad_one`
- `CircFound.FusedPeano.lad_three`
- `CircFound.FusedPeano.lad_two`
- `CircFound.FusedPeano.mul_add'`
- `CircFound.FusedPeano.mul_assoc'`
- `CircFound.FusedPeano.mul_comm'`
- `CircFound.FusedPeano.mul_definable`: × from S and ◯: m·n = k exactly when one side is 0 and k = 0, or m = S m', n = S n', k = S k' and m' ◯ n' = k'.
- `CircFound.FusedPeano.mul_one'`
- `CircFound.FusedPeano.mul_succ'`
- `CircFound.FusedPeano.mul_zero'`
- `CircFound.FusedPeano.recN_succ`
- `CircFound.FusedPeano.recN_zero`

- `CircFound.FusedPeano.sadd_comm`: Rung 1 is commutative: two rods joined either way round.

- `CircFound.FusedPeano.toN_bijective`
- `CircFound.FusedPeano.toN_circ`: Counting carries the circle operation to the circle operation: (a + b + ab) ↦ a ◯ b.

## FusedAlgebra.Foundations.RungOneLineage (10)

- `RungOneLineage.fadd_eq`: `AbstractAlgebra.fadd` (abstract-algebra/lean, section 5, "rung 1 on a ring") is the unified shifted add.
- `RungOneLineage.fl_sadd_unified`: `LinearAlgebraCirc.fl_sadd` says fused-linear maps respect the unified shifted add: they are the linear maps of the rung-1 ring (semimodule maps over it). Restated with the unified name.
- `RungOneLineage.fused_ring_from_unified`: `AbstractAlgebra.fadd_group` and `fused_zero_absorbs` are parts of the unified `RungOne.isCommRing` (additive group with zero −1; −1 absorbs).
- `RungOneLineage.linearAlgebra_sadd_eq`: `LinearAlgebraCirc.sadd` (`LinearAlgebraCirc.sadd` before 1.0; linear-algebra/lean, section 7, "the fused addition, zero −1") is the unified shifted add.
- `RungOneLineage.madd_sadd_from_unified`: `FusedPeano.circ_sadd` (`madd_sadd` before 1.0; axioms/lean/Axioms/FusedPeano.lean), "rung 2 distributes over rung 1", proved there by induction from the fused Peano axioms. Here it is rederived from the unified law: every model is a copy of Nat (`toN_surjective`), counting carries the model's sadd and circ to `RungOne.sadd` and `RungOne.circ` (`toN_sadd`, `toN_circ`), and on Nat the unified law holds.
- `RungOneLineage.multAdd_eq_circ`: `CircleOp.circ` (Base, the project's first operation, called `CircleOp.circ` and "mult-add" before 1.0) is `RungOne.circ`.
- `RungOneLineage.multAdd_fadd_distrib_from_unified`: `AbstractAlgebra.circ_fadd_distrib` (`multAdd_fadd_distrib` before 1.0) is an instance of the unified law.
- `RungOneLineage.multAdd_sAdd_distrib_from_unified`: `PvsNP.circ_sAdd_distrib` (`multAdd_sAdd_distrib` before 1.0) is an instance of the unified law.
- `RungOneLineage.rungs_sadd_eq`: `CircleOp.sadd` (Rungs, `MultAdd.sadd` before 1.0, from ns-rungs/lean/FusedRungs.lean): rung 1 of the fused hyperoperation ladder on Nat. It is the unified shifted add.
- `RungOneLineage.sAdd_eq`: `PvsNP.sAdd` (p-vs-np, Nov 25, 2026, "shifted add on the shifted ruler") is the unified shifted add.

- `RungOne.CommRingLaws.add_inv`
- `RungOne.CommRingLaws.add_zero`

- `RungOne.circK_one`
- `RungOne.circK_pseudoAdd_distrib`: The dial-k distributive law, for every k ≠ 0.
- `RungOne.circ_assoc`
- `RungOne.circ_comm`
- `RungOne.circ_neg_one`: −1 (the wall) absorbs under ∘₁.
- `RungOne.circ_sadd_distrib`: **The unified distributive law.** a ∘₁ (b ⊕ c) = (a ∘₁ b) ⊕ (a ∘₁ c), with ⊕ the shifted add. Proved directly from the definitions.
- `RungOne.circ_zero`
- `RungOne.gen_hom`: The generator g(x) = 1 + kx carries the pair to (+, ×).
- `RungOne.isCommRing`: **The rung-1 pair is a commutative ring**, zero −1, one 0.
- `RungOne.isCommRingK`: **For every k ≠ 0, (pseudoAdd k, circK k) is a commutative ring**, zero −1/k, one 0.
- `RungOne.isCommSemiringNoZero`: Every commutative semiring gives the rung-1 laws (no zero needed). On Nat this is the positive integers' (+, ×) moved down by one.
- `RungOne.pseudoAdd_one`
- `RungOne.sadd_assoc`
- `RungOne.sadd_circ_distrib`: The same law from the right.
- `RungOne.sadd_comm`
- `RungOne.sadd_inv`: The shifted-add inverse of a is −a − 2.
- `RungOne.sadd_neg_one`: −1 is the identity of the shifted add.
- `RungOne.shift_bij`: … and it is a bijection, with inverse x ↦ x − 1.
- `RungOne.shift_circ`: The shift x ↦ 1 + x carries `circ` to ×.
- `RungOne.shift_hom`: The shift is a homomorphism of the whole structure: both operations, zero and one.
- `RungOne.shift_sadd`: The shift x ↦ 1 + x carries `sadd` to +.

## FusedAlgebra.Topics.AbstractAlgebra (62)

- `AbstractAlgebra.CommGroupLaws.inv`
- `AbstractAlgebra.CommGroupLaws.toCommMonoidLaws`
- `AbstractAlgebra.CommMonoidLaws.assoc`
- `AbstractAlgebra.CommMonoidLaws.comm`
- `AbstractAlgebra.CommMonoidLaws.ident`
- `AbstractAlgebra.OpIso.left`
- `AbstractAlgebra.OpIso.map_id`
- `AbstractAlgebra.OpIso.map_op`
- `AbstractAlgebra.OpIso.right`
- `AbstractAlgebra.OpIso.self_op`
- `AbstractAlgebra.circK_commMonoid`
- `AbstractAlgebra.circK_unit_iff`: The same for the weighted law: a is a ◯[k]-unit exactly when 1 + k·a is invertible.
- `AbstractAlgebra.circ_commMonoid`
- `AbstractAlgebra.circ_fadd_distrib`: The circle operation distributes over shifted addition. Lineage: one of four earlier proofs of the rung-1 distributive law; the unified, standalone proof is `RungOne.circ_sadd_distrib` (Foundations.RungOneSemiring), and Foundations.RungOneLineage rederives this one from it. Standard name: the distributive law of the transported ring (generated semiring with g(x) = 1 + x).
- `AbstractAlgebra.circ_not_distrib_add`: Plain addition and the circle operation do not form a ring: ◯ does not distribute over +.
- `AbstractAlgebra.circ_one_wall_mod7`: The circle operation mod p always has exactly one finite wall, −1 (the other wall is at infinity).
- `AbstractAlgebra.circ_unit_iff`: Units of ◯ are the quasi-regular elements: a has a ◯-inverse exactly when 1 + a is invertible for multiplication.
- `AbstractAlgebra.fadd_group`
- `AbstractAlgebra.fadd_lift`
- `AbstractAlgebra.field_unit`: On a field every element off the wall is a ◯-unit.
- `AbstractAlgebra.fin12_group_criterion`: (Fin 12, ◯[k]) is a group exactly when 6 = 2·3 divides k.
- `AbstractAlgebra.fin12_units`: Units of (Fin 12, ◯) are 0, 4, 6, 10: the units 1, 5, 7, 11 of (Fin 12, ·) shifted down.
- `AbstractAlgebra.fin12_wall`: (Fin 12, ◯) is never a group: the wall 11 = −1 absorbs.
- `AbstractAlgebra.fin4_klein`: (Fin 4, ◯[2]): every element is its own inverse (the Klein four-group).
- `AbstractAlgebra.fin4_not_iso_add`: So (Fin 4, ◯[2]) and (Fin 4, +) are not isomorphic, though both are groups of order 4.
- `AbstractAlgebra.fin8_group_criterion`: The same criterion mod 8 (2 must divide k) and mod 9 (3 must divide k).
- `AbstractAlgebra.fin8_weight2_not_cyclic`
- `AbstractAlgebra.fin8_weight4_cyclic`: Mod 8 with weight 4: cyclic of order 8. With weight 2: every element has order ≤ 4.
- `AbstractAlgebra.fin9_cyclic`: Mod 9 with weight 3: 1 generates all nine elements (cyclic, like (Fin 9, +)).
- `AbstractAlgebra.fin9_group_criterion`
- `AbstractAlgebra.fused_zero_absorbs`: The fused zero −1 absorbs under the fused product (it is the wall).
- `AbstractAlgebra.geom_mul`
- `AbstractAlgebra.int_circ_units`: On the integers the ◯-units are exactly 0 and −2 (the shifts of the units 1 and −1).
- `AbstractAlgebra.int_not_group`: (Int, ◯) has no group law: 1 has no inverse.
- `AbstractAlgebra.int_scale_hom`: On the integers x ↦ k·x is an injective homomorphism (Int, ◯[k]) → (Int, ◯): weight k is the sub-monoid kℤ of the circle operation.
- `AbstractAlgebra.int_scale_inj`
- `AbstractAlgebra.int_weight_units`: Weight 2: −1 is a unit (its own inverse). Weight −2: 1 is its own inverse.
- `AbstractAlgebra.mpow_circ`: (a ◯ b)^◯n = a^◯n ◯ b^◯n: each power map is an endomorphism of the circle operation.
- `AbstractAlgebra.mpow_mul`: (a^◯m)^◯n = a^◯(m·n): composing power maps multiplies the exponents.
- `AbstractAlgebra.mpow_one`
- `AbstractAlgebra.mpow_zero_base`
- `AbstractAlgebra.mul_commMonoid`
- `AbstractAlgebra.neg_two_self_inverse`: −2 is its own ◯-inverse: the integer group of ◯-units is the two-element group.
- `AbstractAlgebra.nilpotent_weight_group`: So with a nilpotent weight, (R, ◯[k]) is a commutative group.
- `AbstractAlgebra.nilpotent_weight_inverse`: If kᵐ = 0, every a has a ◯[k]-inverse, namely −a·(1 − ka + (ka)² − …). This is why a nilpotent ideal sits inside the Jacobson radical.
- `AbstractAlgebra.parity_is_xor`: Weight −2 on {0, 1} is exclusive or.
- `AbstractAlgebra.scale_wall`: The scaling carries the weighted wall −1/k to the circle-operation wall −1.
- `AbstractAlgebra.shift_ring_hom`: The shift is a ring isomorphism (R, fadd, ◯) ≅ (R, +, ·): it carries both operations.
- `AbstractAlgebra.shift_wall`: The shift sends the wall −1 to 0, the absorbing element of multiplication.
- `AbstractAlgebra.slope_no_walls_mod11`
- `AbstractAlgebra.slope_no_walls_mod3`
- `AbstractAlgebra.slope_no_walls_mod7`
- `AbstractAlgebra.slope_walls_mod13`
- `AbstractAlgebra.slope_walls_mod5`: Walls of slope addition (a + b)/(1 − ab) are the roots of 1 + a². Mod 5 and 13 there are two (two-walled country, a split torus with p − 1 points); mod 3, 7, 11 none (turning country, a non-split torus with p + 1 points). Split exactly at p ≡ 1 mod 4.
- `AbstractAlgebra.tateQ_commMonoid`: The Tate quotient is a commutative monoid under the circle operation.
- `AbstractAlgebra.tateQ_glued`: In the quotient the class of q − 1 is the identity.
- `AbstractAlgebra.tateQ_inverse`: On a field, every class off the wall has an inverse in the Tate quotient.
- `AbstractAlgebra.tateRel_circ`: The circle operation respects the gluing on each side.
- `AbstractAlgebra.tateRel_refl`
- `AbstractAlgebra.tateRel_symm`
- `AbstractAlgebra.tateRel_trans`
- `AbstractAlgebra.tate_glue`: The glued point: q − 1 sits with 0, i.e. multiplying the lift by q changes nothing. (Stated as 1 + 0 multiplied by q equals 1 + a whenever 1 + a = q.)

## FusedAlgebra.Topics.BeyondOneLoop (9)

- `BeyondOneLoop.dep_extra`: With A = X − 46 and C = X − 16 (in units of 1/6π), C − A = 30 for every matter content X.
- `BeyondOneLoop.dep_v`: β_G = 2G + A G², β_Λ = −2Λ + B G + C G Λ. Then G²·d(Λ/G)/dt = G(B G − 4Λ + (C − A) G Λ): the circle-op form −4v + B plus one extra term (C − A)Λ.
- `BeyondOneLoop.gaussian_boost`: J = [[2, 0], [c, −2]] squares to 4·I.
- `BeyondOneLoop.largeN_u`: u = 1/g: g²·du/dt = −β_g with β_g = (D−2)g − a g², so du/dt = −(D−2)u + a.
- `BeyondOneLoop.largeN_v`: v = λ/g: g²·dv/dt = β_λ g − λ β_g with β_λ = −2λ − a g λ + b g, which equals g(b g − D λ), so dv/dt = b − D v: rate D, the units of the vacuum energy Λ/G.
- `BeyondOneLoop.litim_mismatch`: The exponents are 1/2 at the Gaussian point and 22/46 = 11/23 = 1/θ at the UV point, so θ = 23/11 and the mismatch is 1/2 − 11/23 = 1/46.
- `BeyondOneLoop.litim_partial_fractions`: In y = g/(12π) the flow is dy/dt = y(2 − 46y)/(1 − y). Partial fractions of 1/β, cross-multiplied: (1 − y)/(y(2 − 46y)) = (1/2)/y + 22/(2 − 46y).
- `BeyondOneLoop.plane_exponents_opposite`: With walls r₁, r₂ (so k = −d(r₁ + r₂)), V′(r₁) + V′(r₂) = 0: the exponents are ±.
- `BeyondOneLoop.plane_generator`: One step of the plane law, cross-multiplied: (x ∘ s)(1 − d x s) − x(1 − d x s) = s(1 + k x + d x²). So the flow is generated by V(x) = 1 + k x + d x².

## FusedAlgebra.Topics.BinaryPulsar (10)

- `BinaryPulsar.corner_tiny`: The ◯ corner is the product of two first-order twists, each of size about M/r ≈ 4.35e-6, so it is below 1e-10 of a turn, far under the measurement's 14% error.
- `BinaryPulsar.double_pulsar_within_error`: Twist bookkeeping gives 5.07; measured 4.77 with +0.66 / −0.65. Within one sigma.
- `BinaryPulsar.drag_needed`: **Without the companion's drag** the prediction is 2.26, more than 3σ below 4.77.
- `BinaryPulsar.drag_vanishes`: In that limit the drag twist vanishes: a light partner barely moves the heavy companion.
- `BinaryPulsar.parts_add`: The three parts: velocity 0.75, space 1.51, drag 2.81, which add to 5.07.
- `BinaryPulsar.test_limit`: **Test-gyroscope limit** m₁ = 0: the coefficient is (3/2)·m₂, the Gravity Probe B law.
- `BinaryPulsar.three_twists`: **The three twists sum to the standard spin-orbit coefficient** m₂(4m₁ + 3m₂)/(2M).
- `BinaryPulsar.three_twists_poly`: Polynomial form (no division): 2M · (½m₂ + m₂ + 2m₁) = 4m₁ + 3m₂ times M/M.
- `BinaryPulsar.velocity_not_isolated`: Without the velocity twist the prediction is 4.32, still within one sigma: not isolated.
- `BinaryPulsar.velocity_space_ratio`: Velocity and space twists keep their one-to-two ratio, as in Gravity Probe B.

## FusedAlgebra.Topics.Biology.Behaviour (8)

- `Behaviour.dial_split`: **Dial-stationary discounting.** If 1 + κu multiplies over split delays, u composes by ◯_κ.
- `Behaviour.dial_steps`: An integer-step version: n equal pieces of interest u compose to wpow(κ, u, n).
- `Behaviour.herrnstein`: Herrnstein's hyperbola r/(r + rₑ) is −inv(r/rₑ).
- `Behaviour.hyperbolic_factor`: The hyperbolic discount factor is minus the circle inverse (called mult-add inverse before 1.0) of the interest.
- `Behaviour.hyperbolic_split`: Hyperbolic interest k·D adds over split delays: dial 0.
- `Behaviour.rw_phases`: Two training phases compose by the "or" law.
- `Behaviour.rw_step`: One Rescorla–Wagner step in units of λ: V ↦ V + a(1 − V) is V ◯₋₁ a.
- `Behaviour.subadditive`: **Subadditivity.** Discounting a hyperbolic delay in two pieces raises 1/d by k²D₁D₂.

## FusedAlgebra.Topics.Biology.CellBiology (11)

- `CellBiology.bliss_odds`: **Bliss is the plain circle operation on effect odds**, for any shape of dose–response curve.
- `CellBiology.bliss_or`: Survivals multiply, so effects combine by the "or" law, dial −1.
- `CellBiology.ci_hsa`: α = −1: the index sits on the additivity line exactly when one factor 1 − vᵢ vanishes, i.e. one drug alone already gives the effect. This is the Highest Single Agent model.
- `CellBiology.ci_hsa_field`: The Highest Single Agent reading of α = −1, over a field: on the additivity line one of the two drugs is at its own iso-effective dose.
- `CellBiology.ci_is_circK`
- `CellBiology.ci_loewe`: α = 0: Loewe dose additivity.
- `CellBiology.ci_nonexclusive`: α = 1: Chou's "mutually nonexclusive" index factors as (1 + v₁)(1 + v₂) − 1.
- `CellBiology.ci_shift`: Moving one drug moves the index by the dial lift of the other.
- `CellBiology.ci_wall`: **The wall makes one drug irrelevant.** At v₂ = −1/α the index is −1/α whatever v₁ is. For α = −1 the wall is v₂ = 1: drug 2 alone already gives the effect.
- `CellBiology.loewe_slope_one`: Loewe with Hill slope 1: vᵢ = uᵢ/o, so v₁ + v₂ = 1 exactly when the effect odds o are u₁ + u₂, odds adding, dial 0.
- `CellBiology.one_add_odds`

## FusedAlgebra.Topics.Biology.Development (5)

- `Development.allometry`: Allometry: if x grows by the factor 1 + g, then y ∝ xᵃ grows by 1 + mpow(g, a).
- `Development.bertalanffy`: Von Bertalanffy growth (power 3): size/asymptote = (1 + x)³ = 1 + mpow(x, 3).
- `Development.gompertz_steps`: **Gompertz steps.** n steps of x ↦ mpow(x, λ) give the single power λⁿ.
- `Development.logistic_shortfall`: The logistic shortfall below the asymptote is −inv(x) = x/(1 + x).
- `Development.pow_mul'`

## FusedAlgebra.Topics.Biology.Ecology (7)

- `Ecology.bd`: Beddington–DeAngelis is dial 0.
- `Ecology.budget_states`: The four states' weights (search 1, handling x, interfering from search y, interfering from handling k·x·y) sum to the budget.
- `Ecology.cm`: Crowley–Martin is dial 1, the product of the two saturations.
- `Ecology.corner`: The dial's excess over BD is exactly the handling–interference corner k·x·y.
- `Ecology.search_share`: Balance of the predator chain: if the handling share is x times the search share and the other two states are y and k·x·y times it, and the four shares sum to 1, the search share is 1/(1 + x ◯ₖ y).
- `Ecology.tsallis_two`: q = 2 diversity: ln₂(D) = 1 − 1/D. Hill numbers multiply, so these combine by dial −1.
- `Ecology.tsallis_wall`: For q = 2 the wall −1/k is 1: an entropy at the ceiling absorbs any other community.

## FusedAlgebra.Topics.Biology.Evolution (14)

- `Evolution.add_is_dial`: With slopes proportional to effects, c = k·a, adding a mutation is the dial.
- `Evolution.additive_dominance`: Additive dominance (h = ½ written as two equal copies a) is dial 0.
- `Evolution.common_slope_gap`: A common slope c for two mutations is consistent only if c·(aᵢ − aⱼ) = 0.
- `Evolution.consistent_forces_dial`: **Path consistency forces one dial.** If mutation i has a nonzero effect and every mutation j is path-consistent with it, then c_j = k·a_j with the single k = c_i/a_i.
- `Evolution.epistasis`: Epistasis against the multiplicative null is (k − 1)·ab.
- `Evolution.from_the_wall`: In the shifted coordinate 1 + k·x (fitness measured from the wall) mutations multiply.
- `Evolution.generations_add`: Stages split anywhere: t₁ + t₂ generations are t₁ then t₂.
- `Evolution.mult_null`: The multiplicative null is dial 1.
- `Evolution.order_gap`: Two orders of adding mutations i and j differ by exactly c_j a_i − c_i a_j.
- `Evolution.path_consistent_iff`: **Path consistency.** The two orders agree iff c_j a_i = c_i a_j.
- `Evolution.proportional_consistent`: Proportional slopes are always path-consistent.
- `Evolution.three_mutations`: The dial is associative, so three mutations reach one fitness in any grouping.
- `Evolution.wall_at`: The wall: for k ≠ 0, the background x = −1/k makes every mutation neutral.
- `Evolution.wall_neutral`: At the wall every mutation is neutral: Δ(x) = a(1 + kx) vanishes when 1 + kx = 0.

## FusedAlgebra.Topics.Biology.Genetics (13)

- `Genetics.complete_interference`: Complete interference (c = 0) is plain addition of map lengths.
- `Genetics.haldane`: No interference: r₁₂ = r₁ + r₂ − 2r₁r₂, the dial at k = −2.
- `Genetics.haldane_lift`: The lift of dial −2 is 1 − 2r, so 1 − 2r multiplies along the chromosome.
- `Genetics.haldane_star`: In the coordinate u = −2r Haldane is the plain circle operation (called mult-add before 1.0).
- `Genetics.haldane_three`: Three intervals: the lift is a product of three factors.
- `Genetics.haldane_unlinked`: **The wall is "unlinked".** An interval at r = 1/2 makes the combined pair unlinked, whatever the other interval is.
- `Genetics.interference`: With double-recombinant frequency d₁₂ = c·r₁·r₂, the outer recombination fraction is the dial at k = −2c.
- `Genetics.kosambi_lift`: **Kosambi is plain ◯ after a change of variable.** With R = 2r and R₁₂ = (R₁ + R₂)/(1 + R₁R₂), the quantity 1 + w = (1 + R)/(1 − R) multiplies.
- `Genetics.lens_haldane`: Haldane is the lens law at (−2, 0).
- `Genetics.lens_kosambi`: Kosambi's addition rule is the lens law at (0, −4).
- `Genetics.lens_unlinked`: **Unlinked absorbs exactly on the line 2k + q = −4.**
- `Genetics.reri`: The interaction contrast (RERI) is the dial times the two excess risks.
- `Genetics.risk_additive`: k = 0: excess risks add.

## FusedAlgebra.Topics.Biology.Metabolism (17)

- `Metabolism.bisub_is_circK`
- `Metabolism.bisub_line`: Read as a line in u_A (a double-reciprocal plot at fixed B): intercept 1 + u_B, slope 1 + k·u_B.
- `Metabolism.bisub_shift`: Changing B moves V/v by (u_B − u_B')·(1 + k·u_A); the factor is the dial lift.
- `Metabolism.circK_comm'`
- `Metabolism.free_fraction`: The free (unbound) fraction is minus the inverse: u/(1 + u) = −inv(u).
- `Metabolism.limitation_or`: The shortfall from full growth, 1 − θ₁θ₂, is the "or" of the two shortfalls.
- `Metabolism.limitation_or_three`
- `Metabolism.limitation_pow`: n identical limiting steps: the joint shortfall is a weighted power at dial −1.
- `Metabolism.lines_meet_at_wall`: **The lines meet at the wall.** At u_A = −1/k the rate no longer depends on B: every double-reciprocal line passes through the point (−1/k, 1 − 1/k).
- `Metabolism.meeting_point_unique`: Only the wall does this: if two different B give the same rate at u_A, then 1 + k·u_A = 0.
- `Metabolism.michaelis_menten`: Michaelis–Menten: S/(K + S) = 1 + inv(K/S).
- `Metabolism.monod_additive`: Additive resistance: 1/μ = 1/θ₁ + 1/θ₂ − 1 = 1 + u₁ + u₂, dial 0.
- `Metabolism.ordered_is_dial`: **The ordered mechanism is the circle operation (called weighted mult-add before 1.0) with dial k.** With u_A = K_a/A, u_B = K_b/B and k = K_ia/K_a, the textbook rate law V/v = 1 + K_a/A + K_b/B + K_ia·K_b/(A·B) equals 1 + (u_A ◯_k u_B).
- `Metabolism.pingpong`: Ping-pong (substituted enzyme): no product term, dial 0. Double-reciprocal lines are parallel.
- `Metabolism.random_independent`: Random order with independent sites: dial 1, the rate law factors.
- `Metabolism.random_independent'`
- `Metabolism.saturation`: Saturation is the circle inverse lifted: 1/(1 + u) = 1 + inv(u).

## FusedAlgebra.Topics.Biology.MolecularBiology (28)

- `MolecularBiology.bind2_excess`: The deviation from independence is exactly (ω − 1) times the corner cell.
- `MolecularBiology.bind2_exclusive`: Exclusive sites (ω = 0): only one can be filled, plain addition.
- `MolecularBiology.bind2_half`: Half saturation for identical sites: the bound fraction (x + ωx²)/(1 + 2x + ωx²) is 1/2 exactly when ω·x² = 1. Stated without division.
- `MolecularBiology.bind2_independent`: Independent sites (ω = 1): the polynomial factors, the plain circle operation.
- `MolecularBiology.bind2_independent'`
- `MolecularBiology.bind2_is_circK`
- `MolecularBiology.bind2_wall`: A site sitting at the wall −1/ω makes the binding polynomial forget the other site.
- `MolecularBiology.catch_three`
- `MolecularBiology.catch_two`: A mistake survives two checks only if both miss: catches combine by the "or" law.
- `MolecularBiology.copy_loss`: Copying a genome of L bases with error μ per base keeps the master with chance (1 − μ)^L = 1 + mpow(−μ, L): the copy loss is a circle power.
- `MolecularBiology.eigen_excess`: Eigen: the master's growth factor σ·(1 + l) minus 1 is the circle operation of the advantage s = σ − 1 and the copy loss l.
- `MolecularBiology.eigen_threshold`: **Eigen's error threshold is the circle inverse.** Away from the wall (1 + l ≠ 0), the master just holds its own (s ◯ l = 0) exactly when s is the circle inverse of l.
- `MolecularBiology.mwc_R`: R state: the plain circle power, lifted.
- `MolecularBiology.mwc_T`: T state: the weighted power at dial c, lifted.
- `MolecularBiology.mwc_c_one`: With c = 1 both states bind alike and MWC collapses to independent sites.
- `MolecularBiology.mwc_poly`: The MWC binding polynomial (1 + a)ⁿ + L(1 + c·a)ⁿ written as two circle powers.
- `MolecularBiology.star_def`
- `MolecularBiology.swap_mul`
- `MolecularBiology.swap_one`
- `MolecularBiology.swap_pow`
- `MolecularBiology.tr_swap`
- `MolecularBiology.zb_det`
- `MolecularBiology.zb_free`: σ = 1 (no nucleation penalty): the matrix has determinant 0, so the chain is a free gas whose ring partition function is (1 + s)ⁿ.
- `MolecularBiology.zb_is_dial_gas`: **Helix–coil = dial gas.** If k·σ = 1, the relabelled Zimm–Bragg matrix is the Lee–Yang ring matrix with dial k and fugacity σ·s.
- `MolecularBiology.zb_odd_ring_wall`: **Wall theorem for helix–coil.** At s = −1 every odd ring vanishes, for every σ.
- `MolecularBiology.zb_ring_eq_dial_ring`: So the helix–coil ring has the same partition function as the dial-gas ring.
- `MolecularBiology.zb_ring_relabel`: Relabelling the states does not change any ring's partition function.
- `MolecularBiology.zb_tr`

## FusedAlgebra.Topics.Biology.NeuroScience (14)

- `NeuroScience.T_mul`: **Summation becomes multiplication.** In T(v) = (1 − Av)/(1 − Bv) the lens law is a product.
- `NeuroScience.clear_den`: Bookkeeping for the divided two-port theorem: clearing the lens denominator.
- `NeuroScience.clear_num`: Bookkeeping for the divided two-port theorem: clearing the lens numerator.
- `NeuroScience.div_mul_cancel'`: x / y · y = x for y ≠ 0.
- `NeuroScience.factor_A`: **Factorisation.** With k = −(A + B), q = AB: 1 − A·V = (1 − Aa)(1 − Ab)/(1 − ABab).
- `NeuroScience.factor_B`: The same factorisation with A and B exchanged.
- `NeuroScience.lens_dial`: With q = 0 the lens law is the dial: Hao et al.'s bilinear rule.
- `NeuroScience.odds_vss`: In odds v/(1 − v) the conductance comes back: inputs add, dial 0.
- `NeuroScience.one_compartment`: **One compartment.** Two synapses sum by the lens law with k = −2, q = 1.
- `NeuroScience.reversal_absorbs`: **Reversal absorbs.** A synapse holding the compartment at its reversal potential makes every other input irrelevant: v = 1 is the lens law's wall.
- `NeuroScience.soma_line`: In the soma-line case B = 1 the two constants sum to −1.
- `NeuroScience.soma_reversal_absorbs`: **Reversal at the soma absorbs, on any tree.** On the soma line (B = 1, reversal units) an input clamping the soma to its reversal fixes the response at 1.
- `NeuroScience.twoport`: **The two-port theorem, cleared form.** Currents I₁ (synapse i alone), J₂ (synapse j alone) and I, J (both on) solve the network equations of a passive linear network with transfer impedances Z. Then the soma voltage V = Z_si I + Z_sj J obeys the lens law with the constants above, multiplied through by E_i E_j.
- `NeuroScience.twoport_lens`: **The two-port theorem, divided form.** With nonzero reversals and transfer impedances, the soma voltage is exactly the lens law with the geometric constants k and q.

## FusedAlgebra.Topics.Biology.Physiology (6)

- `Physiology.dose_ratio`: The dose ratio is that partition function; DR − 1 is the circle operation (called weighted mult-add before 1.0) with dial k.
- `Physiology.hill_level_set`: In reduced variables Hill's hyperbola (1 + X)(1 + Y) = 1 + X₀ is X ◯ Y = X₀.
- `Physiology.hill_velocity`: **Velocity is a circle-operation difference.** On Hill's curve Y = X₀ ◯ inv(X).
- `Physiology.same_site`: Same site: dose ratios add (Paton–Rang additivity).
- `Physiology.separate_sites`: Independent sites: dose ratios multiply.
- `Physiology.series_gain`: Splitting clearance across two organs in series beats pooling it in one: (1 + u₁)(1 + u₂) exceeds 1 + u₁ + u₂ by exactly u₁u₂, the corner cell.

## FusedAlgebra.Topics.Biology.Population (5)

- `Population.bh_compose`: **Two seasons are one season.** BH(R₂, m₂) ∘ BH(R₁, m₁) = BH(R₁R₂, m₁ + R₁m₂).
- `Population.bh_room`: **Room shrinks by 1/R.** With K = (R − 1)/m, the room K/N − 1 after one step is (K/N − 1)/R.
- `Population.hassell_one`: Hassell with b = 1 is Beverton–Holt's 1 + aN.
- `Population.interventions`: Two interventions: transmission kept is (1 − e₁)(1 − e₂) = 1 − (e₁ "or" e₂).
- `Population.survival_assoc`: Three age classes, grouped either way.

## FusedAlgebra.Topics.BooleanLattice (9)

- `BooleanLattice.chain4_corners`: Four steps: every non-empty corner of the 4-cube (the tesseract) once, a rank-m corner weighted k^(m−1).
- `BooleanLattice.incl_excl3`: k = −1 is inclusion–exclusion: the chance that at least one of three independent events happens.
- `BooleanLattice.nchain3_corners`: Three steps when order matters: every non-empty corner of the cube once, its factors in chain order.
- `BooleanLattice.plane_chain3_den`: Three steps on the plane, denominator: 1 − d·(U₁·e₂ + U₂·e₃).
- `BooleanLattice.plane_chain3_num`: Three steps on the plane, numerator: corners weighted by the Lucas numbers U₁ = 1, U₂ = k, U₃ = k² − d. (The two-step law is N/D; one more step with z has numerator N + D·z + k·N·z.)
- `BooleanLattice.plane_lift`: The plane's law (x + y + k·xy)/(1 − d·xy) with k = α + β and d = αβ turns into multiplication under x ↦ (1 + αx)/(1 + βx). Stated cross-multiplied, so it holds in any commutative ring.
- `BooleanLattice.tangent3`: The tangent of a sum of three angles, (k, d) = (0, 1): odd corners with alternating signs over even corners.
- `BooleanLattice.velocity3`: Relativistic velocity addition, (k, d) = (0, −1): odd corners over even corners.
- `BooleanLattice.way_back_of_strict_zeta`: Rota and Hall in one line: if no chain is longer than 2 steps (η³ = 0, as for the strict zeta function of a poset of rank 2), the way back of η under the circle operation is −η + η², the alternating count of chains; so 1 + (−η + η²) is the Möbius function. (For dial k, replace η by k·η.)

## FusedAlgebra.Topics.Calculus (29)

- `CircleOp.Calculus.cubes_example`: A worked check: the cubes 0, 1, 8, 27, … have third difference 6 and fourth difference 0, so Newton rebuilds n³ from the numbers 0, 1, 6, 6.
- `CircleOp.Calculus.dif_one`: Each difference at 1 is the difference at 0 plus the next difference.
- `CircleOp.Calculus.dif_shift`: Shifting the input commutes with differencing.
- `CircleOp.Calculus.diff_circ`: The ordinary difference of f ◯ g: two lifted strips and the corner.
- `CircleOp.Calculus.diff_pow`: An exponential's slope is a times itself, exactly.
- `CircleOp.Calculus.ftc`: Fundamental theorem, part 2: a path whose circle-operation steps are d ends at its start ◯ the integral of d.
- `CircleOp.Calculus.ftc_add`: The additive fundamental theorem: differences telescope.
- `CircleOp.Calculus.ftc_box`: The same in box form: the end box is the start box times every step.
- `CircleOp.Calculus.ftc_mslope`: Fundamental theorem with slopes read off: f(n) = f(0) ◯ ∫ mslope f.
- `CircleOp.Calculus.ftc_step`: Fundamental theorem, part 1: the last step of the integral is the integrand.
- `CircleOp.Calculus.isum_add`
- `CircleOp.Calculus.isum_congr`
- `CircleOp.Calculus.jackson_gap`: Stepping the input by the circle operation moves it by h·(1 + x): the gap of Jackson's q-derivative with q = 1 + h, read in lifted coordinates.
- `CircleOp.Calculus.leibniz_corner`: Leibniz's rule with the corner kept: the box f·g grows by two strips and one corner square.
- `CircleOp.Calculus.mint_add`: The integral over two intervals joins by the circle operation.
- `CircleOp.Calculus.mint_circ`: The integral of d ◯ e is the integral of d ◯ the integral of e.
- `CircleOp.Calculus.mint_const`: Straight lines are exponentials: constant circle-operation slope a gives a^(◯n).
- `CircleOp.Calculus.mint_lift`: Under the lift the circle-operation integral is a product integral.
- `CircleOp.Calculus.mpow_mul`: Chain rule for blocks: n blocks of m equal steps is a power of a power.
- `CircleOp.Calculus.mslope_step`: Away from the wall, the circle-operation slope is the step: f(k) ◯ slope = f(k+1).
- `CircleOp.Calculus.newton`: Newton's forward formula: f(n) = Σ_{k ≤ n} layer(n,k) · Δᵏf(0).
- `CircleOp.Calculus.pascal_sum`: Pascal inside a weighted sum: the (n+1)-cube's layers split into two n-cubes.
- `CircleOp.Calculus.product_rule_step`: Product rule = sum rule: if f steps by d and g steps by e, then f ◯ g steps by d ◯ e.
- `CircleOp.Calculus.rate_product`: If F grows by the factor (1 + h·p) over a step of size h and G by (1 + h·q), then F·G grows by (1 + h·(p ◯[h] q)): relative rates combine by the circle operation with weight h. This is time-scale ⊕.
- `CircleOp.Calculus.rate_product_limit`: Weight 0 (step size 0) is ordinary calculus: rates just add.
- `CircleOp.Calculus.step_iff`: Ordinary slope = height × circle-operation slope (the log-derivative rule f' = (1 + f)·r).
- `CircleOp.Calculus.ts_exp`: The time-scale exponential after n steps: 1 + h·p^(◯[h]n) = (1 + h·p)ⁿ.
- `CircleOp.Calculus.ts_exp_mul`: e_p · e_q = e_(p ◯[h] q): the exponential turns the circle operation with dial k into ×.
- `CircleOp.Calculus.ts_inverse`: Time-scale inverse ⊖p = −p/(1 + h·p): exists exactly off the wall.

## FusedAlgebra.Topics.CategoryTheory (38)

- `CategoryTheory.Iso.left`
- `CategoryTheory.Iso.right`
- `CategoryTheory.align_fst`: **Nothing lost**: the left list comes back out of the alignment.
- `CategoryTheory.align_length`
- `CategoryTheory.align_snd`: And so does the right list.
- `CategoryTheory.align_zip`: The ordinary zip is the corner cell of the alignment (the ab term).
- `CategoryTheory.allThese_complete`: Nothing is missed: every value whose parts are listed is in the list.
- `CategoryTheory.allThese_length`: **The count**: |A ◯ B| = |A| ◯ |B|.
- `CategoryTheory.bothL_complete`
- `CategoryTheory.bothL_length`
- `CategoryTheory.circle_wall`: **The circle is the wall**: χ̃(S¹ × Y) = −1 for every Y.
- `CategoryTheory.empty_initial`: And initial: there is exactly one map out of it.
- `CategoryTheory.empty_pair`: The terminal object is the unit of ◯ (checked above: `unitL`), as it must be for a product.
- `CategoryTheory.empty_terminal`: The empty type is terminal in Par: the only partial map into it is "undefined".
- `CategoryTheory.ev_padd`
- `CategoryTheory.ev_pmul`: Counting cells is multiplicative: P(X × Y) = P(X)·P(Y) at every t.
- `CategoryTheory.ev_smul`
- `CategoryTheory.ev_top`
- `CategoryTheory.fm_fst_this`
- `CategoryTheory.fm_snd_that`
- `CategoryTheory.fst_pair`
- `CategoryTheory.map_comp`
- `CategoryTheory.map_id`
- `CategoryTheory.ofPairK_toPairK`
- `CategoryTheory.ofPair_toPair`
- `CategoryTheory.option_empty`: Option of the empty type is the one-point type: the unit goes to the unit.
- `CategoryTheory.pair_unique`: **Uniqueness**: any partial map whose projections are f and g is the pairing. So A ◯ B is the categorical product of A and B in Par.
- `CategoryTheory.reduced_product`: **The reduced count combines by the circle operation**: P(X×Y) − 1 = (P(X) − 1) ◯ (P(Y) − 1), at every t. At t = 1: non-base cells of a pointed product = wedge + smash. At t = −1: χ̃(X×Y) = χ̃X ◯ χ̃Y.
- `CategoryTheory.s2_s2`: Even spheres sit at the circle operation's 1: χ̃(S² × S²) = 1 ◯ 1 = 3.
- `CategoryTheory.snd_pair`
- `CategoryTheory.sphere_rchi`: Spheres sit at ±1: χ̃(Sⁿ) = (−1)ⁿ.
- `CategoryTheory.suspension_split`: Suspension multiplies the reduced count by t, so the splitting Σ(X×Y) ≃ ΣX ∨ ΣY ∨ Σ(X∧Y) has the count t·(a ◯ b) = ta + tb + t·ab.
- `CategoryTheory.swap_swap`
- `CategoryTheory.toPairK_ofPairK`
- `CategoryTheory.toPair_natural`: Naturality: the split commutes with mapping.
- `CategoryTheory.toPair_ofPair`
- `CategoryTheory.torus_cells`: The torus S¹ × S¹: one 0-cell, two 1-cells, one 2-cell.
- `CategoryTheory.wedge_smash`: Pointed sets: the product of A₊ and B₊ has (a+1)(b+1) points; take away the base point and what is left is the wedge (a + b) plus the smash (ab).

## FusedAlgebra.Topics.ChaosComposition (23)

- `CircleOp.ChaosComposition.affine_centre_transfer`: Affine maps: doing q carries the centre of p ∘ q onto the centre of q ∘ p.
- `CircleOp.ChaosComposition.affine_orders`: The two orders stretch by the same factor (the multiplier, hence the Lyapunov exponent, is order-free); only the shift, and so the centre, feels the order, by the wedge.
- `CircleOp.ChaosComposition.dial_iter_units`: Orbit for orbit, x ◯ₖ x is x ◯ x in the units x ↦ kx.
- `CircleOp.ChaosComposition.doubling_shift`: On N-digit binary fractions x/2ᴺ, doubling mod 1 moves every digit up one place: digit j + 1 of D(x) is digit j of x (digits counted from the least significant). Read from the most significant end, D drops the first digit: the list `tail`.
- `CircleOp.ChaosComposition.iter_add`: fᵐ⁺ⁿ = fᵐ ∘ fⁿ: iterating sends + in ℕ to ∘ on maps.
- `CircleOp.ChaosComposition.iter_msq_ne_wall`
- `CircleOp.ChaosComposition.iter_semiconj`: A morphism of dynamical systems (h ∘ f = g ∘ h, a natural transformation between the two functors out of ℕ) carries every orbit of f onto an orbit of g.
- `CircleOp.ChaosComposition.iter_slope`: Lyapunov through the lift, n steps at once: the slope of the n-th iterate of x ◯ x is 2ⁿ (1 + x)^(2ⁿ − 1), written without subtraction as slope·(1 + x) = 2ⁿ (1 + x)^(2ⁿ).
- `CircleOp.ChaosComposition.iter_succ'`: fⁿ⁺¹ = fⁿ ∘ f as well as f ∘ fⁿ.
- `CircleOp.ChaosComposition.logistic_iter_fold`: n steps of the logistic map at r = 4 are n steps of x ◯ x, folded.
- `CircleOp.ChaosComposition.msq_ne_wall`: Squaring never reaches the wall from off it.
- `CircleOp.ChaosComposition.order_invisible`: Shift equivalence: g ∘ (f ∘ g)ⁿ = (g ∘ f)ⁿ ∘ g. The two orders are the same system, seen through g.
- `CircleOp.ChaosComposition.order_is_a_shift`: The cost of order is one shift: p ∘ q = (shift by the wedge) ∘ (q ∘ p). In the affine group every commutator is a pure translation by the wedge, which slopes (and so Lyapunov exponents) cannot see: Lyapunov reads the abelian shadow, the wedge is the rest.
- `CircleOp.ChaosComposition.order_periodic`: A cycle of f ∘ g is carried by g to a cycle of g ∘ f with the same period.
- `CircleOp.ChaosComposition.order_periodic_back`: …and back again by f, so periodic points of the two orders match up.
- `CircleOp.ChaosComposition.periodic_semiconj`: So it carries periodic points to periodic points of the same period.
- `CircleOp.ChaosComposition.quadratic_lift`: The quadratic family z² + c in lift coordinates: square by the circle operation, then shift by c.
- `CircleOp.ChaosComposition.runB_affine`: Every composite is x ↦ 2ⁿx + (its value at 0).
- `CircleOp.ChaosComposition.runB_free`: The list corner: different words of the same length give different composites (the branch monoid is free), so there are 2ⁿ composites of length n: growth rate log 2.
- `CircleOp.ChaosComposition.runT_count`: The bag corner: a composite only counts its 1s, so length n gives just n + 1 composites: growth rate 0.
- `CircleOp.ChaosComposition.runT_perm`
- `CircleOp.ChaosComposition.stretchRun_eq`: The composite stretches about m by 1 + (the circle-operation fold of the letters).
- `CircleOp.ChaosComposition.stretchRun_perm`: Shared centre ⇒ bag corner: the composite depends only on the bag of letters.

## FusedAlgebra.Topics.Checkerboard (4)

- `Checkerboard.circ_chain`: The chain rule of the weighted circle operation holds for non-commuting steps.
- `Checkerboard.circ_three`: Three steps: every non-empty ordered sub-chain once, weight k^(size − 1).
- `Checkerboard.counts_five`
- `Checkerboard.turn_move_order`

## FusedAlgebra.Topics.CheckerboardPotential (3)

- `CheckerboardPotential.scalar_comm`: A potential element v·I commutes with any turn element.
- `CheckerboardPotential.subsetSum_eq_prod`
- `CheckerboardPotential.tick_factor`: One tick with a potential: (1 + k v)(1 + k t) = 1 + k (v ∘ₖ t).

## FusedAlgebra.Topics.Chemistry.ChemAcids (7)

- `ChemAcids.fold_gap_log3`: In the fold, the second and third stepwise constants are 3k/3 = k and k²/(3k) = k/3: their ratio is exactly 3 (a pKa gap of log 3) whatever k ≠ 0 is.
- `ChemAcids.fold_three`: The dial folded over three equal sites: x ◯[k] x ◯[k] x = 3x + 3k x² + k² x³.
- `ChemAcids.fold_unequal_gaps`: The fold's coefficients 3, 3k, k² break that equality by the factor k: β₁³β₃ − β₂³ = 27k²(1 − k), zero only at k = 0 or k = 1.
- `ChemAcids.isoelectric`: Isoelectric point of a two-group amino acid: if h² = K₁K₂, the carboxylate fraction K₁/(K₁ + h) equals the ammonium fraction h/(K₂ + h). Cleared form.
- `ChemAcids.mediant_above`: Mixing two buffers: the mediant of b₁/a₁ ≤ b₂/a₂ is at least the smaller ratio (cleared, a's positive).
- `ChemAcids.mediant_below`: …and at most the larger one.
- `ChemAcids.pairwise_equal_gaps`: Uniform pairwise interaction: β₁ = 3K, β₂ = 3kK², β₃ = k³K³ satisfy β₁³β₃ = β₂³, which says the two stepwise ratios K₁/K₂ and K₂/K₃ are equal (equal pKa gaps).

## FusedAlgebra.Topics.Chemistry.ChemBiochem (8)

- `ChemBiochem.bliss`: …which is Bliss independence for the remaining activity: 1/(1+a) · 1/(1+b) = 1/(1 + a ◯[1] b).
- `ChemBiochem.calvin_efficiency`: Photosynthesis at the 8-photon limit: 478 kJ stored from 1407 kJ of 680 nm photons lies between 33% and 35%.
- `ChemBiochem.independent_lift`: Independent inhibitors (k = 1): the circle operation (called mult-add before 1.0), so 1 + (a ◯[1] b) = (1 + a)(1 + b).
- `ChemBiochem.isobole`: The 50% isobole for interaction k: b(1 + k·a) = 1 − a solves a ◯[k] b = 1.
- `ChemBiochem.nitrogenase_atp`: 16 ATP per N₂ is 8 per NH₃.
- `ChemBiochem.nitrogenase_electrons`: Nitrogenase: 6 of 8 electrons reach ammonia, 3/4.
- `ChemBiochem.series_rate`: Irreversible steps in series: times add, so with t = 1/k the turnover time of three steps is t₁ + t₂ + t₃ and the overall rate is its reciprocal (cleared: kcat·(t₁ + t₂ + t₃) = 1).
- `ChemBiochem.yonetani_theorell`: Yonetani–Theorell: with k = 1/α the binding polynomial minus one is a ◯[k] b.

## FusedAlgebra.Topics.Chemistry.ChemBonding (23)

- `ChemBonding.allyl`: Allyl (3 carbons): P 3 = x (x² − 2): levels 0, ±√2.
- `ChemBonding.band_edge`: Border: at the band edge x = 2 the root t = 1 is double.
- `ChemBonding.band_square`: Completing the square: 4(t² − x t + 1) = (2t − x)² + (4 − x²).
- `ChemBonding.benzene_aromatic`: Benzene: 6 = 4·1 + 2 is a Hückel closed shell.
- `ChemBonding.bonding_norm`: The equal-weight combination φa + φb has length² 2 + 2S = (1+S)·2, the lift of S at weight 1.
- `ChemBonding.butadiene`: Butadiene (4 carbons): P 4 = x⁴ − 3x² + 1, whose roots are ±φ, ±1/φ (golden ratio).
- `ChemBonding.cassini`: Cassini: P(n+1)² − P(n+2)·P(n) = q^(n+1).
- `ChemBonding.cassini_step`: Cassini step: the invariant is multiplied by q at each site.
- `ChemBonding.dial_ladder`: The dial applied three times, (1 + k e)³ = 1 + k·(triple): the associative ladder the checks test against bond energies (it fits C–C to 3% and misses N–N by 13%).
- `ChemBonding.f2_single`: F₂: 8 bonding, 6 antibonding → single.
- `ChemBonding.gap_pair`: Gap (two walls): x = 5/2 has the reciprocal real pair t = 2, 1/2.
- `ChemBonding.huckel_rule`
- `ChemBonding.huckel_unimodular`: A plain carbon chain (q = 1) has invariant 1 at every length.
- `ChemBonding.mobius_rule`
- `ChemBonding.n2_triple`: N₂: 8 bonding, 2 antibonding valence electrons → triple bond.
- `ChemBonding.no_root_in_band`: Turning country: inside the band (x² < 4) the transfer polynomial has no rational root.
- `ChemBonding.o2_double`: O₂: 8 bonding, 4 antibonding → double.
- `ChemBonding.one_pow_rat`: 1ⁿ = 1 over Rat (core has no lemma by this name).
- `ChemBonding.overlap_border`: S = 1 is the border: identical orbitals, coefficients add.
- `ChemBonding.overlap_opposite`: S = −1: opposite phases, coefficients subtract.
- `ChemBonding.overlap_orthogonal`: S = 0: orthogonal orbitals, Pythagoras.
- `ChemBonding.split_asymmetry`: Asymmetric splitting: with bonding (α+β)/(1+S) and antibonding (α−β)/(1−S), rise·(1−S) and fall·(1+S) are both αS − β, so rise/fall = (1+S)/(1−S). Stated cleared of denominators: the two numerators agree.
- `ChemBonding.sq_nonneg_rat`: Squares are nonnegative over Rat.

## FusedAlgebra.Topics.Chemistry.ChemConservation (10)

- `ChemConservation.binding_exact`: With a binding energy B, M = m₁ + m₂ − B: B(2(m₁+m₂) − B) = 2 m₁ m₂ (1 − c), exactly.
- `ChemConservation.bound_lighter`: Bound (c < 1) means the whole weighs less than its parts, for positive masses.
- `ChemConservation.circle_closed`: Rotation (the phase e^{iQφ}) in Gaussian-rational coordinates: if both factors lie on the unit circle, so does their product. The excess of a point of the circle is never the wall −1 unless the point is 0.
- `ChemConservation.cosine_extra`: The cosine law reads as the circle operation with dial k in the masses' squares: with a = m₁², b = m₂² and the corner taken through √(ab) = m₁m₂, the extra piece beyond plain addition is 2c·m₁m₂. At c = 1 it is the perfect square.
- `ChemConservation.deficit`: The mass deficit squared is 2 m₁ m₂ (1 − c).
- `ChemConservation.free_heavier`: Free pairs in motion (c > 1) weigh more than their rest masses.
- `ChemConservation.fugacity_circ`: So the excess z^Q − 1 combines by the circle operation.
- `ChemConservation.lavoisier`: Lavoisier's border: c = 1 gives exactly (m₁ + m₂)².
- `ChemConservation.neutral_identity`: A neutral system (charge 0) has fugacity 1: excess 0, the circle operation's identity.
- `ChemConservation.small_angle`: Small-angle form: if c = 1 − t/2 (t = θ²), the squared deficit is m₁m₂·t.

## FusedAlgebra.Topics.Chemistry.ChemElectro (9)

- `ChemElectro.kok_damped`: The damping eigenvalue α − β + i(1 − α − β) has squared modulus at most 1 when α, β ≥ 0 and α + β ≤ 1, so the four-flash oscillation never grows.
- `ChemElectro.kok_mean`: Kok clock: one flash advances 0, 1 or 2 steps with probabilities α, 1 − α − β, β. The mean advance is 1 − α + β.
- `ChemElectro.luther_balance`: Luther's rule: the combined potential m = (n₁E₁ + n₂E₂)/(n₁ + n₂) balances the two couples.
- `ChemElectro.luther_idem`: Luther's mean is idempotent: a couple combined with itself gives the same potential, so potentials are averaged, not combined by the circle operation.
- `ChemElectro.nernst_midpoint`: Midpoint of the two-electron Nernst curve: with s² = k, at x = 1/s the mean number of electrons 2x(1 + kx)/(1 + 2x + kx²) equals 1. Cleared form.
- `ChemElectro.stage_losses3`: Three stages fold the same way, in either grouping.
- `ChemElectro.two_site`: Two one-electron sites with interaction factor k: Z − 1 = x ◯[k] x.
- `ChemElectro.two_site_disc`: Its discriminant is 4(1 − k): complex zeros exactly when k > 1 (cooperativity).
- `ChemElectro.zscheme_bounds`: Z-scheme bookkeeping in meV: 4 electrons × 1135 meV stored from 4 × 1823 + 4 × 1771 meV of photons, between 30% and a third.

## FusedAlgebra.Topics.Chemistry.ChemEquilibrium (11)

- `ChemEquilibrium.binding_dial`: Two-site binding polynomial minus one is the dial.
- `ChemEquilibrium.cooperative_no_real_zero`: Cooperative pairs (ω > 1) have no real binding zero.
- `ChemEquilibrium.diacid_gap`: Symmetric diacid, association form: β₁ = 2K, β₂ = ωK², so β₂·4 = ω·β₁².
- `ChemEquilibrium.fold_is_mwc`: MWC with exclusive binding: k·(1 + fold) = (k − 1) + (1 + kx)^n, i.e. L = k − 1.
- `ChemEquilibrium.fold_lift`: The dial folded over n copies of x: 1 + k·(fold) = (1 + kx)^n (library wpow_lift).
- `ChemEquilibrium.four_site_coeffs`: Four sites: the fold's coefficients 4, 6k, 4k², k³ (j ligands carry k^(j−1)).
- `ChemEquilibrium.haber_pressure`: Mass action: activities multiply into Q, so log Q adds; here Q for N₂ + 3H₂ ⇌ 2NH₃ scales as p⁻² when every partial pressure is multiplied by p.
- `ChemEquilibrium.independent_double_zero`: Independent pair (ω = 1): the polynomial is (1 + x)², a double zero at x = −1.
- `ChemEquilibrium.sq_nonneg_rat`
- `ChemEquilibrium.two_site_disc`: Two equal sites: discriminant of ωx² + 2x + 1 is 4(1 − ω).
- `ChemEquilibrium.two_site_square`: If ω ≤ 1 the polynomial factors over the reals in principle; for ω > 1 there is no real (here: rational) zero, since ω(ωx² + 2x + 1) = (ωx + 1)² + (ω − 1).

## FusedAlgebra.Topics.Chemistry.ChemGas (12)

- `ChemGas.corner`: The corner of the two corrections is −αβ.
- `ChemGas.critical_Z`: vdW critical constants Vc = 3b, RTc = 8a/(27b), Pc = a/(27b²) give Zc = 3/8.
- `ChemGas.cubic_discriminants`: Their discriminants u² − 4w are 0, 1 and 8.
- `ChemGas.dalton`: Dalton: partial pressures x·P add up to P when the mole fractions sum to 1.
- `ChemGas.fugacity_product`: Fugacity coefficients multiply into an equilibrium: here φ₁²/(φ₂ φ₃³) written without division.
- `ChemGas.mayer_clusters`: Expanded, the three-pair fold is the seven nonempty products: singles, pairs and the triangle.
- `ChemGas.mayer_three`: Mayer for three pairs: (1 + f₁)(1 + f₂)(1 + f₃) = 1 + f₁ ◯ f₂ ◯ f₃.
- `ChemGas.pr_denominator`
- `ChemGas.rk_denominator`
- `ChemGas.two_corrections`: The two corrections multiply: (1 + α)(1 − β) = 1 + α ◯ (−β).
- `ChemGas.vdw_denominator`
- `ChemGas.vdw_inverse_Z`: van der Waals in its own form gives 1/Z = (1 + a/PV²)(1 − b/V).

## FusedAlgebra.Topics.Chemistry.ChemGibbs (11)

- `ChemGibbs.K_excess`: Equilibrium constants multiply; their excesses combine by the circle operation.
- `ChemGibbs.K_three`: Three coupled reactions: the excesses fold associatively.
- `ChemGibbs.Z2_dial`: Z − 1 is the circle operation with weight ω.
- `ChemGibbs.covariance`: Covariance identity, cleared of Z²: Z·(ωxy) − (x + ωxy)(y + ωxy) = xy(ω − 1).
- `ChemGibbs.covariance_pos`: With x, y > 0, the covariance is positive exactly when ω > 1.
- `ChemGibbs.exclusion_adds`: Hard exclusion (ω = 0): occupations simply add.
- `ChemGibbs.gibbs_adds`: Free energies at a temperature add when reactions add (ΔG = ΔH − TΔS is linear).
- `ChemGibbs.haber_enthalpy`: Haber: ΔH° = 2 × (−45.90) = −91.80 kJ.
- `ChemGibbs.haber_entropy`: Haber: ΔS° = 2·192.77 − 191.61 − 3·130.68 = −198.11 J/K (as hundredths).
- `ChemGibbs.independent_circ`: Independent sites: Z − 1 is the circle operation.
- `ChemGibbs.independent_factors`: No interaction (ω = 1): the sites are independent and Z factors.

## FusedAlgebra.Topics.Chemistry.ChemOrganic (9)

- `ChemOrganic.benzene_charpoly`: Benzene (Hückel 6-ring): x⁶ − 6x⁴ + 9x² − 4 = (x − 2)(x + 2)(x − 1)²(x + 1)².
- `ChemOrganic.benzene_resonance`: Benzene's π energy (in β): 2·2 + 4·1 = 8, two more than three ethylenes (6).
- `ChemOrganic.cyclobutadiene_charpoly`: Cyclobutadiene (Hückel 4-ring): x⁴ − 4x² = x²(x − 2)(x + 2), a doubly degenerate zero level.
- `ChemOrganic.ee_compose`: Matched selectivities: with e = (r − 1)/(r + 1), multiplying the ratios composes ee by (e₁ + e₂)/(1 + e₁e₂). Cleared form, with e₁, e₂ given by their defining equations.
- `ChemOrganic.ee_is_landscape`: The ee law is the landscape law at (k, q) = (0, −1): numerator e₁ + e₂, denominator 1 + e₁e₂.
- `ChemOrganic.ee_two_walls`: Its discriminant k² − 4q is 4: the two-walls country.
- `ChemOrganic.ee_wall`: ee = 1 is a wall: a perfectly selective step absorbs any other (cleared: 1 + b = 1·(1 + 1·b)).
- `ChemOrganic.horeau`: Horeau duplication is e ⊕ e: numerator 2e, denominator 1 + e².
- `ChemOrganic.mobius4_charpoly`: Möbius 4-ring: x⁴ − 4x² + 4 = (x² − 2)², levels ±√2, each twice.

## FusedAlgebra.Topics.Chemistry.ChemPeriodic (12)

- `ChemPeriodic.diagCap_closed`: Diagonal s holds 2 · ⌈s/2⌉² electrons, in closed form.
- `ChemPeriodic.diagonals`
- `ChemPeriodic.gnomon`: Each gnomon is corner + two edges of the next square: 2l + 1 = 1 + l + l.
- `ChemPeriodic.gnomons_fill`: The gnomons fill the square: shell n = m + 1 holds n² orbitals.
- `ChemPeriodic.noble_gases`
- `ChemPeriodic.period_is_diagonal`: Period p (≥ 2) and the diagonal pair it covers agree: periods 2k and 2k+1 have the length of diagonals 2k+1 and 2k+2.
- `ChemPeriodic.periods`
- `ChemPeriodic.q_integer`: n copies of 1 under ◯[k] lift to (1 + k)ⁿ: with q = k + 1, that is qⁿ, so the weighted power is the q-integer (qⁿ − 1)/(q − 1).
- `ChemPeriodic.q_integer_classical`: At k = 0 (q = 1) the weighted power of 1 is n itself: the ordinary shell.
- `ChemPeriodic.shell_capacity`: With spin, shell n holds 2n² electrons.
- `ChemPeriodic.shell_square`: The shell square: (m + 1)² = 1 + m ◯ m, with m = n − 1.
- `ChemPeriodic.two_spins`: SO(4): two independent spins with multiplicity n each give n · n states, and (n − 1) combines by the circle operation with itself.

## FusedAlgebra.Topics.Chemistry.ChemQuantum (8)

- `ChemQuantum.cisd_drops_corner`: What truncated CI drops when two fragments are joined: exactly the corner.
- `ChemQuantum.cisd_drops_three`: What CISD drops from three fragments: every pair and the triple.
- `ChemQuantum.cisd_gap`: If e₁ solves the one-molecule problem, N·e₁ misses the N-molecule CISD equation by exactly N(N − 1)e₁², the pairs of molecules both excited.
- `ChemQuantum.cisd_not_extensive`: So N·e₁ solves N-molecule CISD only when N = 0, N = 1 or e₁ = 0: CISD is not size-extensive.
- `ChemQuantum.clebsch_gordan_square`
- `ChemQuantum.fragments_circ`: Two independent fragments: the excess amplitude combines by the circle operation.
- `ChemQuantum.spin_multiplicity`: Multiplicities multiply, so 2s (multiplicity − 1) combines by the circle operation.
- `ChemQuantum.three_fragments`: Three fragments: the corner expansion has pairs and one triple, nothing else.

## FusedAlgebra.Topics.Chemistry.ChemRates (8)

- `ChemRates.arrhenius_steps`: Arrhenius ratios over successive steps multiply (as exponents, they add).
- `ChemRates.first_order_point`: First order is the point (−1, 0): the landscape law reduces to circK (−1).
- `ChemRates.half_order_wall`: Order ½ empties in finite time: s^(1/2) = 1 − kt/2 hits 0 at t = 2/k. Cleared: k·(2/k) = 2.
- `ChemRates.osculating_disc`: The osculating point (−n, n(n−1)/2) has discriminant n(2 − n) (times 1, over Rat).
- `ChemRates.osculating_small`: Order 0, 1, 2 give q = 0, 0, 1 on that curve.
- `ChemRates.parallel_channels`: Parallel channels at a fixed time: survivals multiply too.
- `ChemRates.second_order_border`: (−2, 1) is on the border: k² = 4q.
- `ChemRates.second_order_lens`: Second order: with u = a/(1−a), v = b/(1−b), the sum u + v equals c/(1−c) where c = (a + b − 2ab)/(1 − ab). Cleared: (u + v)(1 − c) = c with all denominators multiplied out.

## FusedAlgebra.Topics.Chemistry.ChemStoichiometry (15)

- `ChemStoichiometry.bromine_121`: Bromine at 1 : 1: Br₂ is 1 : 2 : 1.
- `ChemStoichiometry.chlorine_961`: Chlorine at 3 : 1: Cl₂ is 9 : 6 : 1.
- `ChemStoichiometry.eight_fragments`: Eight fragments: three levels convergent, seven linear.
- `ChemStoichiometry.haber_balanced`: Haber balances: N 2 = 2·1, H 3·2 = 2·3.
- `ChemStoichiometry.loss_assoc`: Losses compose in any grouping (associativity of the fold).
- `ChemStoichiometry.loss_n`: n equal steps: overall loss 1 − (1 − ℓ)^n is the k = −1 power.
- `ChemStoichiometry.loss_two`: Two steps: overall loss is the k = −1 circle operation of the step losses.
- `ChemStoichiometry.magnetite`: Fe₃O₄ = FeO + Fe₂O₃: Fe:O 3:4 from 1:1 and 2:3.
- `ChemStoichiometry.magnetite_between`: 2/3 < 3/4 < 1/1 for Fe:O, by cross-multiplication.
- `ChemStoichiometry.mediant_between`: Mediant: combining a:b with c:d gives (a+c):(b+d), which lies between them.
- `ChemStoichiometry.multiple_proportions`
- `ChemStoichiometry.nitrogenase_family`: Nitrogenase's N₂ + 8 H → 2 NH₃ + H₂ balances, and so does N₂ + 6 H → 2 NH₃: stoichiometry alone leaves the H₂ count free.
- `ChemStoichiometry.respiration_balanced`: Respiration balances C, H, O: C₆H₁₂O₆ + 6 O₂ → 6 CO₂ + 6 H₂O.
- `ChemStoichiometry.ten_steps`: Ten steps at 90%: (9/10)^10 overall, so 1 − (9/10)^10 is lost.
- `ChemStoichiometry.two_atoms`: A two-isotope atom's polynomial p + q x; two atoms multiply to p² + 2pq x + q² x².

## FusedAlgebra.Topics.Clifford (53)

- `CliffordW.B4.anticomm`
- `CliffordW.B4.even_is_tmul`
- `CliffordW.B4.ext'`
- `CliffordW.B4.lift_circ`
- `CliffordW.B4.lift_mul`
- `CliffordW.B4.mul_assoc`
- `CliffordW.B4.theta_sq`
- `CliffordW.B4.u_sq`
- `CliffordW.B4.v_sq`
- `CliffordW.B4.vector_sq`
- `CliffordW.G4.blades_keep`
- `CliffordW.G4.e12_sq`
- `CliffordW.G4.ext'`
- `CliffordW.G4.mul_assoc`
- `CliffordW.G4.rev_mul`
- `CliffordW.G4.rev_star`
- `CliffordW.G4.star_assoc`
- `CliffordW.G4.star_comm_defect`
- `CliffordW.G4.star_lift`
- `CliffordW.G4.star_zero`
- `CliffordW.G4.vector_comm_wedge`
- `CliffordW.G4.vector_corner`
- `CliffordW.G4.vector_dial_walls`
- `CliffordW.circ_dial_null`: The circle operation's dial is null: K = c(1 + e) with e² = 1 has N = 0 and tr = 2c, so K² = 2c·K.
- `CliffordW.circ_form_split`: The circle operation's rate form x² + xy factors as x(x + y): two light lines (a split form).
- `CliffordW.circ_lift_tconj`: The circle operation: lift times conjugate = 1 + a, so the conjugate (1 + a) − aθ is (1 + a)·(lift of the inverse).
- `CliffordW.cmul_assoc`
- `CliffordW.cmul_comm`
- `CliffordW.complex_no_zero_norm`: κ = −1 (complex numbers): the norm vanishes only at 0, so the wall stays one point.
- `CliffordW.dial_discriminant`: The class invariant p² − 4q of the dial is 4κk₁²: the country is the sign of κ.
- `CliffordW.dial_lift`: (1 + aK)(1 + bK) = (1 − N·ab) + (a + b + tr·ab)K: a multivector dial with scalar arguments gives the landscape law with p = tr K, q = N K.
- `CliffordW.dial_sq`: Cayley–Hamilton: K² = tr(K)·K − N(K).
- `CliffordW.division_cases`
- `CliffordW.division_only`: Only R, C, H have no blade squaring to +1 (p + q ≤ 8): the wall of 1 + X stays a single point exactly in the three division algebras Cl(0,0), Cl(0,1), Cl(0,2).
- `CliffordW.dual_circ`
- `CliffordW.dual_nilpotent`: κ = 0 (dual numbers): e² = 0.
- `CliffordW.lift_tconj`: The lift times its conjugate is the rate V(a) = 1 + pa + qa².
- `CliffordW.mul_tconj`: X·X̄ is the scalar x² + pxy + qy² (the norm form, = the rate form).
- `CliffordW.omega_projector`: Clifford's elliptic ω (ω² = 1): (1 + ω)² = 2(1 + ω), so (1 + ω)/2 is idempotent, like θ.
- `CliffordW.omega_split`: x + yω ↦ (x + y, x − y) turns ω-multiplication into slot-by-slot multiplication: the elliptic biquaternion splits into a left half and a right half.
- `CliffordW.quaternion_no_zero_norm`: The quaternion case: the norm of x₀ + x₁i + x₂j + x₃k vanishes only at 0.
- `CliffordW.sig_bott_8`: Bott: eight more positive directions multiply the count by 16 = 2⁴.
- `CliffordW.sig_examples`
- `CliffordW.sig_shift_11`: Cl(p+1, q+1) ≅ M₂(Cl(p, q)): the count doubles.
- `CliffordW.sphere_norm_identity`
- `CliffordW.split_zero_divisor`: κ = +1 (split-complex): (1 + e)(1 − e) = 0, a zero divisor; the wall grows to two lines.
- `CliffordW.sq_eq_zero'`
- `CliffordW.sq_nonneg'`: Squares are nonnegative (core Int has no named lemma for this).
- `CliffordW.tconj_chart_inverse`: The conjugate of a lift, read in the chart, is the landscape inverse: its θ-slot over its scalar slot, −a/(1 + pa), composes with a to 0 (numerator of F vanishes).
- `CliffordW.tconj_mul`
- `CliffordW.tconj_tconj`
- `CliffordW.theta_idem`: θ is idempotent at the circle operation's point.
- `CliffordW.wall_zero_divisor`: The circle operation's wall is a zero divisor: (1 − θ)·θ = 0 when θ² = θ.

## FusedAlgebra.Topics.ClosingRate (7)

- `FusedRate.e_pow_lt`
- `FusedRate.grow`: Growth template: if each step multiplies the gap by at least p/q, then after j steps it is at least (p/q)^j times the start.
- `FusedRate.inv_hi`
- `FusedRate.inv_lo`
- `FusedRate.ladder`: Ladder lemma: if each rung is the one below it run after one step of `s`, then rung j is the bottom rung run after j steps of `s`. On [1,2] the fused-linear rungs satisfy this with f x = b^x and s x = b^(x-1), which gives H_k(b,x) = b^(s^(k-3)(x)).
- `FusedRate.sq_bound`
- `FusedRate.two_lt`

## FusedAlgebra.Topics.Combinatorics (35)

- `Combinatorics.S2_rows_bell`: Row sums are the Bell numbers, the t = 2 rung.
- `Combinatorics.alternating_corners`: k = −1: the signed corners cancel (inclusion–exclusion's engine).
- `Combinatorics.arrangements`
- `Combinatorics.bell_is_E_twice`: E(nonempty sets) = all set partitions: the Bell numbers (rung 2 of the flow on x).
- `Combinatorics.compositions_cube`: Compositions on the cube: m parts, choose which j are nonzero (a corner of the m-cube), then a composition of n into j positive parts. Vandermonde.
- `Combinatorics.cube_is_stirling`: **The cube with axis weights 1, …, n−1 has the Stirling cycle numbers as layers** (grade j = permutations with n − j cycles), checked for n = 1 … 8.
- `Combinatorics.cyc_row_sums`: Row sums are n!.
- `Combinatorics.derangements_incl_excl`
- `Combinatorics.derangements_values`
- `Combinatorics.dial_blocks_minus_one`
- `Combinatorics.ffold_fact`: **n! − 1 is a circle-operation fold**: 1 + (1 ◯ 2 ◯ ⋯ ◯ m) = (m + 1)!.
- `Combinatorics.ffold_small`
- `Combinatorics.flow_at_minus_one`: t = −1 is log(1 + x): signed cyclic orders −1, 2, −6.
- `Combinatorics.flow_at_one`: t = 1 is e^x − 1: 1/2, 1/6, 1/24.
- `Combinatorics.flow_at_three`: t = 3 counts partitions of partitions: 2, 12, 60 (with the factorials).
- `Combinatorics.flow_at_two`: t = 2 counts set partitions: 2!·a₂ = 2, 3!·a₃ = 5, 4!·a₄ = 15.
- `Combinatorics.flow_order2`: Group law, order 2: Eˢ ∘ Eᵗ = Eˢ⁺ᵗ.
- `Combinatorics.flow_order3`: Group law, order 3: x³ coefficient of f(g(x)) is b₃ + 2a₂b₂ + a₃.
- `Combinatorics.flow_order4`: Group law, order 4: x⁴ coefficient of f(g(x)) is b₄ + a₂(2b₃ + b₂²) + 3a₃b₂ + a₄.
- `Combinatorics.foldCirc_replicate`: n copies of a, folded by the circle operation, is the circle power.
- `Combinatorics.graphs_from_connected`: E(connected graphs) = all graphs: 2^C(n,2).
- `Combinatorics.groups_length`: The corners of the n-cube: 2ⁿ sub-lists.
- `Combinatorics.half_rung_x4`: The half rung has a zero x⁴ coefficient ...
- `Combinatorics.kfold_lift`
- `Combinatorics.nonempty_subsets`: On Int: 2ⁿ − 1 nonempty subsets.
- `Combinatorics.nonempty_subsets_lift`: The circle operation of a with itself n times, at a = 1: the lift of 2ⁿ − 1 nonempty subsets.
- `Combinatorics.painted_subsets`: **The dial paints subsets.** The corners of the n-cube, each switched-on axis weighing k, add up to (1 + k)ⁿ: every element is off, or on in one of k colours.
- `Combinatorics.parity_corners`: k = −2: the parity dial gives (−1)ⁿ.
- `Combinatorics.prodL_eq_zero_of_mem`
- `Combinatorics.quarter_rung_negative`: ... and a quarter rung has a negative x³ coefficient: fractional rungs do not count.
- `Combinatorics.rung_three`: E(Bell) = partitions of partitions (rung 3 of the flow on x).
- `Combinatorics.sign_balance`: **Signs of permutations balance.** At k = −1 the entry 1 is on the wall, so the signed count Π(1 − i) of permutations of m + 2 things is 0.
- `Combinatorics.stirling_row4`
- `Combinatorics.wall_entry_absorbs`: **The wall swallows the fold.** If one entry sits on the wall (1 + k·a = 0), the lift of the whole fold is 0.
- `Combinatorics.wfold_lift`: The dial lift of a whole list.

## FusedAlgebra.Topics.ComplexAnalysis (39)

- `ComplexAnalysis.Cx.ext'`
- `ComplexAnalysis.Eis.ext'`
- `ComplexAnalysis.Mob.ext'`
- `ComplexAnalysis.affine_compose`: Composing two of them combines their parameters by the circle operation.
- `ComplexAnalysis.affine_fixing_wall`: An affine map z ↦ αz + β that fixes −1 is the circle operation by α − 1.
- `ComplexAnalysis.cayley_tan`: Cayley: the tan law (p, q) = (0, 1) has walls ±i, and (1 + i·x)/(1 − i·x) multiplies.
- `ComplexAnalysis.circle_closed`: The circle |1 + z| = 1 (centre the wall, through 0) is closed under the circle operation.
- `ComplexAnalysis.circle_equation`: The same circle as an equation: 2x + x² + y² = 0, the k = 1 rotation condition of the linear-algebra page.
- `ComplexAnalysis.corner_bound`: The size bound for one corner: if |a| ≤ A and |b| ≤ B then |a ◯ b| ≤ A ◯ B.
- `ComplexAnalysis.double_wall_adds`: One double wall (p = 2c, q = c²): ψ(x) = x/(1 + cx) turns the law into addition.
- `ComplexAnalysis.eis_root_three`: n = 3: ω − 1 combined by the circle operation three times is 0.
- `ComplexAnalysis.eis_roots_total`: The three roots 0, ω − 1, ω² − 1 = −2 − ω combine by the circle operation to 0 (odd n).
- `ComplexAnalysis.fixed_points_are_walls`: The fixed points of b ↦ F(a,b) solve a·(1 + p·b + q·b²) = 0: the walls, for every a.
- `ComplexAnalysis.fold_bound`: Weierstrass's bound: the fold of the pieces is bounded by the fold of their sizes, |(a₁ ◯ … ◯ aₙ)| ≤ |a₁| ◯ … ◯ |aₙ| = ∏(1 + |aᵢ|) − 1.
- `ComplexAnalysis.fold_wall_iff_int`: On the integers the converse holds too: a fold hits the wall only if a piece does (the zeros of a product are the zeros of its factors).
- `ComplexAnalysis.fold_wall_of_mem`: If one piece sits on the wall, the whole fold sits on the wall.
- `ComplexAnalysis.gauss_root_four`: n = 4: i − 1 combined by the circle operation four times is 0 (any commutative ring).
- `ComplexAnalysis.gauss_root_steps`: …but not before: twice gives −2, three times gives −1 − i (on the integers).
- `ComplexAnalysis.gauss_roots_on_circle`: All four lie on the circle |1 + z| = 1.
- `ComplexAnalysis.gauss_roots_total`: The four roots of the square combine by the circle operation to −2 (even n).
- `ComplexAnalysis.ma_assoc`
- `ComplexAnalysis.ma_comm`
- `ComplexAnalysis.ma_lift`: The lift on the complex plane.
- `ComplexAnalysis.ma_zero`
- `ComplexAnalysis.mroot_of_unity`: If ζⁿ = 1 then ζ − 1 is an n-th circle root of 0.
- `ComplexAnalysis.normSq_mul`: Lengths multiply (Brahmagupta–Fibonacci two-square identity).
- `ComplexAnalysis.omega_cubed`: ω really is a cube root of 1: ω·ω·ω = 1.
- `ComplexAnalysis.prodL_eq_zero_int`
- `ComplexAnalysis.size_nonneg`: Sizes are never negative under the circle operation of sizes.
- `ComplexAnalysis.spin_about_wall`: Seen from the wall, z ↦ z ◯ w is multiplication by 1 + w: a spin and a stretch.
- `ComplexAnalysis.step_is_turned_w`: The step from z to z ◯ w is the vector w turned and stretched by 1 + z (so triangle (−1, 0, w) and triangle (−1, z, z ◯ w) are similar).
- `ComplexAnalysis.tate_glue`: Gluing (1 + a) ~ q(1 + a) respects the circle operation, so the punctured plane folds into a torus.
- `ComplexAnalysis.trans_compose`: Translations compose by the law itself: T(a)∘T(b) = T(a + b + p·ab over 1 − q·ab).
- `ComplexAnalysis.trans_det`: det T(a) is the wall polynomial: the translation degenerates exactly at a wall.
- `ComplexAnalysis.trans_type`: tr² − 4·det = N²(p² − 4q): the discriminant alone sets every translation's type.
- `ComplexAnalysis.two_walls_circ`: The circle operation is the case of one wall at −1 and the other at infinity (r₁ = −1, r₂ = 0).
- `ComplexAnalysis.two_walls_lift`: Two walls (reciprocals r₁, r₂, so p = −(r₁ + r₂), q = r₁r₂): ζ(x) = (1 − r₁x)/(1 − r₂x) turns the law into multiplication. Cross-multiplied, with F = N/D.
- `ComplexAnalysis.wall_distance_mul`: Squared distances from the wall multiply under the circle operation.
- `ComplexAnalysis.wall_fixed`: The wall −1 absorbs: it is the fixed point of every map z ↦ z ◯ w.

## FusedAlgebra.Topics.Consensus (27)

- `ConsensusFused.additive_split`: An additive weight is split-proof: n equal accounts weigh what one does.
- `ConsensusFused.bft_safe`
- `ConsensusFused.bft_unsafe`
- `ConsensusFused.caught`: m independent watchers each catching a double vote with chance d: caught = d ◯[−1] d ◯[−1] … = 1 − (1 − d)^m. Same for the share of an old validator set that has left after t epochs at churn c.
- `ConsensusFused.coin_age_flip`: Coin age s·(1+t): the 20% attacker outweighs the 80% honest side.
- `ConsensusFused.dbl_lift`
- `ConsensusFused.depth_useless`: Powers of the identity do nothing: at q = 1/2 waiting more blocks buys no security.
- `ConsensusFused.either`: k = −1 is "at least one of two independent events".
- `ConsensusFused.fused_age_flip`: Fused exponent (1+s)^(1+t) − 1: the same flip, more strongly.
- `ConsensusFused.half_is_identity`: At the 51% line the step is exactly the circle-operation identity.
- `ConsensusFused.layers_sum`
- `ConsensusFused.linear_keeps`: Plain stake keeps the honest majority.
- `ConsensusFused.maj3_closed`
- `ConsensusFused.maj3_drift`: The pull toward 0 below 1/2 and toward 1 above it.
- `ConsensusFused.maj3_half`
- `ConsensusFused.maj3_mirror`
- `ConsensusFused.maj3_shrinks`
- `ConsensusFused.merge_cut`: Ethereum before the Merge ~23 TWh, after ~0.0026 TWh (CCRI 2022): over 99.98% less.
- `ConsensusFused.mpow_lift`: The circle power climbs the cube: 1 + k·(a ◯[k] … ◯[k] a) = (1 + k·a)^n.
- `ConsensusFused.mpow_lift1`
- `ConsensusFused.one_quorum`: The longest-chain rule needs only a majority: one "quorum" against the rest.
- `ConsensusFused.pietrzak_halves`
- `ConsensusFused.quarter_six`: Catch-up probability as a circle power, checked exactly for q = 1/4, z = 6: r = 1/3, a = −2/3, and 1 + a^(◯6) = (1/3)^6.
- `ConsensusFused.step_formula`: The step a = r − 1 written without division.
- `ConsensusFused.sybil_corner`
- `ConsensusFused.sybil_gain`
- `ConsensusFused.three_quorums`: Three quorums sharing an honest validator need f < n/4.

## FusedAlgebra.Topics.Conventions (7)

- `Conventions.circle_conventions`: k = 1 is the plus convention (mathlib's `PreQuasiregular`), k = −1 the minus convention.
- `Conventions.circle_mirror`: **Both ring-theory conventions are on the dial.** Texts that write the circle operation as a + b − ab are using k = −1; texts that write a + b + ab are using k = +1. The two are mirror images under a ↦ −a: a ∘₋ₖ b = −((−a) ∘ₖ (−b)).
- `Conventions.circle_zero`: k = 0 is plain addition.
- `Conventions.fair_example`: Counting check: at k = 1 one weight x = 1 (a fair element) has probability 1/2, and two fair elements joined (weights 1 ◯ 1 = 3) have probability 3/4 = 1/2 + 1/2 − 1/2·1/2, the "either" rule at k = −1.
- `Conventions.one_sub_prob`: 1 − kμ = 1/(1 + kx).
- `Conventions.prob_inverse`: Flipping twice gets back: prob (−k) (prob k x) ... the inverse map is x = μ/(1 − kμ).
- `Conventions.weight_prob_flip`: **The sign flip.** If weights x, y compose to circK k x y, their probabilities compose by the opposite dial: prob of the composite = circK (−k) of the probs.

## FusedAlgebra.Topics.CornerWeights (12)

- `CornerWeights.blend_three_body`: Straight blend from a diagonal matrix (s = 0) toward unit-diagonal coupling (s = 1): the 3×3 corner is the product of the three pair factors plus a three-body remainder. Cauchy couplings are exactly the ones that make the remainder vanish at s = 1.
- `CornerWeights.cauchy2_cleared`: 2×2 Cauchy minor, denominators cleared: the pair factor's numerator (pᵢ−pⱼ)(qᵢ−qⱼ).
- `CornerWeights.cauchy3_cleared`: 3×3 Cauchy determinant, denominators cleared (row i times ∏ₗ (xᵢ + yₗ)): the minor is a product over the three pairs, with no three-body factor.
- `CornerWeights.companion_cube`: Cayley–Hamilton for the companion matrix N = [[k, 1], [−d, 0]] (entries listed row by row): N³ = U₃·N − d·U₂·I with U₂ = k, U₃ = k² − d.
- `CornerWeights.det3_I_add_tM`: The corner sum is a determinant: det(I + tM) = 1 + t·(rank-1 corners) + t²·(rank-2 principal minors) + t³·det M. Each corner of the 3-cube is a principal minor.
- `CornerWeights.hard_core_is_addition`: Hard core (A = 0) is plain addition.
- `CornerWeights.kdv_pair_velocity`: KdV: with u = (p − 1)/(p + 1) = tanh(θ/2), (pᵢ−pⱼ)/(pᵢ+pⱼ) is the relativistic velocity subtraction (uᵢ − uⱼ)/(1 − uᵢuⱼ). Numerator and denominator, cleared.
- `CornerWeights.kp_pair_cross_ratio`: KP: the pair factor's numerator is the gap between the two cross products, so A = 1 − (pᵢ+qᵢ)(pⱼ+qⱼ)/((pᵢ+qⱼ)(pⱼ+qᵢ)): one minus a cross-ratio.
- `CornerWeights.lucas_two_dets_den`: Lucas weights from two determinants (denominator): α·det(I+βM) − β·det(I+αM) = (α − β)(1 − d·(U₁ e₂ + U₂ e₃)).
- `CornerWeights.lucas_two_dets_num`: Lucas weights from two determinants (numerator): with k = α + β, d = αβ, det(I + αM) − det(I + βM) = (α − β)(e₁ + U₂ e₂ + U₃ e₃), U₂ = k, U₃ = k² − d.
- `CornerWeights.plane_resolvent`: The plane law (x + y + kxy)/(1 − dxy) is our operation with dial α − β in the resolvent coordinate x/(1 + βx): denominator and numerator, cleared.
- `CornerWeights.size_pair_not_iterated`: The size × pair candidate with one shared pair factor A is not an iterated two-input law: three inputs differ from iterating a + b + kA·ab by k²A²(A − 1)·abc.

## FusedAlgebra.Topics.CutIndependence (14)

- `CutIndependence.cassini`: Cassini: u(n+1)² − u(n)·u(n+2) = 1 = dⁿ, the determinant of the step matrix to the n.
- `CutIndependence.dims_r2`: q = e^(iπ/2): [2] = 0, the dimensions run 0, 1, 0 and first vanish at n = 2.
- `CutIndependence.dims_r3`: q = e^(iπ/3): [2] = q + 1/q = 1, the dimensions run 0, 1, 1, 0 and first vanish at n = 3.
- `CutIndependence.disc_factor`
- `CutIndependence.leftoverE_zero_iff`
- `CutIndependence.leftoverF_zero_iff`
- `CutIndependence.step_closes_r2`
- `CutIndependence.step_closes_r3`: It closes up at a root of unity: M³ = −1 for [2] = 1 and M² = −1 for [2] = 0.
- `CutIndependence.swap_assoc`: ((xᵃyᵇ)(xᶜyᵈ))(xᵉyᶠ) and (xᵃyᵇ)((xᶜyᵈ)(xᵉyᶠ)) pick up the same power of q.
- `CutIndependence.u_at_two`: At q = 1 ([2] = 2) the sequence is u n = n: it never vanishes, so nothing truncates.
- `CutIndependence.u_step`
- `CutIndependence.u_two`
- `CutIndependence.w2_r2`
- `CutIndependence.w2_r3`: The vertex weight w² = Σ_{2j+1 < r} [2j+1]²: 2 at r = 3 and 1 at r = 2, both equal to r / (2 sin²(π/r)).

## FusedAlgebra.Topics.DarkSector (17)

- `DarkSector.circ_ge_add`
- `DarkSector.circ_growth_is_exponential`
- `DarkSector.corner_le_quarter`: The corner is at most a quarter of the square on the sum: 4ab ≤ (a+b)². In units of the scale s, the extra pull relative to the plain sum is at most (a+b)/4 of it, so it fades as the fields weaken. Dark matter shows up in exactly the opposite place: where fields are weakest.
- `DarkSector.cube_is_mpow3`
- `DarkSector.cube_layers`
- `DarkSector.deep_mond_square`: Deep-MOND limit: the observed pull is the side of the square whose area is the rectangle g_N × a₀.
- `DarkSector.fexp_growth_trivial`
- `DarkSector.fexp_growth_trivial'`
- `DarkSector.foldCirc_ge_sum`
- `DarkSector.lcdm_is_weighted_cube`: Flat ΛCDM, exactly: Ωm(1+z)³ + (1-Ωm) = 1 + Ωm·(z ◯ z ◯ z). The cosmological constant is the unit cube; matter is the weighted shell of the (1+z) cube around it.
- `DarkSector.minv_lift`
- `DarkSector.mu_is_neg_minv`
- `DarkSector.pow_growth_trivial`: The same in the unshifted form: a(t+s) = a(t)^a(s) with a(0) = 1 forces a ≡ 1.
- `DarkSector.redshift_compose`
- `DarkSector.redshift_compose_int`: The same with integers, so blueshifts (negative z) are covered too.
- `DarkSector.redshift_power`: n equal stretches: (1+z)^n = 1 + (z ◯ z ◯ … ◯ z).
- `DarkSector.simple_mond_square`: Simple-MOND law g_N = g·μ(g/a₀) written without division is g·g = g_N·g + g_N·a₀ : the square on the observed pull equals two rectangles, g_N by g and g_N by a₀.

## FusedAlgebra.Topics.DataCenter (16)

- `DataCenterFused.add_unit_iff`: So add a unit exactly when X² passes (a/c)·k(k+1): the switch point is x*·√(k(k+1)), the geometric mean of k and k+1 in units of the sweet spot x* = √(a/c).
- `DataCenterFused.additive_books_corner`: The additive breakdown PUE = 1 + PLF + CLF books the corner as cooling: CLF = c + p*c.
- `DataCenterFused.cube_layers`: Layers 1, 3, 3, 1 of the fused cube (1+d)³ − 1 = 3d + 3d² + d³.
- `DataCenterFused.equal_share`: Equal sharing is best for a square loss: shifting d from one unit to the other costs 2d².
- `DataCenterFused.foldCirc_lift_nat`
- `DataCenterFused.foldCirc_split`: Splitting the foldCirc at the IT meter: (outside ◯ inside) is the whole foldCirc (TUE = PUE·ITUE).
- `DataCenterFused.foldCirc_swap`: Swapping two neighbouring stages never changes the total: blame by position is not physical.
- `DataCenterFused.lifts_append`
- `DataCenterFused.loss_costs_one_plus_c`: One watt of electrical loss removed saves (1 + c) watts at the meter.
- `DataCenterFused.pue_corner`
