- `DataCenterFused.scale_anywhere`: Position independence: scaling one stage's factor scales the facility total by the same amount, wherever that stage sits.
- `DataCenterFused.spare_fan`: N+1 fans at speed N versus N fans at speed N+1 (same total flow N(N+1)): power ratio N² : (N+1)², so running the spare always helps.
- `DataCenterFused.spare_fan_helps`
- `DataCenterFused.spread`: k fans each moving q: k·q³·k² = (k·q)³, i.e. total power = Q³/k² for total flow Q = k q.
- `DataCenterFused.stack`
- `DataCenterFused.unit_gap`: Exact gap: k(k+1)·[cost(k) − cost(k+1)] = c X² − k(k+1) a.

## FusedAlgebra.Topics.DialAudit (9)

- `CircleOp.DialAudit.curvature_reading`: The curvature reading −Δ/4 = d − k²/4, written without division: 4·(d − k²/4) = 4d − k².
- `CircleOp.DialAudit.discriminant_scales`: The discriminant Δ = k² − 4d scales like unit², so Δ/k² is unit-free and Δ itself is not.
- `CircleOp.DialAudit.homothety_moves_with_units`: k is not: in units λ times bigger the fused law's dial changes from k to k·λ (the repo's units_change_weight, restated here next to the q statement it contrasts with).
- `CircleOp.DialAudit.odds_absorb_k`: Three odds fused at dial k, scaled by k, are the k = 1 fusion of the odds k·t.
- `CircleOp.DialAudit.odds_absorb_k_lift`: The same for the lift: 1 + k·(fusion) is the product of the three factors 1 + k·t.
- `CircleOp.DialAudit.qint_is_circ_power`: [n]_q is the n-fold ◯[q − 1] power of 1: the q of counting is the homothety dial k = q − 1.
- `CircleOp.DialAudit.qint_pow`
- `CircleOp.DialAudit.ratio_unit_free`: Under the unit change k ↦ λk, d ↦ λ²d the ratio d/k² is unchanged (cross-multiplied form).
- `CircleOp.DialAudit.swap_constant_unit_free`: If two moves obey y·x = q·x·y, rescaling them (by scalars α, β) keeps the same q.

## FusedAlgebra.Topics.DialPointProcesses (40)

- `DialPointProcesses.boson_superpoisson`
- `DialPointProcesses.count_parabola`: Every dial count lands on one parabola: p² − 4q = 1, two real walls.
- `DialPointProcesses.count_walls`: Its two walls are the empty mode and the full mode: 1 + p·x + q·x² = (1 + s·x)(1 + (1+s)·x).
- `DialPointProcesses.dial_rescale`: The dial identity behind it: scaling values by c and the weight by 1/c.
- `DialPointProcesses.empty_mode_multadd`: s = 0 (rare points, Poisson): the circle operation.
- `DialPointProcesses.fact2_formula`
- `DialPointProcesses.fano_line`: The Fano line: variance is affine in k, through the Poisson value at k = 0.
- `DialPointProcesses.fermion_subpoisson`: Fermion side (k ≤ 0) is sub-Poisson, boson side (k ≥ 0) super-Poisson.
- `DialPointProcesses.fold_append`: Two independent groups of modes: the fold of the whole is the dial sum of the parts.
- `DialPointProcesses.fold_boson`: Bosons (k = +1): the fold is the plain circle operation of the eigenvalues.
- `DialPointProcesses.fold_fermion`: Fermions (k = −1): the fold is P(at least one point) = 1 − ∏(1 − μᵢ).
- `DialPointProcesses.fold_lift`: The hole lift: 1 + k·fold = ∏(1 + k·μᵢ), the determinant det(I + kK).
- `DialPointProcesses.fold_poisson`: Poisson (k = 0): the fold is the expected count Σμ.
- `DialPointProcesses.fold_scale`: k·fold is the plain circle operation of the scaled eigenvalues k·μᵢ.
- `DialPointProcesses.fold_swap`: Order of the modes never matters.
- `DialPointProcesses.fold_two`: Two modes, written out: the corner carries the dial weight.
- `DialPointProcesses.full_mode_union`: s = −1 (a full fermion mode): the union of failures, k = −1 on the dial line.
- `DialPointProcesses.half_mode_velocity`: s = −1/2 (a half-filled fermion mode): velocity addition with speed limit 2.
- `DialPointProcesses.lifted_kernel_same`: The lifted kernel k·K is what the count sees: unchanged by (k, K) ↦ (k/c, cK).
- `DialPointProcesses.modeVar_empty`
- `DialPointProcesses.modeVar_expand`
- `DialPointProcesses.modeVar_poisson`: Poisson: variance equals mean.
- `DialPointProcesses.modeVar_rescale`
- `DialPointProcesses.modeVar_wall`: On the wall the count is fixed: no variance.
- `DialPointProcesses.mode_fano`: Fano factor of a mode, Var/mean = 1 + kμ (the lift).
- `DialPointProcesses.particle_hole`: Particle-hole: s and −1 − s give the same shape.
- `DialPointProcesses.prodL_zero_of_mem`
- `DialPointProcesses.rate_shift`: The mean's rate, re-centred: V(μ + h) = V(μ) + (1 + 2kμ)h + k·h².
- `DialPointProcesses.shape_is_fano`: Shape p = 2·Fano − 1 (Fano = 1 + s), so the units-free shape p²/(p² − 4q) = (2F − 1)².
- `DialPointProcesses.slots_full`: k = −1/m with eigenvalue m (the wall): each of the m slots is filled for sure.
- `DialPointProcesses.sq_nonneg'`
- `DialPointProcesses.tilt_in_ruler`: Tilted mean: weighting a mode's count by tᴺ gives mean μt/(1 + kμ(1 − t)). Read in the ruler (mean − μ)/Var it is u/(1 − kμ·u), u = t − 1.
- `DialPointProcesses.tilt_lens`: Tilts compose by the circle operation in u, and by the lens law in the ruler: p = 1 + 2s, q = s(1 + s).
- `DialPointProcesses.two_fermion_copies`: Two fermion copies, each with eigenvalue μ/2, leave a mode empty with chance (1 − μ/2)²: the k = −1/2 hole with eigenvalue μ.
- `DialPointProcesses.var_append`: Disjoint groups of modes: variances add.
- `DialPointProcesses.var_formula`: Var N = Σμ + k·Σμ² = tr K + k·tr K².
- `DialPointProcesses.var_from_fact2`: Var = E N(N−1) + E N − (E N)², checked against the two formulas.
- `DialPointProcesses.var_superpose`: Variance of m superposed copies: Var(k/m, mK) = m·Var(k, K).
- `DialPointProcesses.wall_absorbs`: The wall absorbs the fold: once one mode is at −1/k, so is the whole.
- `DialPointProcesses.wall_mode_fills`: An eigenvalue on the wall −1/k (k·μ = −1) kills the lift: 1 + k·fold = 0, so the hole probability (1 + k·fold)^(−1/k) is 0 for k < 0.

## FusedAlgebra.Topics.DialPotential (24)

- `DialPotential.circ_mirror`: A dial against its mirror: k ◯ (−k) = −k².
- `DialPotential.conformal_gamma`: A conformal dial couples to matter with α0² = 1/6 (the same factor that gives slope √(2/3)); then PPN γ = 1 − 2α0²/(1 + α0²) = 5/7, against Cassini's 1 ± 2.3·10⁻⁵.
- `DialPotential.cw_mass_negative`: Coleman–Weinberg (Gildener–Weinberg) Higgs: 6mW⁴ + 3mZ⁴ − 12mt⁴ < 0, so m_h² < 0, with mW = 80.37, mZ = 91.19, mt = 172.57 GeV. Holds with a wide margin (factor 22).
- `DialPotential.cw_mass_negative_robust`: Even with the top 10 GeV lighter and W, Z 10 GeV heavier the sign stays negative.
- `DialPotential.dial_flip`: Flip: the dial −k is the dial k seen through a ↦ −a.
- `DialPotential.dial_rescale`: Rescaling: if k'·s = k, the dial k' on (s·a, s·b) is s times the dial k on (a, b).
- `DialPotential.dial_zero`: At weight zero the dial is plain addition: the symmetric point.
- `DialPotential.exp_fixed_point`: With x² = λ²/6 and y² = 1 − λ²/6 (L = λ²): Ω_φ = x² + y² = 1 and x² − y² = L/3 − 1, so w = L/3 − 1.
- `DialPotential.exp_fixed_point_exists`: The fixed point exists (y² ≥ 0) only for λ² ≤ 6.
- `DialPotential.hump_height`: Hump height = m²v²/8 with m² = 2λv².
- `DialPotential.quartic_mass`: (λ/4)((v + x)² − v²)² = ½(2λv²)x² + λv x³ + (λ/4)x⁴: the mass squared is 2λv².
- `DialPotential.scale_covariant_is_power`: A potential that scales as V(s·k) = sⁿ V(k) is a single power: V(k) = V(1)·kⁿ.
- `DialPotential.slope_coupling`: Slope and coupling are tied: for V ∝ kⁿ the slope is n·√(2/3), so λ² = (2/3)n² = 4n²·α0².
- `DialPotential.veltman_too_heavy`: Veltman: 4mt² − 2mW² − mZ² > 4·mH² (mH = 125.20 GeV), so the Veltman Higgs is over twice as heavy.
- `DialPotential.w_conformal_one`: Conformal slope n = 1 (λ² = 2/3): w = −7/9 at the fixed point.
- `DialPotential.w_conformal_two`: Conformal slope n = 2 (λ² = 8/3): w = −1/9 at the fixed point.
- `DialPotential.well_at_dip`: Expansion at the dip: no linear term, curvature 8k0² (twice the h² coefficient 4k0²).
- `DialPotential.well_breaks_scale`: The double well cannot respect the rescaling for any power n: a scale from outside sets k0.
- `DialPotential.well_dips`: Zero at both dips.
- `DialPotential.well_eq`
- `DialPotential.well_even`: The well is even: it respects the flip.
- `DialPotential.well_hump`: The hump at the symmetric point k = 0 has height k0⁴.
- `DialPotential.well_one_one`
- `DialPotential.well_one_two`

## FusedAlgebra.Topics.DialSurvey (18)

- `CircleOp.DialSurvey.bose_exponents`
- `CircleOp.DialSurvey.cyclotomic_z2`: z² coefficient: (1 − z)^(−M1) gives M1(M1+1)/2, (1 − z²)^(−M2) gives M2; together k². Every two-letter word splits uniquely into Lyndon words.
- `CircleOp.DialSurvey.cyclotomic_z3`: z³ coefficient: k(k+1)(k+2)/6 + k·M2 + M3 = k³.
- `CircleOp.DialSurvey.exponent_ruler`: The uniform dial 2^a − 1 is the exponent a read through the circle-operation ruler: dials combine by the circle operation exactly when exponents add.
- `CircleOp.DialSurvey.fall_full`: At e = m it does not vanish: m copies are allowed.
- `CircleOp.DialSurvey.fall_vanish`: At m (a whole number) the falling product vanishes past e = m: with k = −1/m no element appears more than m times.
- `CircleOp.DialSurvey.fct_succ`
- `CircleOp.DialSurvey.fermi_exponents`: k = −1: exponents M/k on ζ(Ns) are 1, −1, 0: Z = ζ(s)/ζ(2s). k = +1: exponents 1, 0, 0: Z = ζ(s).
- `CircleOp.DialSurvey.kRise_bose`: k = +1: kRise is e!, so every multiplicity has weight 1 (multisets).
- `CircleOp.DialSurvey.kRise_fermi`: k = −1: an element may appear at most once (subsets).
- `CircleOp.DialSurvey.kRise_parafermi`: k = −1/m: m^e · kRise = m(m−1)…(m−e+1).
- `CircleOp.DialSurvey.kRise_poisson`: k = 0: kRise is 1, so the coefficient is 1/e! (the exponential, Poisson).
- `CircleOp.DialSurvey.kRise_succ`
- `CircleOp.DialSurvey.necklace_whole`: For whole k the necklace counts are whole: k² − k is even, k³ − k divisible by 3.
- `CircleOp.DialSurvey.odds_complement`: The complement of a subset with odds t has odds 1/t.
- `CircleOp.DialSurvey.odds_union`: Odds t ↦ probability t/(1+t); "in A or in B" (independent) has odds t ◯ s.
- `CircleOp.DialSurvey.units_change_weight`: In units λ times bigger, weight k becomes weight k·λ.
- `CircleOp.DialSurvey.weight_to_one`: With units 1/k the weight becomes 1: every nonzero k is the circle operation in its own units.

## FusedAlgebra.Topics.DialSweep (28)

- `DialSweep.boost_walls`: κ = 1 (boosts): det(I + aU) = (1 − a)(1 + a), walls at the speed of light ±1.
- `DialSweep.cayley_weighted`: The weighted Cayley transform (I + kS)⁻¹(I − kS) is the Cayley transform of k·s.
- `DialSweep.det_charpoly2`: det(I + kA) = k²·p_A(−1/k) (2×2), for k ≠ 0.
- `DialSweep.det_charpoly3`: det(I + kA) = (−k)³·p_A(−1/k) (3×3), for k ≠ 0.
- `DialSweep.det_one_sub_2`: k = −1 is the characteristic polynomial at 1 (Fredholm's λ is −k).
- `DialSweep.det_one_sub_3`
- `DialSweep.det_top`: The top of the dial: det(I + kA) − k³·det A has only lower powers of k.
- `DialSweep.dialU_sq`
- `DialSweep.dial_det`: det(I + aU) = 1 + k·a + q·a², the landscape's wall polynomial.
- `DialSweep.dial_det_eigen`: With eigenvalues λ₁, λ₂ (k = λ₁ + λ₂, q = λ₁λ₂) the walls are a = −1/λᵢ.
- `DialSweep.dial_discriminant`: The three countries are the sign of the discriminant, the squared eigenvalue gap.
- `DialSweep.dial_lens`: The landscape lens law comes out of the dial matrix: off the wall 1 − q·ab ≠ 0, (I + aU)(I + bU) = (1 − q·ab)·(I + cU) with c = (a + b + k·ab)/(1 − q·ab).
- `DialSweep.dial_product`: (I + aU)(I + bU) = (1 − q·ab)·I + (a + b + k·ab)·U: the landscape law, lifted.
- `DialSweep.kcirc_lift`
- `DialSweep.or_line`: The "or" line: a wall at certainty a = 1 exactly when 1 + k + q = 0.
- `DialSweep.orth_neg_one`: At k = −1 the rotation condition is X + Xᵀ − XᵀX = 0: I − X is orthogonal.
- `DialSweep.rank_one3`: A rank-one weight leaves no corner terms: det(I + u·vᵀ) = 1 + vᵀu.
- `DialSweep.rank_one_dial3`: The matrix determinant lemma: det(I + A·u·vᵀ) = 1 + vᵀ·A·u.
- `DialSweep.rotation_no_wall`: κ = −1 (rotations): det(I + aU) = 1 + a², no real wall.
- `DialSweep.scale2`
- `DialSweep.scale3`
- `DialSweep.shear_is_add`: κ = 0 (shears): det(I + aU) = 1, and the law is plain addition.
- `DialSweep.sylvester2`
- `DialSweep.sylvester3`
- `DialSweep.unitU_sq`
- `DialSweep.unit_product`: (I + aU)(I + bU) = (1 + κ·ab)·I + (a + b)·U: projected, (a + b)/(1 + κ·ab).
- `DialSweep.wall_iff_eigen2`: The matrix sits on the wall of weight k exactly when −1/k is an eigenvalue.
- `DialSweep.winv`: The weighted inverse: off the weighted wall, a ◯[k] (−a/(1 + k·a)) = 0.

## FusedAlgebra.Topics.DialWall (15)

- `CircleOp.DialWall.M2_zero_iff`
- `CircleOp.DialWall.M3_zero_iff`
- `CircleOp.DialWall.M5_zero_iff`
- `CircleOp.DialWall.bose_all_zero`: k = +1: every necklace count from length 2 on is 0 (only ζ(s) is left).
- `CircleOp.DialWall.cancel_half`: The cancellation: three contributors with one zero each cancel at k = -1/2.
- `CircleOp.DialWall.cancel_half'`
- `CircleOp.DialWall.copies_len2`: Copies points, length 2: 2 m² M2(-1/m) = 1 + m.
- `CircleOp.DialWall.copies_len3`: Copies points, length 3: 3 m³ M3(-1/m) = m² - 1.
- `CircleOp.DialWall.fermi_from_three`: k = -1: from length 3 on every count is 0; length 2 gives 1 (the ζ(2s) of ζ(s)/ζ(2s)).
- `CircleOp.DialWall.half_exponent`: k = 1/2: the exponent at ρ/2 is M2/k = -1/4, not a whole number, so a branch point.
- `CircleOp.DialWall.one_contributor`: On the critical line ρ/N has real part 1/(2N); N'·ρ/N is back on the line only for N' = N.
- `CircleOp.DialWall.unit_plus_multiple_ne_zero`: Rational root step: ±1 + m·A is never 0 for a whole m ≥ 2.
- `CircleOp.DialWall.words_4`: Words of length 4 split by period: 4·M4 + 2·M2 + 1·M1 = k^4.
- `CircleOp.DialWall.words_5`
- `CircleOp.DialWall.words_6`

## FusedAlgebra.Topics.DiamondLattice (48)

- `DiamondLattice.attract_identity`: Attracting exactly when (1 − y)(1 + 3y) > 0: (y + 1)² − (2y)² = (1 − y)(1 + 3y).
- `DiamondLattice.bend_recip`: The bend is the reciprocal of the second wall.
- `DiamondLattice.colour_bond`: The colouring bond v = −1 sits at t = 1/(1 − Q).
- `DiamondLattice.colour_wall_doubles`: The parallel law doubles its own colouring wall to itself: the critical value of T.
- `DiamondLattice.crit_colour`: Critical points besides 0, ±1: where t² = 1/(1−Q), i.e. 1 + (Q−1)t² = 0.
- `DiamondLattice.crs_cubic`: The fixed point in the Chang–Roeder–Shrock form q² + 2qv − v³ = 0, v = y² − 1.
- `DiamondLattice.dden_at_fixed`: At a nontrivial fixed point the denominator factors: D = (1 + t)²(1 − t).
- `DiamondLattice.disc_square`: **The bend's discriminant is a square**: p² − 4q = Q² on the "or" line.
- `DiamondLattice.dmap_even`: Series squares, so the step is even.
- `DiamondLattice.dmap_fixed_param`: **Every nontrivial fixed point is t = 1/y with Q = (y−1)²(y+1).**
- `DiamondLattice.dmap_formula`
- `DiamondLattice.fixed_factor`: **T(t) − t factors**: the nontrivial fixed points solve (Q−1)t³ + t² + t − 1 = 0.
- `DiamondLattice.fold_ferro_fixed`: At Q = 32/27 the ferromagnetic point is t* = 3/5 (y = 5/3), slope 25/16.
- `DiamondLattice.fold_ferro_slope`
- `DiamondLattice.fold_identity`: **The fold**: Q(y) = (y−1)²(y+1) touches 32/27 at y = −1/3.
- `DiamondLattice.fold_neutral`: At the fold the slope factor 2y/(y + 1) is −1: neutral.
- `DiamondLattice.fold_point`: The fold point in t and in x = e^K: t = 1/y = −3 and x = y² = 1/9.
- `DiamondLattice.fold_value`
- `DiamondLattice.fortyfive_fixed`
- `DiamondLattice.fortyfive_slope`
- `DiamondLattice.golden_slope`: Percolation (Q = 1): at the golden point t² = 1 − t the slope is 4 − 4t.
- `DiamondLattice.ising_slope`: Ising (Q = 2, tribonacci point t³ + t² + t = 1): slope 4/(1 + t)².
- `DiamondLattice.lift_dmap`: So one diamond step squares in t, then squares in the lift.
- `DiamondLattice.lift_mul`: **The lift turns parallel into multiplication.**
- `DiamondLattice.lift_weight`: The lift of a bond's transmissivity is 1/(1 + v) = e^{−K}.
- `DiamondLattice.mirror_only_three`: The colouring wall mirrors the critical point (1/(1−Q) = −1/y) only for y = 2, i.e. Q = 3.
- `DiamondLattice.parallel_weights`: The circle-operation tie (called mult-add tie before 1.0): parallel joins bond weights by the circle operation, so the lift 1 + v multiplies.
- `DiamondLattice.param_cubic`: Writing Q = (y−1)²(y+1) solves the cubic in y.
- `DiamondLattice.param_fixed`: ... and t = 1/y solves the fixed-point cubic.
- `DiamondLattice.q_one_poly`: Q = 1: the second wall moves to infinity, the series wall, and T is a polynomial.
- `DiamondLattice.q_three_mirror`: Q = 3: the colouring wall −1/2 is the mirror of the critical point 1/2, so it lands on it.
- `DiamondLattice.q_two_inversion`: Q = 2: walls 0, ∞, 1, −1 are harmonic and T(1/t) = T(t).
- `DiamondLattice.q_zero_double`: ... whose fixed point t = 1 is double: T(t) − t = −t(t − 1)²/(1 + t²) ...
- `DiamondLattice.q_zero_map`: Q = 0, where the two walls merge (p² = 4q): T = 2t²/(1 + t²) ...
- `DiamondLattice.q_zero_parabolic`: ... and parabolic: the derivative 4t/(1 + t²)² of 2t²/(1 + t²) is 1 at t = 1.
- `DiamondLattice.quot_helper`
- `DiamondLattice.quotient_rule`: The quotient rule: N'D − ND' = 4t(1 − t²)(1 + (Q−1)t²).
- `DiamondLattice.sixteen_fixed`
- `DiamondLattice.sixteen_slope`
- `DiamondLattice.slope_at_fixed`: **The slope at every nontrivial fixed point is 4/(1 + t)².**
- `DiamondLattice.slope_helper`
- `DiamondLattice.slope_in_y`: In y: 4/(1 + 1/y)² = (2y/(y + 1))².
- `DiamondLattice.slope_num_at_fixed`
- `DiamondLattice.three_fixed`
- `DiamondLattice.three_slope`
- `DiamondLattice.wall_colour`: The second wall: t = 1/(1 − Q) absorbs under the parallel law.
- `DiamondLattice.wall_gap`: The two walls 1 and 1/(1−Q) differ by −Q/(1−Q): they merge only at Q = 0.
- `DiamondLattice.wall_one`: The perfect bond t = 1 absorbs under the parallel law.

## FusedAlgebra.Topics.DiffGeometry (31)

- `DiffGeometry.central_chart`: Central chart, cleared of denominators: if s₁ = r₁c₁ and s₂ = r₂c₂ then the turned point has x = c₁c₂(r₁ + r₂) and t = c₁c₂(1 − K r₁ r₂), so x/t = F(0, K)(r₁, r₂).
- `DiffGeometry.central_chart_unit`: The same with the chart point written as (1, r): (1, r₁)(1, r₂) = (1 − K r₁r₂, r₁ + r₂).
- `DiffGeometry.circ_curv`: The circle operation: conformal curvature −1 (the half plane), projective curvature −1/4.
- `DiffGeometry.cr_base`: With 0 as base point: CR(0, x; P, Q) = P(x − Q)/(Q(x − P)).
- `DiffGeometry.cr_den`: ... and Q(x − P) = −PQ(1 − ux), so CR(0, x; 1/u, 1/v) = (1 − vx)/(1 − ux), the lens.
- `DiffGeometry.cr_num`: With walls P = 1/u and Q = 1/v the two halves are the lens factors: P(x − Q) = −PQ(1 − vx) ...
- `DiffGeometry.curv_chart_free`: Chart changes x ↦ x/(1 − cx) move (p, q) to (p + 2c, q + pc + c²) and keep 4q − p².
- `DiffGeometry.curv_scale`: Scaling the line by λ scales the curvature by λ² (so the radius by 1/λ).
- `DiffGeometry.dbl_den`: ... and the denominators: (1 − qa²)(1 − qb²) − q(2a)(2b) = (1 − qab)² − q(a + b)².
- `DiffGeometry.dbl_is_self`: Doubling is the law applied to (a, a).
- `DiffGeometry.dbl_num`: The doubled numerators combine to the doubled sum: 2a(1 − qb²) + 2b(1 − qa²) = 2(a + b)(1 − qab).
- `DiffGeometry.dbl_of_sum`: Together, dbl_num and dbl_den say doubling respects the law: dbl(F(a,b)) has numerator 2(a + b)(1 − qab) and denominator (1 − qab)² − q(a + b)², the same as F(dbl a, dbl b) after clearing (1 − qa²)(1 − qb²). Here is the doubled sum itself, cleared by (1 − qab)².
- `DiffGeometry.dial_curv`: The dial line: curvature −k², flat only at k = 0.
- `DiffGeometry.dial_flat_iff`
- `DiffGeometry.flat_adds`: Flat case K = 0: the line t = 1 is carried to itself and chart values add (Galilean shear).
- `DiffGeometry.halfplane_horizon`: The wall x = −1 is the horizon y = 0, and dilations never move it.
- `DiffGeometry.halfplane_isometry`: Dilations keep the half-plane metric (dX² + dY²)/Y² (cleared: λ²(dx² + dy²)·y² = (dx² + dy²)·(λy)²).
- `DiffGeometry.hyperbola_closed`: Hyperbola K = −1: t² − x² = 1 is closed under boosts.
- `DiffGeometry.lens_factor`: The lens multiplies under the law (numerator form): with p = −(u+v), q = uv, 1 − qab − v(a + b + pab) = (1 − va)(1 − vb).
- `DiffGeometry.mobius_collinear`: Möbius (Poincaré) addition of collinear a, b: ((1 + 2ab + b²)a + (1 − a²)b)/(1 + 2ab + a²b²) equals (a + b)/(1 + ab), cleared of denominators.
- `DiffGeometry.mobius_den`: Its denominator is the square (1 + ab)².
- `DiffGeometry.parity_curv`: Parity (−2, 0) has the same curvature as velocity addition.
- `DiffGeometry.poincare_klein`: Poincaré to Klein (q = −1): r_K = 2r_P/(1 + r_P²) is the doubling of the velocity law.
- `DiffGeometry.rmul_assoc`
- `DiffGeometry.rmul_comm`
- `DiffGeometry.rmul_form`: Turns keep the conic: form(uv) = form(u)·form(v).
- `DiffGeometry.rmul_one`
- `DiffGeometry.sphere_closed`: Sphere K = 1: the unit circle is closed under turns (complex multiplication).
- `DiffGeometry.stereo_gnomonic`: Stereographic to gnomonic (q = 1): tan θ = 2 tan(θ/2)/(1 − tan²(θ/2)).
- `DiffGeometry.tan_vel_curv`: Tangent addition (0, 1) is a sphere, velocity addition (0, −1) a hyperbolic plane.
- `DiffGeometry.walls_curv`: With walls 1/u and 1/v (so p = −(u+v), q = uv), 4q − p² = −(u − v)²: real walls mean negative curvature.

## FusedAlgebra.Topics.Dynamics (30)

- `Dynamics.cheb_comp`: Chebyshev maps commute like powers: T₂ ∘ T₃ = T₃ ∘ T₂ = T₆.
- `Dynamics.cheb_joukowski`: If 2u = z + z⁻¹ then 2·Tₙ(u) = zⁿ + z⁻ⁿ.
- `Dynamics.dial_power_units`: Dial powers too: k·x^(◯ₖn) = (kx)^(◯n).
- `Dynamics.dial_square_units`: Every dial square is circle-operation squaring in other units: k·(x ◯ₖ x) = (kx) ◯ (kx).
- `Dynamics.dial_wall_superattracting`: The dial's wall −1/k is the superattracting fixed point of x ◯ₖ x.
- `Dynamics.failure_square`: k = −1 (failure union): the dial square is 1 − (1 − x)².
- `Dynamics.flow_time_law`: Flows: running y′ = 1 + y for time s then t is combining the two lifts' parts by the circle operation: (a ◯ (e₁ − 1)) ◯ (e₂ − 1) = a ◯ (e₁e₂ − 1), with eᵢ = e^(tᵢ).
- `Dynamics.fold_eq`
- `Dynamics.fold_inv`: The fold is 2-to-1: w and ι(w) land on the same point.
- `Dynamics.identity_repelling`: The identity repels: h ◯ h = 2h + h² (multiplier 2).
- `Dynamics.inv_inv`: The circle inverse undoes itself.
- `Dynamics.iter_jet_fst`
- `Dynamics.iter_jet_ruler`: In the log ruler L = log(1+x) every step doubles slopes: the slope of fⁿ divided by 1 + fⁿ(x) is 2ⁿ times the starting slope divided by 1 + x (Lyapunov exponent log 2).
- `Dynamics.iter_msq_lift`: n steps: 1 + fⁿ(x) = (1 + x)^(2ⁿ).
- `Dynamics.iter_wall`: The wall stays put under every iterate.
- `Dynamics.iter_zero`: So does the identity.
- `Dynamics.joukowski_circ`: Joukowski in circle-operation terms: z + 1/z = 2 + (w + ι(w)) for z = 1 + w.
- `Dynamics.logistic_T2`: The logistic map at r = 4, read in u = 1 − 2x, is T₂.
- `Dynamics.logistic_fold`: The logistic map at r = 4 is circle-operation squaring folded: 4X(1 − X) = X(w ◯ w).
- `Dynamics.mpow_mpow`: Circle powers compose like ordinary powers: (x^(◯m))^(◯n) = x^(◯mn).
- `Dynamics.msq_inv`: Squaring commutes with the inverse: ι(w) ◯ ι(w) = ι(w ◯ w).
- `Dynamics.msq_lift`: The lift conjugates circle-operation squaring to z ↦ z².
- `Dynamics.parity_logistic`: k = −2 (parity): the dial square is the logistic map at r = 2.
- `Dynamics.periodic_iff`: Periodic points: fⁿ(x) = x exactly when (1 + x)^(2ⁿ) = 1 + x.
- `Dynamics.pflow_add`
- `Dynamics.pflow_ruler`: Its Abel ruler −2/x adds the time: −2/φₜ(x) = −2/x + t (Fatou's coordinate, exactly).
- `Dynamics.pow_mul_R`: (a^m)^n = a^(mn) for any commutative ring.
- `Dynamics.sum_is_neg_product`: Since w ◯ ι(w) = 0, the sum and product of w and its inverse are negatives.
- `Dynamics.wall_fixed_mpow`: Higher circle powers: the wall is fixed by x ↦ x^(◯(n+1)).
- `Dynamics.wall_superattracting`: The wall is superattracting: (−1 + h) ◯ (−1 + h) = −1 + h² (multiplier 0).

## FusedAlgebra.Topics.E (37)

- `CircleOp.E.bernoulli`: **Bernoulli, with room to spare.** (c+1)^(k+2) + (k+1)(c+1)^k ≤ c^(k+2) + (k+2)(c+1)^(k+1).
- `CircleOp.E.bernoulli_strict`
- `CircleOp.E.binomial`: **The cube's corner count.** (1 + x)ⁿ = Σₖ C(n,k)·xᵏ.
- `CircleOp.E.collisions`: **Collisions are few.** n·nᵏ ≤ n·fall n k + C(k,2)·nᵏ: the ordered picks that repeat an axis number at most C(k,2)·nᵏ⁻¹.
- `CircleOp.E.fact_cast_pos`
- `CircleOp.E.fall_le_pow`
- `CircleOp.E.inv_pow_mul`
- `CircleOp.E.layer_fact_le`: **The layer bound.** C(n,k)·k! ≤ nᵏ: distinct picks are at most all picks.
- `CircleOp.E.layer_mul_fact`: **Picking with order.** C(n,k)·k! = n(n-1)…(n-k+1).
- `CircleOp.E.le_div_of_mul_le`
- `CircleOp.E.natcast_ne`
- `CircleOp.E.one_div_le_one_div`
- `CircleOp.E.one_div_nonneg`
- `CircleOp.E.pow2_le_fact`
- `CircleOp.E.rsum_add`
- `CircleOp.E.rsum_le`
- `CircleOp.E.rsum_mono`: More nonnegative terms, bigger sum.
- `CircleOp.E.rsum_mul`
- `CircleOp.E.rsum_nonneg`
- `CircleOp.E.rsum_shift`
- `CircleOp.E.s_bound`
- `CircleOp.E.s_le_stair`: **The floor.** For n ≥ N, the staircase is within 3·C(N,2)/n of s N.
- `CircleOp.E.s_le_u`
- `CircleOp.E.s_lt_three`: **The partial sums never reach 3.**
- `CircleOp.E.s_mono`
- `CircleOp.E.stair_eq_cube`
- `CircleOp.E.stair_eq_mpow`
- `CircleOp.E.stair_le_s`
- `CircleOp.E.stair_le_u`: **The ceiling.** Every step of the staircase sits under every u N.
- `CircleOp.E.stair_lt_succ`: **The staircase climbs.** (1 + 1/n)ⁿ < (1 + 1/(n+1))ⁿ⁺¹ for n ≥ 1.
- `CircleOp.E.stair_nat_lt`: **The staircase climbs** (cleared of fractions): (n+1)^(2n+1) < nⁿ·(n+2)^(n+1) for n ≥ 1, i.e. (1+1/n)ⁿ < (1+1/(n+1))ⁿ⁺¹.
- `CircleOp.E.tri_mono`
- `CircleOp.E.u_anti`
- `CircleOp.E.u_step`: **The upper partner only comes down.** u (N+1) ≤ u N for N ≥ 1.
- `CircleOp.E.wt_ge`: **Each layer is at least 1/k! minus its collisions.** 1/k! ≤ C(n,k)/nᵏ + C(k,2)/(n·k!).
- `CircleOp.E.wt_le`: **Each layer is at most 1/k!.** C(n,k)/nᵏ ≤ 1/k!.
- `CircleOp.E.wt_nonneg`

## FusedAlgebra.Topics.EIPi (17)

- `EIPi.G_lift_hom`: The lift turns The gaussian circle operation into Gaussian multiplication.
- `EIPi.circK_assoc`: Chaining is associative, so "n steps" has one meaning.
- `EIPi.circK_zero`
- `EIPi.complex_counts`: Complex counts K(n) = n² always satisfy it: (a²)(b²) − 1 = (a² − 1) ∘₁ (b² − 1).
- `EIPi.counts_circ`: Hardy's K_AB = K_A·K_B, read as the circle operation of the counts minus one.
- `EIPi.dial_is_direction`
- `EIPi.four_quarter_turns`: Four quarter turns come back to the identity 0 (lift 1).
- `EIPi.full_turn_lifts_to_one`
- `EIPi.half_turn_lifts_to_minus_one`: ... whose lift is −1: the discrete e^{iπ} = −1.
- `EIPi.i_squared`
- `EIPi.lift_three`
- `EIPi.lift_two`: The lift 1 + k·x turns the dial into ordinary multiplication.
- `EIPi.q_lifts_to_i`
- `EIPi.two_quarter_turns`: Two quarter turns: q ∘ q = −2, the mirror point −2/k at k = 1 ...
- `EIPi.two_quaterbits`: Two quaternionic bits: 28 parameters, but local products give 6·6 = 36 cells.
- `EIPi.two_qubits`: Two qubits: 16 = 4·4, and 15 = 3 + 3 + 9 (the Bloch / correlation split).
- `EIPi.two_rebits`: Two real bits: 10 parameters, but local data gives only 3·3 = 9 (one hidden: σy⊗σy).

## FusedAlgebra.Topics.EarlyScience (17)

- `SimpleLaws.boyle_gain_loss`: pressure gains combine at k = +1 while the matching volume losses combine at k = −1: if (1 + a)(1 − d₁) = 1 and (1 + b)(1 − d₂) = 1 then the totals match the same way
- `SimpleLaws.boyle_partner`: on a field the partner change is the circle inverse −a/(1 + a)
- `SimpleLaws.constant_product`: if one factor of a constant product grows by a, the other must change by b with a ◯ b = 0: the lever (weight × arm) and Boyle (pressure × volume) are the same picture
- `SimpleLaws.cooling`: each step closes a fraction a of the temperature gap; after n steps the part closed is 1 − (1 − a)ⁿ, the union-of-failures power
- `SimpleLaws.field_lens`: the wall: a first lens that focuses exactly on the second (d·P₁ = 1) makes the second lens change nothing: the field lens of telescope eyepieces
- `SimpleLaws.inverse_square`: gravity: when the distance grows by ρ the force changes by the circle inverse of ρ ◯ ρ
- `SimpleLaws.kepler_third`: T² ∝ a³ on percent changes: (1 + t)² = (1 + α)³ is t ◯ t = α ◯ α ◯ α (the square's gnomon equals the cube's layers)
- `SimpleLaws.ladder_fib`: n sections ending in a short circuit give F(2n)/F(2n − 1): 1, 3/2, 8/5, 21/13, ...
- `SimpleLaws.ladder_hyperbola`: every ladder value sits on the hyperbola n² − nd − d² = −1, which hugs the golden wall: so Z² − Z − 1 = −1/d² and the ladder walks into the wall
- `SimpleLaws.ladder_walls`: the walls of one section are its fixed points: Z = (1 + 2Z)/(1 + Z) ⇔ Z² = Z + 1, the golden ratio and −1/golden ratio (two real walls)
- `SimpleLaws.lenses_apart`: two lenses a distance d apart: total power P₁ + P₂ − d·P₁P₂, the circle operation at k = −d
- `SimpleLaws.lenses_in_contact`: two lenses in contact: powers add (the point (0, 0))
- `SimpleLaws.nonempty_subsets`: non-empty choices: a set of m things has 2^m − 1 non-empty subsets; joining two separate sets combines those counts by the circle operation (pick from either, or both, but not nothing)
- `SimpleLaws.parallel_is_inverted_addition`: parallel resistors (or springs in series, capacitors in series) are addition read through the inversion lens x ↦ 1/x: (RS/(R + S))·(1/R + 1/S) = 1
- `SimpleLaws.product_error`: relative errors in a product combine by the circle operation, exactly (the textbook rule "they add" is the k = 0 shadow that drops the corner e₁e₂)
- `SimpleLaws.product_error_corner`: the corner that the first-order rule drops
- `SimpleLaws.squeeze_multiplies`: each lens's squeeze factor 1 − d·P (how much a parallel beam narrows over the gap) multiplies

## FusedAlgebra.Topics.Electromagnetism (41)

- `Electromagnetism.EB.ext'`
- `Electromagnetism.S_nsq_mul`
- `Electromagnetism.bi_cap`: With E² = b²D²/(b² + D²), the field stays below the wall b² (stated with the positive denominator cleared): b²·D² < b²·(b² + D²).
- `Electromagnetism.bi_den_pos`: The denominator b² + D² is positive.
- `Electromagnetism.bi_inverse`: And the exact inverse: with e = E²/b² = D²/(b² + D²), D²/b² = e/(1 − e).
- `Electromagnetism.boost_closed`: Boosts (a ◯ ā = 0 in the split plane) are closed under the circle operation too.
- `Electromagnetism.charge_conserved`: **k_μ k_ν F^{μν} = 0 for any antisymmetric F**: in momentum space, k·J = 0 follows from J = k·F alone.
- `Electromagnetism.dop_compose`: So the field's light-cone ratio, under two boosts, rides the circle operation of the two d's.
- `Electromagnetism.dop_vD`: D² is the lift of the speed: D² = (1 + v)/(1 − v), and dop v = D² − 1.
- `Electromagnetism.doppler_circ`: **D² − 1 composes by the circle operation**: (D₁D₂)² − 1 = (D₁² − 1) ◯ (D₂² − 1).
- `Electromagnetism.galilean_cross`: Galilean boosts (q = 0) leave a cross term −2g²v: the wave equation is not Galilean.
- `Electromagnetism.gam_add_gv`
- `Electromagnetism.gam_sq`: γ² − (γv)² = 1.
- `Electromagnetism.gam_sub_gv`
- `Electromagnetism.half_charts`: Half-angle charts: (1 + i t₁)(1 + i t₂) = (1 − t₁t₂) + i(t₁ + t₂) and (1 + j s₁)(1 + j s₂) = (1 + s₁s₂) + j(s₁ + s₂): the landscape law at q = +1 and q = −1.
- `Electromagnetism.inv1_boost`: **E² − B² is the same in every frame.**
- `Electromagnetism.inv2_boost`: **E·B is the same in every frame.**
- `Electromagnetism.lc_minus`: **Light-cone part 2**: E_y − B_z is scaled by D.
- `Electromagnetism.lc_pair2`: The other pair: E_z − B_y scaled by 1/D, E_z + B_y by D.
- `Electromagnetism.lc_plus`: **Light-cone part 1**: E_y + B_z is scaled by 1/D.
- `Electromagnetism.lc_ratio`: The ratio of the two light-cone parts is multiplied by D², the circle-operation lift.
- `Electromagnetism.maC_conj`: **a ◯ ā = |1 + a|² − 1** (real): so a is a unit phase exactly when a ◯ ā = 0.
- `Electromagnetism.maC_lift`: The lift of a ◯ b is the product of lifts (complex multiplication).
- `Electromagnetism.maS_conj`: **The boost version of the same rule**: a ◯ ā = (|1 + a|²_Lorentz − 1).
- `Electromagnetism.maS_lift`
- `Electromagnetism.nsq_mul`: |xy|² = |x|²|y|² for complex pairs.
- `Electromagnetism.null_stays_null`: Light stays light: a null field (E² = B², E ⊥ B) is null in every frame.
- `Electromagnetism.phase_closed`: **Unit phases are closed under the circle operation**: if a ◯ ā = 0 and b ◯ b̄ = 0, the same holds for a ◯ b.
- `Electromagnetism.plaquette`: **A plaquette is the circle operation of its links**, the two backward links entering as circle inverses: 1 + (a₁ ◯ a₂ ◯ a₃⁻ ◯ a₄⁻) = (1 + a₁)(1 + a₂)/((1 + a₃)(1 + a₄)).
- `Electromagnetism.purcell`: **Magnetism from electricity**: a pure E_y, boosted, gets B_z = −γv E_y.
- `Electromagnetism.sq_nn`
- `Electromagnetism.sq_pos'`
- `Electromagnetism.stokes`: **Discrete Stokes**: with link lifts b₁, s, t₁, l (left square) and b₂, r, t₂, s (right square, sharing s), the two plaquettes multiply to the big rectangle and the shared link cancels.
- `Electromagnetism.stokes_circ`: In circle-operation form: the big loop's value is the ◯ of the two plaquette values.
- `Electromagnetism.vD_mul`: **Speeds of composed boosts add by Einstein's rule**: v(D₁D₂) = v(D₁) ⊕ v(D₂).
- `Electromagnetism.wall_not_phase`: The wall a = −1 is never a phase: |1 + (−1)|² = 0.
- `Electromagnetism.wave_boost`: **The wave's amplitude is Doppler-shifted**: after the boost it is the wave of amplitude a/D.
- `Electromagnetism.wave_cross_zero_iff`: Cross-term coefficient of ∂_X∂_T in ∂_t² − c²∂_x² after the dial boost (divided by −2g²).
- `Electromagnetism.wave_energy`: Its energy density (E² + B²)/2 = a² is divided by D², the inverse circle-operation lift.
- `Electromagnetism.wave_null`
- `Electromagnetism.wave_speed_kept`: At c²q = −1 the T-T coefficient is −1/c² times the X-X coefficient: same wave speed.

## FusedAlgebra.Topics.Elliptic (22)

- `EllipticLead.circ_curve_d`: the circle operation's own curve (a1 = −1, a2 = a3 = a4 = 0) has d_n = (−1)^n: the differential dz/(1+z), the log of the circle operation, through layer 6
- `EllipticLead.d4_weight`
- `EllipticLead.d5_weight`
- `EllipticLead.euler_factor_walls`: Euler factors are wall polynomials: 1 − a T + p T² = (1 − αT)(1 − βT) when a = α + β, p = αβ
- `EllipticLead.euler_units`: units: scaling T by λ moves (k, q) to (λk, λ²q), the landscape's units action
- `EllipticLead.frey_c4`: c4 = b2² − 24 b4 with b2 = 4(B − A), b4 = −2AB
- `EllipticLead.frey_c4_at_A`: semistable: at a prime dividing A (A ≡ 0) c4/16 is B²; dividing A + B (B ≡ −A) it is A². Either is nonzero mod the prime when gcd(A, B) = 1, so the reduction is a node, never a cusp.
- `EllipticLead.frey_c4_at_C`
- `EllipticLead.frey_discriminant`: Frey curve y² = x(x − A)(x + B) = x³ + (B − A)x² − AB·x: 16 × discriminant of the cubic
- `EllipticLead.gap6_whole`: no other whole rung passes layer 6
- `EllipticLead.gap6_zero_at_rungs`
- `EllipticLead.hasse_turning`: Hasse (a² < 4p) puts the point (k, q) = (−a, p) in the turning country: k² − 4q < 0
- `EllipticLead.nodal_w`: the nodal curve's formal group: w = −1/y as a function of z is rational, w·(1 − a1 z − a2 z²) = z³ is the curve equation w = z³ + a1 z w + a2 z² w
- `EllipticLead.node_param`: every line y = t·x through the double point meets the curve again at x = t² + a1 t − a2
- `EllipticLead.ochanine_nodal_den`
- `EllipticLead.ochanine_nodal_num`: Ochanine's elliptic genus at ε = δ² (the nodal case): numerator a√R(b) + b√R(a) with √R(x) = 1 − δx² equals (a + b)(1 − δab), so the law is (a + b)/(1 + δab): the signature at δ = 1
- `EllipticLead.slope_discriminant`: discriminant of the slopes equals the map's k² − 4q
- `EllipticLead.slopes_factor`: the two tangent slopes m1, m2 factor the slope polynomial: a1 = −(m1+m2), a2 = −m1·m2, so k = m1 + m2 and q = m1·m2
- `EllipticLead.tate_q0_is_circ`: and a = −z turns it into the circle operation exactly
- `EllipticLead.tate_q0_law`: with z = 1 − v (v = 1/u, Tate's parameter inverted), the law z1 + z2 − z1 z2 is v1·v2
- `EllipticLead.wall_poly_factor`: the wall polynomial in z factors through the branches: 1 + k z + q z² = (1 + m1 z)(1 + m2 z)
- `EllipticLead.walls_are_branches`: the walls are the node's branches: with z = −1/t (so z·t = −1), t²·(1 + k z + q z²) − (t² + a1 t − a2) is a multiple of (z t + 1), with k = −a1, q = −a2

## FusedAlgebra.Topics.EllipticCounting (19)

- `EllipticCounting.golden_fifth_power`: and 5φ + 3 really is φ⁵
- `EllipticCounting.lucas_catalan`: Catalan's identity in the form used below: U(n+d)² − U(d)·U(2n+d) = Q^d · U(n)²
- `EllipticCounting.lucas_eds`: **Lucas counting is elliptic counting, weighted by Q^(n−1).** Writing n = f + 1 and m = f + e + 2 (so m > n ≥ 1): Q^(n−1)·U(m+n)·U(m−n) = U(m+1)U(m−1)U(n)² − U(n+1)U(n−1)U(m)². This is the elliptic divisibility recurrence (with U(1) = 1), with the extra factor Q^(n−1).
- `EllipticCounting.lucas_eds_unit`: with Q = 1 the Lucas sequence satisfies the elliptic divisibility recurrence exactly
- `EllipticCounting.lucas_exchange`
- `EllipticCounting.lucas_exchange_pair`: the general exchange identity: U(a+c)U(a+e) − U(a)U(a+e+c) = Q^a · U(c) · U(e)
- `EllipticCounting.lucas_shift_one`: one-step shift: U(a+1)U(a+e) − U(a)U(a+e+1) = Q^a · U(e)
- `EllipticCounting.lucas_somos4`: the Somos-4 edge (n = 2): Q·U(m+4)U(m) = P²·U(m+3)U(m+1) − (P² − Q)·U(m+2)²
- `EllipticCounting.order_five_lines`: why: the tangent at P is y = 0, cutting x³ − x² = x²(x − 1), so −2P = (1, 0); the chord through P and 2P is y = −x, cutting x(x − 1)², so P + 2P = −(1, −1) = (1, 0)
- `EllipticCounting.order_five_point`: y² + y = x³ − x², a = (0, −1, 1, 0, 0), at (0, 0): W2 = 1, W3 = −1, W4 = −1, W5 = W4·W2³ − W3³ = 0
- `EllipticCounting.order_five_points`: its multiples, all on the curve: P = (0,0), 2P = (1,−1), 3P = (1,0), 4P = (0,−1), and 3P = −2P, 4P = −P (negation is y ↦ −y − 1), so 5P = O
- `EllipticCounting.tate5_disc`: Tate normal form with a point of order 5: y² + (1−b)xy − by = x³ − bx². Its discriminant is b⁵(b² − 11b − 1), so it pinches only at b = 0 and b² = 11b + 1.
- `EllipticCounting.tate5_golden_pinch`: the pinch points b² − 11b − 1 = 0 are φ⁵ = 5φ + 3 and −φ⁻⁵ = 8 − 5φ, for any φ with φ² = φ + 1
- `EllipticCounting.w37_eds`: the elliptic divisibility recurrence, for 1 ≤ n < m, m + n ≤ 24
- `EllipticCounting.w37_odd_somos`: its odd terms are the classic Somos-4 numbers 1, 1, 2, 3, 7, 23, 59, 314, 1529, 8209 (up to sign)
- `EllipticCounting.w37_point`: (0, 0) is on y² + y = x³ − x
- `EllipticCounting.w37_seeds`: the seeds are the curve's division polynomials at (0, 0): a = (0, 0, 1, −1, 0)
- `EllipticCounting.w37_somos4`: Somos-4: W(n+4)W(n) = W(n+3)W(n+1) + W(n+2)²
- `EllipticCounting.w37_strong_div`: strong divisibility, like Fibonacci: gcd(W m, W n) = |W gcd(m, n)|

## FusedAlgebra.Topics.EllipticModulus (11)

- `EllipticModulus.conic_is_square`: Our k = 0 conic laws are the perfect squares: (1 + d t²)² = 1 + 2d t² + d² t⁴.
- `EllipticModulus.fingerprint_general`: Fingerprint of an odd chart with log' = (1 + 2κ t² + ε t⁴)^(−1/2): the generator is u + (κ/3) u³ + ((3ε + κ²)/30) u⁵ + …, and 90·(3/2·c₃² − c₅) = 12κ² − 9ε while 90·c₃² = 10κ², so R = 3/2 − c₅/c₃² = 6/5 − 9ε/(10κ²).
- `EllipticModulus.height_scales_fingerprint`: Heights scale the fingerprint by 1/s: the height-s map has c₃ = s·a and c₅ = s·b + (3/2)s(s − 1)a², and 3(sa)² − 2c₅ = s(3a² − 2b).
- `EllipticModulus.normal_form_inverse`: Normal form: if 10κ²·R = 12κ² − 9, then κ²(12 − 10R) = 9, so every fingerprint but 6/5 has an elliptic chart.
- `EllipticModulus.pendulum_kappa`: Pendulum: with c² + s² = 1 (c = cos(θ₀/2), s = sin(θ₀/2)), κ = 1 − 2m = 1 − 2s² = c² − s² = cos θ₀.
- `EllipticModulus.slope_log_quartic`: The slope chart's law: G'² = 1 + 2κ t² + t⁴. With x = sn², t² = x(1 − m x)/(1 − x) and G' = (1 − 2m x + m x²)/(1 − x), this is the identity below after clearing (1 − x)².
- `EllipticModulus.slope_monotone_sos`: The wall in the slope chart is the only one: G' numerator 1 − 2m x + m x² = (1 − m x)² + m(1 − m)x², a sum of squares (positive) for 0 ≤ m < 1 and 0 ≤ x ≤ 1.
- `EllipticModulus.slope_numerator`: In the slope chart ε = 1 and κ = 1 − 2m; the numerator 12κ² − 9 is 3(16m² − 16m + 1) ...
- `EllipticModulus.slope_numerator_wick`: ... and it is Wick-symmetric, unchanged by m ↦ 1 − m.
- `EllipticModulus.theta_fold_three`: Theta as the lift of a fold: (1 + a)(1 + b)(1 + c) = 1 + ((a ◯ b) ◯ c), three steps of the Tate orbit.
- `EllipticModulus.wick_flips_kappa`: Wick rotation m ↦ 1 − m flips the curvature reading κ = 1 − 2m.

## FusedAlgebra.Topics.EllipticPendulum (7)

- `CircleOp.EllipticPendulum.cap_is_root`: The cap is a root of the law's quartic 1 + 2κt² + t⁴ with κ = 1 − 2m = 1 − 2r²: where G′ = 0.
- `CircleOp.EllipticPendulum.cap_partner`: Over the top (m > 1), write r = √m, q = √(m − 1), so r² − q² = 1. The cap t = r − q and its partner r + q multiply to 1.
- `CircleOp.EllipticPendulum.complementary_time`: Complementary time: with sn(K − u) = c/d, cn(K − u) = k′s/d, dn(K − u) = k′/d, the product G(u)·G(K − u) = (s d / c)·((c/d)(k′/d)/(k′ s/d)) = 1. Cleared: (s d)(c k′) = (c)(k′ s d).
- `CircleOp.EllipticPendulum.halfway_condition`: The halfway point. The reading t = sin θ/θ′ equals 1 where sin²θ = 2(cos θ − cos θ₀), that is, (with sin² + cos² = 1) where cos²θ + 2 cos θ − 1 − 2 cos θ₀ = 0.
- `CircleOp.EllipticPendulum.halfway_root`: ... and its root is cos θ* = 2 cos(θ₀/2) − 1 (write h = cos(θ₀/2), so cos θ₀ = 2h² − 1).
- `CircleOp.EllipticPendulum.kappa_is_energy`: The curvature reading is the energy: with m = E/E_top, E_top · κ = E_top − 2E.
- `CircleOp.EllipticPendulum.quartic_palindromic`: The quartic is palindromic: t⁴ P(1/t) = P(t), written with cleared denominators as P(a, b) = P(b, a) for P(a, b) = b⁴ + 2κ a²b² + a⁴ (t = a/b). So walls and caps come in pairs t, 1/t.

## FusedAlgebra.Topics.EllipticS3 (17)

- `EllipticS3.cayley_hamilton`: Cayley–Hamilton for G: G² = kG − qI, entry by entry
- `EllipticS3.circ_is_1x1`: the circle operation (called mult-add before 1.0) is the 1×1 case G = [1]: (1 + a)(1 + b) = 1 + (a ◯ b)
- `EllipticS3.circle_moments`
- `EllipticS3.lefschetz_count`
- `EllipticS3.lift_11`: top-left entry: (1 + ka)(1 + kb) − q a b = D + k N
- `EllipticS3.lift_12`: top-right entry: (1 + ka) b + a = N
- `EllipticS3.lift_21`: bottom-left entry: −q a (1 + kb) − q b = −q N
- `EllipticS3.lift_22`: bottom-right entry: −q a b + 1 = D
- `EllipticS3.middle_minus_neighbour`: middle layer minus its neighbour: 20 − 15 = 5 and 70 − 56 = 14
- `EllipticS3.nonsplit_frob_is_inverse`: turning (non-split) torus: u^(p+1) = 1, so v = u^p satisfies v·u = 1 and the circle operation of v − 1 and u − 1 is 0: Frobenius sends the point to its circle inverse (a_p = −1)
- `EllipticS3.quat_det_im`
- `EllipticS3.quat_det_re`: det U: real part w² + x² + y² + z², imaginary part 0
- `EllipticS3.quat_discriminant`: trace U = 2w; its wall discriminant is −4 times the squared radius of its latitude 2-sphere: how deep a prime sits in the turning country is how big the sphere of rotations with its angle is
- `EllipticS3.sphere_moments`
- `EllipticS3.split_frob_fixes`: split torus: u^p = u, Frobenius fixes the lift (a_p = +1)
- `EllipticS3.sq_nonneg_rat`
- `EllipticS3.turning_no_wall`

## FusedAlgebra.Topics.EntropyKnots (12)

- `CircleOp.EntropyKnots.deligne_b3`: Positive 3-braids: Deligne's Möbius polynomial factors through the golden polynomial, so they grow like φ, the same rate as the period-3 window.
- `CircleOp.EntropyKnots.disjoint_commute`: Maps on disjoint coordinates commute: f on the first coordinate, g on the last.
- `CircleOp.EntropyKnots.graeffe_period3`: **Graeffe step.** The Cartier–Foata polynomial of three affine maps with two sharing a centre, taken at t², is the period-3 kneading polynomial times its mirror: that trace monoid's growth is the square of the period-3 window's (φ² versus φ).
- `CircleOp.EntropyKnots.indepPath_four`
- `CircleOp.EntropyKnots.indepPath_three`: Three letters in a row (two of them commute): independence polynomial 1 + 3x + x².
- `CircleOp.EntropyKnots.indepPath_two`
- `CircleOp.EntropyKnots.jw_chebyshev`: At u = 1 the recursion is Chebyshev U_{m+1}(d/2), the Jones–Wenzl trace Δ_{m+1}(d).
- `CircleOp.EntropyKnots.jw_four`: Path P₄: zeros at d = 1 and d² = 3 (Beraha B₆ = 3).
- `CircleOp.EntropyKnots.jw_three`: Path P₃: zeros at d² − d − 1 = 0, d = φ (Beraha B₅ = φ²), the growth of "three maps, two sharing a centre".
- `CircleOp.EntropyKnots.jw_two`: Path P₂: zero at d² = 2 (Beraha B₄ = 2).
- `CircleOp.EntropyKnots.markov3_det`: The period-3 pattern's Markov matrix [[0,1],[1,1]]: det(I − tA) = 1 − t − t².
- `CircleOp.EntropyKnots.path_jones_wenzl`: **Trace monoid = Jones–Wenzl.** With u = −x·d², the Jones–Wenzl recursion is dᵐ⁺¹ times the path's independence polynomial: so μ(t) = I_m(−t) vanishes at t = 1/d² exactly where H_m(d, 1) does.

## FusedAlgebra.Topics.EntropyNoise (41)

- `EntropyNoise.alarm_one_coin`: After an alarm, the leftover doubt is exactly one fresh coin of chance p.
- `EntropyNoise.alarm_polar`
- `EntropyNoise.ball_in_cube`: Ball inside the cube.
- `EntropyNoise.bit_collision`: One bit-flip channel's collision sum in the polarization λ = 1 − 2p.
- `EntropyNoise.cancellation_hides`: The net channel after n stacked flips is purer than its fault pattern: ((1 + x)/2)ⁿ ≤ (1 + xⁿ)/2 for x = λ² in [0, 1].
- `EntropyNoise.centre_point`
- `EntropyNoise.certainty_absorbs_iff`: Certainty swallows everything, a ◯[k] 1 = 1 for every a, exactly when k = −1. The statistics page's "or" laws are the line k + q_L = −1 of lens laws; the Tsallis laws have no denominator (q_L = 0), so they meet it only here.
- `EntropyNoise.chain_rule_pair`: The chain rule on one diagonal, for any L with L(xy) = L(x) + L(y) (log is one): a·L(a) + b·L(b) = s·L(s) + [a·L(a/s) + b·L(b/s)] with s = a + b. Entropy of the faults = entropy of the syndrome + entropy left on the diagonal.
- `EntropyNoise.collision_parallel`: Parallel (two qubits, independent faults): the 16 joint fault chances' collision sum is the product, so linear entropy composes by the union rule.
- `EntropyNoise.collision_parseval`: Parseval on the tetrahedron: Σp² = (1 + |λ|²)/4.
- `EntropyNoise.collision_series`: Series: the collision sum of a composed channel, through Parseval.
- `EntropyNoise.compose_stays_in_ball`: Composition multiplies coordinates (QuantumNoise.comp_lx …), and a coordinate of a real channel lies in [−1, 1]. So composing never leaves the ball …
- `EntropyNoise.compose_stays_in_cube`: … nor any cube max|λᵢ| ≤ c about the centre (classical capacity cannot rise).
- `EntropyNoise.compose_stays_in_octahedron`: … nor the octahedron …
- `EntropyNoise.corner_point`
- `EntropyNoise.decoder_failure`: The vote picks the lighter end of each diagonal; the failures add up to the top two layers of the cube (QuantumNoise.maj3).
- `EntropyNoise.depol_point`
- `EntropyNoise.diagonal_crossings`: Along the diagonal (λ, λ, λ) the octahedron's face is λ = 1/3 and the midsphere is λ² = 1/3, so the octahedron is strictly inside there.
- `EntropyNoise.div_pow'`
- `EntropyNoise.edge_midpoint`: The edge midpoint (X with chance ½) is (1, 0, 0): on the cube's face, the sphere and the octahedron at once, with collision sum exactly ½.
- `EntropyNoise.escort_escort`
- `EntropyNoise.escort_powers`: Raising every entry to a then to b is raising to a·b: escorts of escorts.
- `EntropyNoise.gap_two`: At n = 2 the purity gap is a perfect square.
- `EntropyNoise.kw_lens_polar`: The Kramers–Wannier lens M(t) = (1 − t)/(1 + t) sends λ = 1 − 2p to p/(1 − p).
- `EntropyNoise.linear_entropy_choi`: The Choi state's linear entropy 1 − Σp² is (3 − |λ|²)/4: zero at the corners, largest (3/4) at the centre, constant on spheres about the centre.
- `EntropyNoise.lsum_div`
- `EntropyNoise.map_sq_outer`
- `EntropyNoise.midsphere_iff`: Σp² ≤ ½ (collision entropy at least one bit) exactly inside the midsphere.
- `EntropyNoise.mul_bound`: |a·b| ≤ u whenever |a| ≤ u and |b| ≤ 1, written without `abs`.
- `EntropyNoise.mul_ne_zero'`
- `EntropyNoise.nishimori_tanh`: On the Nishimori line e^{−2K} = w = p/(1 − p). Then tanh K = (1 − w)/(1 + w) = 1 − 2p.
- `EntropyNoise.no_alarm_polar`
- `EntropyNoise.octahedron_in_ball`: Octahedron inside the ball: |x| + |y| + |z| ≤ 1 gives x² + y² + z² ≤ 1.
- `EntropyNoise.pattern_linear_entropy`: The n-fold fault pattern (a corner of the n-cube) has collision sum cⁿ, so its linear entropy climbs at weight −1: 1 − cⁿ = (1 − c)^{◯[−1] n}.
- `EntropyNoise.pow_le_one'`
- `EntropyNoise.pow_ne_zero'`: Escort of an escort: P_b(P_a(p)) = P_{ab}(p), when the normalizers are nonzero.
- `EntropyNoise.pow_nn`
- `EntropyNoise.sq_nn`
- `EntropyNoise.synd_antipodal`: Opposite corners give the same syndrome …
- `EntropyNoise.synd_diagonal`: … and corners with the same syndrome are equal or opposite: each syndrome is one body diagonal of the 3-cube.
- `EntropyNoise.syndromes_total`: No alarm: both ends of the main diagonal. Alarm: each of the three other diagonals.

## FusedAlgebra.Topics.Fermat.CounterexampleMap (28)

- `CounterexampleMap.DeepArrows.dim_eq_genus`
- `CounterexampleMap.DeepArrows.mazur`
- `CounterexampleMap.DeepArrows.ribet`
- `CounterexampleMap.DeepArrows.wiles`
- `CounterexampleMap.abc_lt_cube`: F3a. For a + b = c with a, b ≥ 1: abc < c³ (so rad of a Fermat triple is below c³).
- `CounterexampleMap.disc_two_part`: C1. If b = 2^v·m then ((a·b·c)²)^p = 256 · 2^(2pv − 8) · ((a·m·c)²)^p, once 2pv ≥ 8. With a, m, c odd this says the minimal discriminant (256·Δ = ((abc)²)^p) has exactly 2^(2pv − 8) as its 2-part.
- `CounterexampleMap.explicit_abc_kills`: F3b. An explicit abc bound of the shape c^p < (abc)² applied to (a^p, b^p, c^p) leaves only p < 6. (abc itself is not proved, so this is a conditional route, not a proof.)
- `CounterexampleMap.fake_passes_mod`: F1. No congruence can rule a counterexample out: for every modulus n ≠ 0 the fake triple (−1, 32n, −1) has a ≡ 3 mod 4, 32 ∣ b, gcd(a, b) = 1, abc ≠ 0, and a^p + b^p ≡ c^p mod n.
- `CounterexampleMap.flt_of_deep_arrows`: E2. With FermatRoute, the four arrows give Fermat's Last Theorem for every n ≥ 3.
- `CounterexampleMap.frey_bad_primes`: B1. With A odd and 32 ∣ B, a prime divides Δ exactly when it divides ABC. Because the curve is semistable (FreyPieces 3e), its conductor is the product of these primes, rad(ABC) = rad(abc).
- `CounterexampleMap.frey_c4_c6_disc`: A3. c4³ − c6² = 1728·Δ for the Frey model.
- `CounterexampleMap.frey_c6`: A1. 2·c6 of the Frey model is (A − B)(A + 2B)(2A + B).
- `CounterexampleMap.frey_j_denominator`: A4. No prime dividing Δ divides c4³, so j = c4³/Δ is in lowest terms with denominator Δ: every bad prime is a pole of j (potentially multiplicative reduction, Tate's curve).
- `CounterexampleMap.frey_j_identity`: A2. The polynomial identity behind j − 1728: 4c4³ − (2c6)² = 27(ABC)².
- `CounterexampleMap.g12_table`: D2. 12·genus for N = 1, …, 13: zero except at 11 (genus 1, the first weight-2 cusp form).
- `CounterexampleMap.g12_two`: D1. Level 2: μ = 3, ν2 = 1, ν3 = 0, ν∞ = 2, so 12g = 0.
- `CounterexampleMap.germain_mod_11`: F2b. Sophie Germain's auxiliary prime for p = 5: x⁵ + y⁵ = z⁵ forces 11 ∣ x, y or z. This pushes divisibility around (it is how case I is proved) but can never contradict: case II, where p divides abc, escapes every such prime.
- `CounterexampleMap.germain_residues`: F2a. Fifth powers mod 11 are only 0, 1, 10.
- `CounterexampleMap.irregular_37`: F5a. Kummer's gap: 37 divides the numerator 7709321041217 of B_32 (B_32 = −7709321041217/510), so 37 is irregular and Kummer's 1850 proof does not cover it.
- `CounterexampleMap.near_misses`: F6. Two famous near misses, each killed by a tiny modulus: 3 for the first (3 divides 3987 and 4365 but not 4472), 2 for the second (even + odd cannot be even).
- `CounterexampleMap.no_norm_two_three`: F4b. Lamé's gap (1847): in Z[√−5] nothing has norm x² + 5y² equal to 2 or 3, so 2 and 3 are irreducible there, yet 6 = 2·3 has the second factorisation (1 + √−5)(1 − √−5). Unique factorisation, which elementary descent needs, fails; for Fermat it fails in Z[ζ_p] from p = 23 on (class number of Q(ζ23) is 3, cited).
- `CounterexampleMap.odd_prime_coprime_256`: An odd prime does not divide 256 (so it can be cancelled from 256·Δ).
- `CounterexampleMap.six_two_ways`: F4d. The two factorisations of 6.
- `CounterexampleMap.sq_mod_five`: F4a. Squares mod 5 are 0, 1, 4.
- `CounterexampleMap.squeeze`: E1. The squeeze: the four arrows leave no room for a counterexample.
- `CounterexampleMap.two_exponent_not_multiple`: C2. For a prime p ≥ 5 and v ≥ 1, p never divides 2pv − 8: the exponent of 2 in the minimal discriminant is never a multiple of p, so the mod-p representation stays ramified at 2 and the level cannot drop below 2. (The odd primes all drop: FreyPieces 3g.)
- `CounterexampleMap.wieferich_1093_3511`: F5b. The Wieferich primes: 2^(p−1) ≡ 1 mod p² for p = 1093 and 3511. Wieferich (1909) showed case I needs this; it holds for these two, so the criterion alone cannot finish.
- `CounterexampleMap.znorm_mul`: F4c. The norm is multiplicative.

- `Fermat3.Eis.nsmul_re`

- `Fermat3.Eis.one_im`
- `Fermat3.Eis.one_re`
- `Fermat3.Eis.pow_succ'`
- `Fermat3.Eis.pow_zero'`
- `Fermat3.Eis.small_of_sq_le_one`
- `Fermat3.Eis.sq_nonneg'`
- `Fermat3.Eis.sq_pos'`
- `Fermat3.Eis.sub_im`
- `Fermat3.Eis.sub_re`
- `Fermat3.Eis.units_list`: The six units ±1, ±ω, ±ω².
- `Fermat3.Eis.zero_im`
- `Fermat3.Eis.zero_re`
- `Fermat3.Eis.zsmul_im`
- `Fermat3.Eis.zsmul_re`
- `Fermat3.case1`: **First case**: if 3 does not divide xyz, then x³ + y³ ≠ z³.
- `Fermat3.cop_mul_left`
- `Fermat3.cop_mul_right`
- `Fermat3.cop_of_cubes`: In a solution of x³ + y³ + z³ = 0, gcd(x, y) = 1 forces gcd(y, z) = 1.
- `Fermat3.cop_of_dvd_left`
- `Fermat3.cop_of_dvd_right`
- `Fermat3.cop_of_param`
- `Fermat3.cop_of_pow`
- `Fermat3.cop_symm`
- `Fermat3.cop_three`
- `Fermat3.cop_two_of_odd`
- `Fermat3.cube_mod2`
- `Fermat3.cube_mod3`: Fermat mod 3: x³ ≡ x.
- `Fermat3.cube_mod9`: A cube of a number prime to 3 is 1 or 8 mod 9.
- `Fermat3.cube_mod9_all`
- `Fermat3.cube_ne_two`: 2 and −2 are not cubes (cubes mod 9 are 0, 1, 8).
- `Fermat3.cube_ne_zero`
- `Fermat3.cube_of_mul3`: Three pairwise coprime factors of a cube are cubes.
- `Fermat3.cube_of_mul_cop`: If a coprime product a·b is a cube, then a is a cube.
- `Fermat3.cube_of_mul_cop'`
- `Fermat3.dvd_of_dvd_mul_cop`
- `Fermat3.eq_zero_of_cube`
- `Fermat3.exactly_one_three`: Second case: in a coprime solution with 3 ∣ xyz, exactly one of x, y, z is divisible by 3.
- `Fermat3.flt3`: **Fermat's Last Theorem for exponent 3** (integers): no nonzero x, y, z with x³ + y³ = z³.
- `Fermat3.flt3_circ`: In the circle-operation picture: for integers x, y, z ≠ 0, (y/x)³ is never the circle-operation cube of z/x − 1.
- `Fermat3.flt3_circ_iff`: Over ℚ with x ≠ 0: x³ + y³ = z³ iff (y/x)³ is the circle-operation cube of z/x − 1.
- `Fermat3.flt3_nat`: **Fermat's Last Theorem for exponent 3** (natural numbers): x³ + y³ ≠ z³ when x, y ≠ 0.
- `Fermat3.gcd_factors`: For coprime x, y the two factors of x³ + y³ share only 1 or 3.
- `Fermat3.key_cube`: Euler's lemma: if p² + 3q² is a cube, gcd(p, q) = 1 and p + q is odd, then p + q√−3 is a cube: p = a³ − 9ab², q = 3a²b − 3b³.
- `Fermat3.measure_lt`
- `Fermat3.mpow_three`: The circle-operation cube: mpow s 3 = s³ + 3(s + s²); the middle layers carry the 3.
- `Fermat3.mpow_three_mod3`: Circle-operation cubes and plain cubes agree mod 3: (1+s)³ − 1 ≡ s³ ≡ s.
- `Fermat3.no_sol`: No primitive solution exists (infinite descent on |xyz|).
- `Fermat3.norm_one`: The integer heart of the coprimality of p + q√−3 and p − q√−3.
- `Fermat3.odd_norm`
- `Fermat3.odd_of_cop_even`
- `Fermat3.par_ab`: (p + q) odd forces (a + b) odd in Euler's parametrization.
- `Fermat3.pow_emod_of`
- `Fermat3.primitive_of_sol`: Reduction to coprime solutions: dividing by gcd(x, y) gives a solution with gcd 1.
- `Fermat3.second_factor`: The second factor is (x + y)² − 3xy.
- `Fermat3.sol_cop`
- `Fermat3.sol_even`: Some entry of a primitive solution is even.
- `Fermat3.sol_rot`
- `Fermat3.sol_swap`
- `Fermat3.step`: The descent step (Euler): from a primitive solution with z even, build a smaller one.
- `Fermat3.sum_cubes`: The sum of two cubes factors: x³ + y³ = (x + y)(x² − xy + y²).
- `Fermat3.sum_mod3`: Every solution of x³ + y³ = z³ has x + y ≡ z (mod 3).
- `Fermat3.three_dvd_mul`
- `Fermat3.three_dvd_of_cube`
- `Fermat3.three_dvd_of_sq`

## FusedAlgebra.Topics.Fermat.Fermat4 (9)

- `Fermat4.descent_step`: One step of Fermat's descent: a coprime solution of x⁴ + y⁴ = z² (x odd, y ≠ 0) yields a nonzero solution a⁴ + b⁴ = c² with c < z.
- `Fermat4.dvd_sq_self`: A number divides its own square.
- `Fermat4.pyth_param`: Primitive Pythagorean triples: a² + b² = c² with a odd and gcd(a,b) = 1 gives coprime m, n with a = m² − n² (written a + n² = m²), b = 2mn, c = m² + n².
- `Fermat4.sq_inj`: Squaring is one-to-one on the natural numbers: a² = b² gives a = b.
- `Fermat4.sq_mod_four`: Every square is 0 or 1 mod 4: 0 for an even number, 1 for an odd one.
- `Fermat4.sq_of_coprime_mul`: If a·b is a square and a, b share no factor, then a is itself a square.
- `fermat4`: Fermat's Last Theorem for exponent 4.
- `fourth_power_ne_mpow_four`: Circle-operation form of FLT(4): for rationals q ≠ 0 and s, q⁴ is never the circle 4th power s^(◯4) = (1 + s)⁴ − 1.
- `no_x4_y4_z2`: No sum of two nonzero fourth powers is a square.

## FusedAlgebra.Topics.Fermat.FermatRoute (9)

- `FermatRoute.cop_left`: In a solution a^p + b^p = c^p with gcd(a, b) = 1, also gcd(a, c) = 1.
- `FermatRoute.dvd_self_pow`: x divides x^p for p ≥ 1.
- `FermatRoute.flt_of_no_frey_curve`: Fermat's Last Theorem for every n ≥ 3, given only `NoFreyCurve`.
- `FermatRoute.flt_of_prime_case`: FLT for every n ≥ 3 follows from the prime case p ≥ 5 alone (n = 3 and n = 4 are proved).
- `FermatRoute.frey_normalize_cop`: Frey normalization keeping gcd(a', b') = 1: the input the Frey curve needs.
- `FermatRoute.gcd_natAbs_congr`: Int.gcd only sees absolute values.
- `FermatRoute.gcd_one_of_no_prime`: Two nonzero Ints with no common prime factor are coprime.
- `FermatRoute.normalize_core'`: 2d, with coprimality kept: re-signing keeps |a'| = |o1|, |b'| = |e|.
- `FermatRoute.prime_dvd_of_dvd_pow`: A prime dividing x^n divides x (Int).

## FusedAlgebra.Topics.Fermat.FreyPieces (46)

- `FreyPieces.coprime_parity`: 2c. In a coprime solution exactly one of x, y, z is even.
- `FreyPieces.coprime_reduction`: 2b. A solution with x, y ≠ 0 shrinks (divide by gcd x y) to one with gcd x' y' = 1.
- `FreyPieces.euclid`: 2a. Euclid's lemma on Nat: a prime dividing a product divides a factor.
- `FreyPieces.euclid_int`: 2a. Euclid's lemma on Int (through natAbs).
- `FreyPieces.exact_exponent_pth_power`: The exact exponent of a prime in a p-th power is a multiple of p.
- `FreyPieces.factor_out`: Pull every factor q out of a nonzero M: M = q^k·m with q ∤ m.
- `FreyPieces.flt_circ`: 4. x^n + y^n = z^n exactly when (y/x)^n = mpow (z/x − 1) n: an n-th power equals a circle n-th power.
- `FreyPieces.flt_of_coprime`: 2b. So FLT n holds once coprime solutions are ruled out.
- `FreyPieces.flt_of_dvd`: 1a. FLT for d gives FLT for every multiple n of d (x^n = (x^(n/d))^d).
- `FreyPieces.flt_reduction`: 1c. FLT 4 and FLT for every odd prime give FLT for every n ≥ 3.
- `FreyPieces.flt_reduction_frey`: 1d. FLT 4, FLT 3 and FLT for every prime p ≥ 5 (the Frey range) give FLT for every n ≥ 3.
- `FreyPieces.four_or_odd_prime`: 1b. Every n ≥ 3 is divisible by 4 or by an odd prime.
- `FreyPieces.frey_a4_mod_q`: 3f. For an odd prime q dividing A, q divides a4.
- `FreyPieces.frey_at_two`: 3e. At 2: A odd and 32 ∣ B make c4 odd and 4 ∣ Δ, so 2 is a bad prime with a node.
- `FreyPieces.frey_c4`: 3d. c4 of the Frey model is A² + AB + B².
- `FreyPieces.frey_c4_not_dvd`: 3e. If gcd(A, B) = 1, a prime dividing ABC does not divide A² + AB + B².
- `FreyPieces.frey_change_of_vars`: 3c. The identity behind x = 4X, y = 8Y + 4X.
- `FreyPieces.frey_disc`: 3d. 256·Δ of the Frey model is (ABC)².
- `FreyPieces.frey_disc_pth_power`: 3g. With A = a^p, B = b^p, C = c^p: 256·Δ = ((abc)²)^p.
- `FreyPieces.frey_disc_roots`: 3b. 16·(product of root differences)² = 16(ABC)² when A + B = C.
- `FreyPieces.frey_disc_valuation`: 3g bonus: for every odd prime q, the exact exponent of q in Δ is a multiple of p.
- `FreyPieces.frey_minimal_divisibility`: 3c. A ≡ 3 mod 4 and 16 ∣ B give 4 ∣ B − A − 1 and 16 ∣ AB.
- `FreyPieces.frey_minimal_model`: 3c. With these integers, (4X, 8Y + 4X) is on E exactly when Y² + XY = X³ + a2X² + a4X.
- `FreyPieces.frey_normalize`: 2d. Frey normalization: a coprime Int solution for odd p ≥ 5 becomes one with a' ≡ 3 mod 4, b' even, 32 ∣ b'^p, same |abc|.
- `FreyPieces.frey_reduces_to_node`: 3f. Then mod q the model is the node family Y² + 1·XY = X³ + a2X².
- `FreyPieces.frey_semistable`: 3e. Semistability: a prime dividing Δ never divides c4 (node, not cusp).
- `FreyPieces.frey_two_torsion`: 3a. The 2-torsion points (0,0), (A,0), (−B,0) lie on E.
- `FreyPieces.genus_X0_2`: 5. The genus formula 12g = 12 + μ − 3ν2 − 4ν3 − 6ν∞ (cited, not proved here) gives g(X0(2)) = 0.
- `FreyPieces.int_pow_emod_two`: Over Int, x^n % 2 = x % 2 for n ≥ 1.
- `FreyPieces.level2_counts`: 5. μ = 3, ν2 = 1, ν3 = 0, ν∞ = 2 for Γ0(2), by computation.
- `FreyPieces.neg_pow_odd`: (−x)^(2k+1) = −x^(2k+1).
- `FreyPieces.neg_pow_of_odd`: (−x)^p = −x^p for odd p.
- `FreyPieces.node_chord_circ`: 3f. Link: three points of the node are collinear exactly when s1 ◯ s2 ◯ s3 = 0, sᵢ = (m2 − m1)/(tᵢ − m2).
- `FreyPieces.node_chord_identity`: 3f. (m2 − m1)·det = Vandermonde · (∏(tᵢ − m1) − ∏(tᵢ − m2)).
- `FreyPieces.node_point`: 3f. The point (x, t·x), x = nodeX, lies on y² + a1xy = x³ + a2x² with a1 = −(m1+m2), a2 = −m1m2 (library node_param).
- `FreyPieces.normalize_core`: From o1^p + e^p + o2^p = 0 with o1 odd, e even: re-sign to a' ≡ 3 mod 4, b' even.
- `FreyPieces.not_dvd_pow`: A prime not dividing m does not divide m^p.
- `FreyPieces.odd_prime_coprime_two`: An odd prime is coprime to 2 (so to every power of 2).
- `FreyPieces.one_add_div_mul`: (1 + d/b)·b = b + d for b ≠ 0.
- `FreyPieces.pow_dvd_bound`: q^a ∣ q^b·m with q ∤ m forces a ≤ b.
- `FreyPieces.pow_mod_two`: x^n has the parity of x for n ≥ 1.
- `FreyPieces.prime_dvd_gcd_false`: A prime cannot divide two coprime integers.
- `FreyPieces.prime_ne_two_odd`: A prime other than 2 is odd.
- `FreyPieces.rat_div_pow`: (y/x)^n · x^n = y^n for x ≠ 0.
- `FreyPieces.rat_mul_ne_zero`: Nonzero times nonzero is nonzero in Rat.
- `FreyPieces.two_pow_dvd`: b even and p ≥ 5 give 32 ∣ b^p.

## FusedAlgebra.Topics.FluxTubeDial (8)

- `CircleOp.FluxTubeDial.confines_below_threshold`: Below the threshold the exponent is positive: energy grows with distance (confines).
- `CircleOp.FluxTubeDial.deff_from_exponent`: Effective dimension of the flux: D_eff = 2 − (exponent of L) = 1 + (D−1)(s−1).
- `CircleOp.FluxTubeDial.deff_maxwell`: At s = 2 (Maxwell) the flux spreads into all D directions.
- `CircleOp.FluxTubeDial.deff_tube`: At s = 1 the flux has one direction: a tube, in every dimension.
- `CircleOp.FluxTubeDial.pt_map_form`: The Pagels–Tomboulis map is s = 1 + 1/(1+4δ), so s → 1 as δ grows.
- `CircleOp.FluxTubeDial.pt_quarter`: Pagels–Tomboulis: field energy ∝ |E|^(2+4δ) gives |D|^s with s = (2+4δ)/(1+4δ); at δ = 1/4, s = 3/2.
- `CircleOp.FluxTubeDial.threshold_3d`: In three dimensions the threshold is s = 3/2.
- `CircleOp.FluxTubeDial.threshold_exponent_zero`: The confinement threshold: the pair-energy exponent vanishes (log) exactly at s = D/(D−1).

## FusedAlgebra.Topics.ForceReach (6)

- `CircleOp.ForceReach.curvature_reading_k0`: Curvature reading of the law with k = 0, d = K is K itself.
- `CircleOp.ForceReach.disc_is_spread_sq`: Δ is the squared spread of the two exponents of the law's linear form.
- `CircleOp.ForceReach.double_root_iff_border`: Massless exactly on the border: a double exponent (no length scale) happens iff Δ = 0.
- `CircleOp.ForceReach.drift_removal`: Removing the drift: with λ = k/2 + μ, λ² − kλ + d = μ² − Δ/4, so the carrier mass is m² = Δ/4.
- `CircleOp.ForceReach.raychaudhuri_is_law`: Raychaudhuri as the law: if x' = 1 + K x², then θ = −K x has θ' = −K − θ².
- `CircleOp.ForceReach.yukawa_deff`: Yukawa's effective dimension: 1 − r·(d ln F/dr) = 3 + m²r²/(1 + m r), for F = e^{−mr}(1 + m r)/r² (the logarithmic derivative written out).

## FusedAlgebra.Topics.FractalBoxes (15)

- `FractalBoxes.code_inj`
- `FractalBoxes.code_lt`
- `FractalBoxes.cube_layers`: A whole-number box has the familiar Pascal layers (a check of the definition).
- `FractalBoxes.exp_beats_linear`: exponential beats any linear rule somewhere: K·d + L < 2^d at d = 4(K+L+1).
- `FractalBoxes.half_and_three_halves`: Dimensions add when boxes join: a half box and a box of dimension 3/2 make a square (layers 1, 2, 1, 0, ...).
- `FractalBoxes.half_box_squared`
- `FractalBoxes.half_join_stick`: Two half boxes joined give a stick exactly, layer by layer: 1, 1, then 0 (the negative pieces cancel), checked through layer 10.
- `FractalBoxes.half_layers`: The half box: 1 empty piece, half a single side, then −1/8 of a two-side corner, +1/16, −5/128, ... (negative layers alternate from layer 2 on).
- `FractalBoxes.hyper_twice`: Hyperspace count: if a shape shows N separate points at some scale, its space of pieces shows 2^N − 1 (all nonempty subsets). Doing that twice:
- `FractalBoxes.no_affine_half_pow2`: The plainest case: no affine rule d ↦ c·d + e is a half of d ↦ 2^d.
- `FractalBoxes.no_linear_half_pow2`: No operation whose effect on size is bounded by a linear rule A·d + B can, applied twice, give 2^d for every d.
- `FractalBoxes.other_half_squared`: A second half box: three pieces at scale 1/9 (3^m pieces of 9^-m). It is a different shape with the same dimension; squared it is 9^m pieces of 9^-m.
- `FractalBoxes.pieces_join`
- `FractalBoxes.succ_le_two_pow`
- `FractalBoxes.third_join_stick`: Three third-boxes joined give a stick (layers 1, 1, 0, 0, 0, 0).

## FusedAlgebra.Topics.FractionalPowerset (11)

- `FractionalPowerset.dial_inflationary`: `F k` plays the role of `P^(1/2^k)`: each level composed with itself gives the level above, all levels are monotone, and the top level strictly grows (Cantor: `X < P(X)`).
- `FractionalPowerset.flat_add_flat_succ`
- `FractionalPowerset.flat_circ_iff_flat_mul`: The circle operation (called mult-add before 1.0) `m ◯ m = (m + m) + m·m` is flat exactly when multiplication is.

- `FractionalRungs.dial_three`: three at once: layers of the cube weighted 1, k, k² (one per dimension)
- `FractionalRungs.fuse_add`: addition is the first rung the shift touches

## FusedAlgebra.Topics.FunctionalAnalysis (35)

- `FunctionalAnalysis.M2.ext'`
- `FunctionalAnalysis.character_op`: A character (additive, multiplicative map) carries ◯ₒ to ◯ₒ.
- `FunctionalAnalysis.cube_zero_qi`: Cube zero: the quasi-inverse is −a + a².
- `FunctionalAnalysis.det_circ_swap`: Neither does the shifted determinant.
- `FunctionalAnalysis.foldW_lift`
- `FunctionalAnalysis.fredholm_fold`: At k = 1 the same fold is the shifted Fredholm determinant det(I + K) − 1.
- `FunctionalAnalysis.gap_fold`: Chance of at least one point = λ₁ ◯₋₁ λ₂ ◯₋₁ … = 1 − ∏(1 − λᵢ) = 1 − det(I − K).
- `FunctionalAnalysis.jacobson_circle`: Jacobson's circle a + b − ab is the k = −1 dial of the circle operation.
- `FunctionalAnalysis.neumann_miss`: Each partial sum misses by exactly the next term: a ◯ₒ s_n = −(−a)^{n+1}.
- `FunctionalAnalysis.neumann_miss_right`: The same from the other side: s_n ◯ₒ a = −(−a)^{n+1}.
- `FunctionalAnalysis.nilpotent_pair`: N = [[0,1],[0,0]] and t·N' = [[0,0],[−t,0]], both nilpotent: their circle operation has det(1 + ·) = 1 and trace −t, so its eigenvalues λ satisfy (1 + λ)(1 + λ̄) = 1: they ride the circle |1 + z| = 1 and never reach the wall.
- `FunctionalAnalysis.nilpotent_pair_noncomm`: At t = 1 the order matters…
- `FunctionalAnalysis.nilpotent_pair_sixth_root`: …and X = N ◯ₒ N' satisfies X² + X + 1 = 0, so its lift cubes to −1: X is a sixth circle root of 0, though N and N' each have spectrum {0}.
- `FunctionalAnalysis.nilpotent_quasi_inverse`: A nilpotent operator (aⁿ⁺¹ = 0) has the finite Neumann sum as its quasi-inverse.
- `FunctionalAnalysis.opCirc_comm_eq`: On a commutative ring the operator circle operation is the library's circle operation.
- `FunctionalAnalysis.op_assoc`
- `FunctionalAnalysis.op_comm_of_commute`
- `FunctionalAnalysis.op_conj`: Changing basis (conjugating by an invertible u) respects the circle operation.
- `FunctionalAnalysis.op_dial_lift`: The operator dial with a weight k that commutes with a: 1 + k(a ◯ₒ[k] b) = (1 + ka)(1 + kb).
- `FunctionalAnalysis.op_lift`
- `FunctionalAnalysis.op_zero`
- `FunctionalAnalysis.perlis_iff`: Perlis's quasi-regularity is invertibility of the lift.
- `FunctionalAnalysis.qi_of_inverse`: An inverse of 1 + a gives a quasi-inverse c − 1.
- `FunctionalAnalysis.qi_reverse`: Socks and shoes: the quasi-inverse of a ◯ₒ b is b' ◯ₒ a'.
- `FunctionalAnalysis.qi_reverse_left`
- `FunctionalAnalysis.quasi_inverse_unique`: A left quasi-inverse and a right quasi-inverse are the same element.
- `FunctionalAnalysis.resolvent_identity`: The resolvent identity in circle-operation form: a' − b' = (1 + a')(b − a)(1 + b').
- `FunctionalAnalysis.rpow_comm`
- `FunctionalAnalysis.rpow_neg`
- `FunctionalAnalysis.square_zero_qi`: Square zero: the quasi-inverse is −a.
- `FunctionalAnalysis.tr_circ_swap`: Trace does not see the order of a circle operation.
- `FunctionalAnalysis.wall_left`: −1 absorbs from the left…
- `FunctionalAnalysis.wall_no_quasi_inverse`: Nothing undoes the wall, unless the ring is the zero ring.
- `FunctionalAnalysis.wall_right`: …and from the right.
- `FunctionalAnalysis.zero_op`

## FusedAlgebra.Topics.FusedConstants (20)

- `FusedConstants.H_add`
- `FusedConstants.H_mul`
- `FusedConstants.H_pow`
- `FusedConstants.H_succ`
- `FusedConstants.H_two_one`
- `FusedConstants.H_zero`
- `FusedConstants.all_rungs_two_two`: 2 (r) 2 = 4 on every rung.
- `FusedConstants.base_two_clears`: On every rung from power up (r ≥ 2), the base-2 curve is above the diagonal.
- `FusedConstants.base_two_tower_climbs`: The base-2 tower on every rung from tetration up climbs at every step (tower step n+1 = previous rung applied to tower step n), so it never settles.
- `FusedConstants.base_two_tower_unbounded`
- `FusedConstants.fused_one_one`: Fused form: 1 (r) 1 = 3 on every rung.
- `FusedConstants.lt_two_pow'`
- `FusedConstants.mult_add`
- `FusedConstants.rung1_above`: Any base above 0 on rung 1 clears the diagonal everywhere.
- `FusedConstants.rung1_base0`: Rung 1, base 0: adding 0 is the diagonal itself.
- `FusedConstants.rung2_base1`: Rung 2, base 1: multiplying by 1 is the diagonal itself.
- `FusedConstants.slide_fixed`: Fixed points move by exactly one.
- `FusedConstants.slide_gap`: The gap between the curve and the diagonal is the same before and after.
- `FusedConstants.slide_kiss`: A slid kiss: if the original curve stays on or above the diagonal and touches it at x, the slid curve stays on or above the diagonal and touches it at x - 1.
- `FusedConstants.slide_step`: Every step (the whole-number stand-in for a slope) is the same before and after.

## FusedAlgebra.Topics.GRBridges (22)

- `GRBridges.M2.ext'`
- `GRBridges.aether_constraint`: Aether river: (1 + c₂)(tr K)² − (1 − c₁₃) K·K = (c₂ + c₁₃)(tr K)² when (tr K)² = K·K.
- `GRBridges.backward_part_circ`: The backward light-cone part is 1 + ((−β₁) ◯ (−β₂)).
- `GRBridges.boost_norm`: The norm (1 + βσ)(1 − βσ) = 1 − β²: zero exactly at β = ±1, the wall (light speed).
- `GRBridges.boost_product`: The product (1 + β₁σ)(1 + β₂σ) with σ² = 1 has scalar part 1 + β₁β₂ and σ-part β₁ + β₂.
- `GRBridges.brans_dicke`: γ = (1 + ω)/(2 + ω) means γ − 1 = −1/(2 + ω).
- `GRBridges.bundle_area`: Bundle area from two transverse stretches.
- `GRBridges.e2_from_traces`: e2 from traces: (tr K)² − tr K² = 2 e2.
- `GRBridges.flat_focusing`: Straight rays (a = αλ, b = βλ): the second difference of the area is the cross term 2αβ.
- `GRBridges.focusing_cross`: The cross term: θ² − (θ₁² + θ₂²) = 2θ₁θ₂ (the ab part of the circle operation, in rates).
- `GRBridges.focusing_split`: Focusing split: θ²/2 + σ² = θ₁² + θ₂², with θ = θ₁ + θ₂ and σ² = (θ₁ − θ₂)²/2.
- `GRBridges.forward_part_circ`: The forward light-cone part (scalar + σ) of a product is 1 + (β₁ ◯ β₂).
- `GRBridges.front_eq_sand_scalar`: A central (scalar) weight makes the front-loaded law agree with the sandwich law.
- `GRBridges.front_not_assoc`: The front-loaded law fails associativity for a non-central weight (explicit witness).
- `GRBridges.isotropic_einstein`: Isotropic curvature: G = ((n−1) − n(n−1)/2)·q·g = −(n−1)(n−2)/2·q·g.
- `GRBridges.null_null_2d`: In a 2D slice, X(k,k) = 0 for both light directions k = (1, ±1) forces X₀₁ = 0, X₁₁ = −X₀₀ (X = φ·η).
- `GRBridges.sand_assoc`: **The sandwich law is associative for every k.**
- `GRBridges.sand_lift`: **The lift holds for every matrix weight k:** 1 + k(a ◯ b) = (1 + k a)(1 + k b).
- `GRBridges.schur_factor`: (n−1)(n−2)/2 is 0 in dimension 2 and positive from dimension 3 on.
- `GRBridges.schwarzschild_constraint`: Schwarzschild river: radial rate p = −q/2 makes e2 vanish, (tr K)² = K·K.
- `GRBridges.spherical_shell`: Spherical river (rates v′, v/r, v/r, with q = v/r): r²·e2 = v² + 2 r v v′ = (r v²)′.
- `GRBridges.three_way_layers`: The three-way circle operation = sum + pairwise layer (e2) + triple layer.

## FusedAlgebra.Topics.GameTheory (35)

- `GameTheory.banzhaf3_eq`
- `GameTheory.bigger_smaller_bonus`: With synergy (k ≥ 0) the bigger player gets the smaller bonus ratio.
- `GameTheory.bonus_gap`: Bonus ratios φᵢ/xᵢ differ by (x₂ − x₁)(k/2 + k²x₃/3): it is your partners' size that counts.
- `GameTheory.corners3`: The three-player worth expanded over the cube: corners weighted by k^(size − 1).
- `GameTheory.dfold_append`
- `GameTheory.dfold_lift`
- `GameTheory.dfold_one`
- `GameTheory.dial_game_union`: Sugeno's rule: disjoint coalitions combine by the dial. The excess over adding is the corner.
- `GameTheory.edge_compose`: Edges compose like relativistic velocities: landscape point (k, q) = (0, −1).
- `GameTheory.edge_prob`
- `GameTheory.hedge_step`: One round on two experts: the new total is the old total plus ε times the algorithm's gain.
- `GameTheory.kelly_compose`: Two bets at fraction f: the returns combine by the dial with k = f.
- `GameTheory.kelly_foc`: First-order condition: d/df [p log(1 + bf) + q log(1 − f)] = pb/(1 + bf) − q/(1 − f) vanishes.
- `GameTheory.kelly_loss_lift`: … and the loss lift q(1 + b)/b (= q/(1 − π)).
- `GameTheory.kelly_many`: Many bets: wealth is 1 + f·(r₁ ◯_f r₂ ◯_f … ◯_f rₙ).
- `GameTheory.kelly_ratio`: The bookmaker's chance π = 1/(1 + b): the win lift is your chance over theirs.
- `GameTheory.kelly_ruin`: Ruin is the wall: one outcome with f·r = −1 leaves nothing, whatever follows.
- `GameTheory.kelly_stake`: In money terms the stakes' returns f·r combine by the circle operation plainly.
- `GameTheory.kelly_win_lift`: Kelly's fraction f = p − q/b makes the win lift p(1 + b) (= p/π, π = 1/(1 + b)).
- `GameTheory.lifted_regret`: The lifted regret bound: starting from equal weights, the best expert's growth is at most 2 = n times the algorithm's. Taking logs gives Σ log(1 + ε g) ≤ Σ log(1 + ε r) + log 2.
- `GameTheory.marginal`: Joining coalition S is worth x·(1 + k·v(S)).
- `GameTheory.multilinear2`: Owen's multilinear extension, two players: everyone present with chance t gives the dial fold of t·x.
- `GameTheory.multilinear3`: Three players.
- `GameTheory.odds_circ`: Odds multiply along a chain, so odds − 1 combines by the circle operation.
- `GameTheory.odds_is_lift`: The odds are the edge's lift (1 + s)/(1 − s): relativity's Doppler factor squared.
- `GameTheory.prob_compose`: Win chances compose by p₁p₂/(p₁p₂ + q₁q₂).
- `GameTheory.run_expert`
- `GameTheory.run_pos`
- `GameTheory.run_total`
- `GameTheory.shapley2_efficient`
- `GameTheory.shapley2_eq`
- `GameTheory.shapley2_union`: k = −1, "some cause strikes": each cause keeps its own chance minus half the overlap (the actuarial UDD split).
- `GameTheory.shapley3_corners`: Corner by corner: singletons whole, each pair corner k·xy halved, the triple k²xyz in thirds.
- `GameTheory.shapley3_efficient`
- `GameTheory.shapley3_eq`: Closed form: x·(1 + k·e₁/2 + k²·e₂/3) over the other two: each corner shared equally.

## FusedAlgebra.Topics.GeneralRelativity (28)

- `GeneralRelativity.beta_one`: **β = 1**: (1 − U/2)² = (1 + U/2)²(1 − 2U + 2U²) − U³(3 + U)/2, so −g_tt = 1 − 2U + 2U² + O(U³).
- `GeneralRelativity.boost_x_keeps_form`: The boost along x keeps the dial's form: Kᵀ G + G K = 0.
- `GeneralRelativity.boost_y_keeps_form`
- `GeneralRelativity.boosts_close`: **Two boosts close on a rotation, with coefficient −q** (the twist, made infinitesimal).
- `GeneralRelativity.cassini_excludes_dial_only`: **Cassini** (γ − 1 = (2.1 ± 2.3)·10⁻⁶·10): γ = 0 sits (1 − 0.000021)/0.000023 ≈ 43,477 σ away.
- `GeneralRelativity.conformal_dial`: A conformal dial (Nordström, γ = −1): no bending, perihelion backwards by a third.
- `GeneralRelativity.deficits_inverse`: **The two deficits are circle inverses**: (−g_tt − 1) ◯ (g_rr − 1) = 0, i.e. (−2x) ◯ (2x/(1 − 2x)) = 0. One boost, the same factor for time and space.
- `GeneralRelativity.dial_only_half_bending`: **Any dial-only theory (space left flat, γ = 0) bends light by exactly half, whatever β.**
- `GeneralRelativity.dial_only_perihelion`: Its perihelion is 2/3 of GR's for A = 1 − 2U (β = 0) and 1/3 for A = e^{−2U} (β = 1).
- `GeneralRelativity.einstein_combination`: **Lovelock's step in one line**: if ∇·Ric = ½ dR (contracted Bianchi) and the combination a·Ric + b·R·g is to be divergence-free where dR ≠ 0, then b = −a/2.
- `GeneralRelativity.einstein_divergence_free`: And the Einstein combination is divergence-free: ½dR − ½dR = 0.
- `GeneralRelativity.friedmann_newton`: Friedmann is Newton's escape speed for a uniform ball (π kept as a symbol p): (H R)²/2 = ((4/3) p R³ ρ)/R ↔ H² = (8/3) p ρ, for R ≠ 0.
- `GeneralRelativity.full_bending_iff`: Full bending forces γ = 1: space must curve as much as time.
- `GeneralRelativity.galilean_boosts_commute`: At the Galilean point the boosts commute: no twist.
- `GeneralRelativity.gamma_one`: **γ = 1**: (1 + U/2)⁴ = 1 + 2U + U²(3/2 + U/2 + U²/16), so g_ij = (1 + 2U)δ_ij + O(U²).
- `GeneralRelativity.gpb_through_river`: **Gravity Probe B's factor is the gyroscope's speed through the river**: composing the river (radial, v_r² = 2x) with the orbit (tangential, v_o² = x/(1 − 2x)) at right angles, 1 − w² = (1 − v_r²)(1 − v_o²) = 1 − 3x.
- `GeneralRelativity.horizon_is_wall`: **The horizon is the river at light speed** (the velocity wall): 2M/r = 1 ↔ r = 2M.
- `GeneralRelativity.infall_light`: Light falling in from far away, seen by the river: blueshift γ² times Doppler² equals 1/(1 + v)², i.e. γ·√((1 − v)/(1 + v)) = 1/(1 + v). Stated without division.
- `GeneralRelativity.isotropic_radius`: Isotropic radius: with r = ρ(1 + u)² and M = 2uρ, 1 − 2M/r = ((1 − u)/(1 + u))², written without division: (1 + u)²·ρ − 2M equals (1 − u)²·ρ.
- `GeneralRelativity.lapse_bracket_is_rotation`: **The hypersurface bracket with linear lapses** N = b·x, M = c·x: the shift N∇M − M∇N is the rigid rotation field x ↦ (b × c) × x … written out by components.
- `GeneralRelativity.metric_product`: Equivalently g_tt · g_rr = −1: (1 − 2x) · 1/(1 − 2x) = 1.
- `GeneralRelativity.perpendicular_fuse`: Perpendicular speeds compose by the circle operation on −v²: −w² = (−u²) ◯ (−v²) when w² = u² + v²(1 − u²).
- `GeneralRelativity.redshifts_fuse`: **Redshifts combine by the circle operation**: if 1 + z = (1 + z₁)(1 + z₂) then z = z₁ ◯ z₂ (any chain of gravitational, Doppler and cosmological shifts).
- `GeneralRelativity.river_full_bending`
- `GeneralRelativity.river_full_perihelion`
- `GeneralRelativity.rotation_keeps_form`: The rotation keeps the form too, for every q.
- `GeneralRelativity.static_clock`: Static clocks run at 1/γ of the river: 1 − v² = 1 − 2M/r = −g_tt.
- `GeneralRelativity.static_ruler`: Static radial rulers stretch by γ of the river: 1/(1 − v²) = g_rr.

## FusedAlgebra.Topics.GeometryTrig (24)
