- `SimpleLaws.angle_addition`: angle addition: (cos α + i sin α)(cos β + i sin β) at the complex point (0, 1)
- `SimpleLaws.box_corners`: the box: three different sides give the corners a+b+c, ab+bc+ca, abc
- `SimpleLaws.cube_layers`: the cube: the circle operation applied three times gives the layers 3, 3, 1 (with the unit cube, 1, 3, 3, 1)
- `SimpleLaws.double_angle`: double angle: (1 + x i)² = (1 − x²) + 2x·i, so tan 2θ = 2t/(1 − t²)
- `SimpleLaws.euclid_II_4`: Euclid II.4 in full: (a + b)² = a² + 2ab + b²
- `SimpleLaws.euclid_triple`
- `SimpleLaws.euclid_triple_parts`: Euclid's triples: square the triangle q + p·i (circle power 2 in the complex lift)
- `SimpleLaws.fall_is_gnomon`: each step's distance is the gnomon that grows an n-square into an (n+1)-square
- `SimpleLaws.fallen_square`: so the distance fallen is the square of the time: d ∝ t²
- `SimpleLaws.flat_triangle_open`: on the border (k = ±2, angle 180° or 0°) the triangle is flat: c = a + b or c = a − b
- `SimpleLaws.flat_triangle_shut`
- `SimpleLaws.gnomon`: Euclid II.4, the gnomon: the circle operation of a with itself is the L-shaped strip 2a + a²
- `SimpleLaws.gnomon_lift`: the gnomon step is the circle operation's lift on Nat: (n ◯ n) + 1 = (n + 1)²
- `SimpleLaws.law_of_cosines_norm`: the law of cosines is the norm on the line q = 1, with k = −2 cos C: c² = a² + k·ab + b². At k = 0 it is Pythagoras.
- `SimpleLaws.law_of_cosines_right_angle`
- `SimpleLaws.pythagoras_from_areas`: Pythagoras by the "bride's chair": the square on a + b holds the tilted square on c and four right triangles of area ab/2. Given that area count, c² = a² + b².
- `SimpleLaws.rectangle_corner`: the rectangle: (1 + a)(1 + b) = the unit square + two strips + the corner square
- `SimpleLaws.square_cube`: Galileo's square-cube law (1638): grow every length by a; area grows by A = a ◯ a and volume by A ◯ a, so weight per unit of strength grows by the factor 1 + a
- `SimpleLaws.tangent_addition`: tangent addition is the landscape law at (0, 1): numerator x + y, denominator 1 − xy
- `SimpleLaws.triangle_lengths_real`: inside the turning country (k² ≤ 4) every side-squared is a true length, never negative
- `SimpleLaws.triangles_compose`: the right triangle 1 + x·i: combining two of them by the circle operation multiplies squared hypotenuses, (1 + x²)(1 + y²) = (1 − xy)² + (x + y)²; its new slope is (x + y)/(1 − xy)
- `SimpleLaws.triangles_compose_norm`
- `SimpleLaws.triple_angle`: triple angle: (1 + x i)³ = (1 − 3x²) + (3x − x³)·i: the cube's layers 1, 3, 3, 1 land alternately on the real and the i axis, with the signs of i², i³
- `SimpleLaws.unit_circle_closed`: cos² + sin² = 1 survives angle addition: the unit circle is closed under the product

## FusedAlgebra.Topics.GraphTheory (34)

- `GraphTheory.add_div'`
- `GraphTheory.c4_indep`: C₄ is the join of two non-edges: I = (1 + x)² + (1 + x)² − 1 = 1 + 4x + 2x².
- `GraphTheory.c4_strong_c4`
- `GraphTheory.c5_indep`
- `GraphTheory.c5_strong_p2`
- `GraphTheory.cancel_left`
- `GraphTheory.colouring_bond`: The proper-colouring bond v = −1 has t = −1/(Q − 1).
- `GraphTheory.colouring_is_wall`: In weights, the colouring bond is the circle operation's own wall −1.
- `GraphTheory.colouring_wall_absorbs`: Wall 2: that bond swallows anything in parallel (a forbidden pair stays forbidden).
- `GraphTheory.ddiv`
- `GraphTheory.diamondQ_one`
- `GraphTheory.diamond_poly`
- `GraphTheory.golden_fixed`: **The golden fixed point.** If p² = 1 − p (p = (√5 − 1)/2), the diamond step fixes p.
- `GraphTheory.golden_slope`: Slope there: 4p − 4p³ = 4 − 4p (≈ 1.528 > 1, so the fixed point repels).
- `GraphTheory.ising_fixed`: Ising (Q = 2): if t³ + t² + t = 1, the diamond step fixes t.
- `GraphTheory.join_adds`: A join lets a set use only one side, so I − 1 adds (dial k = 0).
- `GraphTheory.p3_strong_p3`
- `GraphTheory.parallel_join`: Two parallel bonds join their ends with weight v₁ + v₂ + v₁v₂: one, the other, or both.
- `GraphTheory.parallel_lift`: The lift of the parallel law is 1 + v: (1 + (Q − 1)t)/(1 − t) = 1 + v.
- `GraphTheory.parallel_tr`: **Parallel is a landscape law.** With tᵢ = vᵢ/(Q + vᵢ), the joined bond v₁ ◯ v₂ has t = (t₁ + t₂ + (Q − 2)t₁t₂)/(1 − (1 − Q)t₁t₂) = F_{Q−2, 1−Q}(t₁, t₂).
- `GraphTheory.perfect_bond_absorbs`: Wall 1: a perfect bond t = 1 swallows anything put in parallel with it.
- `GraphTheory.potts_is_amh`: **Potts parallel is AMH union**, with dependence θ = 1 − Q: the two bonds "fail" together like AMH-dependent events.
- `GraphTheory.q_one`: Q = 1: percolation and reliability, the failure union a ◯₋₁ b.
- `GraphTheory.q_two`: Q = 2: Ising, velocity addition (tanh addition).
- `GraphTheory.q_zero_cusp`: Q = 0: the spanning-forest end, the cusp k² = 4q of the landscape.
- `GraphTheory.series_local`: Series pair through a middle vertex: "ends not joined" weighs Q + v₁ + v₂ (middle alone, or tied to one end), "ends joined" weighs v₁v₂. One bond of weight v with the factor Q + v₁ + v₂ gives the same two numbers.
- `GraphTheory.series_odds`: In odds against, u = 1/p − 1, series is the plain circle operation.
- `GraphTheory.series_tr`: **Series multiplies transmissivity.**
- `GraphTheory.strong_count`: Edge counts: 2m + n multiplies.
- `GraphTheory.strong_entry`: Closed adjacency entries multiply: (a + i)(b + j) − i·j = a·j + i·b + a·b. With i = j = 1 (same vertex in both) this is a ◯ b; the three terms are the Cartesian pieces A⊗I, I⊗B and the tensor corner A⊗B.
- `GraphTheory.strong_entry_diag`
- `GraphTheory.three_state_fixed`: Three colours: the diamond fixed point is exactly 1/2.
- `GraphTheory.two_k2_indep`
- `GraphTheory.wheatstone`: **The Wheatstone bridge** has no series or parallel pair; pivot on the middle bond: contracted, it is two parallel pairs in series; deleted, two series pairs in parallel.

## FusedAlgebra.Topics.GravityProbeB (16)

- `GravityProbeB.corner_is_second_order`: The fused total differs from the plain sum only by the corner a·b (second order).
- `GravityProbeB.factors_multiply`: **The factors multiply to the known total** (squared): (1 − 2x)·(1/γ²) = 1 − 3x.
- `GravityProbeB.fractions_fuse`: **The precession fractions combine by the circle operation**: if 1 − p = (1 − a)(1 − b) then −p = (−a) ◯ (−b).
- `GravityProbeB.frame_dragging_within_error`: Frame dragging: about 40.8 against the measured 37.2 ± 7.2.
- `GravityProbeB.fuse_factors`: And back: the circle-operation form gives the product of factors.
- `GravityProbeB.fuse_weight_minus_one`: The same law is the dial's weight −1: p = a + b − ab ("either twist").
- `GravityProbeB.gamma_sq`: γ² = 1/(1 − v²) = (1 − 2x)/(1 − 3x).
- `GravityProbeB.geodetic_parts`: Space part 4402.6 + velocity part 2201.3 = 6603.9.
- `GravityProbeB.geodetic_within_error`: Twist bookkeeping at r = 7027.4 km gives 6603.9; GP-B measured 6601.8 ± 18.3.
- `GravityProbeB.parts_alone_ruled_out`: Either part alone misses the measurement by more than 100 standard errors.
- `GravityProbeB.polar_average_eight`: And at the eighth points (sin² = 0, ½, 1, ½, …) it is ½ too.
- `GravityProbeB.polar_average_four`: 3 sin² − 1 averaged at the quarter points (sin² = 0, 1, 0, 1) is ½.
- `GravityProbeB.polar_average_pair`: Any orbit point and its quarter-turn partner have sin² + cos² = 1, so a pair averages (3·s − 1 + 3·(1 − s) − 1)/2 = ½ whatever s is: the average is ½ for every set of points closed under quarter turns.
- `GravityProbeB.ppn_geodetic`: In PPN form the geodetic coefficient is γ_PPN + ½; general relativity's γ_PPN = 1 gives 3/2.
- `GravityProbeB.thirds`: The velocity part alone is one third, the space part alone two thirds.
- `GravityProbeB.weak_split`: First-order parts: space 1·x, velocity ½·x, total 3/2·x.

## FusedAlgebra.Topics.GroupsLie (50)

- `GroupsLie.Bl_diag`
- `GroupsLie.Bl_invariant`: Ad-invariance: B([g,f], h) + B(f, [g,h]) = 0, so the light cone Q = 0 is a Lie-theoretic object.
- `GroupsLie.Fld.ext'`
- `GroupsLie.M2.ext'`
- `GroupsLie.T_comp`
- `GroupsLie.T_dilation`: About the wall −1/k every translation is a pure dilation by 1 + ka.
- `GroupsLie.T_eq_circK`
- `GroupsLie.T_scale`: Scaling conjugation moves the dial: T^k_{λa}(λx) = λ·T^{kλ}_a(x).
- `GroupsLie.T_zero_no_fixed`: At k = 0 a nonzero translation has no fixed point, so it is no dilation.
- `GroupsLie.br_antisymm`
- `GroupsLie.br_dial`: Two dial generators 1 + kx and 1 + k'x bracket to a pure translation (k' − k)·∂.
- `GroupsLie.br_dilation_translation`: Translation ∂ and dilation x∂ bracket to a translation: [x∂, ∂] = −∂.
- `GroupsLie.br_jacobi`
- `GroupsLie.bracket_trace`: Brackets are trace-free: they land in sl₂.
- `GroupsLie.circle7_closed`: The circle over F₇ is closed under complex multiplication (the circle group).
- `GroupsLie.circle_counts`: The finite circle: p + 1 points when p ≡ 3 mod 4, p − 1 when p ≡ 1 mod 4.
- `GroupsLie.deriv_numerator`: The b-derivative of F has numerator ∂N·Dn − N·∂Dn = V(a).
- `GroupsLie.f11_cyclic`
- `GroupsLie.f13_cyclic`
- `GroupsLie.f5_cyclic`: (F₅ minus the wall 4, ◯) is a 4-cycle generated by 1.
- `GroupsLie.f7_cyclic`: Mod 7, 1 has order 3 (2 is not a primitive root), but 2 generates all six.
- `GroupsLie.gen_sq`: Cayley–Hamilton for the generator: X² = pX − qI.
- `GroupsLie.invariant_ruler`: Invariant ruler: ∂F/∂b · V(b) = V(F), cross-multiplied by Dn² (so the tick spacing dx/V(x) is carried along by every translation).
- `GroupsLie.landscape_Q`: The landscape point (p, q) is the field 1 + px + qx²; its form is the discriminant.
- `GroupsLie.landscape_no_twist`: On the landscape, a·X and b·X combine by the circle operation the same way in either order.
- `GroupsLie.maM_bracket`: The failure of the matrix circle operation to commute is exactly the commutator AB − BA.
- `GroupsLie.mersenne`: n copies of 1 under the circle operation are the Mersenne number 2ⁿ − 1.
- `GroupsLie.mob_acts`: Acting on a point: the Möbius map of mob a sends b to F(a,b) (cross-multiplied).
- `GroupsLie.mob_circ`: The circle operation itself as 2×2 matrices [[1 + a, a], [0, 1]].
- `GroupsLie.mob_comm`: Landscape matrices commute: the corner cannot twist inside one landscape law.
- `GroupsLie.mob_dial`: On the dial line (q = 0) there is no scale at all: matrices multiply exactly as ◯ₖ.
- `GroupsLie.mob_linear`: mob a = I + a·X: each translation is one step along the generator.
- `GroupsLie.mob_mul`: The law is matrix multiplication up to the scale 1 − qab: mob a · mob b = (1 − qab)·I + (a + b + pab)·X.
- `GroupsLie.mob_mul_entries`: Same statement entry by entry: the product is (1 − qab)·mob(F(a,b)) with F = N/Dn.
- `GroupsLie.mob_zero`
- `GroupsLie.rate_mul`: Rate law: V(a)·V(b) = (1 − qab)²·V(F(a,b)), cross-multiplied.
- `GroupsLie.rate_walls`: The rate splits over its walls: 1 − (u+v)x + uvx² = (1 − ux)(1 − vx).
- `GroupsLie.shift_conj_den`: ... and so do the denominators: (1 + cA)(1 + cB) − qAB = D' + c·N'.
- `GroupsLie.shift_conj_num`: The chart change carries one law to the other. Write ψ(y) = y/(1 + cy) for the way back (ψ undoes φ(x) = x/(1 − cx)). Then F_{p,q}(ψA, ψB) = ψ(F_{p+2c, q+pc+c²}(A, B)): after clearing the factors (1 + cA)(1 + cB), the numerators agree ...
- `GroupsLie.shift_rate`: In the chart y = x/(1 − cx) the rate becomes 1 + (p + 2c)y + (q + pc + c²)y².
- `GroupsLie.theta_circ`: p = 1, q = 0: θ² = θ, and the first slot of (1 + aθ)(1 + bθ) is the lift of a ◯ b.
- `GroupsLie.theta_dual`: p = q = 0: dual numbers, θ² = 0, plain addition.
- `GroupsLie.theta_lift`: (1 + aθ)(1 + bθ) = (1 − qab) + (a + b + pab)θ: the landscape law is θ-multiplication.
- `GroupsLie.theta_tan`: p = 0, q = 1: θ² = −1, the complex numbers; the law is tan addition (a + b)/(1 − ab).
- `GroupsLie.theta_velocity`: p = 0, q = −1: θ² = 1, split-complex numbers; velocity addition (a + b)/(1 + ab).
- `GroupsLie.tmul_assoc`
- `GroupsLie.tmul_comm`
- `GroupsLie.tnorm_mul`
- `GroupsLie.tnorm_one_theta`: The norm of 1 + aθ is the rate V(a).
- `GroupsLie.twist_example`: A 2×2 pair where the twist is visible: [[0,1],[0,0]] and [[0,0],[1,0]].

## FusedAlgebra.Topics.HatPrimes (7)

- `CircleOp.HatPrimes.hats_fib_sq`
- `CircleOp.HatPrimes.hats_first`
- `CircleOp.HatPrimes.period_11`: 11 is 1 mod 5: the counts mod 11 repeat after 10 = 11 − 1 steps.
- `CircleOp.HatPrimes.period_13`: 13 is 3 mod 5: the counts mod 13 repeat after 14 = 13 + 1 steps.
- `CircleOp.HatPrimes.period_7`: 7 is 2 mod 5: the counts mod 7 repeat after 4 steps (4 divides 7 + 1).
- `CircleOp.HatPrimes.refl_first`
- `CircleOp.HatPrimes.union_rule`: The union rule (k = −1 in a + b + k·ab) is the complement of a product.

## FusedAlgebra.Topics.HoloGluing (11)

- `HoloGluing.complement_mul`: ∘₋₁ is "complements multiply" (like the union of independent events).
- `HoloGluing.conformal`: Conformal factors cancel: with d(u,v) = (w v − w u)·ρ u·ρ v (circle: w = tan(θ/2); BTZ: w = e^{2πu/β}), the cross-ratio built from d equals the one built from w.
- `HoloGluing.gluing_cleared`: The gluing law with denominators cleared: (1 − x₁₂) = (1 − x₁)(1 − x₂) when B = (b₁, b₂) is cut at m into B₁ = (b₁, m) and B₂ = (m, b₂).
- `HoloGluing.height_flow`: Height flow: with U = e^t (t the shadow length), odds = U − 1 and adding shadows multiplies U, so the odds compose by ∘₁.
- `HoloGluing.le_circK_one`
- `HoloGluing.monogamy_glued`
- `HoloGluing.odds_glue`: The flip: if X·(1−x) = x and Y·(1−y) = y (odds), then the odds of x ∘₋₁ y is X ∘₁ Y. Stated with u = 1 − x, v = 1 − y and denominators cleared.
- `HoloGluing.of_complement_mul`: So a ∘₋₁ law follows from multiplying complements.
- `HoloGluing.oneSub_numer`: 1 − x(A,B) = (b₁ − a₂)(b₂ − a₁) / denom: the numerator is the shadow ratio f(b₂)/f(b₁) of the Möbius map f(p) = (p − a₁)/(p − a₂) with denominators cleared.
- `HoloGluing.one_le_phase`
- `HoloGluing.ptolemy`: Ptolemy's relation for four points on a line.

## FusedAlgebra.Topics.HoloGluingField (3)

- `HoloGluingField.flip_glue`: The flip φ₋₁(x) = x/(1 − x) carries ∘₋₁ to ∘₁.
- `HoloGluingField.gluing`: The gluing law: x(A, B₁ ∪ B₂) = x(A, B₁) ∘₋₁ x(A, B₂).
- `HoloGluingField.shadow`: Shadow lemma: 1 − x(A,B) = f(b₂)/f(b₁).

## FusedAlgebra.Topics.HyperLadder (80)

- `HyperLadder.Flow.B_assoc`: Every height is associative.
- `HyperLadder.Flow.B_climb`: Heights climb by transport: B_(s+t) = (E_s)₊(B_t).
- `HyperLadder.Flow.B_comm`: Every height is commutative.
- `HyperLadder.Flow.B_id`: 0 is the identity at every height.
- `HyperLadder.Flow.B_one`: If E_1 carries + to a circle operation ◯ (as eᵘ⁺ᵛ − 1 = (eᵘ − 1) ◯ (eᵛ − 1)), then height 1 is ◯.
- `HyperLadder.Flow.B_partner_dist`: Height s + 1 distributes over its partner (E_s)₊(r), where r is the operation ◯ distributes over (rung 1, a + b + 1, for the circle operation). Neighbouring heights do not distribute (`circ_not_dist_add`), so this partner is the right second operation at every height.
- `HyperLadder.Flow.B_zero_height`: Height 0 is addition.
- `HyperLadder.Flow.E_add`
- `HyperLadder.Flow.E_cancel`
- `HyperLadder.Flow.E_cancel'`
- `HyperLadder.Flow.E_fix`
- `HyperLadder.Flow.E_zero`
- `HyperLadder.Flow.exph_lnh`
- `HyperLadder.Flow.lnh_circ`: The hypernatural log turns ◯ (ordinary × in lifted coordinates) into the height-(1 − s) operation: ln_s(XY) = ln_s X +⁽¹⁻ˢ⁾ ln_s Y.
- `HyperLadder.Flow.lnh_compose`: Heights compose: ln_(s+t)(X) = ln_s(1 + ln_t(X)).
- `HyperLadder.Flow.lnh_exph`
- `HyperLadder.Flow.lnh_zero`: ln_s(1) = 0.
- `HyperLadder.arrow_values`
- `HyperLadder.circK_saddK`: Rung 2 at dial k distributes over rung 1 at dial k, for every k ≠ 0 (Pap's generated semiring).
- `HyperLadder.circ_not_dist_add`: Neighbouring heights do not distribute: ◯ (height 1) over + (height 0) fails.
- `HyperLadder.circ_sadd_left`: Rung 2 distributes over rung 1 on the left …
- `HyperLadder.circ_sadd_right`: … and on the right.
- `HyperLadder.fexpK_lift`
- `HyperLadder.fexpK_scale`: Rung 3 at dial k is the k = 1 rung rescaled.
- `HyperLadder.fexpK_succ`: Rung 3 is rung 2 iterated, at every dial k ≠ 0.
- `HyperLadder.fexp_circ_left_fails`: … but not on the left (x^(yz) ≠ x^y · x^z).
- `HyperLadder.fexp_circ_right'`: Rung 3 distributes over rung 2 on the right ((xy)^z = x^z y^z, fused).
- `HyperLadder.ftet_fexp_left_fails`
- `HyperLadder.ftet_fexp_right_fails`: Rung 4 distributes over rung 3 on neither side.
- `HyperLadder.hyp_arrow`
- `HyperLadder.hyp_five`
- `HyperLadder.hyp_four`
- `HyperLadder.hyp_mod3`: A base that leaves 1 mod 3 keeps leaving 1 mod 3 on every rung from 3 up.
- `HyperLadder.hyp_mod7`: A base x with x ≡ 1 (mod 3) and x³ ≡ 1 (mod 7) passes its remainder mod 7 up every rung from 4.
- `HyperLadder.hyp_one_left`: From rung 3 up, 1 is a left absorber of the ordinary ladder: 1 ↑^m b = 1.
- `HyperLadder.hyp_pos`
- `HyperLadder.hyp_right_dist_fails`: Goodstein's ladder: from rung 5 over rung 4 upward, right distributivity fails at 2, 3, 2.
- `HyperLadder.hyp_succ`
- `HyperLadder.hyp_two_mod42`: On every rung from 4 up, H_n(2, y) leaves 16 on division by 42 once y ≥ 3.
- `HyperLadder.hyp_two_two`: Goodstein's 2 # 2 = 4 on every rung from addition up.
- `HyperLadder.lad_arrow`: From rung 3 up the fused ladder is Knuth's arrows seen through x ↦ 1 + x.
- `HyperLadder.lad_assoc_iff`: Only rungs 1 and 2 are associative.
- `HyperLadder.lad_comm_iff`: Only rungs 1 and 2 are commutative.
- `HyperLadder.lad_diag`: One step up at 1 is the rung below on the diagonal: a ⋆ₙ₊₁ 1 = a ⋆ₙ a.
- `HyperLadder.lad_dist_right_at_ones`: The obvious witnesses for rung n + 1 over rung n tell nothing: at 1, 1, 1 both sides agree on every rung (each side is the corner 3 pushed up one rung).
- `HyperLadder.lad_eq_circ`
- `HyperLadder.lad_eq_fexp`
- `HyperLadder.lad_eq_fpent`
- `HyperLadder.lad_eq_ftet`
- `HyperLadder.lad_eq_sadd`
- `HyperLadder.lad_eq_transport`: Every rung is the ordinary hyperoperation transported by x ↦ x + 1 and back.
- `HyperLadder.lad_identity_iff`: Only rung 2 has a two-sided identity in the whole numbers (it is 0). Rung 1's identity is −1, outside Nat.
- `HyperLadder.lad_left_absorb_iff`: 0 is a left absorber exactly from rung 3 up.
- `HyperLadder.lad_left_dist_iff`: Left distributivity of rung n + 1 over rung n holds exactly at the bottom two steps (rung 1 over successor, ◯ over rung 1). From rung 3 over ◯ upward it fails, and one witness serves every rung: 1 ⋆ (0 ⋆' 0) = 1 but (1 ⋆ 0) ⋆' (1 ⋆ 0) = 3.
- `HyperLadder.lad_one_one`: The fixed corner: 1 ⋆ 1 = 3 on every fused rung from 1 up.
- `HyperLadder.lad_one_zero`
- `HyperLadder.lad_right_dist_fails_high`: The same, read on the fused ladder: rung n + 1 over rung n, right side, fails at 1, 2, 1 for n ≥ 4.
- `HyperLadder.lad_right_dist_iff`: Right distributivity of rung n + 1 over rung n holds exactly for n = 0, 1, 2.
- `HyperLadder.lad_right_dist_le_two`: Right distributivity of rung n + 1 over rung n holds at the bottom three steps.
- `HyperLadder.lad_right_dist_three_fails`: …and fails at the fourth (rung 4 over rung 3). Whether it fails at every higher rung is open in Lean; the checks show it failing at rung 5 over rung 4 by tower height.
- `HyperLadder.lad_right_id_iff`: 0 is a right identity exactly from rung 2 up.
- `HyperLadder.lad_succ`: Each rung is the rung below, iterated.
- `HyperLadder.lad_unique`: Uniqueness: any ladder with the fused recursion and the same three starting values (step, a + 1 at rung 1, a from rung 2 up) is `lad`.
- `HyperLadder.lad_zero`
- `HyperLadder.lad_zero_left`: From rung 3 up, 0 is a left absorber of the fused ladder.
- `HyperLadder.lad_zero_one_one`
- `HyperLadder.lad_zero_right`: From rung 2 up, 0 is a right identity.
- `HyperLadder.lift_zero_not_injective`: At k = 0 the lift 1 + kx sends everything to 1, so nothing can be transported along it.
- `HyperLadder.not_transport_of_comm`: No rung other than 1 and 2 is a transport of a commutative operation, by any maps at all. In particular no height of a commutative ladder (Bennett's, or B_s) is fused exponentiation or anything above it.
- `HyperLadder.pow_mod7_cycle`
- `HyperLadder.saddK_lift`: Its lift: 1 + k(a ⊕ₖ b) = (1 + ka) + (1 + kb).
- `HyperLadder.saddK_scale`: Rung 1 at dial k is rung 1 rescaled: k(a ⊕ₖ b) = (ka) + (kb) + 1.
- `HyperLadder.sadd_assoc'`
- `HyperLadder.sadd_comm'`
- `HyperLadder.sadd_not_height`: Rung 1 (a + b + 1) is no height of any flow: every height has identity 0, and rung 1 does not.
- `HyperLadder.tet_two_mod42`: 2 ↑↑ (z + 3) leaves 16 on division by 42.
- `HyperLadder.transport_assoc`: Transport keeps associativity when the maps undo each other on the values that occur.
- `HyperLadder.transport_comm`: Transport keeps commutativity, whatever the maps.
- `HyperLadder.two_pow_mod42`
- `HyperLadder.two_pow_mod42'`

## FusedAlgebra.Topics.Hypersphere (33)

- `Hypersphere.circular_L_v`: Circular orbit under k/r² (k = m = c = 1): γ v² r = 1 and L = γ v r give L·v = 1, so the disc's speed-radius k/L is exactly the speed v of the circular orbit with that L.
- `Hypersphere.deg_eq`
- `Hypersphere.dirac_defect`: Let f = 1/√(1 − α²/κ²) (so f²(κ² − α²) = κ²), x = f − 1 the disc area per lap over 2π, and δ = κx/(1 + x), i.e. δ f = κ(f − 1). Then (κ − δ)² = κ² − α²: Dirac's quantum defect, so E = mc²/√(1 + α²/(n − δ)²) is Dirac's fine structure.
- `Hypersphere.energy_pull_closure`: **Where the 2v comes from** (../orbits/twov): the energy potential E(1 − 1/√(1 − 2x)) has Newton's closure number N = (1 − 5x)/(1 − 2x), which is 1 − 3v².
- `Hypersphere.fock_on_sphere`: The point (2 p0 p, s − p0²)/(s + p0²) has length 1: |2 p0 p|² + (s − p0²)² = (s + p0²)².
- `Hypersphere.gr_geodetic`
- `Hypersphere.gr_orbit_twice_gyro`: **GR's orbit turns twice as much as its gyroscope** (first order).
- `Hypersphere.gr_perihelion`
- `Hypersphere.gyro_split`: **The gyroscope's split** (as on the Gravity Probe B page): (1 − 2x)(1 − v²) = 1 − 3x.
- `Hypersphere.i_mul_j`
- `Hypersphere.i_sq`
- `Hypersphere.isco_two_v`: At the innermost stable orbit r = 6M the orbit's disc radius 2v reaches light speed.
- `Hypersphere.j_mul_i`
- `Hypersphere.kepler_tilt`: With k = m = 1, e² = 1 + 2 E h². Set p0² = −2E (the Fock sphere's radius squared): then (h p0)² + e² = 1, so (h p0, e) is the cosine and sine of the great circle's tilt.
- `Hypersphere.kp_square`: The square has three pieces: Newton's pull E², the cross term 2E·p, and space p².
- `Hypersphere.ks_all`
- `Hypersphere.ks_clock`: Completing the square in p_r (a canonical shift) turns the Kerr-Schild shell into a static one with 1/A = 1 + φ + φ²/(1 − φ), which is 1/(1 − φ): Schwarzschild's clock factor, φ = 2M/r.
- `Hypersphere.ks_newton_cross`
- `Hypersphere.ks_newton_only`
- `Hypersphere.ks_newton_space`
- `Hypersphere.nordstrom_geodetic`
- `Hypersphere.nordstrom_orbit_eq_gyro`: In Nordström's scalar gravity the orbit turns exactly as much as its gyroscope (first order), as the electric orbit does exactly (§5).
- `Hypersphere.nordstrom_perihelion`
- `Hypersphere.norm_mul`: **The norm multiplies** (Euler's four-square identity): what makes the polar split u = r·ω work on S³.
- `Hypersphere.one_plus_three_is_square`: The clock-warp reading's special relativity (1) and energy pull (3) are the double copy's one square.
- `Hypersphere.orbit_fractions_fuse`: The orbit's fractions combine by the circle operation: −6x = (−2x) ◯ (−4v²).
- `Hypersphere.orbit_minus_gyro`: In general: perihelion − geodetic = 3 + 2γ − 2β. It vanishes for Nordström and is 3 for GR.
- `Hypersphere.orbit_split`: **The orbit's split**: (1 − 2x)(1 − 4v²) = 1 − 6x.
- `Hypersphere.photon_sphere_v`: At the photon sphere r = 3M the gyroscope's disc radius v reaches light speed.
- `Hypersphere.six_is_space_plus_square`: The single copy's cross term has strength 1 (Sommerfeld's 1); gravity's has strength 2, from the 2 in 2E·p, so it gives 2² = 4. With space's 2 that is the 6.
- `Hypersphere.six_is_two_one_three`: So 1 − 6x = (1 − 2x)·[(1 − 3v²) − v²]: space, times the energy pull (3v²) and special relativity (v²), which add to 4v² = (2v)².
- `Hypersphere.sommerfeld_eq_thomas`: Hence Sommerfeld's factor 1/(1 − (k/L)²) equals the circular orbit's γ² = 1/(1 − v²): the orbit's advance per lap equals that orbit's Thomas turn per lap.
- `Hypersphere.tangent_sq`: **Every tangent direction at 1 squares to minus its length squared**: on the unit 3-sphere each unit tangent at 1 is an "i" (a whole 2-sphere of them).

## FusedAlgebra.Topics.Infinities (12)

- `Infinities.cantor`
- `Infinities.circOnes_succ`
- `Infinities.evalL_addL`
- `Infinities.half_iter_lower`
- `Infinities.half_iter_upper`
- `Infinities.half_not_poly`: If a half rung stayed under n^k, the whole rung would stay under n^(k·k). Since the whole rung (exponential) outgrows every polynomial, no half rung is polynomial.
- `Infinities.layers_eval`
- `Infinities.single_inj`
- `Infinities.sumOnes_eq`
- `Infinities.wOnes_spec`
- `Infinities.wOnes_zero`
- `Infinities.wkey`

## FusedAlgebra.Topics.InformationFull (29)

- `InformationFull.bsc_from_union`: The binary symmetric channel (s = 2p): the union rule on 2p is the parity rule on p.
- `InformationFull.channel_cascade`: Cascade of [[1−a, a], [b, 1−b]] and [[1−a₂, a₂], [b₂, 1−b₂]]: the new crossover sum is (a + b) ◯₋₁ (a₂ + b₂).
- `InformationFull.channel_det`: The determinant 1 − s multiplies.
- `InformationFull.dary_full`: The complete D-ary tree of depth n: c·[n]_D + 1 = D^n, with D = 1 + c.
- `InformationFull.dary_graft`: D-ary trees by counts: graft (leaves 1 + c·I) — internal nodes compose by the dial c = D − 1.
- `InformationFull.dary_leaves`: ... and leaves multiply: the lift of the dial.
- `InformationFull.flip_weight`: Flipping the weight: the dial at −k is the dial at k read on negatives.
- `InformationFull.full_inner`: It has 2^n − 1 = the circle power of 1 internal nodes: the q-number [n]₂.
- `InformationFull.graft_circ`: Grafting composes internal nodes (decisions) by the circle operation.
- `InformationFull.graft_inner`
- `InformationFull.graft_leaves`
- `InformationFull.kraft_one`
- `InformationFull.leaves_lift`: The lift: leaves = 1 + inner nodes.
- `InformationFull.outer_shan_w`: For any surprise s that turns products into ◯[k], the joint "entropy" Σ p·s(p) of independent sources is (Σr)·S_A + (Σp)·S_B + k·S_A·S_B. k = 0 is the information page's outer-log lemma (Shannon); any k is Tsallis.
- `InformationFull.qexpN_mul`: It turns the dial into multiplication: e(x)·e(y) = e(x ◯_{1/n} y).
- `InformationFull.qexpN_zero`
- `InformationFull.qexp_climb`: Compound interest: e_q(x) − 1 is n circle-operation steps of x/n (rung 2).
- `InformationFull.qlogN_lift`: Its lift is the plain power: 1 + m·ln(x) = x^m.
- `InformationFull.qlogN_mul`: The q-logarithm turns products into the dial with k = m.
- `InformationFull.qlogN_one`
- `InformationFull.qlogN_one_weight`: k = 1 (Tsallis q = 0): ln x = x − 1, the "count minus one".
- `InformationFull.qlogNeg_mul`: The dual logarithm turns products into the dial with the opposite weight.
- `InformationFull.qlog_dual`: Duality: ln_{2−q}(x) = −ln_q(1/x).
- `InformationFull.qlog_neg_one`: k = −1 (Tsallis q = 2): ln x = 1 − 1/x, and it obeys the union rule.
- `InformationFull.qlog_of_root`: The q-exponential inverts it: if y^m = 1 + m·x then ln y = x.
- `InformationFull.shan_map_w`
- `InformationFull.sm_compose`: If R adds over independent sources and E turns + into ◯[k], then E∘R composes by ◯[k]. With R = Rényi of order α and E(x) = (e^{kx} − 1)/k this is Sharma–Mittal H_{α,β}, k = 1 − β.
- `InformationFull.tsallis_engine`: Tsallis' pseudo-additivity, proved as "expectation passes through the dial": S(A × B) = S(A) ◯ₖ S(B) for normalized independent sources.
- `InformationFull.z_cascade`: The Z-channel (b = 0): its crossover composes by the union rule.

## FusedAlgebra.Topics.InformationTheory (52)

- `InfoFused.bec_capacity`: Binary erasure channel: erasures compose by the union rule and capacity 1 − e multiplies exactly.
- `InfoFused.bec_dpi`: BEC: capacity of the cascade ≤ capacity of each link.
- `InfoFused.bsc_cascade`: Binary symmetric channel cascade: the off-diagonal entry of the product of [[1−p, p],[p, 1−p]] and [[1−q, q],[q, 1−q]] is p ◯[−2] q.
- `InfoFused.bsc_cascade_diag`
- `InfoFused.bsc_dpi`: BSC (crossovers taken ≤ 1/2): the cascade's crossover is at least each link's.
- `InfoFused.bsc_polarization`: Polarization 1 − 2p multiplies along a cascade (K1 at k = −2).
- `InfoFused.bsc_stays_below_half`: …and still ≤ 1/2, so it stays on the side where capacity 1 − h(p) falls as p rises. (That monotonicity is real analysis and is not checked here.)
- `InfoFused.campbell`: Campbell's exponential cost: M = Σ pᵢ·D^(t·ℓᵢ) multiplies for concatenated independent sources (K5 on the weights), so M − 1 composes by the plain circle operation.
- `InfoFused.check_node`: Check node: −2·(p ◯[−2] q) = (−2p) ◯ (−2q), the plain circle operation.
- `InfoFused.climb`: K2, climb: the circle power climbs the cube.
- `InfoFused.cube_corners`: Every corner of the cube {0,1}ⁿ: (1 + 1)ⁿ − 1 nonempty corners (K2 at k = 1, a = 1).
- `InfoFused.gini_simpson`: Landmark q = 2 (k = −1): Gini–Simpson G = 1 − Σp² is the chance two draws differ. Collision sums multiply, so G composes by the union rule: "the pair differs" = "A differs or B differs".
- `InfoFused.hamming_perfect`: Hamming codes are perfect: with n = m + r and n + 1 = 2ʳ, the 2ᵐ balls of radius 1 (a corner plus its n neighbours: layers 0 and 1) tile the n-cube.
- `InfoFused.hartley`: Hartley: typical-set sizes multiply, so bits add (log₂ of 2ᵐ·2ⁿ is m + n).
- `InfoFused.hazards_add`: Survival multiplies, so cumulative hazard −log(1 − p) adds (K4).
- `InfoFused.incl_excl3`: Inclusion–exclusion for three independent events falls out of associativity: the signed corners of the circle-operation cube.
- `InfoFused.joint_sums_to_one`: The joint distribution of independent sources is a distribution (K5).
- `InfoFused.kraft_product`: The concatenated code still satisfies Kraft.
- `InfoFused.kraft_slack`: Kraft sums of two prefix codes multiply when you concatenate codewords (K5), and the slack 1 − K composes like failure chances (k = −1).
- `InfoFused.ladder_binary`: c = 1, two letters: a second circK_flip always undoes the first.
- `InfoFused.ladder_four`: c = 1/3, four letters: the depolarizing qubit channel's −4/3.
- `InfoFused.ladder_never_cancel`: c = 0, letters never cancel (huge alphabet, or erasures): the union rule.
- `InfoFused.lengths_add`: Expected length adds for concatenated codes (the k = 0 shadow of Campbell): Σᵢⱼ pᵢrⱼ(ℓᵢ + mⱼ) = Σ pℓ + Σ rm when both sum to 1. Stated on the grid sums.
- `InfoFused.linearize`: K4, linearize: any map that turns products into sums turns ◯[k] into + in the coordinate 1 + k·a. Taking L = log gives log(1 + k·a).
- `InfoFused.lsum_append`
- `InfoFused.lsum_map`
- `InfoFused.n_flips`: n identical flips: 1 − 2·P(odd) = (1 − 2p)ⁿ (K2).
- `InfoFused.odds_to_velocity`: Odds product in ρ coordinates (ρ = (o−1)/(o+1)), with denominators cleared: numerator and denominator of the velocity sum.
- `InfoFused.outer_shan`: K6, outer log.
- `InfoFused.outer_sum`: K5, outer sum: the grid's total is the product of the two edges.
- `InfoFused.parallel_capacity`: Parallel channels: distinguishable messages multiply, capacities add (Hartley).
- `InfoFused.parity3`: Parity of three noisy bits: the odd layers of the circle-operation cube (1·3·3·1).
- `InfoFused.piling_up`: Matsui's piling-up lemma: biases ε = 1/2 − p combine as 2·ε₁·ε₂.
- `InfoFused.power_sums_multiply`: Power sums multiply. With xᵢ = pᵢ^q and yⱼ = rⱼ^q (and (pr)^q = p^q·r^q for real powers), this is Σ (pᵢrⱼ)^q = (Σ pᵢ^q)(Σ rⱼ^q) (K5).
- `InfoFused.qary_cascade`: The q-ary symmetric channel. An error moves a symbol to one of the other letters at random; a second error lands back on the right letter with chance c = 1/(alphabet − 1). Right after two hops: both right, or wrong then sent back. The error composes with weight k = −(1 + c).
- `InfoFused.renyi_additive`: Rényi additivity: Rényi entropy is L(1 + k·S)/k, so it adds where Tsallis combines by the circle operation (K1 + K4). Stated with the 1/k cleared.
- `InfoFused.sdpi_multiplies`: Strong data processing: the contraction coefficient (1 − 2p)² multiplies.
- `InfoFused.series_system`: A series system of n parts, each failing with chance p, fails with chance 1 − (1 − p)ⁿ (K2).
- `InfoFused.shan_append`
- `InfoFused.shan_map`
- `InfoFused.shannon_additive`: Shannon additivity for independent sources, H(X,Y) = H(X) + H(Y), proved for any L with L(xy) = L(x) + L(y) and both distributions summing to 1.
- `InfoFused.shannon_weight`: Landmark q = 1 (k = 0): Shannon, plain addition.
- `InfoFused.support_count`: Landmark q = 0 (k = 1): S₀ = N − 1, support sizes multiply, so N − 1 combines by the circle operation: the N×M grid minus its unit corner.
- `InfoFused.survival`: Survival multiplies (K1 at k = −1).
- `InfoFused.tsallis`: Tsallis pseudo-additivity, S_q(A,B) = S_A + S_B + (1 − q)·S_A·S_B. With k = 1 − q, Tsallis entropy is S = (P − 1)/k where P is the power sum. Stated with the k cleared: if k·S_A = P − 1, k·S_B = R − 1 and k·S_AB = P·R − 1, then k·S_AB = k·(S_A ◯[k] S_B).
- `InfoFused.uniform_tsallis_is_fused_power`: Rung 2: for N equally likely outcomes and k = 1 − q a whole number m, 1 + k·S = Nᵐ, so k·S is the fused power (N − 1)^◯m (K2 with k = 1).
- `InfoFused.union_grows`: Adding an independent part never lowers the chance of a failure.
- `InfoFused.union_is_flipped_circ`: The k = −1 rule is the circle operation read on the negatives (K3).
- `InfoFused.union_rule`: Union rule for independent events: P(A or B) = p + q − pq = p ◯[−1] q.
- `InfoFused.vadd_assoc`
- `InfoFused.variable_node`: Variable node: posterior odds multiply, so o − 1 combines by the circle operation.
- `InfoFused.velocity_corners`: Velocity addition lifts to the circle operation on both corners: (1 + ab) + (a + b) = (1 + a)(1 + b) and (1 + ab) − (a + b) = (1 − a)(1 − b), so (1 + v)/(1 − v), the odds, multiplies.

## FusedAlgebra.Topics.JetFields (46)

- `CircleOp.JetFields.box_minor_zero`: **The minor sums to zero over a periodic box**: the grid version of "∫ (layer 2 of ∇u) = 0" for a 2D map of the torus.
- `CircleOp.JetFields.cayley_hamilton`: Cayley–Hamilton, entrywise: X³ − P·X² + Q·X − R·I = 0.
- `CircleOp.JetFields.cube_depth_one`: At order 1 only layer 1 survives: relative jets just add.
- `CircleOp.JetFields.cube_depth_two`: **Jet order = cube depth.** Three relative 2-jets (no constant term) the circle operation to their sum plus the three pair corners; the triple corner, which would sit at ε³, is gone.
- `CircleOp.JetFields.det2_step`: The 2D step: det(I + hX) = 1 + h·P + h²·Q, the square (1, 2, 1).
- `CircleOp.JetFields.det_diag_cube`: **In principal axes the step's volume change is literally the circle operation:** det(I + h·diag(a,b,c)) = 1 + (ha ◯ hb ◯ hc).
- `CircleOp.JetFields.det_expand`: dDet really is the first-order part of det (the second-order part is dDet Y X).
- `CircleOp.JetFields.det_one_add_smul`: **The volume change of a step is the circle-operation cube.** det(I + h∇u) = 1 + h·P + h²·Q + h³·R.
- `CircleOp.JetFields.det_vorticity_strain`: **Layer 3, the far corner, is strain self-amplification plus vortex stretching.** For P = 0: 8R = det T + ω·Tω, that is, R = det S + ¼ ω·Sω.
- `CircleOp.JetFields.diag_layers`: The layers of a diagonal gradient are the cube's layers of (a, b, c).
- `CircleOp.JetFields.diff_leibniz`: **Discrete Leibniz with the corner.** If f(x+h) = f₀ + h·f' and g(x+h) = g₀ + h·g', then (fg)(x+h) = f₀g₀ + h·(the ε-part of the product in R[ε]/(ε² − hε)). The forward difference of a product is that ε-part.
- `CircleOp.JetFields.dual_inverse`: The dual numbers have no wall: 1 + aε always has the inverse 1 − aε.
- `CircleOp.JetFields.dual_units_add`: With weight 0 (dual numbers, 1-jets) the corner is dropped: relative slopes add. This is the logarithmic derivative of a product.
- `CircleOp.JetFields.e2_expand`: dE2 really is the first-order part of Q.
- `CircleOp.JetFields.e2_vorticity_strain`: **Layer 2 is rotation against strain.** For P = 0: 8Q = 2|ω|² − |T|², that is, Q = ¼|ω|² − ½|S|².
- `CircleOp.JetFields.eps_sq`: ε² = kε: the corner weight is the square of the infinitesimal.
- `CircleOp.JetFields.jma_lift`: The circle operation of jets is multiplication through 1 + x, as everywhere else.
- `CircleOp.JetFields.leibniz2`: The jet product is the product of functions: second-order Leibniz, (fg)'' = f''g + 2f'g' + fg'' (with f'' = 2·c2).
- `CircleOp.JetFields.minor_is_divergence`: **The corner-kept minor is an exact discrete divergence:** minor = Δ₁(u·Δ₂v) − Δ₂(u·Δ₁v).
- `CircleOp.JetFields.nilpotent_inverse`: **No wall in jet space.** A relative jet ν has the finite way back −ν + ν² (the geometric series stops because ε³ = 0).
- `CircleOp.JetFields.ofTwoPt_twoPt`
- `CircleOp.JetFields.off_wall_inverse`: Off the wall, the inverse under the circle operation with dial k is −a/(1 + ka).
- `CircleOp.JetFields.re2_frozen`: **2D restricted Euler is frozen**: −2A² + tr(A²)·I = 0 for every traceless 2×2 gradient, so the self-advection is cancelled exactly by the pressure.
- `CircleOp.JetFields.re_conserved`: **The discriminant is conserved**: along restricted Euler, d/dt (27R² + 4Q³) = 54R·R' + 12Q²·Q' = 0. 27R² + 4Q³ is minus the discriminant of the cube polynomial λ³ + Qλ − R; its zero set is the Vieillefosse tail.
- `CircleOp.JetFields.re_dQ`: **Layer 2 is driven by layer 3**: 3·dQ/dt = 9R (so dQ/dt = 3R).
- `CircleOp.JetFields.re_dR`: **Layer 3 is driven by layer 2 squared**: 3·dR/dt = −2Q² (so dR/dt = −⅔Q²).
- `CircleOp.JetFields.re_traceless`: The flow keeps the gradient incompressible.
- `CircleOp.JetFields.shift_is_corner`: The shift is the corner: S₁Δ₂v = Δ₂v + Δ₁Δ₂v (shift = 1 + Δ).
- `CircleOp.JetFields.sq_nonneg_int`: A square is never negative (core Lean has no ready-made lemma for it).
- `CircleOp.JetFields.sumTo_congr`
- `CircleOp.JetFields.sumTo_sub`
- `CircleOp.JetFields.sumTo_telescope`
- `CircleOp.JetFields.sym_e2_nonpos`: So on the integers (and likewise any ordered ring) Q ≤ 0 for a pure strain: the strain's second layer only ever dissipates.
- `CircleOp.JetFields.sym_e2_sumsq`: For a pure traceless strain, −2Q is the sum of the squares of its entries.
- `CircleOp.JetFields.sym_tr_cube`: For a symmetric traceless S (a pure strain), tr(S³) = 3·det S.
- `CircleOp.JetFields.tr_cube_2d`: In 2D an incompressible gradient has no third invariant: tr(X³) = 0.
- `CircleOp.JetFields.tr_cube_traceless`: For an incompressible gradient, tr(X³) = 3R.
- `CircleOp.JetFields.tr_sq_traceless`: For an incompressible gradient, tr(X²) = −2Q.
- `CircleOp.JetFields.twoPt_mul`: **The weighted dual numbers are two points.** Reading an element at x and at x + k turns the twisted product into the plain product at each point.
- `CircleOp.JetFields.twoPt_ofTwoPt`
- `CircleOp.JetFields.twoPt_weight_zero`: At weight 0 the two points coincide: the secant has become the tangent.
- `CircleOp.JetFields.units_circ`: With weight 1 the units multiply by the plain circle operation.
- `CircleOp.JetFields.units_circK`: **Units multiply by the circle operation**: (1 + aε)(1 + bε) = 1 + (a ◯[k] b)ε.
- `CircleOp.JetFields.wall_no_inverse`: **The wall is a zero at the second point.** If 1 + ka = 0, the unit 1 + aε has no inverse in R[ε]/(ε² − kε): read at x + k, it is 0.
- `CircleOp.JetFields.wmul_assoc`
- `CircleOp.JetFields.wmul_comm`

## FusedAlgebra.Topics.JetRiver (6)

- `CircleOp.JetRiver.e2_addSmul`: **The gradient of the Q layer.** Q(X + tY) = Q(X) + t·(tr X · tr Y − tr(XY)) + t²·Q(Y). The linear term is ⟨(tr X)I − Xᵀ, Y⟩: the vector law of the river (momentum constraint) is the divergence of (tr S)I − S.
- `CircleOp.JetRiver.friedmann_e2`: **A uniform ball:** every rate equal (v = H r), Q = 3H². With Q = 8πGρ this is Friedmann's equation H² = 8πGρ/3.
- `CircleOp.JetRiver.river_e2`: The river cell's second layer: Q = 2ab + b².
- `CircleOp.JetRiver.river_e2_gauss`: **Q is the derivative of r·v², over r².** With b·r = v (sideways rate = v/r) and a = v': r²·Q = v² + 2 r v v' = (r v²)'. So Q = 0 is r v² = constant.
- `CircleOp.JetRiver.river_e2_neg`: Inflow or outflow gives the same Q (the layer is quadratic).
- `CircleOp.JetRiver.vacuum_e2`: **Empty space:** if the radial rate is minus half the sideways rate (v ∝ r^(-1/2), that is v² = 2M/r), the cell's second layer vanishes.

## FusedAlgebra.Topics.KnotTheory (44)

- `KnotTheory.alexander_T2`: T(2, n): (1 + t)·[n]_(−t) = 1 − (−t)ⁿ, so for odd n, Δ(T(2,n)) = [n]_(−t).
- `KnotTheory.closeD_circ`: The "circle-operation value" (called the "mult-add value" before 1.0) P − 1 of a closed diagram turns connected sum into ◯ exactly. (A relabelling: it is the lift of P(K # L) = P(K)·P(L).)
- `KnotTheory.closeD_tsum`: The lift law is connected sum: closing a sum gives the product of the closures.
- `KnotTheory.closeD_wall`: The wall: a tangle whose closure has bracket 0 absorbs everything added to it.
- `KnotTheory.closeN_vsum`: So the numerator closure is the lift for stacking.
- `KnotTheory.conway_add`: Conway's fraction is additive under tangle sum (the dial at weight d = 0).
- `KnotTheory.conway_stack`: ... and 1/F is additive under stacking.
- `KnotTheory.conway_twist`: Conway's fraction F = A²·g/f at A⁴ = −1: a crossing adds 1.
- `KnotTheory.hstep_det`: Both steps keep the determinant p·s − q·r of any two fractions (they are in SL(2, ℤ)).
- `KnotTheory.hstep_inv`: Twist forward, back: the steps are invertible.
- `KnotTheory.kink`: One crossing closed up is a kink: ⟨D(X)⟩ = −A⁻³.
- `KnotTheory.lift_is_closing`: The lift 1 + d·x is the closure, divided by the [0]-coefficient.
- `KnotTheory.loop_zero`: Where the walls merge the loop value is 0 ...
- `KnotTheory.mirror_q`: Mirror image A ↦ A⁻¹ sends q to 1/q: q(A)·q(A⁻¹) = 1.
- `KnotTheory.poly_circ`: The same for any multiplicative knot polynomial (Jones, Alexander, HOMFLY).
- `KnotTheory.potts_Q`: The Potts number of the loop: Q = d² = t + 2 + t⁻¹ with t = A⁻⁴.
- `KnotTheory.q_one`: ... and the tangle q is 1 (the counting point of the dial).
- `KnotTheory.qnum_mul`: [pq]_t = [p]_t·[q]_(t^p) (the q-number behind Δ(T(p,q)) = [q]_(t^p)/[q]_t).
- `KnotTheory.ratio_dial`: In x = g/f, adding tangles is the circle operation with weight d.
- `KnotTheory.rational_fractions`: The seven rational knots of the page: 3_1, 4_1, 5_1, 5_2, 6_1, 6_2, 6_3.
- `KnotTheory.stack_dial`: Stacking is the same dial in z = f/g.
- `KnotTheory.stack_step_y`: One crossing stacked: 1/y ↦ q⁻¹·(1/y) + 1 (the same step at 1/q, seen through 1/y).
- `KnotTheory.stack_zero_zero`: A loop on its own: [0] stacked on [0] is d·[0].
- `KnotTheory.step_about_wall`: The q-step is a rotation (|q| = 1) or scaling about its finite wall c = 1/(1 − q).
- `KnotTheory.tangle_cinquefoil`: Five crossings closed on top: the cinquefoil (mirror of QAcross.Knots' 5₁).
- `KnotTheory.tangle_figEight`: h v h h closed on top (fraction 5/2) is the figure-eight of QAcross.Knots.
- `KnotTheory.tangle_figEight_D`: h h v v closed at the sides (fraction 2/5) is the figure-eight again.
- `KnotTheory.tangle_trefoil`: Three crossings side by side, closed on top: the right-handed trefoil, the mirror of the left trefoil of QAcross.Knots (bracket A⁻⁷ − A⁻³ − A⁵).
- `KnotTheory.tsum_assoc`
- `KnotTheory.tsum_comm`
- `KnotTheory.tsum_infT`: The second wall, x = ∞: adding [∞] leaves only [∞], carrying the closure ⟨D(S)⟩.
- `KnotTheory.tsum_lift`: The homogeneous form of the dial: the [∞]-part of a sum, times d, plus its [0]-part, is the product (f₁ + d g₁)(f₂ + d g₂): the weighted lift 1 + d·x with x = g/f, cleared of fractions.
- `KnotTheory.tsum_zeroT`: [0] is the identity of tangle addition.
- `KnotTheory.twist_closeD`: Closing [n] gives n kinks: (−A⁻³)ⁿ.
- `KnotTheory.twist_formula`: n twists: [n] = Aⁿ·[0] + Aⁿ·A⁻²·[n]_q·[∞].
- `KnotTheory.twist_step_y`: One crossing added: y ↦ q·y + 1, with y = A²·g/f.
- `KnotTheory.twists_close`: At a root of unity qᵐ = 1 the polygon closes: m twists return y to 0.
- `KnotTheory.twists_on_circle`: So the twists sit on a circle around the wall: [n]_q = c − qⁿ·c.
- `KnotTheory.vstep_det`
- `KnotTheory.vstep_inv`
- `KnotTheory.vsum_eq_swap`: Stacking is adding, turned a quarter: vsum = swap ∘ tsum ∘ swap.
- `KnotTheory.walls_merge`: The twist matrix [[A, 0], [A⁻¹, −A⁻³]] has two eigenvalues, A and −A⁻³ (the two walls); they coincide exactly when A⁴ = −1.
- `KnotTheory.weight_is_q_minus_one`: In y = A²·x the dial weight d becomes d·A⁻² = q − 1.
- `KnotTheory.y_scale`: Rescaling by A² turns the d-dial into the (q − 1)-dial.

## FusedAlgebra.Topics.Landscape (27)

- `LandscapeMap.assoc_den`
- `LandscapeMap.assoc_num`: Associativity, projective form. Composing (a ⊕ b) ⊕ c with fractions N/D gives numerator N + c·D + p·N·c and denominator D − q·N·c; the other bracketing is symmetric. The two numerators are equal and the two denominators are equal, as polynomials.
- `LandscapeMap.channel_floor`
- `LandscapeMap.chi_y_line`
- `LandscapeMap.chi_y_touch`
- `LandscapeMap.den_comm`
- `LandscapeMap.den_zero`
- `LandscapeMap.dial_is_multadd`: the dial: k·(a ◯[k] b) = (k a) ◯ (k b); every k ≠ 0 on the straight line is the circle operation in other units
- `LandscapeMap.discriminant`: the parabola: the discriminant of the walls is (u − v)²
- `LandscapeMap.inverse_num`: inverse: F(a, −a/(1 + p a)) = 0, cross-multiplied: the numerator vanishes
- `LandscapeMap.kdv_phase_shift`: the KdV soliton corner: A(f − 1) with f − 1 = e₁ + e₂ + A e₁e₂ is the plain circle operation of A e₁ and A e₂ (each wave moved by its phase shift log A)
- `LandscapeMap.lens_factor_u`: the lift actually factors: D − u N = (1 − u a)(1 − u b), and the same with v
- `LandscapeMap.lens_factor_v`
- `LandscapeMap.lens_lift`: the lift, cross-multiplied: (D − u N)(1 − v a)(1 − v b) = (D − v N)(1 − u a)(1 − u b)
- `LandscapeMap.multadd_point`: the circle operation is the point u = −1, v = 0: walls at −1 and at infinity
- `LandscapeMap.num_comm`: commutativity, numerator and denominator separately
- `LandscapeMap.num_zero`: identity: F(a, 0) = a / 1
- `LandscapeMap.parabola_additive`: the parabola itself (a double wall, u = v): the lens N(x) = x/(1 − u x) turns the law into addition. Cross-multiplied: N·(1 − u a)(1 − u b) = (a(1 − u b) + b(1 − u a))·(D − u N), with D − u N = (1 − u a)(1 − u b).
- `LandscapeMap.parabola_den`
- `LandscapeMap.parity_reaches_floor`: at L = 2 (parity) the floor is reached exactly at full error: eigenvalue −1
- `LandscapeMap.rate_factors`
- `LandscapeMap.riccati_rate`
- `LandscapeMap.rung_discriminant`
- `LandscapeMap.rung_equivalent`: so the unit-free shape p²/(p² − 4q) of rung s is s: (4s)² = s·(16 s)
- `LandscapeMap.units_den`
- `LandscapeMap.units_num`: rescaling by λ moves a point along a parabola through the origin: (p, q) ↦ (λ p, λ² q)
- `LandscapeMap.velocity_point`: velocity addition is u = 1, v = −1: walls at +1 and −1 (the speed of light)

## FusedAlgebra.Topics.LeeYang (39)

- `LeeYang.Mat2.ext'`
- `LeeYang.cayley_hamilton`: Cayley–Hamilton for 2×2 matrices: x² = tr(x)·x − det(x)·I.
- `LeeYang.colourings_on_wall`: Colourings sit on the wall: v = −1 gives t·(Q + v) = v with t = −1/(Q − 1), i.e. 1 + k·t = 0 for k = Q − 1. (Stated without division: (Q − 1)·t = −1 and t(Q − 1) = −1.)
- `LeeYang.cycle_chromatic`: Proper colourings: v = −1. The chromatic polynomial of the n-cycle.
- `LeeYang.edge_gas`: One edge of the dial gas: the two-site lattice gas with pair weight k is the lift of the two fugacities combined by the circle operation (called weighted mult-add before 1.0) with dial k.
- `LeeYang.edge_gas_free`: At k = 1 (q = 2) the two sites are independent: the gas factors.
- `LeeYang.edge_gas_hardcore`: At k = 0 (q = 1) the two sites exclude each other: hard core.
- `LeeYang.even_pow_of_tr_zero`: Even powers of a traceless 2×2 matrix are scalar: x^(2m) = (−det x)^m · I.
- `LeeYang.even_ring_on_wall`: On the wall an even ring is 2·(z(1 − k))^m, not zero (unless k = 1).
- `LeeYang.ising_ring_transfer`: For Q = 2 (Ising) the formula is the trace of the transfer matrix [[1+v, 1], [1, 1+v]].
- `LeeYang.neg_one_pow_cases`
- `LeeYang.odd_pow_of_tr_zero`: With trace 0, every odd power is a multiple of x: x^(2m+1) = (−det x)^m · x.
- `LeeYang.odd_ring_vanishes_on_wall`: **The wall theorem for rings.** The dial gas on a ring of 2m + 1 sites, tr(Tⁿ), vanishes whenever 1 + k·z = 0, that is at z = −1/k, for every weight k.
- `LeeYang.one_pow'`
- `LeeYang.parity_never_zero`
- `LeeYang.pentagon_count`: The 5-ring: 1 + 5z + (5 + 5k)z² + (5k + 5k²)z³ + 5k³z⁴ + k⁵z⁵.
- `LeeYang.pottsRing_lift`: **The Potts ring is the weighted lift of tⁿ.** With t = v/(Q + v) and k = Q − 1: Z = (Q + v)ⁿ·(1 + k·tⁿ).
- `LeeYang.ringT_det`
- `LeeYang.ringT_tr`: Its trace is the weighted lift of z.
- `LeeYang.ring_at_parity`: q = −1 (parity, k = −2): the ring gives (−1)ⁿ(2ⁿ − 2), the signed number of acyclic orientations of the cycle.
- `LeeYang.ring_at_q2_even`
- `LeeYang.ring_disc`: The ring's eigenvalue discriminant: (1 + kz)² − 4z(k − 1) = k²z² + (4 − 2k)z + 1. Its zeros are the ends of the zero arc (Yang–Lee edges). At k = 1 it is (1 + z)².
- `LeeYang.ring_disc_free`
- `LeeYang.ring_edges_vieta`: The two edge points z₁, z₂ of the zero arc satisfy k²·z₁z₂ = 1 (Vieta), so in ζ = k·z they are a reciprocal pair, symmetric under ζ ↦ 1/ζ (the up–down flip of the spins).
- `LeeYang.ring_family_edge`: c = 4 is the edge of the zero arc (the eigenvalue discriminant).
- `LeeYang.ring_family_wall`: c = 0 is the wall, doubled: (1 + kz)².
- `LeeYang.ring_family_zeta`: In ζ = k·z the family reads k·(ζ² + 1) + (2k − c(k − 1))·ζ = 0 after multiplying by k: so ζ + 1/ζ = c(1 − 1/k) − 2 runs from −2 (the wall, c = 0) to 2 − 4/k (c = 4).
- `LeeYang.ring_zero_at_q0`: q = 0 (the "or" point, k = −1) is a zero of every ring.
- `LeeYang.ring_zero_at_q1`: q = 1 (plain addition, k = 0) is a zero of every ring with at least one site.
- `LeeYang.ring_zero_at_q2_odd`: q = 2 (the circle operation, k = 1) is a zero exactly for odd rings.
- `LeeYang.ring_zero_family`: The ring's zero family. A ring of n sites vanishes exactly when the two eigenvalues have ratio e^{iφ} with φ = π(2j+1)/n, which is (1 + kz)² = c·z(k − 1) with c = 4cos²(φ/2) in [0, 4]. Each c gives a quadratic in z:
- `LeeYang.square_count`: The 4-ring: 1 + 4z + (2 + 4k)z² + 4k²z³ + k⁴z⁴.
- `LeeYang.tr_odd_pow_zero`: Odd powers of a traceless 2×2 matrix are traceless.
- `LeeYang.triangle_count`: The transfer matrix counts correctly: the 3-ring (triangle) is 1 + 3z + 3k·z² + k³·z³ (each pair of occupied sites shares an edge).
- `LeeYang.triangle_disc`: The quadratic's discriminant: (3 − k)² − 4k² = 3(1 − k)(3 + k). It is ≤ 0 for k ≥ 1 (the two other zeros are a conjugate pair with product 1/k², on the circle |z| = 1/k: Lee–Yang), and > 0 for −3 < k < 1 (real zeros).
- `LeeYang.triangle_disc_nonpos`
- `LeeYang.triangle_factor`: The triangle splits off the wall: (1 + kz) times a quadratic.
- `LeeYang.two_pow_ge_four`: The parity point is never a zero of a ring with two or more sites (over Int).
- `LeeYang.two_rings`: Two separate rings together: their weights multiply, which in the lift is the circle operation of the two tⁿ, at the dial k = Q − 1.

## FusedAlgebra.Topics.LeeYangCluster (10)

- `LeeYangCluster.beraha10_not_int`: n = 10: k = 1 + golden ratio, k² = 3k − 1, has no integer solution.
- `LeeYangCluster.beraha12_not_int`: n = 12: k = 1 + √3, k² = 2k + 2, has no integer solution.
- `LeeYangCluster.beraha5_not_int`: n = 5: k = golden ratio, k² = k + 1, has no integer solution.
- `LeeYangCluster.beraha7_not_int`: n = 7: k = 1 + 2cos(2π/7) is a root of k³ − 2k² − k + 1, which has no integer root.
- `LeeYangCluster.beraha8_not_int`: n = 8: k = 1 + √2, k² = 2k + 1, has no integer solution.
- `LeeYangCluster.beraha9_not_int`: n = 9: k = 1 + 2cos(2π/9) is a root of k³ − 3k² + 3, which has no integer root.
- `LeeYangCluster.circK_units`: Units: rescaling the variable by λ turns weight λ·k into weight k.
- `LeeYangCluster.leverage_is_compounding`: 2× leverage: weight 2 on unlevered returns is the plain circle operation (called mult-add before 1.0) on levered returns.
- `LeeYangCluster.small_of_mul`: If a·b = c with c ≠ 0, then |a| ≤ |c|.
- `LeeYangCluster.units_bend`: The same move off the axis: the q-coordinate scales by λ², so k²/q is what stays.

## FusedAlgebra.Topics.LeeYangSpill (10)

- `LeeYangSpill.border_orbit`: The border (k = 2u, q = u²): n steps of x from 0 land at n·x / (1 − (n − 1)·u·x). Stated for a general t in place of n, cross-multiplied: the step takes the t-th point to the (t+1)-th. The denominator is linear in t: it passes infinity once, then fills toward the double wall −1/u like 1/t, forever.
- `LeeYangSpill.slowest_step`: On the turning side the slowest step is bounded below by the distance to the border: 4q·(1 + k·a + q·a²) ≥ 4q − k² for every a.
- `LeeYangSpill.sq_nonneg'`: A square is never negative.
- `LeeYangSpill.step_disc`: The step's discriminant: trace² − 4·det = x²·(k² − 4q), the same sign for every step size.
- `LeeYangSpill.step_gap`: One step moves a by x·(1 + k·a + q·a²), over the denominator 1 − q·a·x.
- `LeeYangSpill.tan_fourth`
- `LeeYangSpill.tan_square`: Tangent addition (k = 0, q = 1) with step 1: a ↦ (a + 1)/(1 − a). Its matrix [[1,1],[−1,1]] squared is [[0,2],[−2,0]] and to the fourth is −4·I, a scalar: every orbit spills through infinity and returns to its start in exactly four steps (tan(θ + π/4)).
- `LeeYangSpill.turning_never_stalls`: On the turning side the step never stalls: if q > 0 and 4q > k², the step size is positive.
- `LeeYangSpill.wall_square`: Completing the square: the step's size is a square plus the distance to the border.
- `LeeYangSpill.walls_factor`: Two walls: when k² − 4q = m², the step size factors through the two walls, where it is 0.

## FusedAlgebra.Topics.LinearAlgebra (51)

- `LinearAlgebraCirc.M2.Dk_hom`: The dial homomorphism, for every weight k at once (any commutative ring).
- `LinearAlgebraCirc.M2.add_one_eq_one`
- `LinearAlgebraCirc.M2.cayley_eq`: The Cayley transform of a skew matrix is the rational rotation [[p, q], [−q, p]] with p = (1 − s²)/(1 + s²), q = 2s/(1 + s²).
- `LinearAlgebraCirc.M2.cayley_is_msub`: The Cayley transform is circle-operation subtraction of S from its transpose: I + (Sᵀ ⊟ S).
- `LinearAlgebraCirc.M2.cayley_on_circle`: The Cayley point lies on the unit circle (the matrix is a rotation).
- `LinearAlgebraCirc.M2.cayley_on_wall_circle`: The same point, shifted by −1, satisfies the k = 1 rotation condition 2x + x² + y² = 0: it lies on the circle centred at the wall −1.
- `LinearAlgebraCirc.M2.circK_zero_weight`: At weight 0 the corner is gone: plain matrix addition.
- `LinearAlgebraCirc.M2.circ_assoc`
- `LinearAlgebraCirc.M2.circ_inverse`: Off the wall (det(I + A) ≠ 0) every matrix has a circle inverse: (I + A)⁻¹ − I.
- `LinearAlgebraCirc.M2.circ_not_comm`: Two by two, a matrix never commutes for free: order matters for matrix ◯.
- `LinearAlgebraCirc.M2.circ_zero`
- `LinearAlgebraCirc.M2.coupling_sets_weight`: det(I + M) − 1 = m₁₁ ◯[w] m₂₂ whenever m₁₂·m₂₁ = (1 − w)·m₁₁·m₂₂.
- `LinearAlgebraCirc.M2.det_mul`
- `LinearAlgebraCirc.M2.det_one_add_skew`
- `LinearAlgebraCirc.M2.det_one_add_smul`: The circle-operation cube of a matrix: det(I + kA) = 1 + k·e₁ + k²·e₂ + k³·e₃.
- `LinearAlgebraCirc.M2.diagonal_is_circ`: No coupling (diagonal): the circle operation, weight 1.
- `LinearAlgebraCirc.M2.ext'`
- `LinearAlgebraCirc.M2.lift`
- `LinearAlgebraCirc.M2.msub_undo`: ⊟ undoes ◯: (X ⊟ A) ◯ A = X, off the wall.
- `LinearAlgebraCirc.M2.msub_undo_W`
- `LinearAlgebraCirc.M2.mul_inv`
- `LinearAlgebraCirc.M2.orth_lift`
- `LinearAlgebraCirc.M2.rank_one_is_add`: Rank one (m₁₂m₂₁ = m₁₁m₂₂, determinant 0): plain addition, weight 0.
- `LinearAlgebraCirc.M2.rotation_condition`: For X = [[x, −y], [y, x]], Xᵀ ◯[k] X is (2x + k(x² + y²))·I.
- `LinearAlgebraCirc.M2.rotation_condition_flat`: k = 0 drops the corner: the condition becomes Xᵀ + X = 0 (skew, the Lie algebra).
- `LinearAlgebraCirc.M2.shifted_det_hom`: k = 1: the shifted determinant det(I + A) − 1 turns matrix ◯ into scalar ◯.
- `LinearAlgebraCirc.M2.tr_add`
- `LinearAlgebraCirc.M2.wlift`
- `LinearAlgebraCirc.M2.zero_circ`
- `LinearAlgebraCirc.M3.Dk_hom`: The dial homomorphism, for every weight k at once (any commutative ring).
- `LinearAlgebraCirc.M3.box_cube`: The box picture: stretching the unit cube to (1 + a)(1 + b)(1 + c) adds 3 slabs, 3 beams and 1 corner block: 1, 3, 3, 1.
- `LinearAlgebraCirc.M3.circ_assoc`
- `LinearAlgebraCirc.M3.circ_zero`
- `LinearAlgebraCirc.M3.det_mul`
- `LinearAlgebraCirc.M3.det_one_add`: k = 1: det(I + A) = 1 + e₁ + e₂ + e₃.
- `LinearAlgebraCirc.M3.det_one_add_smul`: The circle-operation cube of a matrix: det(I + kA) = 1 + k·e₁ + k²·e₂ + k³·e₃.
- `LinearAlgebraCirc.M3.ext'`
- `LinearAlgebraCirc.M3.lift`
- `LinearAlgebraCirc.M3.orth_lift`
- `LinearAlgebraCirc.M3.tr_add`
- `LinearAlgebraCirc.M3.triangular_eigen`: For a triangular matrix (eigenvalues on the diagonal) the shifted determinant is the circle operation of the eigenvalues, as the trace is their sum.
- `LinearAlgebraCirc.M3.wlift`
- `LinearAlgebraCirc.circ_is_zoom`: Combining with a by the circle operation is zooming by 1 + a about the wall −1.
- `LinearAlgebraCirc.fl_fixes_wall`
- `LinearAlgebraCirc.fl_sadd`
- `LinearAlgebraCirc.fl_scale`
- `LinearAlgebraCirc.kdv_two_soliton`: The KdV two-soliton: with the Cauchy-type coupling m² = 4k₁k₂/(k₁ + k₂)²·E₁E₂, det(I + M) is Hirota's τ = 1 + E₁ + E₂ + ((k₁ − k₂)/(k₁ + k₂))²·E₁E₂.
- `LinearAlgebraCirc.mono_compose`: Composing monomial maps multiplies exponent matrices (first row shown; the second row is the same statement with the other row of exponents).
- `LinearAlgebraCirc.mono_hom`: Monomial maps are the linear maps of rate space: they turn ◯ into ◯.
- `LinearAlgebraCirc.pow_mul''`
- `LinearAlgebraCirc.rotation_circle`: The weighted rotation condition is a circle through 0 centred at the weighted wall −1/k.

## FusedAlgebra.Topics.LogicSets (35)

- `LogicSets.absorb_defect`: The absorption defect: a ◯[−1] (ab) = a + ab(1 − a).
- `LogicSets.absorb_on_idem`: Absorption, both ways, on idempotents: a ∨ (a ∧ b) = a and a ∧ (a ∨ b) = a.
- `LogicSets.and_keeps_idem`: AND (the product, the corner cell) keeps idempotents for free.
- `LogicSets.bits_closed_iff`: On the integers: 1 ◯[k] 1 is a truth value exactly at k = −1 and k = −2.
- `LogicSets.circ_definable`: The circle operation is definable from the fused exponent alone: b ◯ c = d exactly when fexp (fexp a b) c = fexp a d for every a (the tower law).
- `LogicSets.dial_table`: The dial's table on 0 and 1, for every weight k. Only the corner moves: 1 ◯[k] 1 = 2 + k.
- `LogicSets.dist_defect`: The distributivity defect: a(b ◯[k] c) − (ab ◯[k] ac) = k·abc(1 − a).
- `LogicSets.dist_on_idem`: On an idempotent, AND distributes over every dial value (so over OR and over XOR).
- `LogicSets.fexp_one_inj`: The fused exponent at base 1 is 2^(n+1) − 1, so it tells exponents apart.
- `LogicSets.fold_or_trues`: n trues folded with OR give 1: [n]_q at q = 0.
- `LogicSets.fold_xor_trues`: n trues folded with XOR give n mod 2: [n]_q at q = −1.
- `LogicSets.hamS_bits`: On truth values every Hamacher t-conorm with γ ≠ 0 is OR.
- `LogicSets.hamS_is_lens`: Every Hamacher t-conorm is a landscape law, at p = γ − 2, q = 1 − γ.
- `LogicSets.hamS_miss`: The miss of a Hamacher t-conorm does not depend on γ: 1 − S_γ(a, b) = (1 − a)(1 − b)/(1 + (γ − 1)ab).
- `LogicSets.hamS_odds`: **Hamacher is the dial on odds.** odds(S_γ(a, b)) = odds a ◯[γ] odds b.
- `LogicSets.hamT_mirror`: De Morgan: Hamacher's t-norm is the mirror of its t-conorm, T(a, b) = 1 − S(1 − a, 1 − b).
- `LogicSets.ham_one`: γ = 1 is the probabilistic sum, the dial at k = −1 (no bend).
- `LogicSets.ham_two`: γ = 2 is the Einstein sum (a + b)/(1 + ab): relativity's point (0, −1).
- `LogicSets.ham_zero_cusp`: γ = 0 sits at (−2, 1), on the border parabola p² = 4q (a double wall).
- `LogicSets.hamacher_line`: And they all sit on the line p + q = −1.
- `LogicSets.idem_defect`: **The idempotent defect.** For a² = a and b² = b, (a ◯[k] b)² − (a ◯[k] b) = (k + 1)(k + 2)·ab.
- `LogicSets.lift_iso`: The circle operation alone is multiplication in disguise: the lift x ↦ x + 1 carries (Nat, ◯, 0) onto the positive numbers under ×, one-to-one.
- `LogicSets.one_time_pad`: XOR's wall sits at 1/2: a fair key bit hides any message bit (Shannon's one-time pad). This is the library's `circK_wall` at k = −2, read as a truth value.
- `LogicSets.or_from_xor_and`: OR is XOR corrected by AND: a ∨ b = (a ⊕ b) ⊕ (a ∧ b) on idempotents.
- `LogicSets.or_keeps_idem`: At k = −1 (OR) idempotents stay idempotent.
- `LogicSets.or_self`: OR is idempotent exactly on idempotents: a ◯[−1] a − a = a − a².
- `LogicSets.or_wall`: OR's wall is "true": 1 absorbs everything under ◯[−1].
- `LogicSets.order_iff`: The order of the Boolean lattice, read on the dial: on idempotents a ◯[−1] b = b exactly when a·b = a.
- `LogicSets.sadd_definable`: Shifted add (rung 0) is definable from the fused exponent and the circle operation: b + c + 1 = d exactly when fexp a b ◯ fexp a c = fexp a d for every a.
- `LogicSets.succ_and_add`: With 0 in hand the successor is shifted add with 0, and plain + is shifted add, shifted back.
- `LogicSets.xor_from_or_and`: XOR from OR and AND: a ⊕ b = (a ∨ b)·(1 − ab) on idempotents.
- `LogicSets.xor_keeps_idem`: At k = −2 (XOR) idempotents stay idempotent.
- `LogicSets.xor_self`: XOR of a thing with itself: a ◯[−2] a = 2(a − a²), zero on idempotents (self-inverse).
- `LogicSets.xor_self_idem`
- `LogicSets.zero_definable`: Zero is definable from the fused exponent: the only right identity.

## FusedAlgebra.Topics.MeasureProbability (36)

- `MeasureProbability.Ex_append`
- `MeasureProbability.Ex_one`
- `MeasureProbability.Ex_prod`: Independent product: E[f(X)·g(Y)] = E f(X) · E g(Y).
- `MeasureProbability.Ex_row`
- `MeasureProbability.against_and`: Odds against an independent "and" combine by the circle operation.
- `MeasureProbability.dial_gap_cov`: For any joint law (dependent or not), the gap is k times the covariance: the corner's excess.
- `MeasureProbability.dual_involution`: The dual of the dual weight is the weight again.
- `MeasureProbability.dual_q`: In q = 1 + λ terms the dual is the reciprocal: q* = 1/q.
- `MeasureProbability.dual_weight`: Duality: if the whole space a, b, c is normalized, the dual measure μ*(S) = 1 − μ(complement) obeys the dial with weight circInv λ.
- `MeasureProbability.indep_dial`: The engine: for independent X and Y (total mass 1 each), E[X ◯ₖ Y] = E X ◯ₖ E Y.
- `MeasureProbability.lam_additive`: λ = 0 is an ordinary additive measure.
- `MeasureProbability.lam_append`: Disjoint union: μ(A ∪ B) = μ(A) ◯ₖ μ(B).
- `MeasureProbability.lam_comm_two`: Order of the atoms never matters.
- `MeasureProbability.lam_corners`: Möbius masses are cube corners: 1 + λμ(A) = Σ over sub-lists S of ∏_{i∈S} λgᵢ.
- `MeasureProbability.lam_equal_atoms`: m equal atoms of size g have measure g·[m]_q with q = 1 + λg.
- `MeasureProbability.lam_incl_excl`: Inclusion–exclusion is multiplication: with atoms x (only A), y (only B), z (both), (1 + λμ(A∪B))(1 + λμ(A∩B)) = (1 + λμ(A))(1 + λμ(B)).
- `MeasureProbability.lam_lift`: The lift: 1 + λ·μ(A) = ∏ (1 + λ·gᵢ).
- `MeasureProbability.lam_neg_one`: λ = −1: μ(A) = 1 − ∏(1 − gᵢ), the chance at least one atom fires.
- `MeasureProbability.lam_scale`: λ·μ is the plain circle operation of the scaled densities λ·gᵢ.
- `MeasureProbability.lam_three`: The three-atom expansion: layer 1, layer 2 weighted λ, layer 3 weighted λ².
- `MeasureProbability.martingale_step`: Multiplicative martingale step: independent mean-zero returns keep mean zero.
- `MeasureProbability.no_norm_past_wall`: So when the wall sits in (0, 1] (k ≤ −1), no such set has measure 1: a normalized λ-measure needs λ > −1.
- `MeasureProbability.norm_two`: Two atoms: λ = (1 − g₁ − g₂)/(g₁g₂) solves (1 + λg₁)(1 + λg₂) = 1 + λ.
- `MeasureProbability.norm_two_root`: Any nonzero root of the two-atom normalization is that one.
- `MeasureProbability.norm_two_sign`: Two atoms with positive densities: λ > 0 exactly when g₁ + g₂ < 1.
- `MeasureProbability.odds_lift`: The lift of the odds is 1/(1 − p).
- `MeasureProbability.odds_or`: Odds of an independent "or" combine by the circle operation: odds(p ◯₋₁ q) = odds p ◯ odds q.
- `MeasureProbability.odds_or_three`: Three independent events: the odds of "any" is the three-way circle operation.
- `MeasureProbability.prodL_eq_zero`
- `MeasureProbability.sexp_inverse`: The self-bracket: one step of ℰ(x) against its own circle inverse is flat.
- `MeasureProbability.sure_atom`: At k = −1 the only way to reach 1 is a sure atom: the product of misses vanishes.
- `MeasureProbability.sure_atom_exists`: k = −1, measure 1 ⇒ some atom has density exactly 1.
- `MeasureProbability.wall_blocks`: Below the wall stays below the wall: positive lifts multiply to a positive lift.
- `MeasureProbability.wall_list`: For any list of atoms below the wall, the measure stays below the wall.
- `MeasureProbability.yor`: Yor's formula step by step: ℰ(x) ◯ ℰ(y) = ℰ(x ◯ y) increment-wise, where Δx ◯ Δy = Δx + Δy + ΔxΔy carries the bracket [X, Y].
- `MeasureProbability.yor_step`: One step: the increment of X + Y + [X, Y] is exactly Δx ◯ Δy.

## FusedAlgebra.Topics.NSContinuum (18)

- `CircleOp.NSContinuum.annulus_cells`: **The dyadic annulus is a circle power.** The new cells added when a box of side 2ʲ doubles number 2^(nj) · 1^(◯n): the old box times the n-fold circle operation of 1.
- `CircleOp.NSContinuum.annulus_graded`: **The annulus is graded by the cube's layers.** 2ⁿ − 1 = Σ_{k=1..n} C(n,k): the new cells of a doubled box split by how many of the n coordinates landed in the upper half (k = 1 strips, k = 2 corners, …, k = n the far corner).
- `CircleOp.NSContinuum.box_doubles`: Doubling the side of an n-dimensional box of side 2ʲ multiplies its cells by 2ⁿ.
- `CircleOp.NSContinuum.energy_telescopes`: **Energy only moves.** Summed over shells 0..N, the nonlinear gains telescope to minus the flux out of the top shell.
- `CircleOp.NSContinuum.fan_pivot`: The three known thresholds are rays: critical α = (n+2)/4, the dyadic regularity line α = (n+2)/5 (Barbato–Morandin–Romito) and the dyadic blow-up line α = (n+2)/6 (Cheskidov). In twentieths: 5(n+2), 4(n+2), (10/3)(n+2); all three vanish at the pivot n = −2.
- `CircleOp.NSContinuum.gain_is_model`: The gain is aⱼ times the model's nonlinear term.
- `CircleOp.NSContinuum.k41_3d_positive`
- `CircleOp.NSContinuum.k41_5d_negative`
- `CircleOp.NSContinuum.k41_edge_at_four`
- `CircleOp.NSContinuum.lions_on_2d_ray`: Lions' 3D threshold α = 5/4 is on the same ray as 2D Navier–Stokes (α = 1): both are critical.
- `CircleOp.NSContinuum.mpow_one_nat`: Combining 1 with itself n times by the circle operation counts the nonempty corners of the n-cube: 1^(◯n) + 1 = 2ⁿ.
- `CircleOp.NSContinuum.ns3d_on_bmr_line`: 3D Navier–Stokes lies exactly on the dyadic regularity line A = 4(n+2).
- `CircleOp.NSContinuum.ns3d_on_ray_of_2d_four_fifths`: 3D Navier–Stokes (α = 1) is on the same ray as 2D flow with dissipation power α = 4/5. (2D is smooth there anyway, for a reason scaling cannot see: §5.)
- `CircleOp.NSContinuum.ns3d_supercritical`: 3D Navier–Stokes is off the critical ray: the scaling gap is a quarter power.
- `CircleOp.NSContinuum.onRay_refl`
- `CircleOp.NSContinuum.onRay_symm`
- `CircleOp.NSContinuum.onRay_trans`: Lying on one ray is transitive (away from the pivot dimension n = −2), so the rays split the plane: sliding the dimension and sliding the dissipation are one dial.
- `CircleOp.NSContinuum.planar_stretching_zero`

## FusedAlgebra.Topics.NavierStokes (6)

- `FusedRungs.burgers_corner_defect`
- `FusedRungs.burgers_corner_ok_iff`
- `FusedRungs.homochiral_layers`: The homochiral corners (+++) and (---) are exactly layers 0 and 3.
- `FusedRungs.shell_energy`
- `FusedRungs.shell_helicity`: Helicity is conserved for every sign corner and every shell ratio l, as a polynomial identity (it does not even need s² = 1).
- `FusedRungs.sign_cube_layers`

## FusedAlgebra.Topics.NegativeOdds (22)

- `CircleOp.NegativeOdds.anti_chance_example`: Example: a −1/3 "chance" cancels a 1/4 chance (p + q − pq = 0).
- `CircleOp.NegativeOdds.doom_destiny`: Doom times destiny is the plain multiplier 1: they cancel instead of giving 0·∞.
- `CircleOp.NegativeOdds.flip_is_minus_rewind`: The flip t/(1 + t) (odds → chance, law k = 1 → law k = −1) is minus the rewind.
- `CircleOp.NegativeOdds.kept_assoc`
- `CircleOp.NegativeOdds.kept_comm`
- `CircleOp.NegativeOdds.mirror_fixes_wall`: The complement mirror fixes the wall.
- `CircleOp.NegativeOdds.odds_union`: Union of independent events: odds compose by mult-add.
- `CircleOp.NegativeOdds.past_wall_pair`: Two items past the wall give a positive lift (the sign bit cancels in pairs).
- `CircleOp.NegativeOdds.prob_minus_two`
- `CircleOp.NegativeOdds.prob_mirror`: The mirror is the complement.
- `CircleOp.NegativeOdds.prob_rewind`: The undo of odds t has chance −t.
- `CircleOp.NegativeOdds.prob_third`: The third involution t ↦ −1 − t sends p to 1/p.
- `CircleOp.NegativeOdds.rewind_cancels`: Rewinding cancels.
- `CircleOp.NegativeOdds.rewind_fixed`: Rewind fixes never and the 200% point.
- `CircleOp.NegativeOdds.rewind_mirror`
- `CircleOp.NegativeOdds.rewind_rewind`: Rewind is its own undo.
- `CircleOp.NegativeOdds.third_involution`
- `CircleOp.NegativeOdds.third_swaps`: The third involution swaps never and the wall.
- `CircleOp.NegativeOdds.turn_order_three`: The turn has order 3.
- `CircleOp.NegativeOdds.two_hundred_twice`: 200% twice is never: t = −2 is its own undo.
- `CircleOp.NegativeOdds.wall_absorbs`: The wall swallows every stack.
- `CircleOp.NegativeOdds.wall_absorbs_inter`: The wall also swallows intersection (for any nonzero odds).

## FusedAlgebra.Topics.NewForce (20)

- `CircleOp.NewForce.dual_exponent_mond`
- `CircleOp.NewForce.gamma_from_strength`
- `CircleOp.NewForce.hold_curvature`: Its curvature there: V″(R*) = 30C/R*⁶ (a minimum when C > 0).
- `CircleOp.NewForce.hold_leftover`: The vacuum energy left over: V(R*) = −5C/R*⁴.
- `CircleOp.NewForce.hold_mass_factor`: Radion mass: (2/3)·30 = 20, so m² = 20C/(M̄²R⁴).
- `CircleOp.NewForce.hold_turning_point`: With V = a/R + C R*²/R⁶ and a = −6C/R*³, R* is a turning point: V′(R*) = 0.
- `CircleOp.NewForce.holder_pair`
- `CircleOp.NewForce.klein_strength`
- `CircleOp.NewForce.like_charges_cancel`
- `CircleOp.NewForce.one_circle_gamma`
- `CircleOp.NewForce.one_circle_third`
- `CircleOp.NewForce.opposite_charges_add`
- `CircleOp.NewForce.scalar_over_tensor`
- `CircleOp.NewForce.sphere_shape_factor`: Sphere: ⟨|K|²⟩ = 2R²/3, so the shape factor 4R²/⟨|K|²⟩ is 6 (a circle gives 4).
- `CircleOp.NewForce.strong_b0_five`: Strong force with 5 flavours: b₀ = 11·3/3 − 2·5/3 = 23/3.
- `CircleOp.NewForce.su2_flavour_bound`
- `CircleOp.NewForce.su3_flavour_bound`
- `CircleOp.NewForce.tower_leading_yukawa`
- `CircleOp.NewForce.tower_sum`
- `CircleOp.NewForce.vector_over_tensor`

## FusedAlgebra.Topics.NewTies (13)

- `CircleOp.NewTies.commute_iff`: 3. Two stretches with slopes ≠ 1 commute exactly when they share a centre.
- `CircleOp.NewTies.count_one`: Distinct composites of length 1, 2, 3: 3, 8, 21, the shared-centre count (not 3, 9, 27).
- `CircleOp.NewTies.count_three`
- `CircleOp.NewTies.count_two`
- `CircleOp.NewTies.fixes_wall`: The plane-law map x ↦ (x + a + kax)/(1 − dax) fixes w exactly when d·w² + k·w + 1 = 0 (cleared of the fraction).
- `CircleOp.NewTies.hom_centre`: Every stretch fixes its centre.
- `CircleOp.NewTies.lower_bound_key`: 4. The lower bound's key step: the first-return generators have total weight 1 exactly when the shared-centre dimension equation holds, (1/P − 1)(1/Q − 1) = 1 ↔ P + Q = 1, cleared of fractions.
- `CircleOp.NewTies.moran_special`: 5. One map per box: 1 + Σ[(1 − wᵢ) − 1] = 1 − Σ wᵢ, Moran's equation.
- `CircleOp.NewTies.order_gap`: 2b. The order gap between stretches about two centres.
- `CircleOp.NewTies.same_centre_commute`: 2a. Stretches about one centre commute.
- `CircleOp.NewTies.two_law_threshold`: Two laws with block weights x₁, x₂: the switching matrix [[0, x₁], [x₂, 0]] has spectral radius 1 exactly when x₁·x₂ = 1, which is the same as x₁/(1+x₁) + x₂/(1+x₂) = 1 (cleared of fractions).
- `CircleOp.NewTies.union_is_stretch`: 1. A union in a box of size M, scaled by M: M·(a + x − ax/M) = M·M + (M − a)(x − M), i.e. the stretch about M (the full box) by the factor 1 − a/M.
- `CircleOp.NewTies.wall_line_tangent`: On the border 4d = k², the wall line d·w² + k·w + 1 = 0 becomes (k·w + 2)² = 0 (times 4): a double root, so the laws sharing the wall w form the tangent line to the border at k = −2/w.

## FusedAlgebra.Topics.NumberPlane (26)

- `SimpleLaws.complex_point`: the complex corner (0, 1), u = i: slopes add like tangents, (x + y)/(1 − x y)
- `SimpleLaws.dual_point`: the dual corner (0, 0), u² = 0: Galileo's velocity addition, plain x + y over 1
- `SimpleLaws.four_norm`: 4·N splits as a square plus (4q − k²)·b²: the sign of 4q − k² picks the country
- `SimpleLaws.mul_assoc_re`: associativity, real part and u-part
- `SimpleLaws.mul_assoc_u`
- `SimpleLaws.mul_comm_re`
- `SimpleLaws.mul_comm_u`
- `SimpleLaws.mul_rule`: the multiplication really is "u² = k u − q": expand (a + b u)(c + d u) and replace u²
- `SimpleLaws.multadd_point_re`
- `SimpleLaws.multadd_point_u`: at the circle operation's own point (k, q) = (1, 0) the unit is idempotent (u² = u) and the u-part is the plain circle operation, with real part 1: nothing to divide by
- `SimpleLaws.norm_border`: border: when k² = 4q the norm is one square, (2a + k b)²/4 (Galileo: lengths only one way)
- `SimpleLaws.norm_complex`: Pythagoras at the complex corner: a² + b²
- `SimpleLaws.norm_conj`: the norm is the number times its conjugate: with ū = k − u, (a + b u)(a + b ū) = N
- `SimpleLaws.norm_dual`: Galileo at the dual corner: only a² survives
- `SimpleLaws.norm_mul`: the norm multiplies (the generalized Brahmagupta–Fibonacci identity)
- `SimpleLaws.norm_multadd`: at (1, 0) the norm is a·(a + b): in the coordinates X = a, Y = a + b it is X·Y
- `SimpleLaws.norm_multadd_lift`: the product of two tilted ones has norm (1 + x)(1 + y) = 1 + (x ◯ y)
- `SimpleLaws.norm_multadd_tilted`: the tilted one 1 + x u at the circle operation's point has norm 1·(1 + x): its lift
- `SimpleLaws.norm_nonneg_turning`: turning country: when 4q ≥ k² the norm is never negative (a true length)
- `SimpleLaws.norm_split`: the spacetime interval at the split corner: a² − b²
- `SimpleLaws.norm_walls`: two walls: write k = −(r + s), q = r s; the norm splits into two light-cone factors
- `SimpleLaws.split_point`: the split corner (0, −1), u² = 1: velocity addition, (x + y)/(1 + x y)
- `SimpleLaws.sq_nonneg`: a square is never negative (core Lean has no lemma by this name, so we prove it)
- `SimpleLaws.tilted_re`: (1 + x u)(1 + y u): its real part is the landscape denominator
- `SimpleLaws.tilted_u`: ... and its u-part is the landscape numerator, x and y combined by the circle operation with dial k
- `SimpleLaws.unit_circle_is_hyperbola`: the unit circle N = 1 at the circle operation's point is the curve x ◯ y = 0, i.e. (1 + x)(1 + y) = 1: a hyperbola whose asymptotes are the walls x = −1 and y = −1

## FusedAlgebra.Topics.Numerical (32)

- `Numerical.bernoulli`: Bernoulli: compounding never loses to simple interest, a^(◯n) ≥ n·a for a ≥ −1.
- `Numerical.chain_eq`
- `Numerical.circ_bound`: |a| ≤ u and |t| ≤ M give |a ◯ t| ≤ u ◯ M (the corner of the bounds bounds the corner).
- `Numerical.circ_sensitivity`: Perturbing a value moves a ◯ b by exactly h·(1 + b): the sensitivity is the other lift.
- `Numerical.fma_shape_corner`
- `Numerical.fma_shape_left`
- `Numerical.fma_shape_right`
- `Numerical.gamma_bound`: γₙ: u^(◯n)·(1 − nu) ≤ nu, i.e. (1 + u)ⁿ − 1 ≤ nu/(1 − nu) whenever nu < 1.
- `Numerical.gamma_lift`: (1 + u)ⁿ(1 − nu) ≤ 1 for u ≥ 0: the γₙ bound without division.
- `Numerical.gold_eq`
- `Numerical.gold_lift`: In the lift: (1 − e)(1 + e)(1 + e²)…(1 + e^(2^(n−1))) = 1 − e^(2ⁿ).
- `Numerical.hornerCirc_eq`
- `Numerical.horner_cube`: Starting from 0, the n Horner steps give the whole cube: every corner product once.
- `Numerical.inverse_lift`: The fused inverse undoes the lift's reciprocal: 1 + inv u = 1/(1 + u).
- `Numerical.inverse_sensitivity`: The inverse's exact sensitivity: only 1 + u near 0 (the wall) makes it large.
- `Numerical.lift_amplifier`: The way back from the lift: a relative rounding η of 1 + a becomes η·(1 + a)/a in a.
- `Numerical.mpow_double`: Powers by squaring: a^(◯2n) = a^(◯n) ◯ a^(◯n).
- `Numerical.mpow_horner`: Combining with the same b n times by the circle operation is Horner on the polynomial b(1 + y + … + yⁿ⁻¹), y = 1 + b.
- `Numerical.mpow_nonneg`
- `Numerical.newton_fixed`: At the exact inverse the residual is 0 and Newton stands still.
- `Numerical.newton_iter_residual`: After n steps the residual is −(−e₀)^(2ⁿ): quadratic convergence, digits double.
- `Numerical.newton_residual`: The residual squares (with a sign): the corner e·e is all that is left.
- `Numerical.nonneg_of_mul_pos`
- `Numerical.pow_nonneg_rat`
- `Numerical.relerr_div`: Divide two perturbed values: the relative errors mult-subtract (Fisher's rule).
- `Numerical.relerr_mul`: Multiply two perturbed values: the relative errors combine by the circle operation.
- `Numerical.relerr_mul3`: Three perturbed factors: the errors fold.
- `Numerical.sq_nonneg_rat`
- `Numerical.sterbenz_window_neg`: Sterbenz's window, part 2: for −1/2 ≤ a ≤ 0 (lift in [1/2, 1]) the inverse b lies in [−a/2, −2a].
- `Numerical.sterbenz_window_pos`: Sterbenz's window, part 1: for 0 ≤ a ≤ 1 (lift in [1, 2]) the inverse b lies in [−2a, −a/2], so a + b is computed exactly in floating point.
- `Numerical.theta_bound`: Higham's Lemma 3.1 in circle-operation form: n step errors of size at most u fold to at most (1 + u)ⁿ − 1 = u^(◯n).
- `Numerical.theta_is_fold`: Higham's θₙ: the product of the factors (1 + δᵢ), minus 1, is the circle-operation fold.

## FusedAlgebra.Topics.OrderDial (33)

- `OrderDial.M2.ext'`
- `OrderDial.anticommute_self_zero`: If swapping two copies of x costs a sign (x·x = −x·x), then x·x = 0.
- `OrderDial.app_comp`: `comp` really is composition (functoriality of the one-object category).
- `OrderDial.centre_fixed`: A period's centre: if r·x* = −d then x* is fixed (the interest-only balance).
- `OrderDial.circK_idem_iff`: The grouping-free, order-free, but repetition-counting corner: a ◯ₖ a = a only at 0 and the wall.
- `OrderDial.commute_iff`: Two stretch-and-shifts commute exactly when their wedge vanishes.
- `OrderDial.comp_assoc`
- `OrderDial.comp_id`
- `OrderDial.compounding_corner`: Compounding: deposit-then-grow minus grow-then-deposit is the corner r·d.
- `OrderDial.different_centres`: Two stretches by 2 about different centres (0 and 1) do not commute.
- `OrderDial.k_slice`: The k-family (stretch k·a, shift a; all about the wall −1/k) is a commuting slice, and it is ◯ₖ.
- `OrderDial.k_slice_commutes`
- `OrderDial.ksum_distrib_gap`: The k-sum distributes only where k·c·(c − 1)·a·b = 0.
- `OrderDial.ksum_not_distrib`: Off 0/1 the k = −1 sum fails to distribute: c = 2, a = b = 1.
- `OrderDial.ksum_or`: At k = −1 on 0/1 values the k-sum is OR (relations: is there a path).
- `OrderDial.ksum_zero`: At k = 0 the k-sum is plain addition (bags: count paths).
- `OrderDial.mut_assoc_error`: On E11, E12, E21 the grouping error is αβ times the fixed matrix [[1,0],[0,−1]].
- `OrderDial.mut_assoc_only_at_ends`: So order-in-the-formula is associative on these three only at the ends αβ = 0.
- `OrderDial.mut_comm`: Its order error is (α − β) times the commutator: the order part is a Lie bracket.
- `OrderDial.mut_flexible`: Every weighting stays flexible: (a ◯ b) ◯ a = a ◯ (b ◯ a).
- `OrderDial.no_functor_half`: Sizes of a would-be functor H with H(H X) ≅ P(X): h n = size of H applied to an n-element set. A functor keeps split injections (a nonempty set's injections have retractions), so h is monotone from 1 up; and H(H n) has 2ⁿ elements. Already at n ≤ 2 this is impossible.
