(The case H(1) infinite is ruled out before this skeleton: H(1) injects into H(H 1), which has 2 elements.)
- `OrderDial.one_circ_one`: So the circle operation counts repeats: 1 ◯ 1 = 3.
- `OrderDial.order_gap`: The order gap is a pure shift, equal to the wedge a·d − b·c.
- `OrderDial.over_assoc`
- `OrderDial.over_idem`
- `OrderDial.over_not_comm`: Merge order matters on a shared key.
- `OrderDial.over_right_regular`: Right regular band: x y x = y x.
- `OrderDial.pauli`: Pauli X and Z: each squares to 1 and they anticommute (order dial q = −1).
- `OrderDial.pow_add'`
- `OrderDial.stretch_order_free`: The stretch parts never care about order.
- `OrderDial.wall_absorbs`: The wall −1 absorbs everything (the circle operation's zero, not its unit).
- `OrderDial.weyl`: Shifting after multiplying = λ^a times multiplying after shifting: the q-commutation yx = q·xy with q = λ^a.
- `OrderDial.xxyy_inversions`: Their out-of-order counts give 1 + q + 2q² + q³ + q⁴ (the Gaussian binomial [4,2]_q).

## FusedAlgebra.Topics.OsgoodInverse (53)

- `CircleOp.OsgoodInverse.blk_le`
- `CircleOp.OsgoodInverse.block_start`: **The blocks are the circle-operation lattice.** The j-th block starts at 2ʲ = 1 + 1^(◯j), the lift of the j-fold circle operation of 1.
- `CircleOp.OsgoodInverse.condensation`: **The test is the same one rung down.** For shrinking nonnegative terms, the dyadic partial sums are bounded iff the condensed sums are.
- `CircleOp.OsgoodInverse.const_block`: A constant over a block of length 2ʲ sums to 2ʲ·c.
- `CircleOp.OsgoodInverse.csum_harm`: One rung down, the harmonic terms become all ones: 2ʲ · (1/2ʲ) = 1.
- `CircleOp.OsgoodInverse.csum_isq`: One rung down, the inverse squares become a geometric series: Σ_{j<n} 2ʲ/4ʲ = 2 − 2/2ⁿ.
- `CircleOp.OsgoodInverse.csum_le_dsum`: **Lower squeeze.** Σ_{j≤n} 2ʲ·a(2ʲ) ≤ a(1) + 2·(a(1) + … + a(2ⁿ − 1)).
- `CircleOp.OsgoodInverse.dsum_le_csum`: **Upper squeeze.** a(1) + … + a(2ⁿ − 1) ≤ Σ_{j<n} 2ʲ·a(2ʲ).
- `CircleOp.OsgoodInverse.dsum_succ`
- `CircleOp.OsgoodInverse.fan_distinct`: **Different start times give different solutions**, so the scheme has infinitely many solutions from x(0) = 0.
- `CircleOp.OsgoodInverse.fan_step`: **Every fan member solves the trapezoid scheme of x' = 2√x.**
- `CircleOp.OsgoodInverse.fan_zero`: Every fan member starts at 0.
- `CircleOp.OsgoodInverse.fsum_append`
- `CircleOp.OsgoodInverse.fsum_le_dsum`: Every partial sum a(1) + … + a(N) sits under a dyadic one, so the dyadic sums decide boundedness of all partial sums.
- `CircleOp.OsgoodInverse.fsum_mono`
- `CircleOp.OsgoodInverse.fsum_nonneg`
- `CircleOp.OsgoodInverse.harm_anti`
- `CircleOp.OsgoodInverse.harm_nonneg`
- `CircleOp.OsgoodInverse.harmonic_grows`: **Rung 0 passes.** 1 + 1/2 + … + 1/(2ⁿ − 1) ≥ n/2: the clock never stops.
- `CircleOp.OsgoodInverse.inverse_squares_bounded`: **One power past rung 0 fails the test (the sum converges).** 1 + 1/4 + 1/9 + … + 1/(2ⁿ − 1)² ≤ 2 for every n.
- `CircleOp.OsgoodInverse.isq_anti`
- `CircleOp.OsgoodInverse.le_blk`
- `CircleOp.OsgoodInverse.lipschitz_unique`: **Lipschitz means unique.** The trapezoid scheme of x' = x, x(n+1) − x(n) = (h/2)(x(n) + x(n+1)), started at 0 stays at 0 (for h ≠ 2).
- `CircleOp.OsgoodInverse.lt_pow2`
- `CircleOp.OsgoodInverse.mirror_lift`: The mirrored walk reaches the wall exactly when the climb blows up: 1 + (−u/(1+u)) = 1/(1+u).
- `CircleOp.OsgoodInverse.mirror_step`: **The mirror.** Through the circle inverse v = −u/(1+u), a Riccati step is a step of exactly −h: the climb to infinity is a walk at constant speed into the wall −1. Blow-up at infinity and leaving the wall are one question, swapped by the circle inverse.
- `CircleOp.OsgoodInverse.nr_add`
- `CircleOp.OsgoodInverse.nr_mono`
- `CircleOp.OsgoodInverse.nr_nonneg`
- `CircleOp.OsgoodInverse.nr_pos`
- `CircleOp.OsgoodInverse.nr_pow2`
- `CircleOp.OsgoodInverse.one_div_pos`
- `CircleOp.OsgoodInverse.one_le_pow2`
- `CircleOp.OsgoodInverse.pow2_succ`
- `CircleOp.OsgoodInverse.rad_out`: **The radial field pushes straight out:** x·F = r⁴, so ρ = r² obeys ρ' = 2ρ², the Riccati law of §1, and blows up at time 1/(2ρ₀).
- `CircleOp.OsgoodInverse.rad_speed`
- `CircleOp.OsgoodInverse.recip_diff`: The clock of the Riccati climb: reciprocals of the lift.
- `CircleOp.OsgoodInverse.ric_lift`: Under the lift it is a reciprocal: 1 + y = 1/(1 − t). The time left, 1 − t, is the clock.
- `CircleOp.OsgoodInverse.ric_near_one`: The continuous climb passes every height before t = 1: y(1 − 1/(M+2)) = M + 1.
- `CircleOp.OsgoodInverse.ric_step`: **The exact difference law.** y(t+h) − y(t) = h·(1+y(t))(1+y(t+h)): the trapezoid-free discrete form of y' = (1+y)², with no error term.
- `CircleOp.OsgoodInverse.ric_step_circ`: The same law in the circle operation: the step is h times the lift of y(t) ◯ y(t+h).
- `CircleOp.OsgoodInverse.riccati_clock`: **The discrete Riccati climb runs on a straight clock.** As long as the lift stays off the wall, 1/(1+yₙ) = 1/(1+y₀) − n·h.
- `CircleOp.OsgoodInverse.riccati_clock_step`: Read in the clock coordinate, the climb is uniform: one step of the law lowers 1/(1+y) by exactly h.
- `CircleOp.OsgoodInverse.riccati_hits_wall`: **Blow-up.** If the clock starts at m·h, the climb cannot take m steps with finite values: at step m the clock reads 0, and no finite y has 1/(1+y) = 0. (Continuous form: the blow-up time is ∫₀^∞ ds/(1+s)² = 1.)
- `CircleOp.OsgoodInverse.riccati_quarter_blows_up`
- `CircleOp.OsgoodInverse.root_sq`
- `CircleOp.OsgoodInverse.rot_perp`: **The rotation pushes sideways:** x·F = 0, so d/dt |x|² = 2x·F = 0 and the size never changes, though the speed fails the Osgood test.
- `CircleOp.OsgoodInverse.rot_speed`: Both have speed r³ (speed² = r⁶): the size alone fails the Osgood test.
- `CircleOp.OsgoodInverse.rung1_lift`: **The rung-1 climb never reaches the wall.** If yₙ₊₁ = yₙ ◯ h then 1 + yₙ = (1 + y₀)(1 + h)ⁿ: the clock log₁₊ y advances by log(1+h) per step forever (continuous form: ∫ ds/(1+s) = log(1+s) → ∞).
- `CircleOp.OsgoodInverse.rung1_never_wall`: On the rationals the rung-1 climb stays on the safe side of the wall forever.
- `CircleOp.OsgoodInverse.steps_add`
- `CircleOp.OsgoodInverse.steps_quarter`: With h = 1/4 the Riccati clock started at 1 reads 0 after 4 steps: the climb from y₀ = 0 blows up at step 4 = 1/h.
- `CircleOp.OsgoodInverse.time_left_pos`: … and that time is still before 1: the time left 1/(M+2) is positive.

## FusedAlgebra.Topics.PathNames (5)

- `PathNames.chain3_dial`: **The dial as dilation.** Three steps with dial k: a sub-chain of length m is weighted by k^(m−1). k = 0 keeps only the single steps (the plain sum).
- `PathNames.chain4_landscape`: **The whole landscape of a chain.** Four steps composed: every ordered sub-chain (15 of them) appears exactly once, in its original order.
- `PathNames.circ_one_assoc`: k = 1: the circle operation is associative in any ring.
- `PathNames.circ_swap`: **Order is kept, scaled by k.** Swapping two steps changes the composite by k times their commutator, so nothing about order is lost unless k = 0 or the steps commute.
- `PathNames.scalar_shadow`: Over numbers the order is lost: different chains, same composite. (1 ∘ 2 = 5 = 2 ∘ 1, and the chains 1,2 and 5 alone also give 5.)

## FusedAlgebra.Topics.Patterns (32)

- `Patterns.cassini`: Cassini's identity is the determinant of n steps: U(n+1)² − U n·U(n+2) = Qⁿ.
- `Patterns.cycle_of_lucas_zero`: If U(n+1) = 0 then n + 1 copies of a land back on 0: a cycle.
- `Patterns.disc_square_or_ge_five`: The smallest irrational walls. For whole-number k and q the wall discriminant k² − 4q is either a perfect square or at least 5. So the first integer law whose walls are irrational has discriminant 5: the golden walls, at (k, q) = (±1, −1).
- `Patterns.golden_cassini`: Cassini for the golden law: F(n+1)² − F n·F(n+2) = (−1)ⁿ, the determinant (−1) per step.
- `Patterns.golden_law_fib`: In the law a ⊕ b = (a + b − ab)/(1 + ab), adding 1 to itself n + 1 times gives F(n+1)/F(n+2): 1, 1/2, 2/3, 3/5, 5/8, …, marching into the golden wall 1/φ.
- `Patterns.golden_step`: The Fibonacci law (k, q) = (−1, −1): adding 1 is the Fibonacci step (x, y) ↦ (y, x + y).
- `Patterns.golden_walls`: The golden walls: the Fibonacci law's wall polynomial is 1 − a − a², whose roots are 1/φ and −φ (stated as: w is a wall exactly when w² + w = 1).
- `Patterns.iter_lucas`: The heart of it: n + 1 copies of a, in any law of the map, are (a·U(n+1), U(n+1) − Q·U n) with P = 2 + k·a and Q = 1 + k·a + q·a².
- `Patterns.ladder_is_step`: The resistor ladder of the simple-laws page is one step of the mirror law (1, −1).
- `Patterns.lucas_add`
- `Patterns.lucas_add_pair`: Associativity of the law, read on the counting sequence, is Lucas's addition rule: U(m+n+1) = U(m+1)·U(n+1) − Q·U m·U n. Proved for the pair (n, n+1) together.
- `Patterns.lucas_fib`
- `Patterns.lucas_fib_pair`: The Lucas sequence with P = 1, Q = −1 is Fibonacci.
- `Patterns.lucas_five`: The fifth term: U 5 = (P² − Q)² − Q·P².
- `Patterns.lucas_five_golden`: With Q = 1 (a step of determinant 1, a pure turn), the five-cycle condition factors into the two golden quadratics: U 5 = (P² − P − 1)(P² + P − 1).
- `Patterns.lucas_one`
- `Patterns.lucas_succ_succ`
- `Patterns.lucas_zero`
- `Patterns.mfib_lift`
- `Patterns.mfib_lift_pair`: Flatten it and the exponents are Fibonacci numbers: 1 + x(n+1) = (1 + x0)^F(n) · (1 + x1)^F(n+1).
- `Patterns.mfib_one_one`: From 1, 1: the circle-operation Fibonacci numbers are 2^F(n) − 1 (1, 1, 3, 7, 31, 255, 8191, …), stated as 1 + x(n+1) = (1 + 1)^F(n+2) in any number system.
- `Patterns.mirror_den`
- `Patterns.mirror_num`: Mirror: flipping the sign of every value takes the law at (k, q) to the law at (−k, q).
- `Patterns.multadd_den_one`: The circle operation (k, q) = (1, 0): the denominator is always 1, U(n+1) − (1 + a)·U n = 1.
- `Patterns.multadd_iter`: The circle operation's counting pair is ((1 + a)^(n+1) − 1, 1), via the Lucas formula.
- `Patterns.no_integer_five_cycle`: No whole-number turn has period 5. With determinant 1 and a whole-number trace P, the fifth Lucas term P⁴ − 3P² + 1 is odd, so never 0. A five-cycle needs a golden trace.
- `Patterns.step_den`: The step's bottom entry is the law's denominator: y − q·x·a (for y = 1, 1 − q·x·a).
- `Patterns.step_det`: The determinant of the step is Q = 1 + k·a + q·a²: the law's wall polynomial.
- `Patterns.step_is_law`: With y = 1 the step is exactly the law: numerator circK k x a, denominator 1 − q·x·a.
- `Patterns.step_num`: The step's top entry is the law's numerator: x + a·y + k·x·a (for y = 1, circK k x a).
- `Patterns.step_trace`: The trace of the step is P = 2 + k·a.
- `Patterns.wall_absorbs`: A wall is where the determinant vanishes: adding a wall value w sends every pair onto the wall (top = w · bottom), whatever it started as. This is absorption.

## FusedAlgebra.Topics.Pi (45)

- `PiCircle.G.ext'`
- `PiCircle.G.ext'_iff`
- `PiCircle.circ_self_zero`: in circle-operation units: x ◯ x = 0 (a half turn twice is a full turn) exactly at 0 and −2
- `PiCircle.circle_closed`: the circle |1 + a| = 1 (centre: the wall −1, radius 1) is closed under the circle operation
- `PiCircle.circle_real_points`: On the real line (Rat), the circle (1 + x)² = 1 is exactly the two points 0 and −2.
- `PiCircle.cross_10`: 32 steps of i/10 pass −2; 31 do not
- `PiCircle.cross_100`: 315 steps of i/100
- `PiCircle.cross_1000`: 3142 steps of i/1000
- `PiCircle.div_le_div_of`: a'/d ≤ a/b when 0 < b ≤ d and 0 ≤ a' ≤ a
- `PiCircle.euler`: Euler: one step of i/2, one of i/3
- `PiCircle.gma_assoc`
- `PiCircle.gma_comm`
- `PiCircle.gma_lift`
- `PiCircle.gma_zero`
- `PiCircle.gpow_lift`: the lift of a circle power is an ordinary power: 1 + a^(◯n) = (1 + a)ⁿ
- `PiCircle.greg_even_le_odd`: so [even sum, odd sum] is a nested interval: every even sum sits below every odd sum
- `PiCircle.greg_even_up`: even partial sums climb
- `PiCircle.greg_odd_above`: each odd partial sum sits above the even one before it
- `PiCircle.greg_odd_down`: odd partial sums fall
- `PiCircle.greg_two`
- `PiCircle.gt_nonneg`
- `PiCircle.hermann`: Hermann: two steps of i/2, one back of i/7
- `PiCircle.hutton`: Hutton: two steps of i/3, one of i/7
- `PiCircle.lens`: THE LENS. Slope addition is exactly Gaussian multiplication.
- `PiCircle.lens_conj`: and the conjugates multiply the same way, so the lift W/W̄ of a sum is the product of the lifts: 1 + a(x ⊕ y) = (1 + a(x))·(1 + a(y)), i.e. a(x ⊕ y) = a(x) ◯ a(y).
- `PiCircle.lens_infinity`: slope ∞ lands on the parity point: W = −W̄, lift −1, a = −2
- `PiCircle.lens_on_circle`: every real slope lands on the circle: W and W̄ have the same length, so |W/W̄| = 1
- `PiCircle.lens_zero`: slope 0 lands on the identity: W = W̄, lift 1, a = 0
- `PiCircle.machin`: Machin (1706): four steps of i/5, one step back of i/239
- `PiCircle.machin_50_digits`: 50 decimals: 3.14159265358979323846264338327950288419716939937510 < lo ≤ hi < …511
- `PiCircle.machin_lo_le_hi`
- `PiCircle.machin_nested`: the Machin intervals nest: the lower end climbs and the upper end falls with N
- `PiCircle.machin_slopes`: Machin again, in the turning law itself: 1/5 ⊕ 1/5 ⊕ 1/5 ⊕ 1/5 ⊕ (−1/239) = 1
- `PiCircle.mul_assoc'`
- `PiCircle.neg_one_pow_even`
- `PiCircle.neg_one_pow_odd`
- `PiCircle.norm_conj`
- `PiCircle.norm_mul`: lengths multiply (Brahmagupta–Fibonacci)
- `PiCircle.parity_twice`: the parity point is its own way back: (−2) ◯ (−2) = 0
- `PiCircle.quarter_four`
- `PiCircle.quarter_on_circle`
- `PiCircle.quarter_twice`
- `PiCircle.slope_one_four`
- `PiCircle.slope_one_twice`: the same in slopes: 1 ⊕ 1 = 2/0 = ∞, and four steps of slope 1 come back to slope 0
- `PiCircle.term_shrink`: the terms shrink for 0 ≤ x ≤ 1

## FusedAlgebra.Topics.PowersetDials (48)

- `PowersetDials.W_compl`: **Complement inverts every dial.** The family with dials t, read through A ↦ S ∖ A, is (∏ t) times the family with dials 1/t.
- `PowersetDials.W_ones`: All dials 1: every subset weighs 1, and there are 2ⁿ of them.
- `PowersetDials.W_replicate_one`: W with every dial 1 weighs every subset 1.
- `PowersetDials.cell_compl`: Complement of one element: in becomes out. Weight t on "in" read from the other side is t times weight 1/t on "out".
- `PowersetDials.cell_merge`: **One element merged.** The four ways (in/out, in/out) that land on "out" weigh 1; the three that land on "in" weigh t + s + ts = t ◯ s. Read as coarse-graining: a block of two elements, present when either is, carries the dial t ◯ s.
- `PowersetDials.compl_fixed_dial`: **The only dial complement leaves alone is 1** (the full power set).
- `PowersetDials.compl_swaps_merge`: Complement swaps the two mergers: reciprocal dials combine by the circle operation under intersection exactly as dials combine by the circle operation under union. (1 + 1/t)(1 + 1/s) is the lift of t·s/(1 + t + s), the intersection's dial.
- `PowersetDials.conv_W`: **Merging dialled power sets multiplies nothing new in: the dials combine by the circle operation.** Union-convolving the family with dials ts and the family with dials ss gives the family with dials tᵢ ◯ sᵢ.
- `PowersetDials.euler_squarefree`: **Primes → squarefree numbers.** One prime's dial x = p^(−s) contributes the factor 1 + x; times 1 − x it is 1 − x², the same factor at 2s. Over all primes this is ∏(1 + p^(−s)) = ζ(s)/ζ(2s).
- `PowersetDials.fermi_dirac`: **Naturals as merged squarefree layers (Fermi–Dirac).** The exponent of a prime written in binary: (1 + x)(1 + x²)(1 + x⁴)⋯(1 + x^(2^(k−1))) times (1 − x) is 1 − x^(2^k). So ζ(s) is the merge of the squarefree power sets at dials p^(−s), p^(−2s), p^(−4s), …
- `PowersetDials.half_dial`: **The half dial.** If r·r = 2, then h = r − 1 (that is √2 − 1) has h ◯ h = 1.
- `PowersetDials.half_of_merge`: Merging commutes with halving: if r halves t and r' halves t', then r ◯ r' halves t ◯ t'. (On the line u = log(1 + t), merging is addition and halving is u ↦ u/2.)
- `PowersetDials.half_prob_inter`: The intersection half: two independent copies, intersected, give 1/2.
- `PowersetDials.half_prob_union`: In probabilities (p = t/(1 + t)), the union half is p = 1 − r/2 = 1 − 1/√2: two independent copies, joined, give a fair coin, 1/2.
- `PowersetDials.half_squared_family`: **The half power set, squared, is the power set.** With every dial √2 − 1, the family merged with itself weighs every subset exactly 1: it is P(S).
- `PowersetDials.halves_merge_to_whole`: Exponents of merged pieces add, and complement of participation sends α to 1 − α; the two halves α = 1/2 and 1 − α merge to the whole.
- `PowersetDials.halves_mirror`: In probabilities the two halves are 1 − r/2 and r/2: mirror images about 1/2.
- `PowersetDials.halves_reciprocal`: The intersection half is the reciprocal of the union half: √2 + 1 = 1/(√2 − 1).
- `PowersetDials.inner_sum`
- `PowersetDials.integers_square`: **Integers: ℕ is an exact half of ℤ∖{0}.** Pair n with −n, both carrying the same dial: the total is the square of the naturals' total, so the naturals (a corner half) and the centre half have exactly the same total.
- `PowersetDials.length_vecs`
- `PowersetDials.mth_root_of_full`: m copies merged: the m-fold circle power of the dial. An m-th root of the full power set is a dial with t^(◯m) = 1, that is (1 + t)^m = 2.
- `PowersetDials.no_half_both_ways`: **No single probability is a half both ways.** Joining two copies gives 1/2 and intersecting two copies gives 1/2 cannot both hold.
- `PowersetDials.no_iterate_half`: **No dial construction is a half step of the power set by iteration.** The empty set has exactly one subset (itself), weight 1, so any size rule f coming from dials has f 0 = 1. Then f (f n) = 2ⁿ fails at once: f 1 = f (f 0) = 1, so f (f 1) = 1, not 2. No monotonicity is assumed.
- `PowersetDials.no_iterate_half_rat`: The same over the rationals (real-valued masses): f 0 = 1 forces f 1 = 1.
- `PowersetDials.participation_fixed`: Participation complement c ↦ 1 − c fixes exactly c = 1/2.
- `PowersetDials.partner_fixed_iff`: The fixed points of partnering are exactly the half dials.
- `PowersetDials.partner_one`
- `PowersetDials.partner_partner`: Partnering is an involution.
- `PowersetDials.partner_spec`: Partners combine by the circle operation to 1: (1 + t)(1 + partner t) = 2.
- `PowersetDials.partner_zero`: The corners swap: dial 0 (leave the element out) pairs with dial 1 (all of it).
- `PowersetDials.rational_factor`: **Rationals: the exponent of a prime runs over all of ℤ.** Weighing exponent e by x^|e|, the first 2m + 1 exponents sum to 1 + 2(x + ⋯ + x^m), and times (1 − x) that is 1 + x − 2x^(m+1). The full factor is (1 + x)/(1 − x): the squarefree factor merged with the naturals' factor, so ∏ = ζ(s)²/ζ(2s).
- `PowersetDials.squarefree_from_subsets`: The 32 subsets of the first five primes give 32 different squarefree numbers.
- `PowersetDials.sumL_map_add`
- `PowersetDials.sumL_map_append`
- `PowersetDials.sumL_map_mul`
- `PowersetDials.sumL_map_zero`
- `PowersetDials.sumL_vecs_succ`
- `PowersetDials.summand_split`
- `PowersetDials.tiny_half`: **Tiny dials linearise.** Halving a small dial x by plain participation (x/2) misses the union half only at second order: (x/2) ◯ (x/2) = x + x²/4. Where the dials that matter are tiny (large primes, Poisson points on the line), the naive half and the merging half agree.
- `PowersetDials.total_W`: **The count.** The dialled subsets of an n-element set add up to ∏(1 + tᵢ).
- `PowersetDials.total_W_chain`: The count is the circle-operation chain plus one: ∏(1 + tᵢ) = 1 + (t₁ ◯ … ◯ tₙ).
- `PowersetDials.total_empty`: All dials 0: only the empty set survives, total 1.
- `PowersetDials.total_full`
- `PowersetDials.total_split`: Dials that are only 0 or 1 give 2^(number of ones): the classical split P(S) = P(A) × P(S∖A), the corners of the box of halves.
- `PowersetDials.total_uniform`: All dials equal: (1 + t)ⁿ.
- `PowersetDials.zipWith_replicate`: Merging a constant family with itself combines the dial with itself by the circle operation.
- `PowersetDials.zipWith_zero`: Dial 0 is the unit of merging: the family {∅}.

## FusedAlgebra.Topics.Precession (25)

- `Precession.Q.ext'`
- `Precession.Q.ext'_iff`
- `Precession.Q.mul_assoc`
- `Precession.circ_assoc`
- `Precession.circ_zero`
- `Precession.collinear_commute`
- `Precession.cross_perp_left`
- `Precession.cross_perp_right`
- `Precession.drag_coordinate`: Its relativity-page coordinate 2u/(1 − u) (Doppler factor squared minus one) is ρ − 1, so the ratio of temperatures is the lift.
- `Precession.drag_stack`: Two drags stacked by Einstein addition multiply the temperature ratios.
- `Precession.gyroscope_steady`: Steady precession of a horizontal axle along x: the turn of the angular momentum by the precession, Ωₚ ẑ × L with L = I₃ω₃ x̂ + I₁Ωₚ ẑ, equals the torque exactly when Ωₚ·I₃ω₃ = m·g·l.
- `Precession.larmor_keeps_length`
- `Precession.larmor_keeps_tilt`
- `Precession.lift_circ`: The lift survives non-commutativity.
- `Precession.nsq_mul`: Euler's four-square identity: the quaternion norm multiplies.
- `Precession.pure_commutator`
- `Precession.rodrigues`
- `Precession.rodrigues_same_axis`: Same axis: the cross part vanishes and the tan law (a + b)/(1 − ab) is left.
- `Precession.spinning_bath_drag`: The parallel thread's spinning bath (left movers 4× hotter than right movers): the horizon drags at 3/5 of light speed, against the hotter side.
- `Precession.swap_diff`: The swap's size is k times the commutator.
- `Precession.swap_is_turn`: **Swap = turn**: the swapped result is the original turned (conjugated) by the first input's lift. With L = 1 + ka: L · lift(b ∘ a) = lift(a ∘ b) · L.
- `Precession.swap_keeps_size`
- `Precession.swap_pure`
- `Precession.torque_horizontal`
- `Precession.torque_perp_axle`

## FusedAlgebra.Topics.PvsNP (20)

- `PvsNP.Circ.size_mapC`
- `PvsNP.circ_sAdd_distrib`: On the shifted ruler, ◯ distributes over sAdd (the semiring law, moved by x ↦ 1 + x). Lineage: one of four earlier proofs of the rung-1 distributive law; the unified, standalone proof is `RungOne.circ_sadd_distrib` (Foundations.RungOneSemiring), and Foundations.RungOneLineage rederives this one from it. Standard name: the distributive law of the transported ring (generated semiring with g(x) = 1 + x).
- `PvsNP.evalS_lift`: Transport: a sAdd/◯ circuit is a +/× circuit of the same wiring, seen through x ↦ 1 + x.
- `PvsNP.eval_from_shift`: And back: every +/× circuit is a sAdd/◯ circuit of the same wiring and size.
- `PvsNP.foldCirc_ones`: n gates, 2ⁿ − 1 monomials: 1 + (1 ◯ 1 ◯ … ◯ 1) = 2ⁿ.
- `PvsNP.gf2_circ_is_or`
- `PvsNP.gf2_lift_de_morgan`: The lift 1 + (a ◯ b) = (1 + a)(1 + b) over GF(2) is De Morgan: ¬(a ∨ b) = ¬a ∧ ¬b.
- `PvsNP.gf2_shift_is_not`
- `PvsNP.minplus_assoc`
- `PvsNP.minplus_distrib`
- `PvsNP.modp_period`: The ModₚP family: if qᵖ = 1, the count only matters mod p (through the lift).
- `PvsNP.np_point`
- `PvsNP.np_point_forgets`: q = 0 forgets: one witness and five witnesses give the same answer.
- `PvsNP.parityP_point`
- `PvsNP.qnum_two_nonneg`
- `PvsNP.qnum_two_strictMono`: q = 2 keeps the whole count: different counts give different answers.
- `PvsNP.sAdd_lift`: The shift carries sAdd to +.
- `PvsNP.sharpP_point`
- `PvsNP.shifted_identities`: The identities of the shifted semiring: −1 for sAdd and 0 for ◯; −1 absorbs under ◯.
- `PvsNP.witness_fold`: The dial folded over witnesses is the q-integer of the witness count, q = k + 1.

## FusedAlgebra.Topics.QAcross.Knots (19)

- `QKnots.cinquefoilL_f`: Cinquefoil: V(t) = t⁻² + t⁻⁴ − t⁻⁵ + t⁻⁶ − t⁻⁷.
- `QKnots.figEight_amphichiral`: The figure-eight equals its own mirror image (it is amphichiral), unlike the trefoil.
- `QKnots.figEight_f`: Figure-eight: f = A⁻⁸ − A⁻⁴ + 1 − A⁴ + A⁸, so V(t) = t² − t + 1 − t⁻¹ + t⁻².
- `QKnots.hopf_f`: Hopf link: f = −A⁻¹⁰ − A⁻², so V(t) = −t^(1/2) − t^(5/2).
- `QKnots.jones_at_neg_one`: At t = −1 (the parity point, k = −2) the Jones polynomial gives ± the knot's determinant: 3 for the trefoil (its three 3-colorings beyond the trivial), 5 for the figure-eight and for the cinquefoil.
- `QKnots.jones_at_one`: At t = 1 (the counting point, k = 0) the Jones polynomial only counts components: 1 for every knot, −2 for the two-component Hopf link.
- `QKnots.kink_bracket`: ⟨kink⟩ = −A³ (stored times A³ as −A⁶).
- `QKnots.loop_value_q`: The loop value is minus a q-integer: d = −A⁻²·[2] with [2] = 1 + A⁴ = QDial.qnum (A⁴) 2.
- `QKnots.reidemeister_one`: Reidemeister I: a kink multiplies the bracket by A·d + A⁻¹ = −A³.
- `QKnots.reidemeister_two`: Reidemeister II: in the bracket of two crossings, the cup-cap term has coefficient A² + A·A⁻¹·d + A⁻² = 0, and the parallel term has coefficient A·A⁻¹ = 1.
- `QKnots.state_layers`: The states with k B-smoothings are counted by the cube's layers (Pascal), up to 5 crossings.
- `QKnots.trefoilL_bracket`: ⟨left trefoil⟩ = A⁷ − A³ − A⁻⁵ (stored times A⁹).
- `QKnots.trefoilL_cube`: The trefoil's eight states as (number of B-smoothings, loops). The B-count runs over the layers of the 3-cube (1, 3, 3, 1).
- `QKnots.trefoilL_f`: Left trefoil: f = A⁴ + A¹² − A¹⁶, so V(t) = t⁻¹ + t⁻³ − t⁻⁴.
- `QKnots.trefoilR_f`: Right trefoil: f = −A⁻¹⁶ + A⁻¹² + A⁻⁴, so V(t) = t + t³ − t⁴.
- `QKnots.trefoil_chiral`: ... and the invariant tells them apart: the trefoil is chiral.
- `QKnots.trefoil_mirror`: The two trefoils are mirror images of each other ...
- `QKnots.unknot_f`: The writhe correction removes the kink: the unknot's invariant is 1.
- `QKnots.writhe_cancels`: The writhe factor undoes the kink: (−A³)·(−A⁻³) = 1.

## FusedAlgebra.Topics.QAcross.Partitions (13)

- `QPartitions.eulerProd_30_explicit`
- `QPartitions.euler_inverse_30`: Euler's product times the partition series is 1 (mod q³⁰): the two are inverse.
- `QPartitions.euler_is_circ_30`: 1 + (−q ◯ −q² ◯ …) is Euler's product (the list lift foldCirc_lift, seen on series).
- `QPartitions.four_squares_primes`: So for a prime p, r₄(p) = 8(p + 1): the prime's only divisors are 1 and p.
- `QPartitions.jacobi_four_squares_30`: θ⁴ counts the ways to write n as a sum of four squares (signs and order counted); Jacobi's theorem says that count is 8 × (sum of divisors not divisible by 4). Checked mod q³⁰.
- `QPartitions.lagrange_below_30`: Every n < 30 is a sum of four squares (Lagrange's theorem, checked in this range).
- `QPartitions.no_ramanujan_13`: The congruences are special to 5, 7, 11: for the prime 13 the same pattern p(13n+r) ≡ 0 already fails for every residue r by n ≤ 2 (13n + r ≤ 38).
- `QPartitions.partitions_to_50`
- `QPartitions.pentagonal_30`: Euler's pentagonal theorem, checked mod q³⁰.
- `QPartitions.ramanujan_11`: Ramanujan: p(11n+6) ≡ 0 mod 11, for p(6), p(17), p(28), p(39), p(50).
- `QPartitions.ramanujan_5`: Ramanujan: p(5n+4) ≡ 0 mod 5, for p(4), p(9), …, p(49).
- `QPartitions.ramanujan_7`: Ramanujan: p(7n+5) ≡ 0 mod 7, for p(5), p(12), …, p(47).
- `QPartitions.triple_product_30`: The product side equals θ, mod q³⁰. (θ is the function the Tate gluing makes periodic.)

## FusedAlgebra.Topics.QAcross.QCounting (13)

- `QCounting.gauss_4_2`: The four points of the dial on [4, 2]_q = 1 + q + 2q² + q³ + q⁴: q = 1 counts 6 = C(4,2); q = 2 counts the 35 planes in a 4-dimensional space over the two-element field; q = 0 gives 1; q = −1 gives 2 = C(2,1) (Pascal folded in half).
- `QCounting.gauss_at_neg_one`: At q = −1, [n, k] is C(⌊n/2⌋, ⌊k/2⌋), or 0 when n is even and k odd. Checked n ≤ 8.
- `QCounting.gauss_at_one`: At q = 1 the Gaussian binomial is the binomial coefficient: the layer of the circle-operation cube. (Every n and k, not a finite check.)
- `QCounting.ising_circ`: ... and in circle-operation terms: Z = 2 + u^(◯n) + (−u)^(◯n). Only the even layers of the cube survive (a ring has an even number of domain walls).
- `QCounting.ising_eigen`: The two eigen-directions: a + b = (1 + u)ⁿ and a − b = (1 − u)ⁿ.
- `QCounting.ising_trace`: The partition function of n spins on a ring (up to the factor e^(nβJ)) is the trace: Z = (1 + u)ⁿ + (1 − u)ⁿ.
- `QCounting.peval_padd`
- `QCounting.peval_pzeros`
- `QCounting.q_binomial_theorem`
- `QCounting.quantum_dim_is_loop`: The quantum dimension of the 2-dimensional representation is [2] = q + q⁻¹; with q = A² it is minus the knot loop value d = −A² − A⁻².
- `QCounting.sl2_classical`: The classical limit q → 1: [E, F] = H, i.e. (a + 1)·b − a·(b + 1) = b − a.
- `QCounting.uq_sl2_commutator`: On the irreducible representation of dimension n + 1, with basis v₀ … vₙ, E and F move between neighbours with factors [k]_q and [n − k]_q (symmetric q-integers [m] = (qᵐ − q⁻ᵐ)/(q − q⁻¹)), and K acts by q^(n − 2k). The relation [E, F] = (K − K⁻¹)/(q − q⁻¹) on v_k is [k+1][n−k] − [k][n−k+1] = [n − 2k]. Clearing the denominators (q − q⁻¹)² and writing x = qᵏ, y = q^(n−k) (xi, yi, qi their inverses), it is this ring identity, so the relation holds on every irreducible representation.
- `QCounting.uq_sl2_weight`: K E K⁻¹ = q² E: E moves v_k to v_{k−1}, whose weight q^(n−2k+2) is q² times v_k's.

## FusedAlgebra.Topics.QAcross.QDial (16)

- `QDial.failure_or`: The failure union at k = −1 on {0, 1} is "or". Same truth table as StatFused.or_table, which is over Rat; this one is over Int.
- `QDial.parity_xor`: The parity law is ◯ at k = −2: on {0, 1} it is "exclusive or". Same truth table as StatFused.xor_table, which is over Rat; this one is over Int.
- `QDial.qnum_add`: q-integers add by the circle operation, weight q − 1: [m+n] = [m] ◯[k] [n].
- `QDial.qnum_add_std`: The standard form of the same rule: [m+n] = [m] + qᵐ·[n].
- `QDial.qnum_at_neg_one`: q = −1, k = −2: parity.
- `QDial.qnum_at_one`: q = 1, k = 0: the q-integer is plain counting.
- `QDial.qnum_at_two`: q = 2, k = 1: [n]₂ = 2ⁿ − 1, the circle power of 1 (and the number of nonzero vectors in an n-dimensional space over the two-element field).
- `QDial.qnum_at_zero`: q = 0, k = −1: every nonempty count collapses to 1 (the failure union 1 ◯[−1] 1 = 1).
- `QDial.qnum_eq_wpow`: The q-integer is the circle power of 1 with weight k = q − 1.
- `QDial.qnum_lift`: The lift: 1 + (q − 1)·[n]_q = qⁿ. (So q-counting is circle-operation counting through x ↦ 1 + x.)
- `QDial.qnum_mpow`: (q − 1)·[n]_q is the circle power (q − 1)^(◯n).
- `QDial.qnum_succ`
- `QDial.qnum_three_at_dial`: Small values: [3]_q at the four dial points (counting 3, volume 7, union 1, parity 1).
- `QDial.qnum_zero`
- `QDial.tate_glue_n`: n Tate steps: (1 + a)·qⁿ = 1 + (a ◯ (q − 1)·[n]_q). Going n times round the torus's q-direction is one circle-operation step whose length is (q − 1) times the q-integer [n]_q.
- `QDial.tate_glue_step`: One Tate step: multiplying the lift 1 + a by q is a circle-operation step by q − 1.

## FusedAlgebra.Topics.QuantumAction (22)

- `QuantumAction.M2.ext'`
- `QuantumAction.M2.ext'_iff`
- `QuantumAction.Move.ext'`
- `QuantumAction.Move.ext'_iff`
- `QuantumAction.boost_compose`: Two boosts make one boost, with velocities composed by the plane law w(1 − d·u·v) = u + v.
- `QuantumAction.boost_keeps_form`: The boost keeps the plane's length form t² + d·x², up to the factor 1 + d·u².
- `QuantumAction.galilean_compose`: Galilean end of the dial: at d = 0 velocities simply add.
- `QuantumAction.k_is_a_slide`: Cleared form of: f(x ∘ y) = f(x) ⊕_{d'} f(y), with x ∘ y = (x + y + kxy)/(1 − dxy), f(x) = x/(1 + a·x), a = k/2, d' = d − k²/4, and ⊕_{d'} the k = 0 plane law. Left: numerator of f(N/D) is N, denominator D + aN. Right: numerator x(1+ay) + y(1+ax), denominator (1+ax)(1+ay) − d'·x·y. Cross-multiplied they agree.
- `QuantumAction.mul_assoc`
- `QuantumAction.mul_inv`
- `QuantumAction.mul_one`
- `QuantumAction.newton_from_dial`: The length element √(1 + d·v²) expands as 1 + d·v²/2 + O(d²): the Newtonian term is ½v² for every d. Stated as the exact identity (1 + d v²/2)² = 1 + d v² + (d v²/2)².
- `QuantumAction.one_mul`
- `QuantumAction.parallel_no_cost`: One line, no swap cost: parallel moves commute.
- `QuantumAction.quad_loop`: Going round the quadrilateral 0 → (a,b) → (c,d) → (e,f) → 0 returns with phase = its area.
- `QuantumAction.regge2d_flip`: An edge flip lowers the degrees of the edge's two ends by one and raises the two opposite corners by one, so the total deficit (6 − d₁) + (6 − d₂) + (6 − d₃) + (6 − d₄) is unchanged.
- `QuantumAction.regge2d_sphere`: On a sphere (V − E + F = 2) the deficits add to 12 units of π/3, i.e. 4π (checks E1a, E1c).
- `QuantumAction.regge2d_total`: With equilateral triangles every vertex's deficit is (6 − degree)·π/3. Summed over the vertices, Σ(6 − deg) = 6V − 2E (each edge has two ends), and when every face is a triangle (2E = 3F) this is 6·(V − E + F): six times the Euler number, whatever the triangulation.
- `QuantumAction.swap_costs_area`: ...and differ in phase by exactly the area they span.
- `QuantumAction.swap_same_place`: Swap rule in phase space: the two orders land in the same place...
- `QuantumAction.triangle_loop`: Go along `a`, then `b`, then straight back: a closed triangle.
- `QuantumAction.triangle_loop_reversed`: Reversing a loop reverses its phase.

## FusedAlgebra.Topics.QuantumNoise (21)

- `QuantumNoise.amplitude_damping`
- `QuantumNoise.bit_flip`
- `QuantumNoise.call_rule_gap`: The rule from the call, p + q − pq, is the k = −1 rule. It is right for amplitude damping. For bit-flips it is off by exactly p·q.
- `QuantumNoise.circ_inverse`
- `QuantumNoise.comp_lx`
- `QuantumNoise.comp_ly`
- `QuantumNoise.comp_lz`
- `QuantumNoise.comp_total`
- `QuantumNoise.depolarizing`
- `QuantumNoise.f_fixed`: The vote only has fixed points at −1, 0 and 1 (the clean corner, the wall, the mirror).
- `QuantumNoise.f_helps`: Between the wall and the clean corner the vote always helps.
- `QuantumNoise.faces`: The four faces of the tetrahedron are the four chances (times 4). So "inside the tetrahedron" is exactly "all chances are nonnegative".
- `QuantumNoise.gx_above`
- `QuantumNoise.gx_below`
- `QuantumNoise.gz_above`
- `QuantumNoise.gz_below`
- `QuantumNoise.macwilliams_rep3`
- `QuantumNoise.maj3_polar`
- `QuantumNoise.odd3_polar`: Phase-flips go straight through the bit-flip code: an odd number of them is a logical phase-flip, so the polarization is cubed.
- `QuantumNoise.stacked_bit_flips`: n stacked bit-flips: the chance of an odd number of flips P satisfies 1 − 2P = (1 − 2p)ⁿ.
- `QuantumNoise.undetected_two_ways`: The chance a 3-repetition code misses an error (all three flip), computed two ways: on the code's own cube, and from the dual code's layers in the polarization 1 − 2p.

## FusedAlgebra.Topics.Relativity (30)

- `Relativity.boost_mul`: Boosts multiply as speeds velocity-add: B(u)B(v) = (1 + uv)·B(u ⊕ v).
- `Relativity.boost_mul_vel`
- `Relativity.cone_fixed`: The light cone is fixed: a ray with T = X (or T = −X) stays one.
- `Relativity.cone_fixed'`
- `Relativity.corner_tax`: The corner tax: w = u ⊕ v satisfies w = u + v − u·v·w.
- `Relativity.dop_back`: The way back: u = d/(2 + d).
- `Relativity.dop_lift`: Its lift is the Doppler factor squared (Bondi's k²).
- `Relativity.dop_vel`: **Velocity addition is the circle operation in disguise**: d(u ⊕ v) = d(u) ◯ d(v).
- `Relativity.dop_wall`: d carries the light wall −1 onto the circle operation's wall −1.
- `Relativity.gap_to_light`: How far below light: 1 − u ⊕ v = (1 − u)(1 − v)/(1 + uv).
- `Relativity.hyperbola`: The hyperbola: T'² − X'² = (1 − u²)(T² − X²), and T² − X² = P·M.
- `Relativity.hyperbola_cone`
- `Relativity.kick_step`: One more kick in the numerator/denominator form is velocity addition.
- `Relativity.kicks_lift`: **n kicks lift to the circle power**: (D + N)/(D − N) = ((1 + u)/(1 − u))ⁿ.
- `Relativity.kicks_minus`
- `Relativity.kicks_plus`: The lift of n kicks splits: D + N = (1 + u)ⁿ and D − N = (1 − u)ⁿ.
- `Relativity.landscape_point`: The landscape law (a + b + k·ab)/(1 − q·ab) at k = 0, q = −1/c² is velocity addition.
- `Relativity.landscape_walls`: Its walls 1 + k·a + q·a² = (1 − a/c)(1 + a/c): the light cone.
- `Relativity.rung_natural_k`: Rung s's third-layer dot is (s, s(s − 1)/4). In units λ the dot moves to (λk, λ²q). With s = t² and λ = 2/t it lands at (2t, t² − 1).
- `Relativity.rung_natural_q`
- `Relativity.rung_one_is_multadd`: t = 1: one wall at −1/2, the other at infinity: the circle operation at weight 2.
- `Relativity.rung_parabola`: That curve is the border parabola moved down by 1: q = k²/4 − 1.
- `Relativity.rung_raw_walls`: In raw units rung s = t² has walls −2/(t(1 + t)) and 2/(t(1 − t)): a speed limit near 2/√s as s → 0, light speed growing to infinity at plain addition.
- `Relativity.rung_walls`: Its walls factor: 1 + 2t·a + (t² − 1)a² = (1 + (1 + t)a)(1 − (1 − t)a).
- `Relativity.rung_zero_is_velocity`: t = 0: walls ±1, velocity addition.
- `Relativity.squeeze_minus`
- `Relativity.squeeze_plus`: On the light-cone axes P = T + X, M = T − X a boost is a squeeze.
- `Relativity.squeeze_ratio`: The squeeze ratio is the lift 1 + d.
- `Relativity.under_light`: Two speeds under light, both forward, add to a speed under light.
- `Relativity.velC_units`

## FusedAlgebra.Topics.RepetitionDial (22)

- `RepetitionDial.atoms_compose`: **Atoms compose by ◯[k].** If the k-powers of two atoms are 1 + ka and 1 + kb, their product is 1 + k(a ◯[k] b).
- `RepetitionDial.atoms_compose3`: Three atoms: the order of composing does not matter.
- `RepetitionDial.bose_one`
- `RepetitionDial.bose_pos`: **Bosonic cells never cap:** any number of copies has a way in.
- `RepetitionDial.bose_zero_succ`: One bosonic cell: exactly one way for every n (that is 1/(1 − x)).
- `RepetitionDial.cap_at_m`: **k = 1/m caps each element at m copies.**
- `RepetitionDial.cellCoeff_neg_one`: k = −1: one bosonic cell, every number of copies counted once.
- `RepetitionDial.cellCoeff_one_two`: k = 1: one exclusive cell, so no element twice (coefficient of x² is 0).
- `RepetitionDial.cellCoeff_zero_succ`: At k = 0 each step divides by n + 1: the coefficients are 1/n! (e^x).
- `RepetitionDial.cells_exclusive`: **The k = 1/m coefficients are cell counts:** cellCoeff (1/m) n · mⁿ = C(m, n), the number of ways to fill n of m exclusive cells.
- `RepetitionDial.excl_absorb`: Absorption: (n + 1)·C(m, n + 1) = (m − n)·C(m, n).
- `RepetitionDial.excl_one_two`: One exclusive cell: one way for 0 or 1 copy, none for 2 or more.
- `RepetitionDial.excl_over`
- `RepetitionDial.excl_zero_right`
- `RepetitionDial.fluctuation`: **Fluctuation.** With n = x/(1 + kx): n(1 − kn) = x/(1 + kx)². Fermions (k = 1) quiet the count, bosons (k = −1) bunch it, k = 0 is Poisson.
- `RepetitionDial.gbinom_cells`: **The binomial series is the cell polynomial.** For k ≠ 0, C(1/k, n)·kⁿ = ∏_{j<n}(1 − jk)/n!.
- `RepetitionDial.inverse_gap`: **k from one measurement:** 1/n − 1/x = k.
- `RepetitionDial.natCast_succ_pos`
- `RepetitionDial.semion_no_constant_k`: **No constant k for semions:** two different densities cannot share one gap k.
- `RepetitionDial.semion_one_density`: With w(1 + w) = 1/x² (u = 1/x) and n = 1/(w + 1/2), a constant gap 1/n − 1/x = k means w + 1/2 − k = u, so −2kw + (1/2 − k)² = 0.
- `RepetitionDial.two_thirds_negative`: **A positive k that is not 1/m gives a negative count:** at k = 2/3 the coefficient of x³ is −1/54.
- `RepetitionDial.unlimited_when_nonpos`: **k ≤ 0 never caps:** every coefficient is positive.

## FusedAlgebra.Topics.Riemann (10)

- `CircleOp.Riemann.boson_times_fermion`: ∏(1 + x) · ∏(1 − x) = ∏(1 − x²), written with the two cube folds: (1 + fold₁ l)·(1 − fold₋₁ l) = 1 − fold₋₁ (l²).
- `CircleOp.Riemann.cubeFold_lift`: The fold lifts to the Euler product: 1 + k·fold = ∏ (1 + k·x).
- `CircleOp.Riemann.cubeFold_one`: At k = 1 the fold is the plain circle operation.
- `CircleOp.Riemann.elliptic_no_int_root`: Elliptic side, no integer root: if 0 < d and k² < 4d then 1 + k·u + d·u² ≠ 0 for every integer u. (4d·(1 + ku + du²) = (2du + k)² + (4d − k²) > 0.)
- `CircleOp.Riemann.genus1_p5_elliptic`: The genus-1 curve y² = x³ + x + 1 at p = 5: a = p + 1 − #E = −3 (#E = 9), dial point (k, d) = (3, 5), Δ = 9 − 20 < 0: elliptic. (Worked example, by decide.)
- `CircleOp.Riemann.heat_step_factor`: One heat step: multiplying a kernel by 1 + b²u² acts on its transform as 1 − b²D² = (1 − bD)(1 + bD), a product of two Laguerre steps. Algebraic core.
- `CircleOp.Riemann.int_sq_nonneg`: Squares of integers are nonnegative (local helper).
- `CircleOp.Riemann.localFactor_reciprocal`: Roots of 1 + k·u + d·u² pair up as u and 1/(d·u): the reflection that fixes the circle |u| = d^(-1/2) swaps them.
- `CircleOp.Riemann.prodL_map_mul`: Products of lists multiply pointwise.
- `CircleOp.Riemann.step_identity_kernel`: Kernel side of the Lee–Yang step identity, with y = xᵖ and c = (a+1)p: c·y·(1 − y)^a + c·(1 − y)^(a+1) = c·(1 − y)^a. (−x·d/dx of (1 − xᵖ)^(a+1) is the first term; this is the whole identity.)

## FusedAlgebra.Topics.RiemannElliptic (10)

- `CircleOp.RiemannElliptic.cayley_circle`: |ρ − 1|² − |ρ|² = 1 − 2x for ρ = x + i·y: the unit circle |z| = 1 is the line x = 1/2.
- `CircleOp.RiemannElliptic.dickson_pair`: Powers of z and z' with z·z' = 1, two at a time.
- `CircleOp.RiemannElliptic.genus1_p25_count`: Over F₂₅ the same curve has 25 + 1 − D₂(−3, q = 5) = 25 + 1 − (9 − 10) = 27 points.
- `CircleOp.RiemannElliptic.genus1_p5_elliptic'`: y² = x³ + x + 1 over F₅ has a₅ = −3; its factor 1 + 3u + 5u² is elliptic: 9 < 20.
- `CircleOp.RiemannElliptic.li_term`: The Li term of one zero pair: 2 − zⁿ − z'ⁿ = 2 − Dₙ(z + z').
- `CircleOp.RiemannElliptic.mirror_pair`: Zeta's prime factor with its mirror: (1 − u)(1 − p·u), discriminant (p − 1)².
- `CircleOp.RiemannElliptic.pair_disc`: The pairing lemma: the discriminant of the product is a square.
- `CircleOp.RiemannElliptic.pair_factor`: With z·z' = 1 the pair factor has d = 1 and k = −(z + z').
- `CircleOp.RiemannElliptic.pair_factor_disc`: Its discriminant is (z − z')²: k² − 4 = (z − z')² when z·z' = 1.
- `CircleOp.RiemannElliptic.pair_product`: Two degree-1 factors multiply to the dial point k = −(a + b), d = a·b.

## FusedAlgebra.Topics.RiemannNeighbours (9)

- `CircleOp.RiemannNeighbours.bartholdi_d_eff`: Bartholdi's bump weight t is the d dial in disguise: d_eff = (1 − t)(q + t).
- `CircleOp.RiemannNeighbours.four_normForm`: Completing the square: 4·N = (2m + kn)² + (4d − k²)·n².
- `CircleOp.RiemannNeighbours.ihara_discriminant`: The discriminant of 1 − λu + qu² is our Δ = k² − 4d at (k, d) = (−λ, q).
- `CircleOp.RiemannNeighbours.leeYang_two`: Two coupled spins: 1 + a(z₁ + z₂) + z₁z₂ = 1 + a·(z₁ ◯[k] z₂) whenever a·k = 1.
- `CircleOp.RiemannNeighbours.leeYang_uniform_discriminant`: The uniform field z² + 2az + 1 is the dial point (2a, 1); its Δ is 4a² − 4.
- `CircleOp.RiemannNeighbours.normForm_frobenius`: deg(m − n·F) with F² = a·F − p is the norm at (k, d) = (−a, p).
- `CircleOp.RiemannNeighbours.normForm_frobenius_one`: Weil's degree form for a curve over F_p: deg(1 − F) = p + 1 − a_p, the point count.
- `CircleOp.RiemannNeighbours.selberg_complete_square`: 4(s² − s + λ) = (2s − 1)² + (4λ − 1): the line Re s = 1/2 is the elliptic side λ ≥ 1/4.
- `CircleOp.RiemannNeighbours.selberg_factor_dial`: Selberg's local factor as a dial point: k = −1, d = λ.

## FusedAlgebra.Topics.RiemannRound10 (8)

- `CircleOp.RiemannRound10.fold_at_minus_one`: At k = -1 the layers alternate in sign (the Möbius signs of 1/ζ).
- `CircleOp.RiemannRound10.jet2`: Two-factor rung-2 fold: the layers 1, e1, e2.
- `CircleOp.RiemannRound10.jet3`: Three-factor rung-2 fold: det(I + hS) = 1 + hP + h²Q + h³R for diagonal S.
- `CircleOp.RiemannRound10.newton2`: Newton's inequality for two variables: 4 e2 ≤ e1².
- `CircleOp.RiemannRound10.rung3_log_mult`: Rung 3 read in log coordinates: exponents add.
- `CircleOp.RiemannRound10.wheel210`: Mod 210 the wheel of 2, 3, 5, 7 leaves 48 spokes.
- `CircleOp.RiemannRound10.wheel30`: Mod 30 the wheel of 2, 3, 5 leaves 8 spokes: 30·(1/2)(2/3)(4/5).
- `CircleOp.RiemannRound10.wheel6`: Spokes of the wheel mod 6 left open by 2 and 3.

## FusedAlgebra.Topics.RiemannRound3 (10)

- `CircleOp.RiemannRound3.barrier_sq`: |1 − r·e^{iφ}|² with c = cos φ, q = sin φ, c² + q² = 1.
- `CircleOp.RiemannRound3.conj_disc`: Its discriminant is −4y², negative off the line.
- `CircleOp.RiemannRound3.conj_pair`: A conjugate pair x ± i·y, multiplied out without i: the fused point (2x, x² + y²).
- `CircleOp.RiemannRound3.fused_disc`: The discriminant of the fused point is the squared gap.
- `CircleOp.RiemannRound3.fused_pair`: Vieta: a pair of roots is the fused point (s, p) = (a + b, a·b).
- `CircleOp.RiemannRound3.heat_pair_disc`: The flowed pair's discriminant is a straight line in t.
- `CircleOp.RiemannRound3.heat_pair_gap`: Starting from a real pair with gap g, the discriminant is g² + 8t.
- `CircleOp.RiemannRound3.pointwise_fixes_zeros`: If g fixes 0 and only 0 goes to 0, then g ∘ f has exactly the zeros of f.
- `CircleOp.RiemannRound3.real_ring`: Real shifts ±v: the average adds v² to the constant term (a rewind step).
- `CircleOp.RiemannRound3.turned_ring`: Shifts turned by i (v·v = −w·w): the average subtracts w² (a forward step).

## FusedAlgebra.Topics.RiemannRound4 (12)

- `CircleOp.RiemannRound4.height_free_at_extremum`: Laguerre at height s, at an extremum (f′ = 0): s·0² − f·f″ = −f·f″ for every s, so the local fused discriminant does not depend on the height of the hypernatural log.
- `CircleOp.RiemannRound4.height_is_degree`: Height s = 1 − 1/n (stated as s·n = n − 1): n times the height-s Laguerre expression is Laguerre's degree-n expression (n − 1)·f′² − n·f·f″.
- `CircleOp.RiemannRound4.laguerre_ladder`: With L = h1² − h0·h2, the flow gives L_t = −2h1h3 + h2² + h0h4 (from h_k ↦ −h_(k+2)), L'' = h2² − h0h4, and L(H') = h2² − h1h3. The ladder identity: L_t = −L'' + 2·L(H').
- `CircleOp.RiemannRound4.letter_centre`: A letter with c = 1 fixes z exactly when (1 − u)·z = 1: its centre is the Euler factor 1/(1 − u), u = p^(−s).
- `CircleOp.RiemannRound4.letter_order_gap`: Two letters z ↦ u z + c and z ↦ v z + c: the two orders differ by c·(u − v). With c = 0 (shared centre 0, the Euler product) they commute.
- `CircleOp.RiemannRound4.link_split`: A degree-2 link 1 + k u + d u² splits as (1 − α u)(1 − β u) when α + β = −k and αβ = d.
- `CircleOp.RiemannRound4.local_disc_rate`: At an extremum (h1 = 0) with D = −8h0/h2 and κ = h4/h2 − (h3/h2)² = (log H'')'': the time derivative of D along the moving extremum, times h2³, is −8(−h2² + h0·h4)·h2 + 8·h0·h3², and 8 + D·κ times h2³ is 8h2³ − 8h0(h4h2 − h3²). They agree: dD/dt = 8 + D·κ.
- `CircleOp.RiemannRound4.pair_flow_disc`: The flow on a lone pair: H = (x − a)² − c − 2t solves the flow (H_t = −2 = −H''), and the local fused discriminant −8H(a)/H''(a) is 4c + 8t: a straight line through the merger.
- `CircleOp.RiemannRound4.pair_ladder_rates`: For the lone pair the ladder reads 4 − 8 = −4 under the rewind: the diffusion term L'' = 4 is beaten 2 : 1 by the sink 2·L(H') = 8 (h0 = u² − c, h1 = 2u, h2 = 2, h3 = h4 = 0).
- `CircleOp.RiemannRound4.pair_laguerre`: Laguerre's expression of the lone pair: H'² − H·H'' = 2(x − a)² + 2c, so its least value is 2c, half the fused discriminant 4c.
- `CircleOp.RiemannRound4.pair_local_disc`: For H = (x − a)² − c (roots a ± √c, fused point s = 2a, p = a² − c), the local fused discriminant −8·H(a)/H''(a) = −8·(−c)/2 equals s² − 4p = 4c. Stated with H'' = 2 cleared.
- `CircleOp.RiemannRound4.sin_steady`: sin is a steady state: its jets at a crest satisfy h2 = −h0, h3 = 0, h4 = h0, and then 8·h2³ − 8·h0·(h4·h2 − h3²) = 0, so dD/dt = 0 (the zeros of sin do not move).

## FusedAlgebra.Topics.RiemannRound5 (10)

- `CircleOp.RiemannRound5.bends_meet_twice`: Third-order coefficients: the hypernatural log at height s has s²/4 + s/12, the homothety log has (k² + 3k + 2)/6. At s = 1 + k (same bend) their gap, times 12, is k(k + 1).
- `CircleOp.RiemannRound5.boost_keeps_xp`: A boost multiplies the light-cone coordinates by λ and μ with λμ = 1, so x·p is kept: the Berry–Keating orbits xp = E are boost orbits.
- `CircleOp.RiemannRound5.light_same_speed`: Wick rotation turns the zero speed v into i·v. With i² = −1, (i v)(−i v) = v²: the same speed, at a right angle, so the zero leaves the line.
- `CircleOp.RiemannRound5.lk_factor`: One prime factor of the prime cube: X = (1 − k u)^(−1/k), so X^(−k) = 1 − k u and k·l_k(X) = 1 − (1 − k u) = k u. The homothety log returns u = p^(−s) exactly.
- `CircleOp.RiemannRound5.lk_product`: With a = X^(−k), b = Y^(−k), k·l_k(XY) = 1 − ab and k·l_k(X) = 1 − a. The product law k·l(XY) = k·l(X) + k·l(Y) − (k·l(X))(k·l(Y)) is the circle operation at −k, cleared of k.
- `CircleOp.RiemannRound5.necklace_two`: Necklace step at N = 2: log of a homothety cube at dial c has p^(−2s)-coefficient c²/2, and zeta(s)^c · zeta(2s)^M(c,2) supplies c/2 + M with 2M = c² − c.
- `CircleOp.RiemannRound5.neff_two`: Two real zeros, a_i = 1/(x − r_i): f′/f = a₁ + a₂, f″/f = 2a₁a₂, so (1 − h*)(a₁ + a₂)² = a₁² + a₂², i.e. h* = 1 − 1/N_eff with N_eff = (a₁ + a₂)²/(a₁² + a₂²).
- `CircleOp.RiemannRound5.pair_offline_detected`: A non-real pair: f = (x − a)² + b². Laguerre's expression f′² − f·f″ = 2(x − a)² − 2b², negative at x = a when b ≠ 0, so the critical height there is above 1. (Compare round 4's real pair: + 2c.)
- `CircleOp.RiemannRound5.qmulti_11`: Two letters, once each: the two orderings weigh 1 + q, and the q-multinomial (q;q)₂/((q;q)₁(q;q)₁) is the same: (1 − q)(1 − q²) = ((1 − q)(1 − q))(1 + q).
- `CircleOp.RiemannRound5.qmulti_21`: Exponents (2, 1): the three orderings weigh 1 + q + q², and (q;q)₃ = (q;q)₂ · (q;q)₁ · (1 + q + q²).

## FusedAlgebra.Topics.RiemannRound6 (15)

- `CircleOp.RiemannRound6.disc_scaling`: The local factor 1 + k(tu) + d(tu)² has discriminant t²(k² − 4d): the sign of Δ is kept.
- `CircleOp.RiemannRound6.geometric4`: The m = 0 term of the order dial is ∏_j Odds at k q^j, i.e. ζ to the power k(1 + q + q² + …); after the 1/k power the leading exponent is the geometric sum 1/(1 − q). Finite form:
- `CircleOp.RiemannRound6.hex_design2`: Second moments of the three directions (1, 0), (1/2, ±√3/2) are round, ×4 to clear halves.
- `CircleOp.RiemannRound6.hex_design4`: Fourth moments are round too, ×16.
- `CircleOp.RiemannRound6.hex_p4`: The hexagon's three face projections of a lattice point a·b₁ + c·b₂ are a + c, a and c. Their fourth powers sum to twice the square of the hexagonal form: face power 4 is the round gauge squared.
- `CircleOp.RiemannRound6.hex_six_along_x`: Sixth moments, ×64: along (1, 0) the sum is 66 ...
- `CircleOp.RiemannRound6.hex_six_along_y`: ... and along (0, 1) it is 54, so face power 6 is not round.
- `CircleOp.RiemannRound6.hstar`: L = d(x² − w²), L′ = 2dx, L″ = 2d. Cleared: 2x² · L L″ = L′² · (x² − w²), i.e. h* = (x² − w²)/(2x²) = 1/2 − w²/(2x²): at most 1/2 when the roots are real (w² ≥ 0), above 1/2 when they are complex.
- `CircleOp.RiemannRound6.necklace_four`: Necklace step at N = 4: c⁴ = M(c,1) + 2M(c,2) + 4M(c,4), with M(c,1) = c, 2M(c,2) = c² − c, 4M(c,4) = c⁴ − c².
- `CircleOp.RiemannRound6.parabola`: The scaled point (tk, t²d) stays on the parabola d = c k² through (k, d), cleared of division.
- `CircleOp.RiemannRound6.seventeen_is_sum`
- `CircleOp.RiemannRound6.thirty_four_not_sum`: Fourth powers above 34 start at 3⁴ = 81, so a, b < 3 covers every case.
- `CircleOp.RiemannRound6.two_is_sum`
- `CircleOp.RiemannRound6.wick_disc`
- `CircleOp.RiemannRound6.wick_hex`: x = a + c/2, y = (√3/2)c; Wick rotation y ↦ iy turns x² + y² into x² − y². Cleared (×4): (2a + c)² − (s c)² = 2(2a² + 2ac − c²), a form of discriminant 2² + 4·2 = 12.

## FusedAlgebra.Topics.RiemannRound7 (18)

- `CircleOp.RiemannRound7.bbm_im`
- `CircleOp.RiemannRound7.bbm_re`: With s = σ + iγ, E = i(2s − 1) = −2γ + i(2σ − 1): multiplying by i = (0, 1) on pairs gives real part 0·(2σ − 1) − 1·(2γ) and imaginary part 0·(2γ) + 1·(2σ − 1).
- `CircleOp.RiemannRound7.bbm_real_on_line`: On the critical line (σ + σ = 1) the imaginary part vanishes: E is real.
- `CircleOp.RiemannRound7.curvature_square`: 4d(1 + k u + d u²) = (2 d u + k)² + (4d − k²): with Δ = k² − 4d < 0 the factor is a sum of a square and a positive constant, and its two roots are conjugate with product 1/d, i.e. on |u| = d^(−1/2).
- `CircleOp.RiemannRound7.cyclotomic_six`: Cyclotomic identity at N = 6: Σ_{d | 6} d·M(t, d) = t⁶, with M(t,1) = t, 2M(t,2) = t² − t, 3M(t,3) = t³ − t, 6M(t,6) = t⁶ − t³ − t² + t.
- `CircleOp.RiemannRound7.fermi_local`: Fermi local factor: 1 + u = (1 − u²)/(1 − u), so Z_F = ζ(β)/ζ(2β). Cleared:
- `CircleOp.RiemannRound7.gauss21_at_minus_one`: Gaussian binomial [2 choose 1]_q = 1 + q vanishes at q = −1: two distinct primes in a word cancel at the q = −1 end of the order dial.
- `CircleOp.RiemannRound7.gauss31_at_minus_one`: [3 choose 1]_q = 1 + q + q² equals 1 = (1 choose 0) at q = −1 (one odd part).
- `CircleOp.RiemannRound7.gauss41_at_minus_one`: [4 choose 1]_q = 1 + q + q² + q³ vanishes at q = −1 (n even, k odd).
- `CircleOp.RiemannRound7.gauss42_at_minus_one`: [4 choose 2]_q = 1 + q + 2q² + q³ + q⁴ equals 2 = (2 choose 1) at q = −1: the rule [n; m]_{−1} = multinomial(⌊n/2⌋; ⌊m_i/2⌋) when at most one m_i is odd.
- `CircleOp.RiemannRound7.ihara_factor`: Ihara: (1 − a u)(1 − b u) = 1 − λ u + q u² with λ = a + b, q = a b.
- `CircleOp.RiemannRound7.ihara_spread`: The roots' spread is the discriminant: (a − b)² = λ² − 4q. Real spread (|λ| > 2√q) is exactly a non-Ramanujan eigenvalue.
- `CircleOp.RiemannRound7.ladder_geometric`: Fugacity ladder: the exponent of log ζ in G_k is q^k + q^(k+1) + … = q^k/(1 − q). Finite form with p = q^k:
- `CircleOp.RiemannRound7.lee_yang_bond`: Lee–Yang for one ferromagnetic bond: Z(z) = 1 + 2 a z + z² = (z + a)² + (1 − a²); with 0 < a < 1 the roots are conjugate with product 1, so they lie on |z| = 1.
- `CircleOp.RiemannRound7.rung_gap`: Neighbouring rungs differ by exactly one power of ζ: e₀ − e₁ = (1 − q)/(1 − q). Cleared:
- `CircleOp.RiemannRound7.witt_half`: M(1/2, 2) = −1/8 is not whole: with 2t = 1, 8·M(t,2) = 4(t² − t) = −1.
- `CircleOp.RiemannRound7.witt_three`: M(3, N) for N = 2, 4 is 3, 18.
- `CircleOp.RiemannRound7.witt_two`: M(2, N) for N = 2, 3, 4, 6 is 1, 2, 3, 9 (Lyndon words in two letters).

## FusedAlgebra.Topics.RiemannRound8 (12)

- `CircleOp.RiemannRound8.area_genus`: Gauss–Bonnet at curvature −1: area/π = −2χ = 4g − 4 for genus g.
- `CircleOp.RiemannRound8.cm_two`: Two-particle heat flow: velocities ±2/d give relative speed 4/d, and −2·4·d = −8·d (the cleared form of z̈ = −8/d³).
- `CircleOp.RiemannRound8.cover_chi`: The orientation double cover of k crosscaps is orientable of genus k − 1 (χ doubles).
- `CircleOp.RiemannRound8.fermi_residue`: Fermi end of the fused residue: (1 + 1/p)(1 − 1/p) = 1 − 1/p², cleared.
- `CircleOp.RiemannRound8.fused_absorb`: The fused centre absorbs: if k c = −1 then a ∘ₖ c = c.
- `CircleOp.RiemannRound8.fused_shift`: Fusion is multiplication after the shift a ↦ 1 + k a.
- `CircleOp.RiemannRound8.glide_det`: Flat glide weight: the transverse map of a glide is −1, and |1 − (−1)| = 2.
- `CircleOp.RiemannRound8.klein_sectors`: Klein bottle: even sector (2a − b) plus odd sector (2a + b) gives the torus 4a.
- `CircleOp.RiemannRound8.lax_two_nilpotent`: The 2×2 Calogero–Moser Lax matrix (x, x; −x, −x) squares to zero: nilpotent.
- `CircleOp.RiemannRound8.mobius_circle`: Möbius re-centring w = 1/(s − 1): |w + 1| = 1 ⇔ |s| = |s − 1| ⇔ 2 Re s = 1.
- `CircleOp.RiemannRound8.pair_collision`: Rewinding z² − a: the zeros meet when a + 2t = 0; with half-gap² a = d²/4 that is t = −d²/8.
- `CircleOp.RiemannRound8.rung`: One rung of the twisted ladder: a glide's even and odd ladders multiply to the cover's rung.

## FusedAlgebra.Topics.RiemannRound9 (12)

- `CircleOp.RiemannRound9.curvature_roots`: Curvature dial: roots a, b of 1 − x + d x² (a + b = 1, ab = d) give (1 − a)(1 − b) = d.
- `CircleOp.RiemannRound9.empty_interval`: An empty Gram interval means S falls by one across it.
- `CircleOp.RiemannRound9.fermi_factor`: The Fermi primon gas (k = −1): (1 + u)(1 − u) = 1 − u², i.e. ζ(s)/ζ(2s).
- `CircleOp.RiemannRound9.good_parity`: A good Gram point (S even) stays good when S moves by two.
- `CircleOp.RiemannRound9.gram_interval_count`: Zeros in a Gram interval: 1 plus the jump of S.
- `CircleOp.RiemannRound9.gram_law_iff`: Gram's law holds in an interval exactly when S does not jump across it.
- `CircleOp.RiemannRound9.rosser_block`: Zeros in a Gram block of length l: l plus the jump of S.
- `CircleOp.RiemannRound9.rosser_iff`: Rosser's rule holds in a block exactly when S takes the same value at its two ends.
- `CircleOp.RiemannRound9.three_exact`: The same for three intervals: the counting step behind a "perfect rule".
- `CircleOp.RiemannRound9.time_reversal_double`: Time reversal pairs each orbit with its reverse: the weight doubles (A = 2).
- `CircleOp.RiemannRound9.turing_squeeze`: Turing's squeeze: zeros found on the line ≤ all zeros, and all ≤ found, so equal.
- `CircleOp.RiemannRound9.two_exact`: Two intervals, each with at least one zero, and two zeros in all: one each.

## FusedAlgebra.Topics.RiemannUnified (7)

- `CircleOp.RiemannUnified.antiderivative_offline`: Antiderivative parent: if Jensen's formula asks for at least 2N zeros in a disc but at most N + 1 of them are real, then at least N - 1 are off the line.
- `CircleOp.RiemannUnified.half_filling`: Two-level bath: k′ = −(1 + E) with E = e^{β(Δ−μ−ΩJ)}; half filling (E = 1) is exactly k′ = −2.
- `CircleOp.RiemannUnified.rolle_gaps`: Rolle bookkeeping: n real zeros of a parent leave n - 1 gaps, each holding a zero of the child.
- `CircleOp.RiemannUnified.three_level_threshold`: Three-level symmetric bath with weights (w, 1−2w, w), w = m/N with 0 < 2m < N: the factor 1 − 2w + 2w·cosh is a Pólya universal factor iff (N − 2m) ≤ 2m, i.e. w ≥ 1/4.
- `CircleOp.RiemannUnified.turan_control`: The control sequence 1/(k+1) is strictly log-convex: k(k+2) < (k+1)², so every Turán test fails.
- `CircleOp.RiemannUnified.two_spin_antiferro`
- `CircleOp.RiemannUnified.two_spin_ferro`: Two spins near a zero of G: Z ≈ G² − J·G′². At the zero, Z = −J·G′², negative for J > 0 (two real zeros split off) and positive for J < 0 (none).

## FusedAlgebra.Topics.RuleBreakers (14)

- `RuleBreakers.cycle_rule`: the 4-cycle 0–1–2–3–0 with diagonals 1: the rule is the product of (w − 1)² over its pairs
- `RuleBreakers.cycle_two_breaks`: the cycle breaks the rule when its weights avoid 1: an example with weight 2
- `RuleBreakers.diamond_rule`: the 4-cycle with one diagonal, one weight k
- `RuleBreakers.fourth_wave_forces`: four waves need the pair of roots to be the cube roots of unity (t² + t + 1 = 0); against the quadratic that leaves (k + 2)·t = 0, so k = −2
- `RuleBreakers.omega_cube_root`: ω² really is −1 − ω in this arithmetic: ω·ω + ω + 1 = 0
- `RuleBreakers.path_obeys`: a path of three pairs obeys the rule for all weights
- `RuleBreakers.paw_obeys`: a triangle with a pendant wave obeys the rule for all weights
- `RuleBreakers.star_obeys`: a star obeys the rule for all weights
- `RuleBreakers.tetrahedral_waves`: the waves (1, −1/2), (ω, −ω/2), (ω², −ω²/2): every pair has weight −2 (denominators nonzero), and each has weight a/b = −2 to the wave (0, ∞)
- `RuleBreakers.third_wave`: with waves (0, ∞) and (1, 1/k), a third wave (t, t/k) has weight k to both exactly when t is a root of t² − (k + 1)t + 1 (numerator of the cross ratio minus k)
- `RuleBreakers.triangle_obeys`: a triangle with a lone fourth wave obeys the rule
- `RuleBreakers.uniform_minus_two_obeys`: … and k = −2 is
- `RuleBreakers.uniform_rule`: every pair of four waves at weight k
- `RuleBreakers.uniform_three_breaks`: so k = 3 (or any k other than 0, 1, −2) is not carried by four point-waves

## FusedAlgebra.Topics.Rulecraft (10)

- `CircleOp.Rulecraft.balance`: Balance: moving d away from the even split loses exactly d².
- `CircleOp.Rulecraft.balance_le`
- `CircleOp.Rulecraft.boundary`
- `CircleOp.Rulecraft.fexp_not_comm_values`
- `CircleOp.Rulecraft.fused_price_expand`: Plain and fused prices for k extra votes differ by the 2k strips.
- `CircleOp.Rulecraft.fused_price_step`: Fused voice price: total voice V costs V² - 1, so the next unit of voice costs 2V + 1.
- `CircleOp.Rulecraft.panel_grid`: A panel of two: area = own corners + cross corners.
- `CircleOp.Rulecraft.recovery`: Losing n parts of n+1 needs a gain of n: 90% lost (n = 9) needs +900%.
- `CircleOp.Rulecraft.sq_nonneg'`
- `CircleOp.Rulecraft.thermostat_residue`: The thermostat: undoing +d by -d leaves the corner square -d².

## FusedAlgebra.Topics.RungShape (17)

- `RungShape.addPow_eq`: Rung 0, count d: the line d·a.
- `RungShape.axis_rung1`: Flat along the axes: rung 1 adds nothing on the a-axis (the identity line).
- `RungShape.cube_count`: Rung 1, count d, edge 1: the d-cube's corners, 2^d (so the circle power is 2^d − 1).
- `RungShape.flow_closed`: The near-identity flow in closed form: φ_t(x) = 2x/(2 − t x) satisfies its defining relation.
- `RungShape.flow_heights_add`
- `RungShape.flow_zero`: Whole rung height 0 is the identity of the near-identity flow.
- `RungShape.ruled_rung1`: Rung 1's graph is ruled: along any fixed a it is a straight line in b.
- `RungShape.ruled_rung1'`: ... and along any fixed b it is a straight line in a (doubly ruled saddle).
- `RungShape.sheet_diagonal`: The diagonal point of the sheet: a ⊕ a = c exactly when ψ a + ψ a = ψ c (so a is the flow carrying half of ψ c back out).
- `RungShape.sheet_dial`: Corner-dial slice: a ◯[t] b = c is b = (c − a)/(1 + t·a).
- `RungShape.sheet_edges`: Every slice passes through (c, 0) and (0, c): the sheet's two fixed edges.
- `RungShape.sheet_iff_line`: A point is on the sheet (rung value c) exactly when its lens image is on the straight line u + v = ψ c.
- `RungShape.sheet_rung0`: Rung 0 slice: a + b = c is the straight line b = c − a.
- `RungShape.sheet_rung1`: Rung 1 slice: a ◯ b = c is the hyperbola b = (c − a)/(1 + a).
- `RungShape.sheet_rung1_lens`: Rung 1 with no logarithms: its lens is x ↦ 1 + x into multiplication, and the slice a ◯ b = c is the "straight line" (1 + a)(1 + b) = 1 + c of that lens.
- `RungShape.twist_dial`: The corner dial a + b + t·ab has twist t (the corner weight).
- `RungShape.twist_rung1`: Mixed second difference of the circle operation (called mult-add before 1.0) = h·k, the area of the corner rectangle. (Rung 0 has twist 0: a flat plane. Rung 1 has twist 1: the saddle.)

## FusedAlgebra.Topics.SeveralWaves (23)

- `SeveralWaves.config_one_rule`: … and they obey the rule
- `SeveralWaves.config_one_weights`: waves (0, 2), (1, 5), (3, −1), (7, 4): the weights
- `SeveralWaves.config_two_rule`
- `SeveralWaves.config_two_weights`: waves (1, −3), (2, 6), (4, 0), (9, 5): the weights
- `SeveralWaves.cross_ratio_affine`: an affine change p ↦ λp + μ scales numerator and denominator by λ² alike
- `SeveralWaves.cross_ratio_inversion`: p ↦ 1/p keeps the cross ratio (cross-multiplied, with p ↦ 1/p written as p ↦ P/p on the common denominator P = a_i b_i a_j b_j)
- `SeveralWaves.cross_ratio_minus_one`: numerator minus denominator of the cross ratio: A − 1 carries (a_i − b_i)(a_j − b_j)
- `SeveralWaves.excess_agree`: the two counts agree for every number of waves
- `SeveralWaves.excess_curves`: Riemann's period numbers minus curves, doubled: the same excess
- `SeveralWaves.excess_from_four`: the excess is zero up to three waves and positive from four
- `SeveralWaves.excess_kdv`: the symmetric KdV waves: g − 1 numbers, excess (g − 1)(g − 2)/2
- `SeveralWaves.excess_points`: pair weights minus the numbers 2g points carry, doubled
- `SeveralWaves.free_iff_trivial`: the pair weight is 1 exactly when one of the two waves is trivial
- `SeveralWaves.kdv_cross_ratio`: the KdV case q = −p: the cross ratio becomes ((p₁ − p₂)/(p₁ + p₂))²
- `SeveralWaves.kdv_excess_from_three`: for KdV it is positive already from three waves
- `SeveralWaves.kp_dispersion`: every pair of points p, q gives a wave: (p − q, p² − q², p³ − q³) solves the dispersion
- `SeveralWaves.kp_pair_weight`: Hirota's two-wave condition: P(k₁ + k₂)·A + P(k₁ − k₂) = 0, with A the cross ratio (written with denominators cleared)
- `SeveralWaves.resonance_iff`: the pair weight is 0 exactly when the waves share a point (resonance)
- `SeveralWaves.rule4_all_two`: the rule is a real constraint: an array of all 2s breaks it, so no four waves on a line have every pair weight 2
- `SeveralWaves.rule4_second_value`: five weights fix the sixth up to two values: with the first five of configuration one, both 5/2 (the actual weight) and 92/77 satisfy the rule
- `SeveralWaves.tau4_exclusion`: weight 0 between waves 1 and 2: no term has both of them on
- `SeveralWaves.tau4_free`: every pair weight 1: four separate circle operations
- `SeveralWaves.tau4_splits`: a fourth wave with weight 1 to the others splits off as its own circle-operation factor

## FusedAlgebra.Topics.ShapeDependencies (21)

- `ShapeDependencies.addition_is_dial_zero`
- `ShapeDependencies.circ_needs_add_and_mul`: the circle operation needs addition and multiplication, nothing else
- `ShapeDependencies.cusp_collinear`: the cusp y² = x³ on the line y = m x: (m², m³); collinear ⇔ m₁m₂ + m₁m₃ + m₂m₃ = 0, i.e. 1/m₁ + 1/m₂ + 1/m₃ = 0: addition
- `ShapeDependencies.cusp_is_addition`: cleared of denominators: m₂m₃ + m₁m₃ + m₁m₂ = m₁m₂m₃ (1/m₁ + 1/m₂ + 1/m₃)
- `ShapeDependencies.law_is_addition_plus_corner`
- `ShapeDependencies.merged_is_addition`: at u = v the law is the border law, and the lens x ↦ x/(1 − u x) makes it addition: cross-multiplied form of N(a ⊕ b) = N(a) + N(b)
- `ShapeDependencies.nodal_collinear`: collinearity determinant of three nodal points: it vanishes exactly on m₁m₂ + m₁m₃ + m₂m₃ + 1 = 0 (for distinct slopes)
- `ShapeDependencies.nodal_law_is_circ`
- `ShapeDependencies.nodal_negation`: negation (x, y) ↦ (x, −y) sends m to −m, which inverts φ: so P ⊕ Q has φ = φ(P)·φ(Q), and in a = φ − 1 that is the circle operation (the lift)
- `ShapeDependencies.nodal_on_curve`: the point on y² = x³ + x² on the line y = m x through the node
- `ShapeDependencies.nodal_phi`: the same factor in φ = (m − 1)/(m + 1), cleared of denominators: collinear ⇔ φ₁φ₂φ₃ = 1
- `ShapeDependencies.turn_den`
- `ShapeDependencies.turn_disc`
- `ShapeDependencies.turn_keeps_border`: the border (disc = 0) is fixed by the turn
- `ShapeDependencies.turn_num`
- `ShapeDependencies.two_walls_difference`: lens factors (1 − u a)(1 − u b) and (1 − v a)(1 − v b): their difference is the wall gap times the law's numerator, for the law with walls 1/u and 1/v (k = −(u + v))
- `ShapeDependencies.velocity_turns_to_slope`: velocity addition (k, d) = (0, −1), denominator 1 + ab, turned by i is slope addition, denominator 1 − ab: numerator i(a + b)
- `ShapeDependencies.walls_merge_disc`
- `ShapeDependencies.zoom_den`
- `ShapeDependencies.zoom_disc`
- `ShapeDependencies.zoom_num`

## FusedAlgebra.Topics.ShapeDirections (36)

- `ShapeDirections.additive_at_five`
- `ShapeDirections.additive_at_three`
- `ShapeDirections.additive_at_two`: p ∣ k: the p-fold power is p times a whole number (the law is additive at p).
- `ShapeDirections.cusp_psi3`: Cusp y² = x³ (addition): ψ₃ = 3x⁴, whose only root is the cusp itself: no point other than the start comes back in three steps.
- `ShapeDirections.fermat_three_a`
- `ShapeDirections.fermat_three_b`
- `ShapeDirections.glue_commutes`: Gluing a ↦ a ◯ (q − 1) commutes with the law, for every q.
- `ShapeDirections.glue_descends`: The law descends to the quotient: glued inputs give a glued output, whatever the gluings. So the torus ℂ*/q^ℤ carries the circle operation for every nome, and the nome is invisible near 0.
- `ShapeDirections.glue_twice`: Two gluings are one gluing by the product: the gluings form the group q^ℤ (in circle-operation form).
- `ShapeDirections.graph_weight_survives`: On the graph the weight survives: if a rescaling of the sites (z ↦ λz) and of the whole sum (by m) turns the coefficients for weight k into those for weight k', then k = k'.
- `ShapeDirections.jet_rank`
- `ShapeDirections.ladder_forces_c3`: The ladder at fourth order forces c₃ of any rung with c₂ = s/2.
- `ShapeDirections.ladder_forces_c4`: The ladder at fifth order then forces c₄.
- `ShapeDirections.ladder_jets_commute`: ...and the forced jets do satisfy the ladder (so the rung exists to this order).
- `ShapeDirections.lens_jets_at_minus`: The jets of E^{−s} are the forced jets with s replaced by −s.
- `ShapeDirections.mult_at_three`: p ∤ k: the p-fold power is k^{p−1}x^p plus p times a whole number, and k^{p−1} is 1 more than a multiple of p (shown for p = 3; the law is multiplicative at p, height 1).
- `ShapeDirections.mult_at_two`
- `ShapeDirections.node_psi3`: Node y² + xy = x³ (the circle operation): ψ₃ = x³(3x + 1). Besides the node (x = 0) the root x = −1/3.
- `ShapeDirections.node_three_points`: At x = −1/3 the node's equation y² + xy − x³ = 0 reads y² − y/3 + 1/27 = 0, whose discriminant 1/9 − 4/27 = −1/27 is not 0: two points. With the start, three: the circle operation's three cube roots of 1. (The Tate torus has nine: see checks.py, 5a.)
- `ShapeDirections.pair_weight_survives`: No separate rescaling of the waves changes the pair weight: if τ_A(λ₁w₁, λ₂w₂) = τ_{A'}(w₁, w₂) for all w₁, w₂, then λ₁ = λ₂ = 1 and A = A'. (A single law's weight is units; a pair weight is not.)
- `ShapeDirections.path3_sum`: The list of all eight occupation patterns adds up to the closed form.
- `ShapeDirections.periods_outnumber_curves`: Period matrices for g waves have g(g+1)/2 entries; curves of genus g have 3g − 3 moduli. The first exceeds the second exactly from g = 4 on (checked for 2 ≤ g < 40): Schottky's problem.
- `ShapeDirections.pseries_five`
- `ShapeDirections.pseries_three`
- `ShapeDirections.pseries_two`
- `ShapeDirections.qcount_is_weight`: The q of the q-integers (and of the Potts colours) is the weight plus one.
- `ShapeDirections.ruler_den`: Cross-multiplied denominator: (1+ca)(1+cb) − q·ab is the denominator of N applied to F_{p+2c, q+pc+c²}(a, b). Together with ruler_num: N⁻¹(F_{p,q}(N a, N b)) = F_{p+2c, q+pc+c²}(a,b).
- `ShapeDirections.ruler_keeps_disc`: The ruler keeps the discriminant: (p+2c)² − 4(q+pc+c²) = p² − 4q. The ruler's orbits are the parabolas q = (p² − D)/4, one for each D.
- `ShapeDirections.ruler_num`: Cross-multiplied numerator of F_{p,q}(N a, N b) with N(x) = x/(1+cx): it is the numerator of F_{p+2c, ·}(a, b).
- `ShapeDirections.rung_crossings`: The rung crosses the plane at rungs 0 and 1 (r₃ = 0 there), crossing, not touching: dr₃/ds = −(2s−1)/12 is +1/12 at s = 0 and −1/12 at s = 1.
- `ShapeDirections.rung_disc_is_height`: Through third order the rung's discriminant is its height: r₁² − 4r₂ = s. So rung s looks, to third order, like the law with one period 2πi/√s (rung 1: 2πi, the circle operation; rung 0: no period; rung −1: real period 2π, a circle).
- `ShapeDirections.rung_leaves_plane`: Every point of the landscape plane has rate 1 + p a + q a² exactly, so r₃ = 0 there; the rung's r₃ = −s(s−1)/12 differs from 0 at s = 2, s = −1 and s = 1/2.
- `ShapeDirections.rung_rate_inverts`: The rung-s law is plain addition in the ruler ℓ = E^{−s}, whose jets are the forced ones at −s: ℓ₂ = −s/2, ℓ₃ = s(3s+1)/12, ℓ₄ = −s(2s+1)(3s+1)/48. Its rate R = 1/ℓ′ has coefficients r₁ = s, r₂ = s(s−1)/4, r₃ = −s(s−1)/12: these satisfy ℓ′·R = 1 through a³.
- `ShapeDirections.star_def`
- `ShapeDirections.weight_is_units`: Zooming the inputs by λ and the weight by 1/λ gives the same law: the weight is units.
- `ShapeDirections.zoom_disc`: Units zoom the discriminant: (λp)² − 4(λ²q) = λ²(p² − 4q). Only its sign is free of units.

## FusedAlgebra.Topics.SizeDialResidual (18)

- `SizeDialResidual.S2_eq_S`
- `SizeDialResidual.alt_doubling`: **Doubling law (η = (1 − 2c)·ζ in finite form).** With c = 2^(−s): the "at most once" share is the whole minus twice c times the first half.
- `SizeDialResidual.alt_eq_sum_sub_evens`: **The finite η–ζ law.** Alternating sum = plain sum − 2 × even terms.
- `SizeDialResidual.at_most_m`: **At most m copies.** (1 − x)(1 + x + … + x^m) = 1 − x^(m+1); m = 1 is the circle operation's "at most once", m → ∞ is full repetition.
- `SizeDialResidual.at_most_one`: m = 1 is the circle-operation factor.
- `SizeDialResidual.distinct_times_doubled`: Subsets times multisets-at-doubled-size: element by element, ∏(1 + x) · ∏(1 − x²)⁻¹… in the finite form ∏(1 + x) · ∏(1 − x) = ∏(1 − x²).
- `SizeDialResidual.even_half`: **Class group of order 2.** The principal part (even number of non-principal primes) is half of ∏(1 + x) + ∏(1 − x): the sign dial is the missing piece.
- `SizeDialResidual.evens_scale`: If doubling scales a weight by c, the even terms are c times the first half.
- `SizeDialResidual.prod_minus_even_odd`
- `SizeDialResidual.prod_plus_even_odd`
- `SizeDialResidual.sharp_weight_commutes`: **Only a sharp count commutes.** If w·w = w (w is 0 or 1 in a domain), weighing and merging commute.
- `SizeDialResidual.stacked_dials`: Dial 1/2 under weight w is the dial w/2: the uniform dial and the weight multiply.
- `SizeDialResidual.twisted_half`: **The half under a twist.** If (1 + k·h)² = 1 + k then k·(h ◯ₖ h) = k.
- `SizeDialResidual.two_sign_dials`
- `SizeDialResidual.two_sign_dials_example`: Two non-principal primes make a principal product (the xy term), one alone does not.
- `SizeDialResidual.weight_is_twist`: **A size weight is a twist.** Weighing two dials by w and combining by the circle operation is the twisted circle operation at k = w, scaled by w.
- `SizeDialResidual.weight_one`: Weight 1 is the plain count.
- `SizeDialResidual.weighted_merge_gap`: **Weighing does not commute with merging.** Union of the weighted dials vs the weighted union dial: the gap is t·s·w·(w − 1).

## FusedAlgebra.Topics.SolitonsRungs (26)

- `SolitonsRungs.circK_three_corner`: the circle operation with dial k, applied to three numbers, has triple corner k², not k³: it is the circle operation rescaled, while solitons weight every pair
- `SolitonsRungs.face_iff`: two waves: the exponent n₁(n₁−1) + n₂(n₂−1) vanishes exactly on the unit square {0,1}², the corners of the circle-operation cube
- `SolitonsRungs.four_body_half`: the quartic is not zero at s = ½ (16 × its value there, written with S = 2s = 1, is −2): so the three-body meeting at s = ½ does not survive to four bodies
- `SolitonsRungs.four_body_roads`: four bodies: 2·(soliton corner A⁶ − rung corner) factors as s(s−1)·(quartic)
- `SolitonsRungs.glue_is_circ`: multiplying by q in the w picture is the circle operation by q − 1 in the 1+x picture
- `SolitonsRungs.kdv_weight_below_one`: for positive wave numbers, (k₁ − k₂)² < (k₁ + k₂)²: the weight A is below 1
- `SolitonsRungs.kdv_weight_nonneg`
- `SolitonsRungs.kdv_weight_zero`: the weight is 0 exactly when the speeds match
- `SolitonsRungs.orbit_product`: a truncated theta product: (1+w)(1+wq)(1+vq), v standing for 1/w, is 1 + the circle operation of three points of w's q-orbit
- `SolitonsRungs.orbit_product_q0`: and at q = 0 only the soliton factor 1 + w is left
- `SolitonsRungs.rung_three_body`: the rung flow's three-body term: triple corner minus (pair)², times 2, is s(s−1)
- `SolitonsRungs.rung_three_body_zero`: on whole numbers the rung flow has no three-body term only at rungs 0 and 1
- `SolitonsRungs.soliton_vs_circK_corner`: the two agree exactly when k³ = k², i.e. k = 0 or k = 1 (rung 0 or rung 1)
- `SolitonsRungs.tau2_excess`: read in the 1+x picture, the weight is the circle operation plus an excess (A − 1) per pair
- `SolitonsRungs.tau2_free`: weight 1: no interaction, the tau function is the plain circle-operation lift (1+a)(1+b)
- `SolitonsRungs.tau2_is_circK`
- `SolitonsRungs.tau2_merge`: weight 0: equal speeds, the two exponentials just add (rung 0)
- `SolitonsRungs.tau2_scaled`: for two solitons the law is rung 1 in a rescaled coordinate: A·(τ − 1) = (A a) ◯ (A b)
- `SolitonsRungs.tau3_equal_corner`: equal weights k: the triple corner is k³ (three pairs)
- `SolitonsRungs.tau3_free`
- `SolitonsRungs.tau3_splits`: a soliton that interacts with nobody (weights 1) factors off
- `SolitonsRungs.theta2_face`: at q₁ = q₂ = 0 the theta series is the circle-operation cube 1 + (w₁ ◯[A] w₂)
- `SolitonsRungs.theta2_free_face`: with A = 1 the two-wave theta splits into two one-wave pieces (no interaction)
- `SolitonsRungs.three_body_roads`: three bodies: 2·(soliton corner − rung corner) = s(2s−1)(s−1): the roads meet at s = 0, ½, 1
- `SolitonsRungs.tri_nonneg`
- `SolitonsRungs.tri_zero_iff`: the lattice weight q^(n(n−1)/2) is free of q only for n = 0 and n = 1

## FusedAlgebra.Topics.SpinningRiver (14)

- `CircleOp.SpinningRiver.area_law`: **Area law (D1a).** A shell turning at rate Ω has turn f = Ω (r²+b²) sin²θ; its clock gap 2π f is 2 Ω times the area π (r²+b²) sin²θ of the latitude circle (π kept as a symbol).
- `CircleOp.SpinningRiver.cellMap_mul`: **Two cell maps compose by adding their shears** (boosts and turns at the flat middle add): (I + S₁)(I + S₂) = I + S₁ + S₂.
- `CircleOp.SpinningRiver.clock_multiply`: **Clock rates multiply (O1, rule B).** Each body alone slows clocks by 1 − 2U + 2U² at slow-orbit order. Multiplying the two bodies' factors gives the one-body form with U = U₁ + U₂, cross term 4U₁U₂ included, plus only terms of higher order (U₁U₂ times another U).
- `CircleOp.SpinningRiver.complex_distance`: **The complex distance (S1a).** With an element i, i·i = −1, and a lattice point x² + y² = (r² + a²) s², z = r c, c² + s² = 1: the squared distance to the source moved up the axis by i·a is (r − i a c)².
- `CircleOp.SpinningRiver.conj_product`: **Its real part gives the speed law (S2a).** (r − i a c)(r + i a c) = r² + a² c² = ρ², so M/(r − i a c) = M (r + i a c)/ρ², whose real part is M r/ρ².
- `CircleOp.SpinningRiver.horizon_rate`: **The horizon turns at the shell rate (D6b).** Where r² − 2 M r + a² = 0, the dragging rate −g_tφ / g_φφ equals a / (r² + a²), written without division: (−g_tφ) (r² + a²) = a g_φφ.
- `CircleOp.SpinningRiver.kerr_period`: **Kerr's circular-orbit period, cleared of roots.** With r = s², M = m²: m·(s⁶ − m²a²) = (m s³ − m² a)(s³ + m a), so T = 2π (s⁶ − m²a²)/(m s³ − m² a) = 2π (s³/m + a).
- `CircleOp.SpinningRiver.ray_norm`: **The ray has unit length (T1).** For real p, q with k = p + q × p (before dividing by |p|²): |p + q × p|² = (p·p)(1 + q·q) − (p·q)². With p·p − q·q = 1 and p·q = 0 (E = p + iq a complex unit vector) this is (p·p)², so k/|p|² is a unit vector.
- `CircleOp.SpinningRiver.rim_at_light`: **Rim at light speed (D4).** The innermost shell turns at Ω₀ with Ω₀ b² = κ; if its rim speed Ω₀ b is 1, then b = κ.
- `CircleOp.SpinningRiver.shear_layers`: The cell map keeps volume: no layer but the 1 survives (P = Q = R = 0 for the shear).
- `CircleOp.SpinningRiver.shear_sq`: **The cell map is a dual number:** its shear part squares to zero.
- `CircleOp.SpinningRiver.strut_push`: **The strut's push (B4).** With e^γ = (ℓ² − (M₁+M₂)²)/(ℓ² − (M₁−M₂)²) on the strut, (e^−γ − 1)/4 = M₁M₂/(ℓ² − (M₁+M₂)²): the numerators differ by exactly 4 M₁M₂.
- `CircleOp.SpinningRiver.tphi_block`: **The t–φ block (D6a).** With V = v², V ρ² = 2 M r, ρ² = r² + a² c², c² + s² = 1: g_tφ² − g_tt g_φφ = s² (r² − 2 M r + a²), where g_tt = V − 1, g_tφ = −V a s², g_φφ = (r²+a²) s² + V a² s⁴.
- `CircleOp.SpinningRiver.velocity_addition`: **Two bodies combine by velocity addition (B3).** Cross-multiplied form of (1 − u)/(1 + u) = ((1 − u₁)/(1 + u₁))·((1 − u₂)/(1 + u₂)) for u = (u₁ + u₂)/(1 + u₁u₂): on the axis each body's clock factor (1 − u)/(1 + u) multiplies, and the u's add like speeds.

## FusedAlgebra.Topics.Statistics (98)

- `StatFused.Ex_add`
- `StatFused.Ex_congr`
- `StatFused.Ex_const`
- `StatFused.Ex_smul`
- `StatFused.Ex_sub`
- `StatFused.Ex_sumL`: Linearity over a finite list of functions.
- `StatFused.amh_cusp`: θ = 1: a double wall at certainty, the border parabola k² = 4q (the cusp).
- `StatFused.amh_independent`: θ = 0: independence, the union rule a ◯[−1] b.
- `StatFused.amh_lens_multiplies`: The lens M(x) = (1 − x)/(1 − θx) turns the law into multiplication, so it is associative: two independent-looking "survivals" multiply in this coordinate.
