- `StatFused.amh_line`: Every θ lands on the line k + q = −1 of the landscape plane.
- `StatFused.amh_union_is_lens`: **Union under AMH dependence is a lens law** at k = −(1 + θ), q = θ: "neither happens" follows the AMH copula, so "at least one happens" is (p + q − (1 + θ)pq)/(1 − θpq).
- `StatFused.amh_velocity`: θ = −1: walls at ±1, velocity addition, the same law as pooling opinions.
- `StatFused.amh_walls`: Its walls: 1 + k·x + q·x² = (1 − x)(1 − θx). One wall sits at certainty, x = 1; the other at 1/θ.
- `StatFused.amh_zero`: The AMH copula at θ = 0 is independence: P(both) = uv.
- `StatFused.and_ind`
- `StatFused.bayes_odds`: Bayes' rule in odds form: posterior odds = prior odds × likelihood ratio.
- `StatFused.bern_kmoment`: A coin's k-moment is p at every k: one number, every weight.
- `StatFused.bern_stats`
- `StatFused.bern_var_parity`: A coin's variance is half its self-parity: Var = ½·(p ◯[−2] p), the chance two copies differ, halved.
- `StatFused.binom_kmoment`
- `StatFused.binom_mean_var`: Binomial mean np and variance np(1 − p), from additivity alone.
- `StatFused.bp_above`
- `StatFused.bp_layers`: **Binomial = Pascal layers**: P(j heads of n) = C(n, j)·pʲ·qⁿ⁻ʲ.
- `StatFused.bp_zero_col`
- `StatFused.certainty_absorbs_iff`: **Why the line.** An "or" law must let certainty swallow everything: a ⊙ 1 = 1. A lens law does that exactly when k + q = −1, i.e. when it has a wall at 1.
- `StatFused.clt_kurt`
- `StatFused.clt_skew`: **CLT, the exact part.** For a sum of n independent copies the shape numbers fall as 1/n: skewness²(Sₙ) = skewness²(X)/n and kurtosis(Sₙ) = kurtosis(X)/n. Only the mean and variance survive, which is the normal law.
- `StatFused.complement_cancels`
- `StatFused.complement_is_inverse`: The complement is the way back: odds(1 − p) − 1 is the circle inverse of odds(p) − 1.
- `StatFused.convPow_k2`
- `StatFused.convPow_k3`
- `StatFused.convPow_k4`
- `StatFused.convPow_mass`
- `StatFused.convPow_mean`
- `StatFused.count_table`
- `StatFused.cov_is_corner_excess`: Covariance of two indicators is the corner's excess over the independent corner.
- `StatFused.de_morgan`: De Morgan: "not (A or B)" = "not A and not B"; complement x ↦ 1 − x turns ◯[−1] into ×.
- `StatFused.de_morgan_and`: The complement conjugates OR into AND: 1 − ((1−x) ◯[−1] (1−y)) = x·y.
- `StatFused.evidence_circ`: Two independent pieces of evidence: o·L₁·L₂ − 1 = (o − 1) ◯ (L₁ − 1) ◯ (L₂ − 1).
- `StatFused.fair_coin_kurt`: Fair coins: skewness 0 and kurtosis −2/n, falling to the normal's 0.
- `StatFused.gen_append`
- `StatFused.gen_conv`: Generating functions multiply under independent sums: the volume reading (1+a)(1+b).
- `StatFused.gen_is_lit_corners`
- `StatFused.gen_neg_one`
- `StatFused.gen_nshift`
- `StatFused.gen_zero`
- `StatFused.groups_map`
- `StatFused.heads_kmoment`
- `StatFused.heads_none`: t = 0 (k = −1): G(0) = P(no heads) = Π(1 − pᵢ), the union rule's survival.
- `StatFused.heads_parity`: t = −1 (k = −2): G(−1) = P(even) − P(odd) = 1 − 2·(p₁ ◯[−2] p₂ ◯[−2] …): piling-up for unequal coins.
- `StatFused.incl_excl_two`
- `StatFused.inclusion_exclusion`: **Inclusion–exclusion for n events**: 1 − P(union) is the sum over every corner g of the n-cube of the signed product Π_{i∈g}(−xᵢ).
- `StatFused.inclusion_exclusion_general`: **Inclusion–exclusion, n events, any dependence.** With total weight 1, P(no event happens) = Σ over every corner g of the cube of (−1)^|g|·P(all of g).
- `StatFused.ind_cases`
- `StatFused.k3_add`
- `StatFused.k4_add`
- `StatFused.kmoment_law`: **The dial.** If a and b are the k-moments of X and Y, read through 1 + k·a = G(1 + k), then the k-moment of X + Y is a ◯[k] b.
- `StatFused.kurt_scale`
- `StatFused.m1_append`
- `StatFused.m1_conv`: Moments of a sum: the Pascal rows 1·1, 1·2·1, 1·3·3·1, 1·4·6·4·1.
- `StatFused.m1_shift`
- `StatFused.m2_append`
- `StatFused.m2_conv`
- `StatFused.m2_shift`
- `StatFused.m3_append`
- `StatFused.m3_conv`
- `StatFused.m3_shift`
- `StatFused.m4_append`
- `StatFused.m4_conv`
- `StatFused.m4_shift`
- `StatFused.mass_append`
- `StatFused.mass_conv`: Total weight multiplies: the grid of outcomes.
- `StatFused.mass_shift`
- `StatFused.mean_add`
- `StatFused.not_ind`
- `StatFused.or_ind`
- `StatFused.or_table`: Truth tables.
- `StatFused.pool_odds`: Pooling multiplies odds: the same law in the odds coordinate.
- `StatFused.pool_velocity`: In the coordinate ρ = 2p − 1 pooling is velocity addition, the lens law at landscape point (0, −1), walls at ±1 (certainty).
- `StatFused.prodL_neg_sgn`
- `StatFused.pw_neg_one_parity`
- `StatFused.repeated_evidence`: The same evidence n times: o·Lⁿ − 1 = (o − 1) ◯ (L − 1)^(◯n), the climb (rung 2).
- `StatFused.scale_k`: Cumulant r scales as cʳ: the reason the shape terms die after standardizing.
- `StatFused.scale_m`
- `StatFused.sgn_map`
- `StatFused.skewSq_scale`
- `StatFused.sq_diff_is_xor`: The squared difference of two indicators is their XOR (k = −2).
- `StatFused.union_corners`
- `StatFused.union_disjoint`: Disjoint events (lift 0): k = 0, plain addition, Kolmogorov's axiom.
- `StatFused.union_general`: Union of two events, dependent or not: P(A ∪ B) = P(A) + P(B) − P(A ∩ B).
- `StatFused.union_lift`: **Dependence is the weight.** If the overlap is `lift` times what independence predicts, P(A ∩ B) = lift·P(A)·P(B), then P(A ∪ B) = P(A) ◯[k] P(B) with k = −lift.
- `StatFused.union_misses`: Nothing happens: 1 − union = product of the misses.
- `StatFused.union_neg_foldCirc`: The k = −1 fold is the circle operation read on the negatives.
- `StatFused.var_add`
- `StatFused.xor_complement`: XOR is self-dual under complement of both inputs.
- `StatFused.xor_ind`
- `StatFused.xor_lift`: Exclusive or with dependence: k = −2·lift.
- `StatFused.xor_table`

## FusedAlgebra.Topics.Thermodynamics (68)

- `ThermoFused.bond_infinite_T`: At infinite temperature (a = 1) a bond's Lee–Yang square is the plain circle-operation square.
- `ThermoFused.bond_zero_T`: At zero temperature (a = 0) only the diagonal corners survive.
- `ThermoFused.boson_fermion_dual`: Boson–fermion duality, one mode: Ξ_B(z) · Ξ_F(−z) = 1.
- `ThermoFused.boson_mode`: A boson mode: Ξ_B = 1/(1 − z) = 1 + circInv(−z). The excess z/(1 − z) is the way back from a negative fermion.
- `ThermoFused.carnot_fridge`: Carnot fridge: 1 + u = Th/Tc.
- `ThermoFused.carnot_series`: Carnot: η(Th,Tc) = η(Th,Tm) ◯[−1] η(Tm,Tc); the lift 1 − η is Tc/Th.
- `ThermoFused.chain3_circ`
- `ThermoFused.chain3_disc`
- `ThermoFused.chain3_factor`: It factors as (1 + z)(1 + (m − 1)z + z²) with m = a ◯ b.
- `ThermoFused.chain3_ferro_bound`: Ferromagnetic bonds (0 ≤ a, b ≤ 1) give 0 ≤ a ◯ b ≤ 3, so the quadratic's discriminant (m − 1)² − 4 = (m − 3)(m + 1) ≤ 0: its zeros lie on the unit circle (the turning country), as Lee–Yang says.
- `ThermoFused.cooling_step_pos`: One cooling step that keeps T above 0 (ratio 1 + a > 0), followed by another, still keeps T above 0.
- `ThermoFused.curzon_ahlborn_half`: Curzon–Ahlborn (efficiency at maximum power): with s·s = Tc/Th, η_CA = 1 − s and η_C = 1 − s·s. Two CA steps make one Carnot step: η_C = η_CA ◯[−1] η_CA. CA is the half-step of the k = −1 climb.
- `ThermoFused.decimation_den`: …and their sum is (X₁ + 1)(X₂ + 1), so tanh K' = tanh K₁ · tanh K₂.
- `ThermoFused.decimation_num`: Summing out the middle spin of two bonds (X = e^{2K}): the "agree" weight minus the "disagree" weight is (X₁ − 1)(X₂ − 1)…
- `ThermoFused.doppler_is_lens`: A Doppler factor D² = (1 + β)/(1 − β) is the reciprocal of the lens, so temperature ratios of boosted blackbody light multiply as boosts add.
- `ThermoFused.engine_cascade`: Two engines in series, the second running on the first's waste heat: total work / heat in = η₁ ◯[−1] η₂ (the failure-union rule).
- `ThermoFused.equipartition`: Equipartition: n quadratic degrees of freedom are n half-steps of T. If s·s = T/T₀ then 1 + (s − 1)^(◯2m) = (T/T₀)^m.
- `ThermoFused.fermi_velocity_den`: …and denominator: (t₁ + t₂)/(1 + t₁t₂) = (E₁E₂ − 1)/(E₁E₂ + 1).
- `ThermoFused.fermi_velocity_num`: Fermi function: with Boltzmann odds E = e^x the occupation is 1/(1 + E) and t = 1 − 2n = (E − 1)/(E + 1). Adding energies multiplies odds, and then t composes by velocity addition: numerator…
- `ThermoFused.fermion_cube`: …and that is the sum over every corner of the cube: every many-fermion state is one corner (one subset of occupied modes).
- `ThermoFused.fermion_lift`: Fermion grand partition function: each mode empty or full (Pauli), so Ξ = Π(1 + zᵢ) = 1 + (z₁ ◯ … ◯ zₙ).
- `ThermoFused.free_energy_adds`: Free energy (and any log) is the linear coordinate: it adds.
- `ThermoFused.fridge_cascade`: Two fridges in cascade (the second pumps the first's rejected heat): work per unit of cold heat u = W/Qc composes by the plain circle operation.
- `ThermoFused.fridge_lift`: …so 1 + u is the reciprocal of the engine's lift 1 − η.
- `ThermoFused.hess`: Hess's law: coupled reactions multiply equilibrium constants.
- `ThermoFused.ising_series`: In the chance p that two linked spins disagree (tanh K = 1 − 2p), bonds in series compose at k = −2, the parity weight (Matsui's piling-up rule).
- `ThermoFused.jarzynski_composes`: Jarzynski: for independent processes the averages ⟨e^{−βW}⟩ multiply (weights times probabilities form a grid), so ⟨e^{−βW}⟩ − 1 combines by the circle operation.
- `ThermoFused.landauer_states`: Landauer: n bits have 2^n states, 2^n − 1 = 1^(◯n).
- `ThermoFused.lens_den`
- `ThermoFused.lens_fixed_point`: The self-dual point (2D Ising critical point, tanh K_c = √2 − 1) is the fixed point of the lens: any t with t² + 2t − 1 = 0.
- `ThermoFused.lens_involution`: Kramers–Wannier duality t ↦ e^{−2K} = M(tanh K) is an involution.
- `ThermoFused.lens_num`: The lens turns velocity addition (bonds in parallel) into multiplication (bonds in series): polynomial form.
- `ThermoFused.lens_vel`
- `ThermoFused.map_mul_map`
- `ThermoFused.never_the_wall`
- `ThermoFused.outer_map`
- `ThermoFused.outer_sum`: A grid's total is its edges multiplied.
- `ThermoFused.partition_excess`: Put each ground state first with weight 1 (energies measured from it). Then the joint partition function is 1 + (zA ◯ zB), where z is the sum of the excited weights: zA is "A excited, B in its ground state", zB the reverse, and the corner zA·zB is "both excited".
- `ThermoFused.partition_multiplies`: The joint partition function of two systems is the product of theirs (the weights of joint states are products of weights).
- `ThermoFused.partition_square`: Independent systems: Z_AB = Z_A · Z_B, so Z − 1 composes by the circle operation.
- `ThermoFused.pauling_ice`: Pauling's ice: W = (3/2)^N, so W − 1 is the circle power (1/2)^(◯N).
- `ThermoFused.perfect_stage`: The k = −1 wall is the perfect engine: one perfect stage makes all perfect.
- `ThermoFused.power_sum_multiplies`: Power sums of independent sources multiply (f = x ↦ x^q is multiplicative).
- `ThermoFused.qlog_qsum`: The q-logarithm ln_q u = (u^k − 1)/k obeys ln_q(uv) = ln_q u ◯[k] ln_q v.
- `ThermoFused.ratio_series`: CA engines in series also compose by ◯[−1] (and so do Otto cycles, whose 1 − η = r^(1−γ) multiplies with the compression ratios).
- `ThermoFused.renyi_adds`: Rényi entropy log(1 + k·S)/k adds: the log of the lift.
- `ThermoFused.residual_degeneracy`: Residual entropy: ground-state degeneracies compose by the circle operation of g − 1.
- `ThermoFused.reverse_is_inverse`: Running an engine backwards is the circle inverse: u = η/(1 − η) = circInv(−η).
- `ThermoFused.second_law_composes`: The second law composes: stages below their Carnot bounds (1 − ηᵢ ≥ rᵢ, rᵢ = T_out/T_in ≥ 0) make a cascade below the overall bound 1 − r₁r₂.
- `ThermoFused.shields`: n identical plates: Rₙ = nR/(1 + (n − 1)R) has N(Rₙ) = n·N(R), so n radiation shields cut the heat by 1/(n + 1), not exponentially.
- `ThermoFused.sq_nonneg'`
- `ThermoFused.stokes_additive`: …so N turns the law into plain addition (addition in disguise).
- `ThermoFused.stokes_den`
- `ThermoFused.stokes_double_wall`: The rate 1 + k·x + q·x² at (k, q) = (−2, 1) is (1 − x)²: one double wall.
- `ThermoFused.stokes_identity`
- `ThermoFused.stokes_num`: The lens N(R) = R/(1 − R) = 1/ε − 1: polynomial pieces…
- `ThermoFused.stokes_on_border`
- `ThermoFused.stokes_wall`: A perfect mirror (R = 1) is the wall.
- `ThermoFused.sumL_map_mul`
- `ThermoFused.third_law_identity`: As T → 0 every excess goes to 0, the identity, and the identity composes to the identity: a composite's entropy also goes to 0.
- `ThermoFused.tsallis_composes`: Tsallis pseudo-additivity: if 1 + k·S is the power sum for A, for B and for the pair (whose power sum is the product), then S_AB = S_A ◯[k] S_B.
- `ThermoFused.two_spin_no_real_zero`: Two spins, ferromagnetic (0 ≤ a < 1): Z = 1 + 2az + z² has no real zero.
- `ThermoFused.unattainable`: Unattainability: any finite list of cooling steps, each short of the wall, stays short of it. T = 0 is the wall −1, and the circle operation only reaches the wall if some step is already on it.
- `ThermoFused.vel_identity`: Infinite temperature (t = 0, n = 1/2) is the identity, not a wall.
- `ThermoFused.vel_wall_neg`
- `ThermoFused.vel_wall_pos`: The two walls ±1 (n = 0 and n = 1, i.e. T = 0⁺ and T = 0⁻) absorb.
- `ThermoFused.wick_den`: …and denominator. On the k = 0 axis thermal tanh addition (two walls) becomes quantum tan addition (a turn).
- `ThermoFused.wick_num`: With i·i = −1, the law at (k, q) read in units i·a is the law at (i·k, −q): numerator…

## FusedAlgebra.Topics.Topology.CubeProofs (23)

- `CircleOp.Cube.MEq.add`
- `CircleOp.Cube.MEq.mul`
- `CircleOp.Cube.MEq.refl`
- `CircleOp.Cube.MEq.symm`
- `CircleOp.Cube.MEq.trans`
- `CircleOp.Cube.MEq.zero_of_dvd`
- `CircleOp.Cube.aks_middle`: **The AKS identity on the cube.** For n ≥ 2: every middle layer of the n-cube has a multiple of n corners exactly when n is prime. (Equivalently (1 + x)ⁿ ≡ 1 + xⁿ mod n: the circle n-th power equals the plain n-th power, which is the identity behind the AKS test.)
- `CircleOp.Cube.binomial`: **The cube's corner count.** (1 + x)ⁿ = Σₖ C(n,k) xᵏ: a box of side 1 + x cut at 1 on every axis falls into the n-cube's corner pieces.
- `CircleOp.Cube.blockCount_eq`
- `CircleOp.Cube.bsum_add`
- `CircleOp.Cube.bsum_congr`
- `CircleOp.Cube.bsum_dvd`
- `CircleOp.Cube.bsum_mul`
- `CircleOp.Cube.bsum_shift`
- `CircleOp.Cube.coprime_of_lt`
- `CircleOp.Cube.divmod_of_eq`: Reading n in base p: n = p·q + r with r < p pins down q and r.
- `CircleOp.Cube.exists_prime_factor`
- `CircleOp.Cube.fermat`: **Fermat's little theorem.** aᵖ ≡ a (mod p) for every natural number a. Each step a → a + 1 adds one circle-operation unit, and frobenius says the p-th power passes straight through it mod p.
- `CircleOp.Cube.frobenius`: **Circle powers are plain powers mod p.** (1 + x)ᵖ ≡ 1 + xᵖ: in the p-cube only the two end corners survive mod p.
- `CircleOp.Cube.layer_zero_left`
- `CircleOp.Cube.lucas`: **Lucas's theorem.** For n, k with at most f base-p digits, C(n,k) ≡ product over digits of C(nᵢ, kᵢ) (mod p).
- `CircleOp.Cube.lucas_step`: **Lucas, one step.** C(n,k) ≡ C(n div p, k div p) · C(n mod p, k mod p).
- `CircleOp.Cube.prime_dvd_middle`: **The middle of the prime cube.** For a prime p, every middle layer of the p-cube has a multiple of p corners.

## FusedAlgebra.Topics.Topology.HopfInvariantOne (17)

- `CircleOp.HopfOne.commute_equation`: ψ² ψ³ = ψ³ ψ² on a gives Adams and Atiyah's equation.
- `CircleOp.HopfOne.cp2_case`: CP² meets every hypothesis of Part 2 with n = 1, h = 1: μ₂ = C(2,2) = 1, μ₃ = C(3,2) = 3, and ψ² ψ³ = ψ³ ψ².
- `CircleOp.HopfOne.hopf_invariant_one`: **Part 2.** Commuting Adams operations, the mod-2 rule and an odd Hopf invariant h force n = 1, 2 or 4.
- `CircleOp.HopfOne.le_of_dvd_two_pow_odd`: If 2^n divides 2^e times an odd number, then n ≤ e.
- `CircleOp.HopfOne.level_three_pow`: The level of 3^n: 1 if n is odd, k + 2 if n = 2^k · odd with k ≥ 1.
- `CircleOp.HopfOne.mpow_formula`: (1 + x)^k − 1 = k x + C(k,2) x², written without division.
- `CircleOp.HopfOne.odd_mul`
- `CircleOp.HopfOne.odd_three_pow`
- `CircleOp.HopfOne.oneMod_oddPow`: Raising to an odd power keeps the level (for j ≥ 1).
- `CircleOp.HopfOne.oneMod_sq`: Squaring raises the level by one (for j ≥ 2).
- `CircleOp.HopfOne.one_two_four_work`: And the three survivors really work.
- `CircleOp.HopfOne.only_one_two_four`: **Part 1.** 2^n | 3^n − 1 forces n = 1, 2 or 4 (for n ≥ 1).
- `CircleOp.HopfOne.pow_ge`: 2^k ≥ k + 3 once k ≥ 3.
- `CircleOp.HopfOne.psi_compose`: Circle powers compose: ψ^j ψ^k = ψ^(jk), for every j, k.
- `CircleOp.HopfOne.three_pow_two_pow`: 3^(2^k) is one more than 2^(k+2) times an odd number, for k ≥ 1.
- `CircleOp.HopfOne.two_adic_split`: Every positive n is 2^k times an odd number.
- `CircleOp.HopfOne.two_pow_dvd_of_mul_odd`: Euclid's step for powers of 2: an odd factor can be cancelled.

## FusedAlgebra.Topics.Topology.KummerWilson (30)

- `CircleOp.Cube.S_add`
- `CircleOp.Cube.S_bsum`
- `CircleOp.Cube.S_mul`
- `CircleOp.Cube.S_pow`: **Inclusion–exclusion on the cube.** Σₖ (−1)ᵏ C(n,k) (c − k)ᵐ is n! when m = n and 0 when m < n. With c = n it counts the orderings of n things: corner K of the n-cube is the set of values a map is made to miss, and the signed count of maps that miss them leaves exactly the maps that miss nothing.
- `CircleOp.Cube.S_sub`
- `CircleOp.Cube.S_succ`: Pascal's rule for the signed sum: adding an axis takes a difference.
- `CircleOp.Cube.S_zero`
- `CircleOp.Cube.Val.mul`
- `CircleOp.Cube.Val.unique`
- `CircleOp.Cube.alt_layer`: Along the (p−1)-cube, the layer counts alternate ±1 mod p.
- `CircleOp.Cube.bsum_meq`
- `CircleOp.Cube.bsum_one`
- `CircleOp.Cube.carries_dsum`: Adding digit by digit: each carry turns p units of digit sum into 1.
- `CircleOp.Cube.carries_eq`
- `CircleOp.Cube.carries_zero`
- `CircleOp.Cube.dsum_eq`
- `CircleOp.Cube.dsum_succ`: Adding 1: the digit sum goes up by 1, minus p−1 for each trailing digit p−1.
- `CircleOp.Cube.dsum_zero`
- `CircleOp.Cube.dvd_fact`: n! divides into every number from 1 to n.
- `CircleOp.Cube.euclid`
- `CircleOp.Cube.fermat_unit`: Fermat for a unit: if p does not divide a, then a^(p−1) ≡ 1.
- `CircleOp.Cube.kummer`: **Kummer's theorem (1852).** The exact power of p that divides C(j+k, k), the number of corners of the (j+k)-cube with k switches on, is the number of carries when you add j and k in base p.
- `CircleOp.Cube.layer_pos`
- `CircleOp.Cube.layer_succ_self`
- `CircleOp.Cube.layer_val_dsum`: The cube count and the digit sums: (p−1)·v_p(C(j+k,k)) = s(j) + s(k) − s(j+k).
- `CircleOp.Cube.val_of_mod_eq`
- `CircleOp.Cube.val_of_mod_ne`
- `CircleOp.Cube.val_spec`
- `CircleOp.Cube.wilson`: **Wilson's theorem (Lagrange 1771).** (p − 1)! ≡ −1 (mod p) for every prime p. Lean's route: inclusion–exclusion on the (p−1)-cube counts the (p−1)! orderings; mod p every layer but the last adds exactly 1, so the count is p − 1 ≡ −1.
- `CircleOp.Cube.wilson_iff`: **Wilson's test.** For n ≥ 2, (n − 1)! ≡ −1 (mod n) exactly when n is prime.

## FusedAlgebra.Topics.Toric.ProjectivePlane (16)

- `CircleOp.Toric.P2.chart_cover`: The three charts cover P².
- `CircleOp.Toric.P2.chart_injective`: Each chart is injective.
- `CircleOp.Toric.P2.chart_trans`
- `CircleOp.Toric.P2.chart_wind`: So the winding is one well-defined map of P², read the same way in every chart.
- `CircleOp.Toric.P2.fan_count`
- `CircleOp.Toric.P2.inChart_iff`: A point of chart c lies in chart d exactly on the transition's domain.
- `CircleOp.Toric.P2.inv_inv'`
- `CircleOp.Toric.P2.inv_ne`
- `CircleOp.Toric.P2.inv_pow'`
- `CircleOp.Toric.P2.origin_in_one_chart`: Each chart's origin is one of the three fixed points [0:0:1], [1:0:0], [0:1:0], and it lies in that chart only.
- `CircleOp.Toric.P2.pow_succ_eq_zero`
- `CircleOp.Toric.P2.torus_in_all`: Points of the torus (both coordinates nonzero) lie in all three charts.
- `CircleOp.Toric.P2.trans01_circ`: In circle-operation coordinates, the chart change c0 → c1, (a, b) ↦ (1/a, b/a), is (u, w) ↦ (u⁻, w ◯ u⁻): the circle inverse, then a circle operation.
- `CircleOp.Toric.P2.trans02_circ`
- `CircleOp.Toric.P2.trans_cocycle`: The cocycle condition, from injectivity: c → d → e equals c → e on the triple overlap.
- `CircleOp.Toric.P2.trans_wind`: Windings commute with every chart change: monomial maps commute with x ↦ xⁿ.

## FusedAlgebra.Topics.Toric.ToricCharts (16)

- `CircleOp.Toric.chart1_cover`: The two charts cover P¹.
- `CircleOp.Toric.chart1_injective`
- `CircleOp.Toric.chart1_trans`
- `CircleOp.Toric.chart2_cover`: The four charts cover P¹ × P¹.
- `CircleOp.Toric.chart2_injective`
- `CircleOp.Toric.chart2_trans`: Where two charts overlap, the transition carries one chart's coordinates to the other's.
- `CircleOp.Toric.corner_all_on`: The top corner (all switches on) is the torus.
- `CircleOp.Toric.corner_scale`: Scaling by the torus (nonzero factors) never moves a point to another corner, so the corners label the torus orbits.
- `CircleOp.Toric.fan_count`: With a = q - 1 points on the circle: one torus (a²), four circles (a each), four fixed points. In ordinary coordinates it is (q + 1)².
- `CircleOp.Toric.fixed_point_in_one_chart`: Each chart's origin is one of the four fixed points, and it lies in that chart only.
- `CircleOp.Toric.inChart2_iff`
- `CircleOp.Toric.origin_in_one_chart`: The origin of chart c lies in chart c and in no other chart.
- `CircleOp.Toric.torus_in_all`: Points of the torus lie in all four charts.
- `CircleOp.Toric.torus_in_both`: Points of the circle (nonzero, finite) lie in both charts.
- `CircleOp.Toric.trans1_cocycle`: The cocycle condition: c → d → e equals c → e, so the gluing is consistent on every triple overlap.
- `CircleOp.Toric.trans2_cocycle`

## FusedAlgebra.Topics.Tunnelling (10)

- `Tunnelling.continuum_log_unique`
- `Tunnelling.continuum_mean_unique`
- `Tunnelling.hyperbolic_direct_wins`
- `Tunnelling.mean_law_curvature`
- `Tunnelling.pair_identity`
- `Tunnelling.piling_up`

## FusedAlgebra.Topics.TwistedBoosts (36)

- `TwistedBoosts.C.ext'`
- `TwistedBoosts.C.ext'_iff`
- `TwistedBoosts.M.ext'`
- `TwistedBoosts.M.ext'_iff`
- `TwistedBoosts.boost_det`: For two boosts: |1 − q·ū·v|² + q·|u + v|² = (1 + q|u|²)(1 + q|v|²).
- `TwistedBoosts.boost_mul`: **Two boosts**: B(u)·B(v) = [[X + iY, u + v], [−q·(ū + v̄), X − iY]].
- `TwistedBoosts.boost_mul_real`: In one dimension at q = −1 this is the relativity page's (1 + uv)·B(u ⊕ v).
- `TwistedBoosts.boost_real`: At q = −1 on the real axis it is the relativity page's [[1, u], [u, 1]].
- `TwistedBoosts.boost_shape`: Boosts have the shape.
- `TwistedBoosts.collinear_no_twist`: Collinear boosts never twist.
- `TwistedBoosts.corner_split`: **The corner splits**: ū·v = (u·v) + i(u × v).
- `TwistedBoosts.corner_swap`: The other order is the conjugate: v̄·u = conj(ū·v).
- `TwistedBoosts.cross_anti`
- `TwistedBoosts.cross_collinear`: Collinear speeds (both on the real axis) have no cross part.
- `TwistedBoosts.cross_is_area`
- `TwistedBoosts.den_swap`
- `TwistedBoosts.diag_conj`: The diagonal entries are conjugates.
- `TwistedBoosts.dot_symm`
- `TwistedBoosts.entry_br`
- `TwistedBoosts.entry_tl`: The top-left entry is 1 − q·u·v̄ and the bottom-right is 1 − q·ū·v.
- `TwistedBoosts.galilean_no_twist`: The Galilean dial q = 0 never twists, in any directions.
- `TwistedBoosts.half_kick`: The half-velocity t composed with itself gives the velocity: t ⊕ t = 2t/(1 + t²).
- `TwistedBoosts.half_kick_ruler`: On the circle-operation ruler, the velocity is the half-velocity combined with itself by the circle operation: d(t ⊕ t) = d(t) ◯ d(t). The half-velocity is half a kick.
- `TwistedBoosts.light_wall_2d`: **The light wall in two dimensions** (q = −1): |1 + ū·v|² − |u + v|² = (1 − |u|²)(1 − |v|²).
- `TwistedBoosts.order_swap`: **Order swap**: u ⊕ v = twist(u, v) · (v ⊕ u).
- `TwistedBoosts.sdet_mul`: **The determinant multiplies** (a two-square identity for each q).
- `TwistedBoosts.shape_mul`: **The shape is closed under products**: one group for each q (q = −1 the Lorentz spin group SU(1,1), q = 0 the Galilean one, q = +1 SU(2)).
- `TwistedBoosts.sphere_no_wall`: At the sphere end q = +1 there is no wall: the determinant of a boost is ≥ 1.
- `TwistedBoosts.star_def`
- `TwistedBoosts.twist_eq`: The twist is exactly the unit-circle point (cos ω, sin ω) of section 3.
- `TwistedBoosts.twist_from_corner`: The twist's tangent, written with the circle-operation corner: Y/X = q(u × v)/(1 − q(u·v)), and both parts come from the one corner ū·v.
- `TwistedBoosts.twist_half_angle`: **The half-angle of the twist**: tan(ω/2) = sin ω/(1 + cos ω) = Y/X = q(u × v)/(1 − q(u·v)).
- `TwistedBoosts.twist_on_circle`: It lies on the unit circle.
- `TwistedBoosts.twist_trivial_iff`: **No twist exactly when the cross part is switched off**: X + iY = X − iY ⇔ q·(u × v) = 0.
- `TwistedBoosts.twist_turns`: The twist turns X − iY into X + iY: e^{iω}·(X − iY) = X + iY.
- `TwistedBoosts.under_light_2d`: **Below light stays below light, in any directions**: |u|, |v| < 1 ⇒ |u + v| < |1 + ū·v|, so |u ⊕ v| < 1.

## FusedAlgebra.Topics.WarpWormhole (6)

- `WarpWormhole.cross_ratio_cocycle`: One minus the cross-ratio of A = [a₁, a₂] and B = [b₁, b₂] is N/D with N = (a₁ − b₂)(a₂ − b₁), D = (a₁ − b₁)(a₂ − b₂). For next-door B₁ = [b₁, b₂], B₂ = [b₂, b₃]: N₁ N₂ D₁₃ = N₁₃ D₁ D₂, i.e. (1 − x₁)(1 − x₂) = 1 − x₁₃.
- `WarpWormhole.glue_is_union`: And (1 − x)(1 − y) = 1 − z says z = x ∘₋₁ y: the union rule.
- `WarpWormhole.river_light`: Null directions of −dt² + (dx − v dt)²: (w − v)² − 1 factors as (w − (v+1))(w − (v−1)).
- `WarpWormhole.shear_cross_term`: Strain of the river v = (v_x(x,y,z), 0, 0): K = [[p, s₁, s₂], [s₁, 0, 0], [s₂, 0, 0]]. Its cross term (sum of the three principal 2×2 minors) is −(s₁² + s₂²).
- `WarpWormhole.tfd_cross_ratio_split`: With p = e^{k x₁}, q = e^{k x₂}, u = e^{k t} (k = 2π/β), the left points are p/u, q/u and the right points −q u, −p u. Cleared of the common factor u², the cross-ratio's denominator (p + q u²)(q + p u²) splits as (p − q)² u² + p q (1 + u²)²: the first part is the numerator z₁₂ z₃₄ (times u²), the second is 1 − η's numerator. So the odds η/(1−η) = (p−q)² u² / (p q (1+u²)²) = sinh²(πℓ/β) / cosh²(2πt/β).
- `WarpWormhole.zero_expansion_cross_term`: If the three strain rates sum to zero (no volume change), twice their cross term is minus the sum of their squares, so the Eulerian energy density e2/8π can never be positive.

## FusedAlgebra.Topics.WhyI (21)

- `WhyI.J_keeps_length`
- `WhyI.J_sq_one`
- `WhyI.J_sq_two`
- `WhyI.chain_three`
- `WhyI.chain_two`
- `WhyI.comm_antisymm`
- `WhyI.comm_diag_zero`
- `WhyI.comm_square_negative`: So the commutator is c·J with c = comm12, and (c·J)² = −c²: the product of its two off-diagonal entries is −c², the determinant-side fact behind eigenvalues ±ic.
- `WhyI.dial_line_circ`: ... and the shift part of the composite is the circle operation.
- `WhyI.dial_line_closed`: Composing (λ₁, s₁) after (λ₂, s₂) gives (λ₁λ₂, λ₁s₂ + s₁). On the dial-k line it stays on the line.
- `WhyI.dial_twist`: Circle-operation element a at dial k is the pair (k·a, a). Two dials twist by (k − k₂)·a·b.
- `WhyI.levy_three`: Three steps v₁, v₂, v₃ from the origin: the sum of the pairwise twists equals the shoelace sum of the polygon 0, v₁, v₁+v₂, v₁+v₂+v₃ (twice its signed area).
- `WhyI.mirror_conj_J_one`: R J R = −J, by components: R(J(R(x, y))) = (−y, −x)·… = −J(x, y).
- `WhyI.mirror_conj_J_two`
- `WhyI.mirror_flips_omega`: Mirror R(x, y) = (x, −y).
- `WhyI.omega_eq_dot_J`: The compatible-triple relation ω(u, v) = g(Ju, v).
- `WhyI.omega_u_Ju`: J is "positive" for the orientation: ω(u, Ju) = |u|² ≥ 0.
- `WhyI.same_dial_no_twist`
- `WhyI.survival_two`: k = −1 is survival analysis: the chance of the event over two periods is 1 − (1 − h₁)(1 − h₂).
- `WhyI.wedge_antisymm`
- `WhyI.wedge_self`

## FusedAlgebra.Topics.YangMillsGap (10)

- `YangMillsGap.abelian_plaquette_order_free`: 4. Abelian plaquette: shared dial k, any order of the four links gives the same holonomy.
- `YangMillsGap.casimir_ratio`: 6. Rung costs. SU(2): C₂(j) = j(j+1); C₂(1)/C₂(1/2) = 8/3. U(1): rung n costs n².
- `YangMillsGap.circle_class_no_wall`: 1'. Over the rationals: d > 0 and Δ < 0 force v(x) > 0 for every x (no wall, circle class).
- `YangMillsGap.generator_square`: 1. Completing the square for the dial generator.
- `YangMillsGap.half_rung_tie`: 5a. At half a rung the rungs n and −1−n tie (twist t = 1/2, written with 2t = 1).
- `YangMillsGap.lift_order_gap`: 7. Non-abelian links: the order gap of the lifts is k² times the commutator. Stated for an arbitrary (possibly non-commutative) ring via `Lean.Grind.Ring`. (Compiled Nov 26: the products are expanded by hand before `grind`, which does not normalize non-commutative products.)
- `YangMillsGap.parity_dial_xor`: 3. The parity dial k = −2 on {0, 1} is XOR (the Z₂ gauge group).
- `YangMillsGap.rat_sq_nonneg`: Squares are nonnegative over Rat (same proof as DialPointProcesses.sq_nonneg').
- `YangMillsGap.twisted_gap`: 5b. The twisted gap between rung −1 and rung 0: (1 − t)² − t² = 1 − 2t.
- `YangMillsGap.u1_rung_ratio`

## FusedAlgebra.Topics.ZoomDial (10)

- `ZoomDial.half_box_walls`: The fractal page's sets A to E at s = 1/2: the natural weights √rᵢ add to 1, so x(1/2) = 1. (A: two at 1/4; B, C: three at 1/9; D: 9/100 and 49/100; E: four at 1/16.)
- `ZoomDial.halving_weight`: Zoom 1/2 in dimension β (whole β) weighs (1/2)^β, the size dial's 2^(−β).
- `ZoomDial.sumL_flatMap_cons`
- `ZoomDial.uneven_depth_three`: Uneven set D at depth 3: the eight words still weigh exactly 1.
- `ZoomDial.wall_every_depth`: **At the wall every depth weighs 1:** if the pieces' weights add to 1, so do the words of every depth.
- `ZoomDial.wall_partial`: At the wall the partial sums are n: the series never settles.
- `ZoomDial.word_series`: **All depths together are the k = −1 element:** (1 − x)·Σ_{j<n} xʲ = 1 − xⁿ.
- `ZoomDial.words_weigh`: **Words weigh x(s)ⁿ.** The words of depth n, each weighing the product of its pieces, add up to (Σ pieces)ⁿ.
- `ZoomDial.zoom_compose`: **Zooms compose by multiplying:** zoom r₁ then r₂ weighs (r₁r₂)ⁿ = r₁ⁿr₂ⁿ (n a whole-number exponent; checks.py covers real s).
- `ZoomDial.zoom_is_twist`: **Zoom is twist.** Shrinking two dial values by the natural weight w = r^d and combining by the circle operation is the twisted circle operation at k = w, scaled by w.
